mr president, i would like to draw your attention to the situation at the eastern border of the baltic states. for many years now, before each christmas, there has been a continual blocking of trucks from all over europe at the eu’s eastern external borders. this situation causes serious disturbances both for the carriers and for the eu companies. it places even more strain on relations between the eu and neighbouring third countries. i believe that the newly established european external action service needs to react to this current situation and to cooperate more actively with the third countries involved to resolve this situation.
mr president, in recent days there have been a number of complaints about the ineffectiveness and slowness of the response to the haitian crisis. many point to the increasing insecurity, the growing despair among those who survived the quake, and the appalling lack of food and clothing. it is true that these problems exist and we need to solve them as soon as possible. however, we should applaud the engagement of various international actors, ranging from governments to civil society organisations and to individuals worldwide. the european union is certainly among those who have expressed their wholehearted solidarity with the haitian people. again, many have criticised the supposedly slow reaction of the eu to the crisis even today, but the eu is not only about decisions made in brussels. national governments have already sent thousands of men and women to help the haitians and have allocated millions of euros in aid. the role of the eu should be concentrated more on medium- and long-term aid and aimed at the reconstruction of haitian towns and villages, of their infrastructure, schools and hospitals. the eu can certainly be the forum, the umbrella under which europe’s medium- and long-term aid can be distributed and coordinated. the pledge recently made by the commission and the member states to earmark almost eur 500 million is certainly a big and significant step in this direction, and we should all stand behind it.
in fact, today, after the treaty of lisbon, the most serious problem has probably just happened and we need to understand the situation of the high representative, or high representative, who at the very beginning of his mandate has to face a rather difficult task, one that i would even say has never been a practical one before. yes, indeed, today we see certain shortcomings, that in the future we will need a more prepared joint european union force, capable of participating in rescue operations, and i think that the biggest contribution today would be that the money that was mentioned today reaches the republic of haiti as soon as possible, that it is implemented as soon as possible and spent on the reconstruction of the infrastructure itself, even as the president of haiti mentioned, that it is intended to be used for perhaps also removing those non-smoking fireworks that are lying in the streets today.
madam president, i have a short comment and two questions to put to baroness ashton. it seems that the eu is now faced with a dilemma. on the one hand, we need to support more robust action against terrorists who directly pose danger to the security of european citizens. various islamist extremist groups in yemen have been more active than ever before, and al-qa’ida sees yemen as one of its most important areas to plan attacks on western targets and to train militia and potential suicide bombers. the failed operation against the northwest airlines plane is the most recent example of the threat we face. on the other hand, one should know that the yemeni authorities have more than once been accused by various human rights organisations of torture, inhuman treatment and extrajudicial executions. arbitrary arrests of citizens and searches of homes are widespread, and the need to fight terrorism has been cited as the main excuse for such unlawful actions. in this context, baroness ashton, referring to the council conclusions on yemen, what kind of support has the eu to offer to yemen in terms of fighting terrorism? in addition, the eu considers that there is no military solution for the crisis in the area. at the same time, washington has signed the agreement with the yemeni authorities on closer military cooperation. so, i would appreciate your opinion and the position of the european union on the decision by the americans to engage more actively in yemen to fight terrorists, especially by signing the agreement on military intelligence and training.
i agree with the commission and my colleagues that the situation in yemen is tense. the country is devastated and impoverished by endless guerrilla wars and separatist fighting, and the people are suffering from poverty. such economic and political instability has already caused concern in the neighbouring countries of the arabian peninsula and threatens not only regional but also global security. the recent increase in the activity of terrorist groups is cause for concern. consider, too, the failed attempt to blow up a plane bound for the united states and the threats against embassies in yemen. america has already declared that it will pay particular attention to the situation in this country, and i therefore believe that it is the duty of the european parliament, the european commission and other institutions, especially after the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, to take joint and coordinated action with the international community in the implementation of the common foreign policy.
in recent years, the european union has significantly strengthened its links and influence in the middle east. the european union has always paid close attention to iraq and has been involved, together with the international community, in both reconstruction efforts and peacekeeping and security missions. the country is still in the midst of a power struggle between different factions, which is hampering the implementation of reforms in various areas. the ban on 14 parties from participating in the upcoming elections is also a cause for concern. the european union and other states have a duty to demand and, if necessary, to help iraq to respect its international obligations and respect human rights and freedoms. i think iraq is really ready for close cooperation with the european union. the eu-iraq memorandum of energy cooperation signed on monday clearly demonstrates its commitment to developing a long-term and mutually beneficial relationship. iraq is of vital importance to the european union from an energy perspective, but ensuring the security of energy supply is inextricably linked to the economic and political stability of the country, which has been under great pressure in recent times. it is a long process, of course, but i believe that the european union, as high representative, and the international community, must propose measures and solutions to ensure that human rights and the rule of law are the main pillar of the future policy in iraq.
recent events in lithuania, where an organised gang profiting from human trafficking was arrested, once again show that this crime is a widespread phenomenon, which has intensified during the economic and financial crisis. today, nearly 90 percent of trafficking victims are women and children, most of whom are victims of poverty and survival. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment from harmful substances. it is also necessary to ensure the safety and full integration of victims of trafficking in human beings. all participants, organizers, sponsors of this terrible crime must not escape responsibility.
i also hope that the spanish presidency will be a success and that, in addition to the priorities set, spain will not ignore the most pressing issue of our time, namely human rights and freedoms. after the lithuanian parliament's investigation into the cia prisons, it turned out that in another country, a european country, the infrastructure for the illegal imprisonment of a person was created. the european union and the united states are like two poles of world politics and economics, equal allies and partners, but that does not mean that in sovereign states, the cia can be the master of its own backyard. i think that the european parliament should reopen its inquiry into the illegal transport and detention of persons in european countries. the outcome of this study should be a document that commits the member states to guaranteeing the rights and freedoms of every citizen unconditionally.
i have only a minute, so i will concentrate on the rights of citizens. the spanish presidency is an opportunity to put our house in order. one example is the canary islands and the people who live there. last summer i was there to take a spanish course. to my surprise, i found out that if i take a spanish course in a school on the peninsula or in the balearic islands, the expenses are reimbursed, but if it is in a school in gran canaria, no, because it is not part of europe. it is part of the european union, but it is not part of europe, and the consequence is that language schools in these islands, even if the quality of the courses is excellent, are deprived of the opportunity... in writing. spain takes over the presidency of the european union following the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. this means that the presidency will have to work even more closely with the european parliament in implementing its ambitious programme. i agree with the main priorities of the spanish agenda on the eu 2020 strategy, on ensuring employment and social progress, on initiatives in the fields of education and innovation and on energy security issues. spain is committed to continuing the implementation of the long-term programmes it has started and it is therefore necessary to draw up a plan of specific measures for the implementation of the stockholm programme. as a member of the european parliament from lithuania, it is very important to me that the presidency continues to implement the baltic sea strategy adopted during the swedish presidency. this year is the european year for combating poverty and social exclusion. i would ask that spain, during its presidency, take the necessary measures to combat poverty and guarantee minimum social guarantees. the swedish presidency of the northern eu was a success, and i would like to wish the southern eu country a fruitful year for spain in the interests of eu citizens.
in writing. − i support this commission proposal for a temporary suspension of the application of the community customs tariff, because i believe that the eu must demonstrate, rather than merely declare, its solidarity with regions struggling with the consequences of the economic crisis. i believe that this measure is in line with the european economic recovery plan, as the economic crisis has affected different eu member states and regions in different ways, and therefore measures must be ensured to meet the specific economic needs of each member state or region. as these islands are dependent on tourism, the decline in tourist flows has created the threat of unemployment and the collapse of small and medium-sized enterprises, which would be particularly painful for the inhabitants of these remote islands. in applying the duty exemption, the community must also ensure that the measure achieves its primary objective of encouraging local entrepreneurship and helping local farmers and small and medium-sized enterprises to survive in this difficult period, and that these principles are also applied to other eu member states.
in writing. − the second revision of the cotonou agreement is taking place in a very challenging environment when the global economy is facing crisis. i believe that the revision of the agreement will allow the major principles of cooperation between the eu and the acp countries to be refreshed and reinforced. the situation has changed since the agreement was first signed and new challenges and problems have arisen. the negotiations on the agreement should encompass such extremely important points as the fight against climate change in developing countries, the practical use of immense renewable energy resources, the food crisis and the acquisition of arable land. a lot of attention should be given to migration problems. in recent years we have witnessed hundreds of young africans drowning off the shores of the eu. massive immigration is a result of failing economies, impoverishment of the people, human rights violations and many other causes. those things should be addressed clearly in the revised agreement.
madam president, i very much welcome the efforts of my colleagues insisting on a strategy for the danube region. coming from lithuania, i am very well aware that member states alone are unable to achieve significant progress in terms of economic development, environmental protection, sustainable transport and tourism, energy and many other areas. i am positive that regional strategies can be more effective and should be promoted because member states are then acting in the common interest. i wish my colleagues the determination to achieve their goals and i believe that their voice will be heard by the new commission, especially because sustainable regional development is among the top priorities on the commission’s agenda.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, the european union is now negotiating a new framework agreement with china, which will set a further path for the development of economic relations with the country. those relations are very tight, but we must not close our eyes before repeated human rights violations and with respect for the rule of law. the european union must strengthen the eu-china human rights dialogue. this dialogue, established in 2000, proved to be inefficient. the eu, and especially the high representative, should ensure coordinated and effective eu common foreign policy towards china. respect for human rights must serve as a basis for this policy.
on behalf of the s&d group if the average european is asked about what is happening in the philippines, and he probably cannot answer, and he says that the tv shows nothing, it is probably quiet there. but political killings, clan clashes, burying people alive, chainsaws, warfare - these are the daily realities of the philippines. fifty-seven people were killed recently, half of them journalists, the highest number of journalists killed even on a global scale. we call on the government, we demand that it stop these things, that it unleash its private armies and that it finally put an end to impunity. this is particularly important in the run-up to the election.
i would like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, mrs tarabella, for her work. investing in human capital and social infrastructure, enabling women and men to reach their full potential, is crucial to overcoming the economic downturn. the european union is now close to the lisbon strategy target of 60% female employment by 2010. however, there is a great disparity in the employment of women between member states. the commission and the member states must therefore take effective measures to implement the directive on equal treatment for men and women in matters of employment and occupation. today, there is a wide disparity between the pay of women and men in the member states and member states should be urged to apply the principle of equal pay for equal work. the commission has not yet submitted a proposal to review the application of the principle of equal pay for men and women. it is necessary to promote equal sharing of responsibilities in personal and family life between women and men, by a more appropriate distribution of paid and unpaid work. member states must take all possible measures to ensure the availability of childcare facilities for pre-school children. the issue of paternity leave is still unresolved and a common solution to the directive on paternity leave needs to be found as soon as possible. in addition, the commission's consultation on the new 2020 strategy does not pay sufficient attention to gender equality issues. gender equality needs to be strengthened and mainstreamed in the new strategy. in conclusion, i would like to thank the commissioner from the bottom of my heart for our longstanding cooperation in the fields of gender equality, employment and social affairs. thank you and good luck!

in writing. − inter-institutional relations between the parliament and the commission are moving in a different direction with the lisbon treaty. we parliamentarians are sending a clear message to the commission that the european parliament never again will be a mere observer but an equal player in the shaping of european policy. policy actions at the community level lack coherence and leave europe totally disabled when unexpected situations come up. and when they do come up we are unable to respond to them in an effective and coordinated manner. the president of the commission has committed himself to engage in an open, transparent and constructive relationship with the european parliament in order to set clear and feasible policy aims together and to ensure high quality legislation. it is now time for mr barroso to remember his promises and to ensure that the requests of the european parliament are clearly reflected in the framework agreement.
in writing. − i support this framework agreement because cooperation between the european parliament and the european commission is of paramount importance in strengthening the sustainability and efficiency of the european union. under the agreement, the european commission will have to respond to a request for legislation from the european parliament within one month and prepare the relevant european union law within one year. if the european commission refuses to prepare the requested act, it will have to give a full justification for its decision. until now, only the european commission could initiate european union legislation, but the treaty of lisbon provides for a european parliament majority to legislate for the european union. parliament and the commission will cooperate closely at an early stage in all cases of requests for legislative initiatives following a citizens' initiative. the european parliament's experts will also be included in the debates when international agreements are signed. the agreement will give parliament the right to participate as an observer in some of the european union's international negotiations, as well as the right to receive more information on international agreements.
in writing. − today we have approved the new composition of the european commission, although it must be admitted that the doubts raised by the commissioners-designate in their hearings have unfortunately not been completely dispelled. the commission's guidelines for action and the programmes of individual commissioners are both the most important social aspects. the impression is given that the main criterion on which the formulation of objectives and targets is based is the consolidation of europe's role in the world, while the rights and expectations of our citizens and social protection are relegated to a secondary position. the s-d group decided to support the commission because, when europe is beset by an endless economic and financial crisis and rising unemployment, and when europe is a disappointment to the citizens, the european parliament and europe in general must not become a place where positions or oppositions are divided. the key now is to focus on the main issues on the political agenda, so that europe can end its state of uncertainty and instability as quickly as possible and tackle the most pressing problems, such as the financial crisis and unemployment, more quickly and effectively.
in writing. − it should be in the interests of citizens and institutions in all eu countries that the best, strongest candidates become commissioners. the responsibility is great, both for the countries that put forward candidates and for the members of the european parliament. unfortunately, some countries have not followed this provision. it seemed that eu governments sometimes sent weak or 'uncomfortable' politicians to brussels to free up space in the capitals. this is a dangerous trend. however, the european parliament is keen to show a 'red card' to weak candidates who have both the mandate of their governments and the endorsement of the president of the european commission, jean-michel barroso, or whose financial interests are unclear. five years ago, there were two candidates, this year there's one. unfortunately, meps do not yet have the right to vote for individual commissioners, but we do vote for the college of commissioners. as most of the commissioners-designate had made a good impression, i voted for the new european commission.
people across the european union face problems with home renovation, but the situation is particularly difficult in the new member states of the european union. the new member states have one thing in common: they all inherited uneconomical apartment blocks, which are very expensive to maintain and which are very slow to be renovated or not renovated at all. it is essential that the application of this regulation is not limited to exceptionally large socially excluded communities, but that its provisions are also applicable to other most socially vulnerable groups, such as the disabled, the poor, young families with limited resources, migrants and others who also suffer from social exclusion and lack the possibility to renovate their housing. i am convinced that this document will enable every region of the european union to invest in social infrastructure, with the help of the structural funds, ensuring affordability of housing, which in turn will not only help to reduce social exclusion but also contribute to the development of sustainable social, economic and environmental policies throughout the community.

madam president, i have a brief comment on turkey. turkey still has some very serious problems in the area of human rights. one of the outstanding issues is setting the rights of ethnic and religious minorities – kurds, christians, alevists and others. these minorities are suffering from constant violations of their rights. cyprus and greece are, and will remain, of the utmost importance to the european union. so, commissioner, we therefore have to make it clear to our turkish partners that it is very difficult to speak about turkey’s european integration until problems related to turkey’s religious and ethnic minorities are solved.
today's debate has shown that, while members of the european parliament have different views on turkey's progress, we all seem to agree on one thing: the key to membership of the european union is the proper and high-quality implementation of fundamental reforms. it is too early to set specific dates, but we must monitor and demand progress from turkey, because the recent events, with the banning of political parties, are worrying and do not reflect turkey's commitment to respecting civil and political freedoms and rights. but we must give it the opportunity to reconsider its actions and to expect turkey to be a democratic and free country in the future.
mr president, i supported this report because i believe that it represents a step forward in combating fraud and tax evasion on a european scale. despite the willingness of member states to cooperate in the field of taxation, tangible results have not been achieved, and tax fraud remains at an extremely high level in the eu and brings far too many negative implications both to our economies and our citizens. i very much welcome the proposed new improvements, which will hopefully produce tangible results in the fight against fraud and tax evasion, especially the extension of the scope of the directive on all taxes, including social security contributions, the automatic exchange of information and improved cooperation between the member states in matters of taxation.
in writing. − the application of the reverse charge vat system is welcome. but i see a flaw in it. for example, entity a sold to entity b. subject b was served by subject c. entity c to the final consumer or to the non-vat payer. in contrast, entity a does not pay vat because it is not the final seller. the vat is paid by entity c only when the goods are sold to the final consumer. the point is that entity b remains completely exempt from taxation despite the essence of its business: buy cheaper sell more expensive. it is therefore proposed that entity b should pay the vat on the price difference to the treasury. there are advantages to this scheme everywhere except for one thing: no one will go to the state treasury for a vat refund, but if entity c is a fraud, it will simply not pay vat as a final seller. this means that there will be no negative balance in the reverse vat payment scheme, since no one will intervene in the vat refund and i think that the administration of this scheme will also be easy, since the difference in the prices of goods will be very easily detected by the state tax inspectorates. if i am wrong, i would be pleased to receive a written reply as to the suitability or inadequacy of the scheme i am proposing.
in writing. − i support this framework agreement because cooperation between the european parliament and the european commission is of paramount importance in strengthening the sustainability and efficiency of the european union. under the agreement, the european commission will have to respond to a request for legislation from the european parliament within one month and prepare the relevant european union law within one year. if the european commission refuses to prepare the requested act, it will have to give a full justification for its decision. until now, only the european commission could initiate european union legislation, but the treaty of lisbon provides for a european parliament majority to legislate for the european union. parliament and the commission will cooperate closely at an early stage in all cases of requests for legislative initiatives following a citizens' initiative. the european parliament's experts will also be included in the debates when international agreements are signed. the agreement will give parliament the right to participate as an observer in some of the european union's international negotiations, as well as the right to receive more information on international agreements.
in writing. − in order to improve the absorption of european union funds for the renovation of buildings, housing and taking into account national practices and the difficulty of co-financing, it should be proposed to national governments to create a common fund to which they could pool funds by covering co-financing with public funds. in other words, until the co-financing is covered by the state, the building managers and the apartment owners have to pay the same absolute amount as before the renovation. the reason is that property managers and landlords often do not have the possibility not only to cover the co-financing from their own money but also to take out a bank loan for this purpose. another welcome element of this initiative is the targeting of renewal funds to rural areas. most private houses in rural areas are heated autonomously, i.e. without central payment for heating, and it is therefore proposed to set an absolute monthly amount that could cover the co-financing of this housing over time. this would make it easier for national states to co-finance the renovation of rural housing legally.
in writing. − trafficking in human beings is one of the most horrific and serious crimes. the fight cannot be effective without a coherent policy approach focusing on prevention, protection of victims and effective sanctions for traffickers. free movement within the eu has brought substantial benefits to our citizens but at the same time has opened many ways for traffickers. tens of thousands of young women and children from new member states become victims of human trafficking every year. the european parliament will have a crucial role to play in combating trafficking in human beings. it will be for us to ensure that prevention, protection and support for victims are put high on the political agenda. we must demand that member states fully implement current eu policies and other instruments on trafficking in human beings and ensure that tougher penalties and sanctions are put into place.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because the european union must fight illegal migration and trafficking in human beings. as unemployment rises, many people are at risk of becoming victims of trafficking or exploited for forced labour. first, people who have lost their jobs in their own country, who have lost hope of a better life, will look for success elsewhere. criminal groups can take advantage of this situation. the main trafficking of children, especially girls and women, has not changed for many years. sexual exploitation in slavery is particularly prevalent in eastern europe, which is a transit route for human trafficking to the west. we must develop a strategy of measures to combat trafficking in human beings, with a strong focus on combating and preventing trafficking in human beings, protecting victims and sanctioning them. all member states must take strong measures to combat trafficking in human beings, ensuring a minimum harmonisation of national legislation. it is necessary to pursue closer cooperation between all stakeholders on trafficking in human beings.
in writing. − europe and the world had high hopes for the copenhagen conference. the eu was determined to lead the way and come back with a legally binding treaty, but the assembly ended with very little clarity on how the climate change situation would develop. the copenhagen accord, which lacks ambitious targets and commitments, is a failure. the ambitious eu 20/20/20 goal may remain a distant vision if the issue is not addressed globally. the eu, through its external action service, must start to lead the strategy of climate change diplomacy as soon as possible and to work to ensure that europe, in particular, speaks with one voice and takes a principled position in negotiations with other countries in order to reach a binding international climate agreement as soon as possible.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because, in the face of a complex economic, financial and social situation, it is more important than ever to implement one of the most important founding principles of the european union: equality between women and men. each member state must ensure that workers of both sexes are paid equal pay for work of equal value. to promote equality between women and men, it is necessary to ensure that women and men share family and household responsibilities. it is essential that the directive establishes parental leave more quickly, allowing the father to contribute to the care of the children. the victims of trafficking in human beings are mostly women, and i therefore urge those member states which have not ratified the council of europe convention on action against trafficking in human beings to do so immediately.
in writing. − i worked in the delegation for relations with macedonia (the former yugoslav republic) during the last term of office, visited the country and am pleased with its achievements. 2009 was a successful year for macedonia. the eu has introduced a visa-free regime with this country. this is particularly helpful for developing human relationships. last year, macedonia demarcated its border with kosovo, improved relations with greece. in the resolution on macedonia's progress in 2009 towards meeting the eu membership criteria, we highlighted the need for the government to take more sensitive account of national minority issues and to promote greater media transparency. we have called on the eu institutions to help resolve the name dispute between skopje and athens over the name of the macedonian state. we also invited the eu council of ministers to approve the opening of accession negotiations with macedonia in march. the progress made in 2009 was due, among other things, to the eu's magnetism. there is no doubt that the prospect of eu membership is pushing macedonia to change for the better. this country is making remarkable reforms, and i voted in favour of a resolution that praises them but also highlights the way forward.

in writing. while ensuring the security of air transport in the fight against terrorism is undoubtedly essential, we must examine the impact of the measures designed to ensure such security on our health, fundamental rights and freedoms, privacy and dignity. body scanners, as one of the possible technical solutions, are a very serious issue for the safety of citizens in the use of new technologies, and i hope that the commission will finally present an assessment of the impact of body scanners on personal health and fundamental human rights and freedoms. i would point out that the introduction of new technical measures aimed at achieving a high level of security at airports is also essential to establish the boundaries between human rights and security itself, since these measures concern not only the protection of citizens but also the impact on their rights and freedoms. the commission has not yet submitted an impact assessment report confirming that it is the body scanners that will provide effective protection of persons during air travel and therefore there are still doubts as to whether these technical security measures will be truly safe, effective and justified. so, having assessed the current situation, we must continue to work on this sensitive issue.

acting president of the council. - madam president, the internal energy market and the security of energy supply are absolutely linked. this has been gradually accepted in the european union, and in this sense energy security has become a strategic priority for the union. it had its climax, precisely, just over a year ago, with the gas crisis between russia and ukraine. we realised at that time that an internal energy market, an effective market which is fully functioning and interconnected, is an essential condition for a secure energy supply in europe. security of supply is therefore a strategic, essential element which must be improved by increasing energy efficiency, diversifying energy suppliers, sources and supply routes, promoting the union's energy interests vis-à-vis third countries and, ultimately, creating a genuine common energy market which does not yet exist. we shall be able to do so with the help of the treaty of lisbon, which for the first time makes explicit reference to this competence in the union at the level of primary law. it is therefore a matter of making the union stronger in its relations with third countries. and in its relations with third countries, the union must speak with one voice. this was made very clear by the spanish minister for industry, trade and tourism when, on 27 january 2010, he presented the priorities of the spanish presidency to the relevant committee of the european parliament. the linking of the energy islands in the european union to the internal market is therefore an essential objective of the union in the context of the second review of the energy strategy. in july 2009, when the commission presented the baltic energy interconnection action plan, an initiative to foster energy market integration and the development of energy infrastructure in the baltic region, the european council welcomed it as an essential contribution to increasing the union's energy security. the baltic energy market initiative is now part of the wider context of the eu strategy for the baltic sea region, which was one of the major priorities of the swedish presidency. the council of october 2009 then endorsed the initiative, welcomed the progress made in energy infrastructure and interconnections in the baltic area and thus aligned itself with the commission's report.

acting president of the council. - we - i think we can say it loud and clear - are human rights defenders and therefore have not the right but the obligation to report cases of human rights violations wherever they occur. it is also true that the european union must respect the competences of the member states and therefore certain matters must be investigated by the member states, they cannot be investigated by the european union. the lisbon treaty makes it very clear that, for example, the supervision of intelligence services in the territory of the member states is a matter for the member states. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in any event, the possible existence of such an agreement, of which we do not know all, would in no way prevent the member states from having to fulfil their obligations under international law and under humanitarian law. but of course, madam member, the european union is clearly the place - or one of the places - in the world where human rights are most respected, and the european union has the obligation - and it always has the obligation - to denounce human rights violations, which is a matter for humanity and not just for the individual competent states. that's the line we're gonna stay on and dig deep. and now we have in the european union another even more important reference, the charter of fundamental rights of the citizens of europe.

madam president, (inaudible) is in a very difficult situation already because of the global recession and the decreasing demand for a number of products worldwide. however, protectionism further hurts international trade and the eu’s interests directly. one of the international trade actors which have enacted additional and counterproductive trade barriers is russia. since the beginning of the financial crisis, russia has imposed a number of so-called ‘temporary’ anti-crisis tariffs on a number of imports, such as meat and milk products, furniture, and also some steel products. another instance which may be recalled is the temporary tariff on drugs that russia has imposed on eu exporters. what is even more alarming is that, on 1 january 2010, a customs union came into existence between russia, belarus and kazakhstan. as a consequence, a total of 30% of the eu’s product lines have seen increased tariffs. the main problem is of course that russia, like belarus and kazakhstan, is not a member of the wto, and is therefore not bound by the wto rules restricting the unilateral increase of import tariffs and other trade-restrictive measures. since russia is not a member of the wto, we do not have a dispute settlement mechanism with them. commissioner, i have two questions for you. how does the commission assess the current eu trade problems with russia, and do we have a particular strategy on how to address the issue of russia’s membership of the wto?
perhaps no one can deny that gambling, like other forms of addiction, causes serious social problems that affect not only the gambler but society as a whole. this is a complex problem. with the rapid expansion of internet access in a global world, a new form of addiction is on the rise, namely online gambling. in the 14 years since 1996, the gambling market has grown dramatically. as the market grows, so does the total income from gambling in the world. until a common european union regulatory framework for online gambling is in place, these figures can only please those in the online gambling business. the european court of justice has held that gambling services must also enjoy freedom of movement and that member states should regulate them themselves in accordance with their own values and traditions. however, lithuania, for example, is among the european union countries that ban online gambling. however, the free movement of services ensures the possibility of gambling, the free access to online gambling, and a ban on gambling throughout the european union will still not protect us from gambling registered elsewhere in the world. it is therefore necessary to establish a common european union regulatory framework for online gambling, taking into account the protection of groups at risk, with particular attention to the protection of minors and the control of cash settlements. for minors, it is not only gambling that is associated with monetary expression, but also games that are addictive, often of an aggressive nature, with aggressive content, that are also a major problem. and this is almost identical to the issue of co2 emissions that we're so often talking about. the internet has no borders, so if we have different rules and regulations for online gambling, it's going to be like the climate change problem, a lot of languages and, unfortunately, little results.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the 2003 hague convention on the international recovery of child and other family maintenance is primarily a child protection instrument, since the vast majority of these claims relate to child maintenance. the purpose of this convention is to help people to facilitate the recovery of benefits and to ensure that claims for maintenance obligations abroad are recognised and effectively enforced. it is welcome that the council has consulted the european parliament and is preparing to take a decision on this convention, given that the free movement of persons within and between eu member states and the increase in divorce rates has led to an increase in crossborder cases of maintenance claims. i would like to emphasise that the adoption of this commission proposal on the recovery of maintenance will make it easier and simpler for a person in the territory of one of the contracting parties to recover maintenance from a person under the jurisdiction of the other contracting party. the adoption of this proposal will therefore provide a legal basis and harmonised framework for relations between the parties to the convention and the member states of the european union in this area.
in writing. − as ordinary eu citizens are the ones who have felt the effects of the economic and financial recession the most, the main challenge for eu policy today is to prevent unemployment from rising, to create more jobs and to create the conditions for economic recovery. the crisis has fundamentally changed european labour markets and it is therefore necessary to ensure that the necessary measures are in place to enable both workers and enterprises to adapt more easily to the changing environment. i support this report because i believe that additional funding is needed for the progress programme to keep people in the labour market and to help small businesses and their development.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is an excellent initiative that will help socially disadvantaged people in europe, including women and young people, who have lost their jobs or who have had no opportunities to enter the labour market during a difficult period, to access financial support and to promote their entrepreneurship. the community employment and social solidarity programme progress is aimed at the most vulnerable groups of people, helping them to create alternative jobs and secure their employment, as unemployment affects the most vulnerable in society in the first place. i am pleased that the european parliament has been able to reach an agreement with the council and the commission on the targeted financing and implementation of this programme in the trialogue. i would like to stress the importance of this initiative, because it is precisely at this time when the unemployment rate is rising that the social exclusion of the most vulnerable is increasing. i would therefore like to note that the successful and effective implementation of the 'progress' programme will achieve the eu's social priorities of creating new jobs and increasing employment, providing more access to the labour market and meeting labour market needs.
in writing. following several exchanges between the european parliament and the european council, the informal trilogue of the last few days resulted in an agreement on the financing of the european microfinance facility. i would like to congratulate all those involved in these negotiations, because the sooner the decision is taken, the sooner the citizens will be able to have this european microfinance instrument at their disposal. i supported today's vote on the joint financing of this european microfinance instrument, which has a volume of 100 million euro: 60 million euro redeployed from progress and 40 million euro from the margins under ceilings. this agreement will, for example, allow european citizens to obtain a loan to buy, via a micro-credit, glasses for their children who have reading problems at school, if their traditional bank does not want to grant them this loan.

acting president of the council. - mr president, the european union is preparing in geneva, in brussels, in capitals of third countries, the main session of the human rights council this year. the human rights council is a forum for discussion on the human rights situation in all regions of the world and on the efforts of the international community to improve the human rights situation, which is ultimately one of the principles, elements, essential characteristics of the soul of the european union, which gives it its true identity in the world. the presidency of the council has taken very seriously the role it has to play at this very moment. the challenges facing the european union during these sessions of the human rights council have been taken very seriously, and this is shown by the presence of the first vice-president of the spanish government - who, in this case, therefore acts as the representative of the presidency of the council of the european union - maría teresa fernández de la vega, in the so-called 'high-level segment' which will open the thirteenth session of the council. the presidency will therefore be actively involved in the council's work. we will defend the european union's positions on issues with special implications for many member states and, in addition to national initiatives, the european union will present country resolutions. we still think that the council must have instruments to defend human rights, to respond to situations of serious human rights violations, either through a specific mandate/country - as is the case, for example, of burma or the democratic people's republic of korea - or through specific, thematic mandates in a country, as we hope will be the case in the democratic republic of congo. these situations must be monitored by the international community and by the human rights council if we want the council to retain its credibility. one of the items that are going to be on that agenda of the council sessions that are going to start immediately is something that we are going to talk about later, in another item this afternoon, which are the special sessions on gaza and the goldstone report. the european union considers - and we will say this later, too, we will repeat this - that this report is a serious analysis and the european union stressed the importance of proper and serious investigations into possible violations of international human rights law and international humanitarian law. i would like to refer to the problem of the council's review. this year is going to be a crucial year for the negotiations on the review of the council's work, which will take place in 2011 specifically. the european union is just getting started, but it is seeking to consolidate a clear position, a clear strategy of the union so that we have a position that maintains an active participation and commitment to the protection and respect of human rights. in any event, it is clear that the european union will continue to defend, as it has always done, the independence of the office of the united nations high commissioner for human rights, the independence of the special procedures, the participation of non-governmental organisations in the human rights council, the council's capacity to deal with key and serious human rights violations, and the establishment of country mandates. spain, the spanish presidency, is aware that it has assumed the rotating presidency at a crucial moment for the union, for the united nations, to protect, to promote human rights in the world. the lisbon treaty has also opened a new phase in this, as in so many other things, a new phase in the union's external action, and we hope that joint work under the leadership of the high representative will therefore make the european union's voice more visible, stronger, clearer in defending the basic principles of the work of the human rights council, and that this phase towards a more active, transparent and efficient council will also benefit from this transitional phase, precisely because the union is living under a presidency - which in this case i represent - that will do everything possible to ensure that the path of the union and that of the council from now on lead to the same objective, which is the promotion and protection of human rights in the world.
member of the commission. − mr president, i find it very appropriate that i am here today for the first time on the issue of human rights, which fits the priorities of my portfolio so well. the forthcoming session and how the eu would align with those priorities. the first concerns thematic issues. the eu and the group of latin american countries are jointly working on a draft resolution on millennium development goals, as well as the human rights of internally displaced persons who, unlike refugees, are not adequately protected by international conventions, and we will press on in the principle of gender equality is of crucial importance for the european union in achieving its objectives of growth, employment and social cohesion. the european union has made great progress in implementing the beijing agenda, but we cannot be completely satisfied with the current situation. the report on the implementation of the beijing platform for action showed that the objectives of the platform for action for the european union have not yet been achieved. it is essential that the beijing indicators are used to develop a gender perspective in the national reform programmes as well as in the national reports on social protection and social inclusion. there is still a lack of sufficient reliable and comparable data at both national and eu levels for the identification of social indicators, including women's poverty, violence against women, institutional mechanisms. one of the tasks of the european institute for gender equality is to work with comparable data. the institute's work programme would be particularly useful in achieving the targets set in beijing. i am convinced that institutional mechanisms for gender equality need to be strengthened in times of economic recession.
mr. chairman, please! i would like to refer to the situation of national minorities in lithuania, whose rights are constantly being diminished. in lithuania, in areas of dense national minority population, there is a widespread order to remove bilingual information boards that have been present for 20 years. minority schools have been cut back and are being prepared to close as part of the so-called reform. the original spelling of names cannot be used on identity documents. moreover, the poles in lithuania are having difficulty reclaiming land seized by the communist system, and recently even the cultural minority has been hit. the oldest polish national song and dance team has had its funding cut four times by many government institutions and has lost three jobs, leaving only one. the rights of national minorities in lithuania should be respected as the union demands in its motto 'united in diversity'.

in writing. − with regard to human rights at all levels and in all areas of the eu, it is important to stress that our duty as meps is not only to criticise and draft resolutions on third countries, but also to monitor eu member states closely, drawing attention to even the slightest negative phenomena that violate human rights. in the event of a breach of human rights, the ep draws up a resolution addressed to the party concerned. to criticize others, we must first prevent human rights violations within the eu, and then we can criticize others and try to help them as much as possible.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because we need to further develop the gender equality perspective across the european union. the european commission should develop an action strategy for the guidelines on equality between women and men, taking into account the economic and financial crisis, sustainable development, as well as the current priorities of the guidelines, equal economic independence for men and women, reconciliation of work, family and private life, equal participation of women and men in decision-making. the current lack of data is particularly marked in the areas of gender equality, violence against women, institutional mechanisms. it is very important that member states cooperate as closely as possible with the european institute for gender equality, one of whose tasks is to work with comparable data. the institute will produce statistics, will carry out studies to provide an analysis of gender equality issues, will carry out studies on indicator statistics, on data explanations. the objectives set out in the institute's work programme would be particularly helpful in achieving the targets set in beijing.
in writing. - i would like to express my full support for the meps and at the same time my concern that various benefits are being increased, both for meps and for staff, especially in times of crisis. i would like to draw particular attention to those expenditure lines which are difficult to control or not at all controllable. it is these lines of expenditure that should not be increased in times of crisis.
in writing. − the fisheries sector in europe is going through a difficult period. fishermen are losing their only source of livelihood, and this is particularly true in those eu regions where there are few social and economic alternatives. i therefore fully support the commission's proposal in the green paper that a fundamental and comprehensive reform of the fisheries policy is necessary to adapt the sector to the changing market. it is a pity that, more than 27 years after the creation of the common fisheries policy, the sector is not functioning as it should and the way it is dealing with the problems is rather sluggish. the problems that were relevant in 2002 have persisted and have been exacerbated by recent developments in the economic crisis and the impact of climate change on fish stocks. the priorities of the overall reform of fisheries must continue to be the restoration of stocks, sustainable management and the maintenance of fishermen's livelihoods. fisheries are of vital importance to the eu as a whole and should not be treated as an activity but as a sector that is a direct source of employment.
the travel market has developed very dynamically over the last two decades. more and more people are self-completing their trips by purchasing services from different operators and service providers. however, the provisions of the current directive do not apply to these new packages, which means that our citizens are not travelling safely. i believe that the revision of the directive requires that its scope be defined as precisely as possible and that we should not allow rigid rules to leave our citizens without adequate protection. the commission notes that the airport services agreement concluded between ryanair and ams was signed on the same day. over the past decade, more than 70 airlines have gone bankrupt, leaving travelers in the dark. i therefore believe that this issue must be clearly emphasised in the review of the provisions of the directive.
in fact, perhaps no one today doubts that microenterprises must be fully encouraged and supported, and i believe that the best way forward is to reduce taxes as much as possible in each member state of the european union, as well as simplifying the requirements for registration, deregistration and many other things. there was a thought today that in order to take a loan from a bank, you're still going to have to provide some information and not just statements, but you're going to have to provide some additional information for the banks to know. i am in favour of the fact that the state should also know what processes are going on, and therefore, as far as the reporting itself is concerned, i think it is very important, however, to simplify these reports, but in a way that does not violate the basic requirements of transparency and corporate principles.
as a shadow rapporteur, i would first like to congratulate my colleague sophia in't veld on her excellent report. i think the report has very clearly highlighted the areas to which the commission should pay particular attention. firstly, it is the control of state aid measures. during the crisis, the european commission gave member states the option of exclusive application of state aid measures. as such measures were introduced in a hurry, the commission must examine whether they were used for their intended purpose and whether they were productive, and whether the crisis did not provoke protectionist reactions in the member states, since protectionism and fragmentation of the single market are harmful to competition and only weaken the european union's position in the global economy. i am also pleased that the rapporteur has taken account of the itre committee's opinion on the problems of the internal energy market in the european union, and in particular that it is impossible to ensure the competitiveness and the interoperability of this market as long as there are energy islands and no interconnected and properly functioning energy infrastructure.
member of the commission. − mr president, as the rapporteur pointed out, the aim of this proposal is to facilitate movement within the schengen area for third-country nationals, who are legally residing in one of the member states on the basis of a long-stay or ‘d’ visa. according to the current schengen acquis, third-country nationals who hold a residence permit can travel freely within the schengen area because the residence permit is equivalent to a visa. however, there has been a recent trend in member states not to convert long-stay visas into residence permits upon arrival. that is why we are having this discussion here today; obviously the commission, the council and the parliament had to find a solution to the problem. the legal and practical situation has considerable negative consequences for third-country nationals legally staying in our member states on the basis of a d visa. these persons can neither legally travel to another country nor can they transit through the territory of another country when they want to return to their country of origin. the absurdity of this situation was illustrated by the example that the rapporteur, mr coelho, raised. the best solution would, of course, be that all member states issue the necessary residence permits and that they do this in time. unfortunately this is not the situation today, and therefore we have the present proposal which aims at extending the principle of equivalence between a residence permit and short-stay visas to long-stay d visas. a third-country national holding a long-stay d visa issued by a member state will thereby be able to travel to the other member states for three months in any half year under the same conditions as the holder of a residence permit. this would restore the basic philosophy underlying the creation of an area without internal borders, namely that the person can travel around in the schengen area for short stays with the documents based on which he or she is legally present in a member state. i was very pleased to learn that mr carlos coelho had expressed his support for this proposal from the very beginning and the rapporteur, together with the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs and the committee on legal affairs, has understood that action needs to be taken in order to facilitate the life of third-country nationals, because we want to encourage people legally residing in our area. i want to thank the rapporteur for his constructive approach to this. i do not need to remind you that we have to find a solution quickly, especially due to the visa code which will be applicable from the 5 april this year and will abolish the so-called d+c visas which were intended to partly remedy the situation of d visa holders. i believe that the final text on the draft regulation satisfied all the parties since the compromise text was agreed between all the institutions. in order to tackle some of the concerns expressed by the european parliament and the member states – for instance concerning security – several amendments have been made to the initial text. to give a few examples, the proposal shortens the period of validity for long-stay visas. they should have a period of validity of no more than one year. after this one year the proposal established the obligation for member states to issue a residence permit. the requirement of systematic checks in the schengen information system (sis) is also reinforced. where a member state considers issuing a residence permit or a d visa, the responsible authority should systematically carry out a search in the schengen information system in order to avoid a situation where we have an alert at the same time as a long-stay visa. in order to give a response to the security concerns regarding biometrics – and this is of course a major issue for many member states – a political declaration has, as you know, been attached to the draft regulation in which the commission is invited to study the possibility of the use of biometric identifiers with respect to long-stay visas and to present the results in the study to this plenary and the council by 21 july 2011. the commission also accepts that a reporting obligation has been included in the draft regulation on the application. to conclude, in order to find a compromise response to the major concern of the european parliament concerning the guarantee for high-level data protection in cases where an alert has been issued in the sis, a joint declaration has been agreed. the commission is invited by the council and the european parliament to present the necessary legislative proposals amending the relevant data-protection provision for the schengen convention if there are further substantial delays in implementing the sis ii that go beyond 2012. i believe that, with these changes, we have reached a reasonable, well-balanced solution that will considerably facilitate the lives of the third-country nationals that are residing legally in our countries. it will also very much correspond to the philosophy of a europe without internal borders. i want to thank once again the libe committee, the juri committee and the rapporteur for their very constructive approach to this.
on behalf of the s&d group i congratulate the rapporteur, mr coelho, on the preparation of this report and also agree with the importance of ensuring as soon as possible the free movement of third-country nationals legally present in a member state on long-stay visas within the schengen area. according to current practice in the member states, the process of replacing a long-stay visa with a residence permit for third-country nationals takes a considerable amount of time for a variety of reasons. i could cite several examples from the european union, including my own country, lithuania, where, for example, a long-distance driver who has obtained a d visa is unable to carry out his direct duties. the current practice in this area infringes the legitimate expectations of third-country nationals coming to the european union to work or study. member states should also take appropriate measures to simplify the procedure for issuing visas. a long-stay visa should have the same effect on the movement of persons within the schengen area without internal borders as a residence permit. the key issue here is not the length of time a d visa holder can travel to another member state, but the possibility of better meeting his or her needs in the schengen area itself. i therefore support the proposal that a third-country national holding a long-stay visa issued by a member state may travel to another member state for 3 months in any 6-month period under the same conditions as a residence permit holder. at the same time, it is essential to ensure that the simplified movement of third-country nationals through the schengen area does not create additional threats to the security of member states. i therefore call for the adoption of this amended regulation without delay.
madam president, i supported this report. effective consumer enforcement policy is central to the functioning of the single market. we need to have a real and well-functioning internal market with a high level of consumer protection, which, unfortunately, is not the case today. we have the legislation in place, but it is not enforced properly in the member states. most importantly, our consumers do not feel safe because they do not know the rules and, in many cases, the compensation mechanisms are not working in the way they should. the commission should increase its efforts, ensuring that member states apply directives correctly and that citizens are informed about their rights and, most importantly, that they are able to exercise those rights in practice.

in writing. − i voted in favour of the new amendments to this regulation, as third-country nationals with long-term visas have until now faced problems with restrictions on free movement. they were unable to travel freely between the member states of the european union and even had difficulty returning to their home countries. this regulation extends the scope of the principle of equivalence between residence permits and long-stay visas issued by member states fully implementing the schengen acquis governing long-stay visas. it should be underlined that a long-term visa should have the same effect on free movement within the schengen area without internal borders as a residence permit. i would like to stress that it is essential that the simplification of the movement of third-country nationals within the schengen area does not compromise the security guarantees in the member states. the implementation of this regulation should not reduce security, as it provides for an obligation for states to check the data of a person in the schengen information system and, if necessary, to consult other eu countries before issuing a long-term visa. until now, this has been done only by issuing a residence permit.

acting president of the council. - mr president, the debate we have had on the roma population, on the forthcoming cordoba summit, makes it clear once again that the social and economic integration of the roma is a problem of a european dimension and that it therefore requires a european strategy. and that is precisely what we intend to do, after some progress has been made, not enough, at the cordoba summit, which has been mentioned so many times. a strategy which must be based on a work plan which the council intends to follow and which must be based on the overall programme envisaged by the trio - a programme which therefore has a long-range scope - a programme of action, a programme of work, which must have as one of its essential tools, without doubt, the structural funds - the most powerful tool europe has for social cohesion - and which must be projected into specific actions. some of these actions, in my view, should be actions that are designed to address specific problems of the roma population - problems of discrimination suffered by the roma population as such - for example, the problem of access to public services or employment. or the problem of roma women, who suffer particularly from poverty, who suffer from problems of access to housing and problems of discrimination or violence. or the case of young roma, whose lack of qualifications makes it even more difficult for them to find employment in the current economic crisis. or the problem of children: gypsy children - it has been said - have a life expectancy of ten years less than the average of all european children. but at the same time, we must also act from a general point of view with actions that favour the roma population itself, because as one of the most discriminated populations, any anti-discrimination policy will favour them. this is the case with action against gender violence, which is why it is so important to have this directive as soon as possible, the directive on the protection against gender violence, which will particularly favour roma women, or the directive on non-discrimination, which will particularly favour roma people. and all this in a completely paternalistic way, because the first thing to be done with regard to the roma population is to respect their identity and their cultural characteristics.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, i would like to thank the house for the many suggestions and proposals that have been made. they will be taken on board by commissioner andor and myself when we present our communication before the summit. in this communication it will also be made very clear that the commission cannot and will not accept that the roma are discriminated against and excluded from our society because of their ethnicity. now, we do have instruments; we do have policies. the question is, how do we utilise them? how do we mainstream the question and the problems of the roma in these instruments and policies? i believe that we do not need a roma directive or a roma fund. what we need is to take roma issues fully into account when we apply eu law and when we implement eu funds. the key to this – and it has been said by many of you – is partnership and the cooperation of all the key actors. the commission pursues this approach in the european platform for roma inclusion and with its internal procedures. but i would also like to underline very clearly that, while, of course, roma exclusion has a lot to do with fundamental rights, mostly it has to do with social and economic issues. for instance, i would like to quote the world bank’s study on the economic cost of roma exclusion and to see that this is a very important piece of evidence showing that we need to find solutions for the sake of our society in general. so to apply the instruments which we have in the most effective way will need a strategic approach, of course – mainstream, as some of you have said. this approach is based on cooperation, on mobilisation of the necessary resources and on a strategy learning from success as well as from failure. what we must avoid doing is creating a strategy which exists only on paper. results are needed; results which lead to the integration of roma into mainstream schools. i have heard many of you speaking about schooling. i am looking forward to seeing the details of our action on pre-schooling, how this has been working out. the mainstream labour market; here my colleague mr andor is going to look for precise targets to be achieved, and the mainstream society which is the responsibility of all our policies.
in writing. i would like to emphasise that the roma issue has become part of european human rights policy as the european union and the member states share responsibility for non-discrimination and integration of roma in society. the second roma congress in cordoba is therefore an occasion to discuss the social problems of the roma and the ways in which they can be solved. i am pleased that spain, as the presidency of the council of the eu, is organising this congress, because it is necessary to raise and acknowledge the existing roma problems. i would like to emphasise that in a democratic and free society it is unacceptable for certain people to be excluded from society and for the fundamental rights and freedoms of individuals to be openly violated. roma communities face racist attacks, lack of access to all social and public services, and there is a huge gap in living conditions and education. it is also necessary to take into account that roma face not only direct discrimination but also invisible, indirect discrimination, such as the exclusion of roma from employment and, at the same time, from social life. i therefore agree that it is necessary to call on the european commission to encourage member states, regions and local authorities to better implement eu projects for roma. i would also urge the commission to take concrete actions and initiatives to combat direct and indirect discrimination against roma in europe.
