vice-president of the commission. − within the framework of the third european action programme on road safety up to 2010, a number of actions were implemented to combat speeding, drug and drink driving as well as to improve road infrastructure. many of those actions have involved the european parliament under the codecision procedure. but, of course, further efforts are needed. the commission is currently working on the european road safety strategy for the next decade. it will stress the importance of proper enforcement and sanctions for dangerous behaviour, in particular drink driving and excessive speed. citizens of member states cannot understand why other eu nationals are not sanctioned when they violate the law. for this reason it is urgent to resume discussions on the proposals for a directive on cross-border enforcement, which received the full support of the european parliament but was blocked by the council. the commission is determined to move forward with this proposal. besides control and sanctions, education and awareness are areas to which the commission will give strong emphasis. specific actions should be proposed regarding alcohol and speed, such as alcohol locks in certain vehicles or more stringent requirements for novice drivers. drug driving is an increasing problem. the commission expects the ongoing druid research project to provide ideas for concrete action. as far as infrastructure is concerned, the european parliament and the council have adopted legislation regarding safe management and safety requirements for roads and tunnels of the trans-european network. the commission, of course, will closely follow their proper implementation by the member states. but safety of road infrastructure is not limited to the major ten roads; 56% of road deaths occur on rural roads. so the commission will therefore examine the extension of current legislation on safe management to the secondary road network of member states. finally, the commission will also verify that infrastructure projects receiving eu funding or loans take road safety requirements into account. i must also underline that road safety is a shared responsibility between the eu institutions, member states, local and regional bodies, associations and, of course, citizens. to reach maximum efficiency, solutions must respond to concrete problems on the ground. the next european road safety strategy will propose a number of actions based on this principle. its main goal is to establish a common european road safety area which will form part of a single european transport area where all eu citizens will benefit from the same level of safety all over europe.
vice-president of the commission. − of course i can only share your concern. the problem with drugs, as you know very well, is that, although we have relatively developed technology to find drivers who are under the influence of alcohol, it is much more difficult to detect the influence of drugs. we really must carry out research to find the necessary technology, because today it is only a visible observation by the police, who then send people for medical testing, and only then can it become clear that there is a problem. we do of course need more.
thank you for your suggestions on how to solve this problem. i'd like to ask you, do you think that using cell phones while driving is one of the reasons that in some studies, it's been equated to having a slower reaction rate, just like being intoxicated with alcohol and drugs? another issue is heavy goods transport. it's an object of increased risk, especially in the dark, and also heavy transportation, you know very well, worsens the condition of the roads, which also contributes to the number of accidents. do you think that the policy of moving goods from road to rail should be increased and made more efficient?
member of the commission. − mr president, the current gsp scheme expires on 31 december 2011. the commission is already working on a substantive updating and review of the current scheme. later this month, i will launch a wide public consultation on possible improvements and changes followed by a thorough impact reassessment. the commission proposal for a successor regulation can therefore be expected to be ready during the first quarter of 2011. this proposal will of course be subject to the ordinary legislative procedure, which may well last beyond the expiry date of the current scheme, 31 december 2011. you will all agree that we must avoid a situation whereby gsp beneficiaries lose all their preferences on 1 january 2012. we will therefore submit, in parallel with the launch of this essential preparatory work for a new gsp scheme, a proposal to extend the current regulation granting continuity until the new scheme is in place. this should give you the time to work in earnest on the successor scheme whilst ensuring that gsp beneficiaries are not left out in the cold. you should receive this document in april. i have noted your question on all concerns as regards the way in which the commission monitors beneficiary countries’ compliance with the current criteria for gsp+ treatment. the basic criterion for gsp+ is the ratification and effective implementation of 27 international conventions in the fields of human rights, core labour standards, sustainable development and good governance. it is the commission’s task to closely monitor all beneficiary countries’ compliance with these criteria. the commission is obliged and determined to operate gsp in a fair, objective manner. in this respect, we base our monitoring and evaluation of effective implementation of the gsp+ arrangements as much as possible on the findings and reports of international organisations such as the united nations, the ilo and other relevant agencies, as well as on the monitoring mechanisms envisaged in the conventions themselves. this provides for an unambiguous and impartial review process. the monitoring is also supported by the commission’s bilateral dialogue with the gsp+ countries on implementation issues. if such reports contain information that gsp criteria are not being fully respected, the gsp regulation provides for the possibility that the commission undertake an investigation to clarify the factual situation and propose appropriate action. this investigation tool is a serious instrument that should be deployed when the situation justifies it, but launching an investigation is not a step to be taken lightly as it may impact on our wider relationship with the partner countries. think, for example, of the recent case with sri lanka. as the objective of the gsp+ scheme is to incentivise countries to adhere to international good governance standards, gsp+ countries should first be given the opportunity to prove their commitment to the gsp+ objectives, their willingness to cooperate with international monitoring bodies and to address the shortcomings identified. this approach gives credit for the steps already taken by those countries and is in line with the general incentive-based approach that underpins the gsp+. i am eager to engage with you in a debate on the future of the gsp scheme and of the gsp+ in particular. in preparing the review of the current scheme, which will also concern the gsp+ criteria and the monitoring of respect, the themes raised by the european parliament will be carefully looked at. since this review will now follow the ordinary legislative procedure, the european parliament will be in an equal position with the council in determining the final shape of the new gsp scheme.

in writing. − i support this report because i believe that a clear and coordinated common foreign and security policy can make a significant contribution to strengthening the european union's international standing. the eu's increasing energy dependence on supply sources and transit channels and the need to prevent the eu's energy dependence on third countries are undoubtedly among the key issues of the cfsp. i would like to urge the vice-president of the commission, ms ashton, to implement decisively parliament's recommendations for a coherent and coordinated policy, in particular by promoting eu cohesion through a constructive dialogue with energy suppliers, in particular russia, and transit countries, supporting the eu's energy priorities and defending the common interests of the member states, developing effective diplomacy in the field of energy, developing more effective crisis management tools and promoting the diversification of energy supply, the sustainable use of energy and the development of renewable energy sources. i am convinced that only by acting together can the eu ensure the uninterrupted and secure supply of gas and oil to its member states in the future and increase the energy independence of the eu as a whole.
today we are discussing a very important issue. the effects of climate change are fundamentally changing the situation in the arctic. the arctic is opening up new sea lanes and opportunities for the transport of gas, oil and other natural resources as a result of global warming, but at the same time it is becoming highly vulnerable to ecological problems and the sharing of its resources between states, with potentially negative consequences for international stability and european security interests. as regards the impact of the environment and climate change on the region, one of the main objectives of arctic policy must be to mitigate the negative impacts of climate change and to help the region adapt to the inevitable changes. no treaty regime has been applied in the arctic region so far. the european parliament has repeatedly stressed the importance of arctic governance. i believe that the situation has changed and that it is necessary to gradually start to develop an autonomous policy for the european union, and the european commission should play an active role in this regard. the situation in this region has long been a source of tension between the arctic states, and this tension will only increase as the region becomes more accessible. i believe that the european union needs to initiate a comprehensive dialogue with other countries, which would open up new opportunities for cooperation. i think it is time to start thinking about an international convention on the management of this region, the preservation of the arctic environment and the sustainable use of its resources.
the lisbon treaty provides for a stronger role for civil society in the european institutions and for european citizens to initiate a legislative proposal. the right of citizens' initiative would allow at least one million citizens to ask the commission to take a legislative initiative in a specific area. however, it is very difficult to collect such a large number of voters' signatures and, therefore, in order to create an effective participatory democracy mechanism and to ensure that this initiative is accessible to citizens, it is necessary to provide the possibility to initiate a proposal in a clear and simplified manner. unfortunately, the right of citizens' initiative is no longer a practical option after the entry into force of the lisbon treaty. so far, all aspects of the citizens' initiative have not been discussed, there is no concrete action plan to guarantee transparency and democratic accountability of initiatives, so that citizens do not become victims of the representation of the interests of large companies. the citizens' dialogue is therefore the most important element and the necessary basis of the european and lithuanian model of democracy, and i would therefore urge the commission to take all measures to ensure that this right of citizens' initiative is put into practice as soon as possible.

member of the commission. − mr president, i would like to thank parliament for putting this issue up for debate today, because i think that it is worth discussing and i think we should start by stating the fact that our relationship with china – which we consider to be very important and strategic – has made enormous progress over the last number of years. having such a strong strategic partnership would and does definitely permit us to tackle all issues, including the most sensitive ones. we have constructed an impressive framework of high-level interactions where we regularly address the global challenges that our citizens are facing without neglecting the issues where our views may differ – and tibet is definitely one of them. it is evident that differences with china on tibet remain. we have real concerns about the human rights situation in tibet, about the fact that tibet has remained largely closed to international media, diplomats and humanitarian organisations and also about the lack of progress in talks between the representatives of the dalai lama and the chinese authorities. the eu position does not leave any room for misinterpretation. therefore, let me stress: the eu respects the sovereignty and territorial integrity of china, including tibet. we respect the ‘one china’ policy. however, we have always supported peaceful reconciliation through dialogue between the chinese authorities and the representatives of the dalai lama. this dialogue has to be constructive and substantive, addressing all core issues such as the preservation of tibet’s unique culture, religion and traditions and the need to achieve a system of meaningful autonomy for tibet within the chinese constitution. the dialogue should also address the participation of all tibetans in decision-making. for the eu, tibet is a human rights issue. we have consistently passed this message on to our chinese counterparts and listened carefully to their views, and we make every effort to understand their position in a spirit of mutual respect. but human rights are universal, and the situation in tibet is a legitimate concern for the international community, a point that we regularly make to our chinese interlocutors. in the sino-tibetan dialogue, the tibetan side has recently submitted an updated memorandum on genuine autonomy for the future of tibet. we welcome that the tibetan side has reiterated its firm commitment not to seek separation or independence. we are also pleased that the dalai lama remains committed to the middle-way approach and to dialogue as the only means for achieving a mutually acceptable and lasting solution. the eu welcomes the fact that both parties continue to hold talks even if we note with regret the lack of results and the lack of momentum. let me conclude by appealing to the representatives of the two sides to continue and intensify the dialogue with an open spirit and with a view to achieving a durable solution in tibet. from our side i can guarantee the eu’s wholehearted support to such a process.

the european union's economic recovery has been the talk of the town lately, but the crisis is far from over in many member states. in public, the talk of crisis is limited to the state of public finances, although unemployment is rising very rapidly and has already reached a critical point in some eastern european union member states. it is strange to hear high-ranking officials of the european union and the european parliament warmly praising these governments for their excellent work, even though the unemployment rate in those countries is rising catastrophically every month, social security is being reduced, and the number of people living below the poverty line is increasing. people in these countries no longer understand the european union's social policy of reducing or increasing poverty. i think that governments that have failed to solve at least the problem of stabilizing unemployment should not be praised. the european commission should take greater responsibility and responsibly monitor the implementation of national governments' crisis management plans, especially in the area of social reforms, and assess the impact of such reforms on people.
i am pleased that the european union and the member states have committed themselves to fighting poverty by declaring this year the european year against poverty and social exclusion, as almost 80 million europeans are already below the poverty line. many europeans, even those who have a job, struggle daily with poverty and lack the opportunity to enjoy a decent life. the budget for the implementation of the actions foreseen for europe 2010 is eur 17 million and will be used to organise information seminars, working groups and public consultations to overcome the stereotypes of poverty management. this is a very good initiative, but how can we ensure that these funds are used efficiently and transparently and reach directly the citizens who need them most? i want to emphasize that it is not possible to prevent the increase in poverty in one year. i would therefore suggest that the european union's structures and the member states take concrete action and make long-term commitments at all levels of government to ensure that funds allocated to help those directly affected by poverty are allocated in a transparent and targeted manner.
in view of the difficult economic situation in the member states, the budget for the coming year should be planned with the utmost care. the main focus should be on preserving and creating jobs. supporting and promoting the activities of small and medium-sized enterprises, which employ the majority of the citizens of the european union, would help to achieve this. more flexible and accessible financial support instruments are needed to ensure the survival of start-ups or firms in financial difficulty. areas that add value not only to individual member states but also to the community as a whole should not be overlooked. such areas could include transport, which should give priority to the development of a sustainable, safe and sustainable transport system, and the development of trans-european networks. adequate financing of energy interconnection projects ensuring energy independence must also remain a priority in the 2011 budget.
the products of the agricultural sector of the european union are known and appreciated on the world market for their high quality. as regards the future prospects of the cap, i am convinced that product quality must remain a key objective in the future. high-quality agricultural products are not only our business card on a global scale, but also a very important part of the economic and social life of the european union region. the quality policy should be developed by providing greater clarity in product certification and labelling, which should be understandable to every consumer in the european union. in order to preserve the production of quality agricultural products, it is necessary to clearly specify the labelling of products which may contain genetically modified organisms. this problem has become particularly acute following the commission's decision to authorise the use of genetically modified potatoes for animal feed. until labelling and certification standards are established for such products, the future of the european union's food quality policy and the health of all of us are at risk.
it is a matter of great pleasure that the g20 labour and employment ministers are meeting for the first time. at the 2009 g20 leaders' meeting in pittsburgh, heads of state agreed that national recovery plans should focus on job preservation and job creation. unfortunately, the situation has deteriorated since that meeting, both in the european union and globally. the daily rise in unemployment has reached a critical level in some member states, and the crisis plans of some national governments are making people's lives significantly worse by cutting pensions and other key social guarantees. small and medium-sized enterprises are closing down en masse. i believe that the g20 should focus on agreeing on concrete measures to stabilise unemployment, because people's frustration and fear will not help our countries' economic recovery any faster.
first of all, i really welcome the fact that for the first time the g20, the ministers of the world's most powerful countries, the ministers of labour and employment, are meeting to discuss the most important issues. it is how to tackle unemployment and how to increase employment. indeed, this meeting will take place in a very difficult environment, with some 5.5 million young people out of work in the european union alone. the number of long-term unemployed is also growing. in my country, lithuania, there are already 300,000 registered unemployed this week. this has never happened in 20 years of my country's independence. it is therefore essential that the ministers of the strongest countries meet to identify the concrete measures that should be taken to ensure that people in the european union have not just any job, but the right job, a job of quality, a job with normal pay, from which they can live, rather than living in poverty. i would therefore like to see such concrete decisions taken at this meeting.
we have to fight tb, because every year, a lot of people die from tb, and almost a third of the world's population is infected. i would like to point out that tb is the most widespread infectious disease in the world, and is strongly linked to social and economic problems, i.e. poverty, unemployment, alcoholism, drug addiction, hiv/aids, as well as to the inadequate health care system in poor countries and to delayed diagnosis. to overcome these causes of tuberculosis, the european commission, the member states of the european union must take concrete and urgent action to develop common measures to combat this terrible disease, to find a common political dialogue on financial support and to adopt a common action plan to combat it. despite the inclusion of tuberculosis in the list of priority diseases, the fight against tuberculosis is still underfunded and it is therefore necessary to review the allocations of the european union budget in this action programme. i would also call for greater investment in research to combat tb.

the recovery from the complex economic and financial crisis has not been as effective as might have been expected. when the crisis began, the member states' bank support programmes were not aligned with the common european conditions and some banks used the additional funds provided by the ecb to cover their losses. the aid for economic activities, in particular for small and medium-sized enterprises, was also not harmonised. the consequences of these actions are obvious: the massive closure of small and medium-sized enterprises due to the lack of timely access to loans from banks. the euro area countries that have been provided with liquidity by the european central bank have been better able to overcome their difficulties. if we believe in european solidarity, if we operate in an open market on equal terms and if we all want to get out of this difficult situation as quickly as possible, i believe that the european central bank should have ensured the supply of liquidity to the non-euro area member states, which have been particularly hard hit by the crisis.
i voted in favour of the resolution on the priorities for the 2011 budget for a number of reasons, but the issue of youth unemployment was the most prominent. the current economic and social situation in many countries of the european union requires a special approach to the growing gap between the youth and the labour market, even though it is recognised that investing in young people, in education, is an investment in the future. experience shows that in times of economic downturn, young people prefer to stay in education or go to college rather than look for work. similar trends are now being observed in our own countries. so i would say that the measures that are envisaged, the more active link between the labour market and the education system, are very important. entrepreneurship skills development, specific programmes are very much needed both for the first job in erasmus and for other measures. i hope and i hope very much that the european union will have the political will not only to adopt very important documents but also to implement them.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the european parliament drew the commission's attention to the fact that, as the developed countries gradually emerge from the global crisis, the situation of the developing countries continues to deteriorate. the european central bank (ecb), the commission and the euro area member states therefore have a role to play in promoting the process of economic and monetary integration within the european union and in supporting the development of the euro area. i agree with the call for the ecb to support the efforts of non-euro area member states to adopt the euro, in particular where member states demonstrate the ability to maintain sound and stable fiscal discipline. i would point out that in order to prevent future financial crises, it is necessary to call for greater coordination of the eurogroup, the council and the ecb in their exchange rate policy. thus, despite the crisis, little progress has been made in the transition to a common international representation for the euro area. the most worrying aspect is that, despite all efforts to stabilise monetary and fiscal policies, employment in the eu continues to decline, unemployment and social exclusion to increase.
in writing. - i have endorsed the candidacy of rassa budbergytė for the european court of auditors. her candidacy is very well received in lithuania and in europe, as she has a lot of experience and has developed a high-quality, independent audit system in lithuania. the nomination of rassa budbergytė was very well received by the committee on budgetary control almost all members supported her nomination. she is a competent professional and her professional experience and personal qualities will enable her to perform all the duties of a member of the european court of auditors properly. in addition, rasa budbergytė has publicly promised to organise her activities on the basis of independence and audit standards and in compliance with ethical requirements if she is appointed to this position. it promises to do its work according to two principles. first, it is the strict adherence to international standards on auditing and the audit practices and procedures established by the european court of auditors. secondly, productive work in the performance of her direct personal duties as a member of a group or small college and as a representative of the european court of auditors as a collegiate body. it plans to strengthen the interinstitutional cooperation of the european court of auditors with the ep, and in particular with the committee on budgetary control. i am convinced that the auditor has demonstrated her competence and professionalism in the field of auditing. i am sure that her impeccable work will make a great contribution to the european union as a whole.
in writing. − mr fazakas's candidacy for the european court of auditors has led to a heated and controversial debate in the committee on budgetary control. although his candidacy had been known since november, it was only a few days before the hearings that information about mr fazakas' possible collaboration with the ss secret services was circulated in the committee of candidates. i am surprised that the question of mr fazak's competence is raised only now, when hungary is preparing for elections, when for five years, during which mr fazak was a member of the european parliament, chairman of the committee on budgetary control and quaestor, there was no question of his competence and ability to perform his duties. i am convinced that the european parliament is not the place for opaque political games, and that is why i supported mr fazakas's candidacy. i believe that there was sufficient time between november and the hearing for interested parties to provide properly reasoned information on the circumstances that could have affected mr fazakas' appointment as eio and to ensure a thorough parliamentary assessment, but this was not done.
in writing. − i fully endorse these recommendations. i think that the eu’s strengthened external role should be used to enhance dialogue with key partners and to build a stronger eu. the eu and its member states are significant contributors to the un’s budget. in order to ensure that the union’s values and interests are represented in a coherent and effective way in the un system, it is necessary that the eu speaks with a single voice. the eu should play an active role in supporting the reform process of the un system and especially the reform of the security council. i believe that an eu seat in an enlarged security council should remain a goal of the european union.
in writing. − the majority of european roma became citizens after the 2004 and 2007 enlargements and they and their families enjoy the right to move and reside freely within the territory of the eu. however, in some member states with a significant share of roma population, the roma are still faced with numerous problems such as segregation in education, housing, very low employment rates and unequal access to health care and public services. the eu and member states share a responsibility to promote and ensure the inclusion of roma and to uphold their fundamental rights as european citizens and to increase their efforts to achieve visible results.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because the roma of europe continue to suffer from severe discrimination and, in many cases, from extreme poverty and social exclusion. i would like to point out that the situation of the majority of roma living in many countries of the european union is different from that of other european national minorities and that it is therefore necessary to take the necessary measures at eu level and to develop a common strategy to combat discrimination against roma. i support the european parliament's calls for the new commissioners to give priority to roma issues in their areas of competence and to give due attention to the roma inclusion strategy. i would like to stress that in a democratic and free society we must respect the fundamental rights and freedoms of all people. the commission and the member states must therefore coordinate and find a common dialogue on the situation of the roma and take measures to combat discrimination against them. only when a common european approach to european roma issues is found will the strategy be actively implemented. i also support the parliament's position that representatives of the roma community should be included in the process of developing eu roma policy.
in writing. − i support this report because the circumstances in which the 2011 budget will be adopted will be exceptional, due to the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon and the continuing economic and financial crisis. the budget will need to be carefully balanced to ensure the successful achievement of the objectives set out in the lisbon treaty, such as the creation of a single internal energy market. budgetary planning should also focus on the effects of the financial crisis, which are still being felt in many countries, and on efforts to address them effectively. the main priority must remain the preservation and creation of jobs, which is closely linked to the need for financial support for small and medium-sized enterprises, which are the main creators of jobs. the adoption of the european parliament's budget for 2011 must pay particular attention to the ep's priority high-level legislation and provide all the necessary means to this end. the question of the efficient organisation of the ep's work, including the allocation of one mep's place of work, must also be properly addressed.
in writing. − i fully support this report. the global financial and economic financial crisis caused great disturbances in developed economies but it had even more profound effects on emerging and developing countries. the achievement of millennium development goals is now at stake because the progress that has been made in those counties over the last decade has stalled. financial aid alone cannot ensure economic progress in developing countries. thus the commission should urge the reform of international development cooperation. moreover, i believe that assistance to developing countries should be constantly adapted to the circumstances in those countries.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because we have a duty to help developing countries, especially in these difficult economic times. in many developing countries, particularly in the least developed countries, the sharp decline in export earnings has slowed down growth and development in the southern regions. it is of particular importance to agree on an economic partnership to enhance eu policy coherence for development purposes, and in particular to promote decent work, prosperity and job creation, and to ensure that trade commitments are properly implemented and that there is an appropriate transition period for those commitments. aid to alleviate poverty and exclusion, measures contributing to development and measures necessary to overcome the crisis in developing countries. the european union must take the lead and act with greater determination in implementing these actions and all the institutions of the european union must make greater commitments to this end. we cannot allow the crisis to stop the progress these countries have made in sustainable economic growth over the last decade, and i believe that more development assistance is necessary.
in writing. europe is currently facing a steady rise in unemployment and almost 17% of europeans are already below the poverty line. many europeans, even those who have jobs, struggle daily with poverty and lack the opportunity to enjoy a full life, as the recession has pushed many people, even those with middle incomes, into poverty. the problem of working poverty should therefore be given special attention in the first place. employment must be increased by creating new jobs not just any jobs, but by ensuring full employment of high quality, taking into account the requirements of the labour market. the main focus should be on one of the most painful problems facing society: the rise in youth unemployment. if young people are not given the opportunity to enter the labour market, then europe risks losing a whole generation of young people! the demographic situation in europe has long been a cause for concern about the employment of older people. it is necessary to provide employment-friendly employment relationships and to ensure lifelong learning opportunities. i would like to point out that the employment of disabled people should also be discussed. it is important to enable them to integrate into the labour market not only because of demographic change but also because of their own dignity and self-esteem. so i would like to ask the commission how europe will create new jobs. what are the real opportunities for people to enter the labour market? how can we ensure quality employment to reduce the poverty of the working population?
in writing. − i supported this report because i believe that the provisions on the fight against fraud need to be strengthened. tax evasion has serious implications for national budgets, violates the principle of fair taxation and can distort competition, affecting the functioning of the internal market. the current rules do not ensure effective cooperation between member states, although vat fraud is often organised across several countries and therefore requires cooperation between member states to prevent it. the new wording of the regulation is to be welcomed as it will strengthen the existing community database of vat payers and their transactions, giving member states access to information, improving administrative cooperation and making it possible to combat vat evasion more effectively.
in writing. − the events of recent weeks have shown that security at airports is an important task for the member states and that all essential precautions must be taken. each member state shall decide on the means of financing aviation security and the application of aviation security. it is essential that the directive establishes the basic principles and procedures governing the relationship between the safety authority and the air carriers. the legal requirements for airport regulation vary widely between member states, but the operator must provide air carriers with sufficient information to make consultations between airport and air carrier representatives meaningful. the directive therefore sets out the information that the airport managing body must provide on a regular basis. air carriers should, in turn, provide information on their flight forecasts, expected fleet utilisation, current and future specific airport requirements in order to enable the airport managing body to optimise its capital and capacity allocation.
in writing. - eu: 5 million km of roads (62 000 km of motorways), 215 000 km of railways and 41 000 km of inland waterways. by 2020, traffic between member states is expected to double. a united europe is not possible without a coordinated and efficient trans-european transport network (ten-t). under the eu agreement, the ten-t investment will amount to some eur 500 billion. it is therefore important to ensure european cooperation and to select priority projects carefully. the ten-t is designed to link the land, sea and air transport networks across europe by 2020. the main objective is to ensure the rapid and easy movement of goods and people between member states. lithuania's largest port, klaipėda, is connected to vilnius by a european highway and a railway line to the east and moscow. in order to remain competitive, the port needs to modernise its existing infrastructure and remove administrative barriers. railways and inland waterways should be used in particular for long-distance transport and roads for short-distance transport. increased efforts for transit of goods and inland waterway transport (more cost-effective and energy efficient, cleaner environment, safe). the safety and security of passengers is of the utmost importance. the financial crisis has affected transport policy. however, the ten-t can be used for job creation and monitoring of social and economic cohesion. the importance of transport policy for the european economy is recognised in the europe 2020 strategy. the free movement of people and goods is defined by the eu. this is only possible with a good ten-t.
in writing. − it is important to note that the burden on national financial resources is increasing and calls for further action to reduce it better use of community funding, mobilising all available funds and promoting their activities in order to overcome the crisis, in particular by using the european social fund (esf) to implement the measures for rapid economic recovery as provided for in the abovementioned communication. it is particularly important to increase efforts to facilitate the management of community funding in order to accelerate the allocation of funding to beneficiaries most affected by the economic downturn. it is important to pursue the overall objective of accelerating co-financed investments in member states and regions and increasing the impact of funding on the economy as a whole, in particular on small and medium-sized enterprises and employment. small and medium-sized enterprises are the driving force of the eu economy and a key driver of sustainable economic growth, creating many quality jobs. further simplification and refinement of the rules governing cohesion policy will undoubtedly have a positive impact on the speed of programme implementation, in particular by providing national, regional and local authorities with clearer and less bureaucratic rules, which will allow for greater flexibility in adapting programmes to new challenges.
in writing. − the global financial crisis has affected all eu countries. i think the baltic states have been hit the hardest. the financial hurricane has led to severe measures and job losses. but we have a unique opportunity to turn this crisis into a tragedy. the intention to give young lithuanians a promising lietuvos ateitį, to avoid the growing brain drain. this task is not possible without the eu structural and cohesion funds in particular the european regional development fund (erdf) and the european social fund (esf). the structural funds account for a large part of the eu's funding of 277 billion for the 2007-13 budget. the erdf promotes economic development and economic recovery in lessfavoured areas of the eu. it helped to finance measures such as the rebuilding of industrial areas affected by the decline of urban and rural areas. the scope of important regional programmes, such as the baltic sea region programme, is to strengthen recognition of the identity of the region. cohesion funds are important in reducing disparities between eu countries. the trans-european transport networks are a special case. the esf also has a crucial role to play today (2007-2013) in helping businesses and workers adapt to new market conditions by supporting innovation in the workplace, lifelong learning and increased mobility. lithuania's esf programme addresses labour shortages by mobilising human resources to develop skills and qualifications. since joining the eu, lithuania has experienced a huge outflow of money". the best way to do this is to invest eu structural funds in young professionals.
in writing. − the competitiveness of european maritime transport should remain one of the strategic objectives of the european union's maritime transport policy. to achieve this objective, it is necessary to ensure the necessary support for innovation, research and development, which will accelerate the modernisation of seaport infrastructure and ensure the application of the latest technologies in the shipbuilding industry. reducing administrative burdens and red tape would increase private and public sector investment in the seaports and shipping sectors. the development of trans-european transport networks, the provision of motorways of the sea and the development of vehicle intermodality would create a competitive and innovative european maritime transport system. it is also necessary to address the issue of the harmonisation of the taxation of crews flying the flag of the european community.
in writing. − more than 80% of world trade is carried by sea, and maritime transport remains the backbone of international trade. the eu is the world's leading exporter and second largest importer. shipping and related services are therefore essential to enable european businesses to compete on a global scale. coastal shipping is an important part of the european transport chain, carrying 40% of intra-european freight. more than 400 million passengers use european ports every year, and maritime transport has a direct impact on the quality of life of european citizens. the ep is one of the defenders of maritime policy in the eu. the eu's maritime transport policy also supports other policies, in particular the integrated maritime policy. the global financial crisis has also affected the maritime transport sector. it is therefore now time to unlock the economic potential of european shipping in a special way. to promote economic growth and social and environmental stability. the cornerstone of eu maritime policy is the long-term competitiveness of european shipping. this strategy promotes safe, clean and efficient shipping, and creates jobs in the european maritime policy industry. the strategic vision, looking at the development of shipping, ports and similar sectors, is important for simplifying the eu's maritime policy to meet future challenges such as the fight against piracy and reducing the environmental impact of shipping. an integrated, cross-sectoral approach is needed, covering fisheries, transport, environment, energy, industry, research and innovation policies. the era of competing european neighbours is over. this applies to lithuania as well as to the rest of europe.
in writing. − i supported the report, as i believe that access to cultural and educational information must be a priority in order to improve educational and living standards in europe. in view of the benefits for all eu citizens of accessing ‘europeana’ library, its availability in all the official languages should be envisaged as soon as possible. as also people with disabilities should have the benefit of digital technology and enjoy easier access to education and information through accessible formats and adapted technologies. the availability of ‘europeana’ should be improved ensuring free-of-charge access for pupils, students and teachers in secondary schools, universities and other educational institutions. therefore it is essential to guarantee and simplify universal access to european cultural heritage and to ensure that it be promoted and preserved for generations to come.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is essential to pursue a european animal welfare policy and to legislate for common animal welfare standards in the european union. good animal health and good livestock conditions are important not only for the welfare of animals but also for public health in general. given that all animals are considered sentient beings under community legislation, it is necessary to strengthen animal welfare checks and to ensure that animal protection standards are respected. unfortunately, the commission did not develop a specific strategy on animal welfare standards during this period and limited itself to a report submitted in october 2009. i support the european parliament's call for the commission to draw up a new action plan for the period 2011-2015 and to provide the necessary funding for it. the eu budget must provide sufficient funds to enable the commission to monitor, support producers where necessary and combat the decline in competitiveness resulting from the adoption of new and revised animal welfare standards. member states must also ensure that effective penalties are applied for breaches of eu animal welfare legislation. it is therefore only by strengthening animal protection legislation and its implementation that the protection of animals and the avoidance of non-compliant livestock products on the internal market can be ensured.
in writing. − i gave my full support to this report. the upcoming cap reform will have to take into account many issues, including climate change. it is already clear that climate change will have a negative impact on eu agriculture, especially in the south and south-east regions. the new cap, thus, will have to meet growing public demand for a more sustainable agricultural policy. for the time being, the cap does not address environmental issues in a consistent manner. the new challenges of climate change, water management, renewable energies and biodiversity were not fully taken on board at the time of the cap health check. i am certain that the cap must be turned into an agricultural , food and environment policy with fairer and more sustainable farmer support systems while ensuring the preservation of rural areas, biodiversity conservation , carbon capture and food security.
in writing. − climate change can affect agriculture, including water shortages, new diseases and heat-related deaths. agriculture can help to slow down climate change, but it should also be prepared to adapt to the effects of global warming. the cap must recognise the impact of global warming and take action to mitigate climate change. this can be achieved by promoting sustainable and renewable energy, carbon storage in the soil and limiting greenhouse gas emissions. however, the costs associated with cap adaptation and climate change mitigation are still unclear. a detailed cost-benefit analysis is required. climate change is a real threat, but better management of resources is needed in the near future. eu enlargement has had a major impact on eu agriculture. the then 6 million eu farmers were joined by 7 million farmers. rural areas cover 90% of the eu's territory, and more than half of them are used for farming. this fact alone underlines the importance of farming for the eu's natural environment. at the warsaw meeting in february 2010, lithuania and the other 8 eu countries signed a declaration on the new cap, thus continuing to show solidarity and fairness. europe must not be divided into 'new' and 'old' member states. a strong european agricultural policy is needed to guarantee european farmers a stable and fair income beyond 2013 and to mitigate climate change.

in writing. − progress has been made in implementing the 2008 budget, but the number of errors detected in the structural and cohesion funds, rural development, research, energy and transport remains high. the amount of the wrongly paid amounts to 11%. this results in complex rules and regulations that member states must comply with. therefore, in the implementation of the budget for the coming years, particular attention should be paid to simplifying those rules and regulations, improving the mechanism for the recovery of undue payments and introducing more effective supervision and control systems. the implementation of these measures is likely to improve the implementation of the community budget, to ensure more effective control of budgetary resources and to provide greater added value for the development of the various economic and other sectors for projects carried out by the member states.

in writing. − we must seek to strengthen transatlantic cooperation in the fields of justice, freedom and security, with respect for human rights and civil liberties. the system for the protection of personal data shall be implemented in a transparent and unambiguous manner. european legal requirements for the fair, proportionate and lawful processing of personal information are of paramount importance and must be respected at all times. the exchange of target data is not possible at present. the decisions should provide for limitations on the volume of data to be transmitted, the types of data that the designated suppliers may filter and sample, and the list of data that may be transmitted. it is therefore of particular importance to encourage the council and the commission to examine ways of establishing a transparent and legally sound procedure for the authorisation of the transfer and the selection of the data concerned, as well as for the exchange of data and the supervision of data exchange. the eu-us agreement must provide for strict enforcement and oversight safeguards regarding the selection of data transferred on a daily basis, the access to and the use of data transferred under the agreement by us authorities. provision should be made for an appropriate eu-designated authority to monitor the implementation of these measures.
in writing. − i supported this resolution. whereas in our digital age, data protection, the right of informational self-determination, personal rights and the right to privacy have become values that play an ever increasing role and must therefore be protected with special care. in order to protect these rights in an appropriate way it should be ensured that all transfers of personal data from the eu and its member states to third countries for security purposes are based on international agreements with the status of legislative acts. even more the use of pnr data should be based on a single set of principles that would serve as a basis for agreements with third countries and must be in line with european data protection standards.
in writing. − i agree with the suggestions in this european parliament resolution that a privacy impact assessment and a reasonable proportionality approach should be followed before adopting a new legal measure, as it is necessary to assess whether existing legal measures are inadequate. in today's world, where technology and mobility are essential, individual rights and the right to privacy have become values that need to be safeguarded and carefully protected. i agree with parliament's call for the examination of the measures for advance passenger information and passenger data recording, since the fight against crime must at the same time ensure that the existing measures are proportionate and do not infringe the fundamental rights of the individual. the transfer of passenger data must therefore comply with european union data protection standards and the data must be used only in relation to specific crimes or risks. given the use of passenger data records for security purposes, the conditions for data transfers need to be established in international agreements with the eu, ensuring legal certainty for eu citizens and airlines. the new eu agreements also need to provide for appropriate supervisory and control measures to coordinate the transfer and use of passenger data records.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the european partnership against cancer, proposed by the european commission for the period 2009-2013, is an excellent initiative to take a more active role in the fight against this terrible disease. the fight against cancer is one of the main areas of community action in the field of public health, with 3.2 million europeans diagnosed with cancer each year, the second leading cause of death after heart disease. the aim of the partnership proposed by the commission is to support the member states of the european union in the fight against cancer and to enable the identification and sharing of information, capacity and knowledge in the field of cancer prevention and control. i would like to point out that only by involving all eu stakeholders in collective action to fight cancer can we significantly reduce the number of cancer cases in europe. i agree with the european parliament's call for the commission and the member states to further develop and consolidate initiatives providing support directly or indirectly to people suffering from cancer. i also agree that the commission and the member states must ensure that cancer medicines are equally available to all patients who need them in all member states. this partnership launched by the commission to fight cancer is therefore a major step towards a pan-european social and political partnership to reduce the burden of cancer in europe.
in writing. − cancer is the biggest health problem in europe and even worldwide. sadly, cancer is still an epidemic today. over 3 million people are diagnosed with cancer in the eu each year and 1.7 million die from it. it is the second-largest killer in terms of mortality and morbidity. currently, one in three people in the eu will be diagnosed with cancer in their lifetime, and experts predict that the burden of cancer will increase dramatically as a result of an ageing population. urgent action is needed to improve cancer control and prevention in the eu. the situation in lithuania is particularly bad. statistical indicators of the incidence of various cancers are among the worst in the eu. i therefore particularly welcome the ep resolution on the fight against cancer, which, in the context of the enlarged eu-ec european partnership against cancer for the period 2009-2013, is a new attempt to bring together stakeholders to work together. cancer is not only a health problem, but also a social and political problem. it requires joint action at european, national, regional and local levels. i would like to stress that the treaty of lisbon clearly defines the union's competence to support, coordinate and complement the actions of the member states. our collective objective is to help member states fight cancer by providing a system for identifying and sharing information, capacity and knowledge in the field of cancer prevention and control.
in writing. − i support the ec's initiative to use information and communication technologies (ict) to improve the union's energy efficiency and increase the competitiveness of european industry. according to the ec's calculations, ict is a great tool for reducing energy consumption and, crucially, for reducing carbon emissions by up to 27%. so the damage to the environment would be reduced. i fully agree with and support the rapporteur's position that the use of ict will stimulate european industry and the market for new technologies, which should contribute to the revival of the market or the creation of new jobs. i believe that every effort should be made to introduce ict both in those member states where it has not yet been introduced and to improve it where it is already in use. i would like to stress in particular the importance of ict in planning the new european transport policy. logistics is an important factor in streamlining transport and reducing co2 emissions. it is important to recognise the need to increase public and private investment in ict tools in order to create an advanced energy infrastructure in transport. advanced transport systems for road and other modes of transport can reduce congestion and its negative environmental impact. as a member of the regi committee, i would like to emphasise the need to encourage the exchange of good practice between member states and to encourage local decision-makers to be more aware of the potential of information and communication technologies that must be exploited.
in writing. − i would like to emphasise that the fight against fraud and corruption is an important responsibility of the european institutions and of all states. however, i would like to draw particular attention to the fact that in some countries corruption in the allocation of european union funds is directly linked to the discrediting of policies within these countries. separate political and financial clans are emerging in these countries, seeking to control the distribution of eu funds. the european parliament, the european commission and the other major institutions of the european union should therefore be alert to the discrimination and discrediting of politicians, political organisations, political opponents and opposition leaders at national level. as a promoter of democracy, the european union must first and foremost ensure that its member opposition parties are able to work, express themselves and monitor anti-corruption actions. it would also be good to point out that the european union's money should be used to improve the infrastructure of the member states, to educate people, etc. investing european money only in infrastructure would benefit both the country and its businesses. there would be no more desperate businessmen asking for help but not getting it. moreover, this would not have to control the distribution of eu funds to thousands of beneficiaries, which would mean that thousands of racketeering entities would disappear. so all the focus would be on the public use of that money.
on 24 february, together with the representatives of latvia and poland, i submitted a written statement on equal treatment for agricultural workers, which highlighted the unequal funding of farmers in the community countries. the level of benefits varies from one community country to another by up to seven times, with an average of four times, and many of the new member states are below the union average. such a situation is contrary to one of the community's most important principles, the principle of solidarity. i call on the council, the commission and parliament to harmonise direct payments or at least to reduce the disparities, and at the same time to put an end to the disparate treatment of farmers in the member states. i would ask my colleagues in the european parliament to support the aforementioned written statement no 11 on equal treatment for agricultural workers in the european union.
member of the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the commission has already made a number of proposals. in politics, there is rarely an opportunity to present proposals and measures as a win-win solution. today we have such an opportunity: the forthcoming adoption of the revised directive on the energy performance of buildings. buildings account for 40% of energy demand and 36% of carbon dioxide emissions in the eu. implementing economic measures to reduce energy consumption in homes can make a significant contribution to achieving our 2020 greenhouse gas reduction and energy saving targets. we are also strengthening our energy security and creating growth and jobs in the construction sector. the newly adopted directive on the energy performance of buildings is helping to raise national building codes to a more ambitious level in terms of energy consumption in homes and is helping to reduce consumers' energy bills. energy efficiency is the cheapest way to combat climate change. i would like to highlight three elements of the new directive which we consider to be a major step forward from the situation as it stands. firstly, the national building codes for new construction and renovation will result in buildings that are significantly more energy efficient. this directive also covers smaller buildings of less than 1 000 m2 and minor energy-related renovations, as well as the replacement of boilers or windows. secondly, the new version will improve the information available to our citizens. homeowners and tenants are well informed with concrete information on the energy consumption and energy saving potential of a building. the aim is to stimulate the market for the construction of low-energy houses and for large-scale renovations. third, all new buildings must meet the ultra-rigorous low-energy building standard from 2020. the commission also proposes that member states draw up national action plans to increase the number of buildings in the eu to the lowest energy building standard. for all these reasons, the new directive is a success of european energy policy. i thank the members of this parliament and you, mrs ţicău, for the good cooperation you have shown in the past few months and also for the unusually rapid adoption of the directive. they have already stressed that the new approach can only achieve its full energy saving potential if it is implemented effectively and rapidly and if there are supporting instruments. to this end, we must make better use of existing financial instruments, such as the european regional development fund, where the possibility of calling up 4% of the budget for energy efficiency measures has so far been little used by the member states. we also want to support the efforts of the member states to transform the building stock. we are preparing these days - as already announced in the committee on industry - to redistribute at least eur 150 million of uncalled funds from the european economic recovery programme to projects in the field of renewable energy and energy efficiency. i am sure that with the support of this parliament we are taking an important step today. thank you all for your cooperation.
on behalf of the s&d group first of all, i would like to congratulate my colleague silvia ţicău on her hard work in preparing this important report. the agreement reached with the council is, in my view, very ambitious, representing a new leap in quality for the whole sector. it is therefore essential that member states now implement the provisions of the directive properly and in a timely manner. the issue of energy performance of buildings is of particular relevance in the context of the overall internal energy market. the building sector is one of the sectors with the greatest potential for energy savings in the european union. this is particularly relevant for residents of the new countries of the european union, as these countries have the highest number of old and energy-poor residential buildings, and residents living in them and earning the lowest incomes are forced to pay the most for utilities. the approach to near-zero energy consumption in buildings means that the cartel for builders is set even higher than has been the case in the past in the discussion of passive house technologies. it is indeed very important, and i repeat, very ambitious, and it is to be hoped, commissioner, that it will be put into practice.
as many have already said, the most cost-effective way to achieve energy security is to reduce carbon dioxide emissions by saving energy. i would like to join in congratulating all my colleagues, especially the rapporteur, and all the other colleagues, on the successful revision of the directive. the building sector, as a whole, has a great deal of untapped potential not only for energy saving but also for creating new jobs and new technologies. for example, in lithuania, my home country, over 80 percent of the buildings are more than 20 years old. and it's very uneconomical to do that. the revision of this energy performance of buildings directive is therefore very timely and necessary in the current crisis. the agreement reached with the council on the new wording of the directive was well balanced full account was taken of the principle of subsidiarity. the minimum requirements for new and renovated buildings laid down in the directive will help to optimise the use of energy resources and save money for citizens and the state. meanwhile, stricter building certification and information rules will encourage citizens to change their consumption habits. mr president, it has been said many times, but i would like to stress once again that the success of the directive will depend on its rapid implementation in the member states. this requires the provision of effective financial support measures at both member state and european union level. energy efficiency, one of the priorities of the european union, should also become a policy priority for each member state.
i voted in favour of this report and i think it is important to note that women in the european union are still paid on average 17.4% less per hour than men. over the past 15 years, this gap has narrowed only slightly, and in some countries it has even widened. i support parliament's calls for regular reports on pay disparities in the european union and i support all measures that help reduce discrimination against women in the european union.
madam president, i would like to express my concern regarding the situation of banana producers in the canary islands, guadeloupe, martinique and madeira, which the treaty include among the outermost regions. their situation has been made more difficult especially since the eu, within the world trade organisation, concluded an agreement with latin american countries on lowering tariffs for banana imports from that region. i would like to draw attention to the fact that in 2006 the eu reformed its common market organisation for bananas. it committed budgetary funds for aid to banana producers in those regions. since the reform, the eu has committed eur 208 million each year to supporting banana producers in the canaries, the french antilles, madeira and, to a lesser extent, in the azores. although we should welcome such support, for many banana producers this support will not be enough. so i would like to urge the competent institutions to adopt the necessary measures to ensure that traditional branches of the economy in the eu are not compromised for more strategic trade goals.
in writing. − the eu has identified the building sector as one of the most promising areas for energy savings. energy-efficient buildings save on average 30% of energy compared to conventional buildings. in the new eu countries, as well as in lithuania, many apartment blocks waste about 60% of their heat energy. there are over 35,000 of these apartment buildings in lithuania alone. not only do their residents pay huge sums for heating, but they are responsible for tons of co2 emissions. the conservative government in lithuania is unable to start heating the buildings. after nearly two years of promises that renovations would soon begin, not a single building had been renovated. i voted in favour of this recommendation on the energy performance of buildings, as it sets the objective of achieving near-zero energy consumption in buildings. this will ensure that eu buildings become energy sustainable over time. implementing this directive will help the eu to reduce its energy consumption by one fifth by 2020. investments in energy efficiency will create millions of jobs and contribute to eu economic growth.
in writing. − the stockholm programme provides for the establishment of a robust and sustainable common asylum policy in the european union. however, the implementation and safeguarding of the objectives of the reception policy requires the encouragement of the voluntary participation of member states in the eu refugee resettlement scheme, and the european parliament therefore seeks to give as many member states as possible the opportunity to participate in eu refugee resettlement schemes. the european parliament supports the commission's proposal and considers that the resettlement of refugees should be one of the key aspects of the eu's external asylum policy. it should be further developed and extended to become an effective safeguard measure for the eu.
in writing. − the purpose of this proposal is to ensure the transition from the schengen information system, which is currently in its sis 1+ form, to the second generation schengen information system (sis ii). it is indeed regrettable that the start of the system is delayed. the european parliament has made every effort to ensure that the system is operational as soon as possible and that the implementation process of sis ii is fully transparent, but it is necessary to seek the agreement of all the institutions. it is important to note that the commission should apply an alternative programme in the event of the failure of the sis ii project and that parliament should be involved in the decision-making process on the transition. the commission should inform the european parliament in detail of the results of the tests before the transition to the new sis ii system, while giving the parliament the opportunity to express its opinion.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the issue of refugees remains of great concern to the community. currently, the resettlement of refugees in the european union is not properly coordinated and only 10 member states resettle refugees annually, which reduces the strategic use of resettlement as an eu external policy instrument. i welcome the commission's decision to set up a european asylum support office, which will be able to provide support to member states in implementing relocation initiatives while ensuring policy coordination at community level.
in writing. − i supported this report because i believe that the eu needs to improve the quality of education and training. unfortunately, the guidelines set out in the lisbon strategy will not be implemented before the end of 2010. despite the progress made in recent years, many european citizens still lack the necessary skills and one third have very low levels of education. another concern is the growing number of young people who leave school early and do not acquire any qualifications later on. i think it is very important to start implementing policies to raise the quality of education and training in the member states as soon as possible. the european commission, together with the member states and employers, should work closely with the developers of education and training programmes, ensuring higher levels of education and a wider range of applications and meeting the needs of the professional sectors and the labour market.
in writing. − i voted for this report because it is a very good time to review the education and learning objectives set out in the lisbon strategy and to assess what has failed in this area. it is good to see that the new europe 2020 strategy has among its main objectives the improvement of education levels, the reduction of the number of out-of-school children and the increase in the proportion of people with tertiary education or equivalent. first of all, i would like to point out that the rapid development of information and new technologies is making the educational environment increasingly complex and diverse, and that it is necessary to adapt to the new needs of a changing world. it is therefore necessary to reform and improve curricula in schools and universities to meet the changing needs of the market. secondly, i call for greater efforts to increase literacy and skills training for the socially disadvantaged. one of the main objectives of this programme is to modernise vocational training and higher education. this means that it is necessary to maintain contacts with the business world and to provide new, more attractive prospects for qualifications and mobility for those in vocational training. i am pleased that the european parliament has given great attention to the need to facilitate the integration of people with disabilities in education and learning. i also agree that adequate funding is needed to ensure the integration of people with disabilities in learning.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, the european seatbelt law is taking effect as a painful, strict measure. sacrifices and compromises are already commonplace in most eu sectors, but it is important (especially in times of economic difficulty) that we do not abandon our commitments in key policy areas such as education. education is something worth fighting for, as history has shown - whether it's in the nazi-occupied european underground educational institutions or the dissident "flying university" of stalin's time. the global economic crisis means that we are again forced to adapt. vocational education and training must be adapted to the needs of the labour market. the european literacy rate needs to be improved in this area, europe lags far behind the us and japan. we have to fill the void. it is important not only to raise the literacy, numeracy and digital skills of young people, but also of unemployed adults from the lower socioeconomic class. lifelong learning from cradle to grave also known as 'second chance' programmes for adults with limited capacity are important not only for accelerating the economy and the labour market, but also for social inclusion and active citizenship. our duty is to raise the standard of quality in education across europe and to create a more attractive image of education. it's a long fight, but it's worth it.
