in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the development and implementation of an eu youth strategy is of great importance, especially at a time when the economic and financial crisis has had a negative impact on youth employment and continues to put their future well-being at risk. as the future of europe is young, it is our duty to help it by implementing a well-designed youth strategy. first, therefore, member states must ensure that all children and young people have access to public education and that everyone has equal educational opportunities, regardless of their social background and financial situation. i would like to draw attention to the urgent need to take measures to reduce the number of out-of-school children, as set out in the new europe 2020 strategy. only by improving the learning conditions for all young people and ensuring the right to education and training for all will we be able to give young people a foothold in the labour market. i would also like to stress the need to develop specific policies in the field of employment policy and the fight against youth unemployment. it is essential to promote youth entrepreneurship and to facilitate access to micro-credit and micro-finance instruments for young people.
in writing. − i voted for this document because i am part of the same young people who are shaping the future of the eu and i do not care what the future holds for me and my peers. one of the main problems facing young people in my country, lithuania, and in other eu countries, is unemployment, which is already causing the youth of today to be referred to as the lost generation of our times. another existing problem is the mismatch between the educational system and the needs of the labour market. while we welcome the commission's communication on a youth strategy, i am concerned that it, like many of the other strategies we adopt, may remain mere paper orders. we, the european parliament, the other eu institutions, the member states and the non-strategies, must realise that if we do not take care of young people now, all our other strategies, including the eu 2020 strategy, may not be implemented because there is simply no one left to implement them. i therefore call on the commission, the other responsible institutions and the member states to take concrete action as soon as possible to make this strategy a reality.
in writing. - the global economic crisis has dealt a severe blow to us all. the sad thing is that the people who have suffered the most are the ones who are the ones who are the ones who are going to have to take our future. the financial tsunami of the past few years has left many young people unemployed or underemployed. according to the report, in most eu countries, the unemployment rate among young people has risen to almost double the rate of the working population as a whole. now we have the opportunity to turn this crisis into an opportunity. we owe it to the next generation of europeans. it is important for my country, lithuania, and for most of its neighbours, to prevent the increasing outflow of skilled workers, 'brains'. as the title of the report suggests, this can be achieved through investment and opportunity. education and employment are key areas from an investment perspective. a smoother transition between education and the labour market needs to be facilitated. but these essential investments will have limited impact if we do not provide opportunities for our young people. we need to encourage our young people to become more active citizens both socially and politically. the low voter turnout among young people in eastern europe requires a renewed interest in the political process. civil society and relevant ngos can support eu interests, for example the project 'my voice' has contributed to the political awareness of young people through innovative internet technologies. it is also necessary to establish a constructive dialogue with young people and youth organisations in europe, freeing them from bureaucratic knots and technical jargon.
in writing. − investing in human capital and social infrastructure is crucial to overcoming the economic downturn, enabling women and men to reach their full potential. the european union is now close to the lisbon strategy target of 60% female employment by 2010, but there is a great disparity in female employment across the member states. the commission and the member states must therefore take effective measures to implement the directive on the application of the principle of equal treatment between men and women engaged in an activity in a self-employed capacity as soon as possible. maternity protection for the spouse or cohabiting partner of a self-employed woman and, accordingly, of a self-employed person is of particular importance. it must be recognised that they require an allowance to enable them to cease their activities for a sufficient period of time to ensure the normal course of the pregnancy and the physical recovery of the mother after a normal birth, in order to provide women with an adequate maternity allowance. i therefore particularly welcome the rapporteur's opinion on the proposals for the second review of this directive. in addition, the commission's consultation on the new strategy 2020 does not pay sufficient attention to gender equality issues. the gender dimension needs to be strengthened and mainstreamed in the new strategy.
in writing. − i voted for this report not only because it is important and because i believe it will affect millions of eu citizens. i know a lot of artists, lawyers and other people who are going out on their own, and i know that very often they have family members who help them, usually their wives and children. the fact that they now have the right to social protection will undoubtedly improve their material situation, their mood and their motivation to work. this is particularly important for lithuania, as many families are now on the verge of extinction, especially as the crisis has hit the baltic states particularly hard. we cannot continue to watch from the sidelines how women or men in self-sustaining families suffer social injustice. the adoption of this report is a clear signal to eu governments to support the spouses of self-employed people, to guarantee their right to a pension, to paid leave, to maternity or paternity leave, and to help them reintegrate into the labour market.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this document because i believe that the simplification of cap regulations and provisions that started in 2005 must be continued. the burden of red tape for farmers and other economic operators in the agricultural sector should be reduced and requirements that are not necessary to achieve policy objectives and ensure the proper management of taxpayers' money should be abolished. if we want to improve the competitiveness of the agricultural economy, create or maintain jobs and develop rural areas properly, the cap must be simplified. in some member states, there are a high number of errors in the application form, which hamper the timely receipt of aid by farmers. member states should establish schemes for potential beneficiaries that are simple, clear and transparent, and i would therefore urge the commission to include this issue in bilateral discussions with member states.
in writing. − i supported this report because a well-functioning and transparent public procurement system is essential for the internal market to promote cross-border competition, innovation and optimum benefit for public authorities. the objectives of the 2004 revision of the procurement directives, in particular the simplification of procurement rules and the provision of greater legal certainty, have not been achieved so far. the very high number of cases before the european court of justice for irregularities in the field of public procurement shows that the public service directives are not being properly implemented in the member states. e treaty of lisbon recognises for the first time in the primary law of the european union the right of regional and local authorities and strengthens subsidiarity. taking into account that the purpose of public procurement law is to ensure the sound and efficient use of public funds and to enable interested companies to participate in competitions on the basis of fair competition, i would urge the commission to simplify public procurement procedures in order to help local authorities and companies avoid the high costs of time and money associated with dealing with purely bureaucratic issues and to ensure greater opportunities for smes to participate in public procurement on a more equal and fairer basis.
in writing. − public procurement accounts for a significant proportion of the member states' budgets and i therefore agree with the rapporteur that all measures must be taken to ensure that public funds are used transparently and are available for public scrutiny. public contracts must be concluded on transparent terms, with equal rights for all interested parties and with the price-performance ratio as the main criterion in order to award the contract to the best tenderer rather than to the lowest bidder. to increase the transparency of public procurement, it is necessary to simplify procurement procedures in order to help local authorities and companies avoid the high costs of time and money associated with dealing with purely bureaucratic issues. this will also give small and medium-sized enterprises more opportunities to participate in public procurement and to participate on a more equal and fairer basis. the commission's attention to the procedure for applying social criteria is particularly important in the context of globalisation processes and therefore guidelines or other practical assistance are needed to provide particular information to public authorities and other public institutions in preparing sustainability-related public procurement.
in writing. − the sanctioning systems in the eu member states vary widely, making the legal situation difficult for operators, especially drivers in international transport. the current regulations and the directive on social rules for road traffic leave a wide margin of interpretation for the member states, which has led to a heterogeneous implementation of these laws across the member states. the lisbon treaty provides for the possibility of harmonising the provisions of national criminal and other laws. i believe that the commission must take this opportunity to develop and promote harmonised approaches to control and regulatory action to remove obstacles to the european internal market and to improve road safety. in order to ensure the most effective implementation of social rules in road transport, the commission should develop a uniform and binding interpretation of this regulation on driving and rest periods, which should be taken into account by the national control authorities.
on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, the review conference of the international criminal court (icc) is a long-awaited and important event in the evolution of the court itself and, more broadly, international criminal justice. the review conference is a good opportunity for the stakeholders to consider the existing deficiencies of the icc and propose possible changes or directions for reform. i would like to mention several issues that eu member states, as well as the ep delegation, should have on the agenda. one of the issues is certainly the position of our transatlantic partners on the icc. i would like to point out that the lack of participation of the united states in the icc is the single most important factor reducing the relevance and authority of the icc. it would therefore be very useful if our american partners could express more clearly their current position and commitment to the icc. i would also like to urge the eu to insist on discussing – and hopefully revising – article 124, also known as the transitional provision, of the rome statute, which allows states to choose not to have their nationals subject to the icc’s jurisdiction over war crimes for a seven-year period after the ratification of the statute. this is a regrettable loophole that should be removed from the rome statute. last but not least, i would like to point out that the eu should strive for the widest ratification and implementation of the rome statute in relations with those countries that have not yet done so. it must be an objective of the eu during the enlargement negotiations and accession phases of the new eu member states. it should be high on the agenda in our dealings with russia as well, especially taking into account the fact that we are currently negotiating the modalities of the partnership agreement with our russian partners.
madam president, the treaty of lisbon recognises the importance of the availability and accessibility of social services and the roles played by local and regional authorities in providing these. the issues of social housing are of extreme importance to people with low incomes, and it is a reality that the crisis has left a large number of people unemployed, who are in need of social housing. currently, member states apply different standards when deciding their criteria for social housing. it is understandable that the economic and social situations differ significantly in the member states. in order to implement the right of access to universal services, i believe it would be reasonable to define general principles and to set the recommended upper income limit at eu level. however, we need to allow broad discretion for the member states to make this lower, taking into account economic and social differences.

for the efd group i will not repeat the thoughts expressed by most of the speakers on the consequences of the icelandic volcano, the closure of airspace, the losses to airlines and consumers, i.e. passengers who were trapped in airports. it is good to hear today that decisions on the creation of a single european sky are coming and that the airspace coordinator has already been operational since may, as we heard from the commissioner. however, while we are not sure today that we will be able to coordinate such or somewhat similar volatile situations in the future, i think we should consider how to develop and give more attention to other, alternative modes of passenger transport and mobility, such as the railway, mentioned by the german representative. in our region, the baltic region, which is represented by lithuania, latvia, estonia (which is on the periphery of north-eastern europe), this situation with the volcanic eruption has particularly highlighted our need for good rail connections, and i am referring to the rail baltics project. i understand that other regions in europe may have the same problem, not much better (or what?), but i am talking about my region today, and therefore i would like to see that in such cases we have the possibility, through poland, of connecting our railway line with poland, to connect with western and central europe. otherwise we will be cut off from the european union's communications system. other countries have railways and perhaps mobile trains, but our three baltic states do not yet have them. i would therefore ask the commission whether it will pay sufficient attention in the near future to the proper implementation of alternative mobile projects. with the assistance of the european union, will the necessary political will be achieved and mobilised and will alternative means of transport for passengers be provided to avoid such consequences in similar crisis situations?
the consequences of the volcanic ash crisis have had a negative impact on the transport system across europe, with the ash of the erupting volcano causing the cancellation of more than 100 000 flights and disrupting the journeys of 10 million passengers. the crisis has particularly affected the eastern european union, which has remained completely cut off from the western european part and, unlike the other member states of the european union, has had no alternative transport options. this is further proof of the importance of developing transport infrastructure, developing trans-european transport networks and creating a system of alternative means of transport for both freight and passengers. the commission therefore considers that the aid is compatible with the internal market if it is granted in accordance with the rules on state aid. it is essential to develop a transport system that ensures not only efficient passenger transport but also the smooth functioning of the economy. i agree with the suggestions made about the implementation of additional links and would therefore call for support for the project of a competitive european rail freight corridor, which would link the baltic states mentioned above with other western european countries and create added value for the community as a whole. the project would also provide an alternative to air transport for the region's inhabitants.
i also voted in favour of this important document, the human organ transplantation quality standards. and it would be really good to have a system of organ transplantation that is transparent and clean, that is quality and safety at the european union level, and that is developed and regulated at community level. this is one of the more important goals. there are 56,000 citizens waiting for donors, and this shortage of organs for transplantation is causing other problems, criminal activity, criminal problems. i therefore believe that this document will contribute to the development of an appropriate framework to ensure safe and reliable organ transplantation.
mr president, i voted in favour of the resolution on the transplantation of human organs. many people die every day because they suffer from organ failure and because organs are unavailable. the eu can help patients waiting for organs across europe and as a consequence save lives. what we need is a well-coordinated system of organ donation and transplantation. i would like to stress once again a very important point, which is a political point: namely the principle of voluntary and paid donation for human body parts. reports from the world health organisation and the council of europe say that in several countries in the world people are given a great deal of money to donate an organ. there are even reports that people are deliberately killed to get their organs: for example, practitioners of falun gong in china. we are also aware of cases in some countries where the medical risk for the donor is high and the transplantation is often done under very bad medical conditions. so, finally, i would like to thank those who initiated the resolution we adopted today, and especially our rapporteur mr mikolášik.
this is a rather sensitive subject indeed, and one which has generated a lot of debate in the european parliament and, of course, in the public. i do not think that banning a product or additive is the answer, and i would perhaps have to agree with the argument that perhaps it is the consumer who has the right to choose, with all the information he has, what he wants to buy and what he wants to consume. on the other hand, it would be quite difficult to control the process. and in this age of public knowledge, in this age of information, we haven't yet formulated a socially acceptable, clear, un-speculative formulation where the person buying a product can actually get the right information and understand what the ingredients are in a product. so i think there's a lack of consultation with the same public, with their education, and maybe some additional consultation with scientists.
i voted in favour of the resolution on the conference on the revision of the rome statute of the international criminal court, which will begin at the end of this month in kampala, uganda. in 1998, 138 states agreed to establish the international criminal court and adopted the rome statute, which has been the basis for the court's work for seven years, since 2003. it is time to review the rome statute, in particular the so-called 'interim provisions' which were not agreed in 2002. all eu member states have ratified the rome statute. but some of our partners, such as the united states, russia and china, have not joined the icc's work. today, in its resolution, the european parliament again called on these countries to ratify the rome statute and to cooperate with the international criminal court. in the 21st century, those responsible for genocide, crimes against humanity and war crimes must not only be morally condemned by the international community, but also be held to account in a legal sense. there can be no impunity for such crimes.
in writing. - i have supported this important report. the energy labelling directive plays a crucial role in achieving the eu's 20% energy efficiency target by 2020. to this end, it has a key role to play in the fight against climate change, in the transition of the eu towards an efficient, sustainable and competitive economy and in strengthening europe's energy security. our aim is to create conditions and conditions that benefit both the market and consumers by ensuring that each consumer has access to the right information and that each person fully understands the impact of the choice. the agreement reached on the energy labelling directive has important added value in this respect. i welcome the european parliament's position on maintaining the a-g scale model, which the survey has shown is the most acceptable and understandable to consumers. all advertising for energy-related products must include information on the price and energy consumption of the product or object.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the resolution on the labelling of electricity-using products and the provision of basic information to consumers. we talk about citizens' rights in this house a lot - the right to choose, the right to accurate and truthful information. i think this is especially important in all areas of life. in this case, the issue is related to the electricity used and its efficiency. people actually want to save energy, and they usually do it for two reasons: economic and environmental. today, with technological progress, people can choose electrical household appliances according to their energy efficiency, the labelling we have endorsed will contribute to making people make a conscious choice, to saving energy (thus making people choose more economical solutions) and to preserving the environment. i am sure that europeans will take advantage of the opportunity to choose more efficient and environmentally friendly products and equipment as they become aware of the energy they use. i welcome once again the european parliament's position on this issue and hope that such decisions will be a real incentive and opportunity to fulfil its commitment to reduce energy consumption by 2020.
in writing. − i support this report on the setting of standards for the quality and safety of human organs for transplantation. with the growing demand for organ transplantation in the european union and the imbalance between the number of patients waiting for transplantation and the number of organs donated, it is necessary to avoid the commercialisation of donation and to prevent the illicit trade in organs. it is therefore necessary to apply strict legislation on living donors, to ensure the transparency of organ waiting lists, to establish strict confidentiality rules to protect the data of donors and waiting persons and to define the liability of medical practitioners. the adoption of common quality and safety standards would allow for cross-border organ exchanges, which could lead to an increase in the number of transplants performed.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution, which aims to limit the use of food additives that are harmful to health in foodstuffs, thus protecting the health of consumers. the legislation in force in the european union provides that food additives may be used if they are of benefit to the consumer. as the benefits of thrombin (meat glue) for consumers have not been demonstrated and the process of assembling individual pieces of meat increases the risk of bacterial contamination of the meat, thus threatening the safety of the final product and the health of consumers, i did not support the proposal to authorise this food additive in beef and pigmeat products. it is also necessary to prevent meat products treated with such meat glue from reaching catering establishments.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because i agree that the use of food additives must be moderate and justified only if it provides an additional benefit to consumers. unfortunately, i do not think that food glue called thrombine meets this requirement. the widespread use of 'food glue' would make it difficult for consumers to distinguish between real meat and meat glued together from small pieces. as mentioned in the report, this increases the risk of meat becoming infected. the only benefit of thrombin would be to producers who could market parts of the meat that would otherwise be useless. the european union is committed to combating counterfeiting and falsification in all other industries. i think the food industry should be no exception.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, the statistics speak for themselves. according to this report, there are now 56 000 patients waiting for a suitable organ donor in the european union. every day, 12 people die waiting for an organ transplant. unfortunately, due to the severe shortage of organ donors and the lack of cross-border coordination, many people are simply dying. this sensitive issue involves a variety of legal and cultural aspects. but the situation is further complicated by the different national policies in the eu member states and by the substantially different rates of organ donation. some of these shortcomings can be mitigated by the eu database and certification system, which would provide information on the supply of organs and guarantee their quality and legality. it is also important to mobilise and inform the public. most eu citizens are generally not opposed to donating, but they are afraid to put their name on the register. donors should therefore be made as accessible as possible incentives can often be effective. for example, i welcome forms where citizens are given the opportunity to add their name directly to the organ donor register when applying for a driver's licence. the shortage of transplantable organs is also a powerful incentive for organ trafficking and human trafficking. the eu should develop better coordination of donation and transplantation, as we see europe's poorest regions becoming fertile ground for the illegal organ trade.
in writing. − the lisbon treaty provides the legal basis for eu accession to the echr the main instrument for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms in europe and the heart of europe. the eu's accession to the convention will further strengthen the eu's framework for the protection of fundamental rights. i support this proposal because it is a historic opportunity to enable citizens of the european union and the member states to enjoy equal protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms. the european court of human rights in strasbourg will be given the power to examine whether acts of the eu institutions, bodies and agencies, including judgments of the european court of justice, are compatible with the provisions of the european convention on human rights. it is very important that individuals have a new opportunity to defend their rights. by exhausting all national remedies, they will be able to bring actions before the european court of human rights for violations of fundamental rights committed by the eu this will encourage the development of a more coherent system of case law in the field of human rights. the uniform and full application of the charter of fundamental rights at eu level is also essential to ensure trust in the european union. as accession to the convention will have a major legal impact on the creation of a coherent system for the protection of human rights, i would urge the commission and the member states to consider the possibility of drawing up guidelines which clearly set out all the implications of accession to the convention, the impact on citizens' rights and the complaints procedure.
in writing. − as you know, respect for human rights is a fundamental value of the european union, enshrined in the ec treaty. there is no doubt that the eu's accession to the european convention on human rights could - if all goes well - become a historic opportunity to strengthen the human rights situation in the eu and across europe. this could be our chance to guarantee human rights and fundamental freedoms for eu citizens. the eu's accession to the echr would provide europe with a great opportunity to act as a moral beacon, to set an example. this would not only enhance the eu's credibility in its relations with non-eu member states, but also improve public perception of eu structures. but that is what would happen if we made a concerted effort to eliminate double standards in our political program and our legal system. sadly, as events in recent years have shown, often the blame has been purely political. the courts - both in my country and in the rest of europe - are still very often vulnerable to political manipulation. this must change if we are to prevent promising developments such as the eu's accession to the echr from being a waste of time. i therefore wholeheartedly support accession to the echr if it is a genuine defence of fundamental human rights and freedoms, and above all within the eu itself.
in writing. in recent months, in my country, lithuania, a study has been carried out on the adaptation of higher education institutions to the new needs of the labour market and the creation of employment opportunities for graduates immediately after finishing higher education. unfortunately, the survey found that many universities are producing professionals whose market is already overcrowded. this means that higher education institutions do not always take account of the needs of the market and that the young people who have just finished their studies are the most affected. i therefore fully support the initiative to provide a specific strategy or framework in the future, with a strong commitment and a strong commitment to strengthen university-business relations at local, national and international level. i would like to point out that the lack of jobs and the harsh working conditions affect the lives of every individual, and young people are particularly vulnerable in this situation. i would also like to emphasise that the dynamic market, the rapidly changing working environment and the increasing development of new technologies also require higher education institutions to adapt to new developments. it is necessary to improve and reform curricula in order to ensure a high level of education and to address the issue of graduate employment. the contribution of universities alone is not enough, but also the contribution of business and public authorities.
i would like to congratulate my colleague, rebecca harms, on her well-written report and i support the document under consideration and the proposal to provide eur 300 million in aid to bulgaria for the closure of the four units of the kozloduy nuclear power plant over a period of three years, while, of course, strengthening control over the use of the funds received. it can be said that equal solidarity is being demonstrated, as other new countries - lithuania and slovakia in a similar situation - have already received such financial support. the four nuclear power plants have been out of operation for some time and logically they must be closed. this will reduce environmental risks and help to make better use of the knowledge and professional experience of the staff. the closure of part of the plant will put new challenges on the plant's staff. lithuania's experience has shown that unforeseen obstacles arise, and it is difficult to focus on the established timetables. the fact that the bulk of the funds should be devoted to the closure of the blocks inevitably raises the question of the installation of storage facilities. but it is no less important to care for people. i believe that the bulgarian government, with the help of the european union, will help the workers and the community around the power plant to adapt to the changed conditions, and it is very important to help them to retrain and apply their knowledge in other areas, to promote the creation of new sustainable jobs and to transition to new energy, which is environmentally friendly.
some central and eastern european countries that have joined the european union have made politically and economically complex commitments in their accession treaties to close nuclear power plants and ensure safe management of radioactive waste. they are nuclear power states and inherited nuclear power plants that do not meet the safety standards and requirements of today's european union and obviously could not raise the necessary funds to close them themselves. the strict requirements in the european union for the decommissioning and safety work, the financial and economic crisis, and the additional obstacles to the timely and proper execution of the closure and fuel burial work by the countries closing nuclear power plants. the community is understandably concerned about the recycling of spent nuclear fuel outside the european union, which could also threaten our future generations. i therefore welcome the commission's decision to provide additional support to bulgaria from the european union budget, as it is clear that without community support, as in the case of lithuania, it would not be possible to ensure the implementation of the necessary decommissioning works, the common community security policy and the necessary measures for the safety of european citizens and the protection of the environment.

in writing. − perhaps no eu member state doubts that cooperation between education and business is necessary. in a time of crisis, when the problem of unemployment is highlighted, and when the importance of education meeting the needs of the market is also highlighted, it is gratifying that the european parliament, by endorsing this document, is seeking to stimulate a dialogue between academia and business. this should be seen as a long-term perspective from the very beginning of higher education: encouraging various exchange programmes, internships in enterprises, perhaps even consulting universities and enterprises in the development of curricula. if future employers are involved in the education system from the outset, they are more likely to produce professionals who are better able to respond to employers' needs in the labour market.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, the closure of the bulgarian kozloduy nuclear power plant and the lithuanian ignalina nuclear power plant was one of the conditions for accession to the european union. the closure of the ignalina nuclear power plant in 2009 had a strong impact on lithuania. since lithuania regained its independence in 1991, the ignalina nuclear power plant has made a significant contribution to our economy and has covered more than 70% of our country's energy consumption. the closure forced us to abandon our status as an electricity exporter and become a broadband importer. the ignalina nuclear power plant was a source of cheap energy for industry and consumers, as well as a source of export revenues. like the closure of kozloduy, the disappearance of ignalina will result in the loss of businesses and jobs for the local economy. the full eu financial assistance to kozloduy and ignalina is important in order to remedy some of the economic and social consequences of the closure of the nuclear power plant. the financial turmoil of recent years has hit lithuania and most eu countries hard, temporarily dashing hopes of building new state-of-the-art nuclear power plants in the near future. but it would be foolish on our part to abandon the nuclear power plant altogether. nuclear technology is by no means an antidote to our energy security and natural disasters, but the efficient and safe use of nuclear energy could contribute to overcoming long-term strategic challenges.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, lithuania and several of our neighbouring eu countries almost reached bottom in 2009. the griffins may not be rolling over our heads, but we still don't feel safe: lithuania's gdp fell by 4.1% from quarter to quarter and in the first quarter of 2010. most lithuanians understand that the need for painful sacrifices and drastic measures will not disappear overnight. the long-term sustainability of public finances is essential for stability and growth. reducing the budget deficit is the way to go. the report rightly notes that high debt and deficit levels threaten sustainability and could have a devastating impact on employment, public health and pensions. the decision we have to make cannot be taken lightly the growing government deficit is becoming a heavy burden for future generations. financial stability is important for the recovery of the lithuanian and european economies. i therefore support the measures discussed this week, such as the proposals for a european systemic risk board and a european system of financial supervisors. now more than ever, we need smoother markets and risk insurance funds, and better coordination and supervision. we must also continue to take our international commitments seriously. the most important thing is to restore public confidence and economic self-respect in each of our countries. this can be achieved by ensuring the long-term sustainability of public finances, speaking out and being transparent in all activities.
madam president, the constitution of pakistan differentiates between muslims and non-muslims, thereby enabling discrimination on the basis of religion. in this context it is important to recall that in december 2009 president asif ali zardari reiterated the pledge of the ppp (pakistan people’s party) to uphold the rights of minorities to be treated as equal citizens. unfortunately, reports and surveys done by independent agencies reveal that minorities in pakistan are deprived of basic civil liberties and equal opportunities in jobs, education and political representation. so the overall picture concerning religious freedom in pakistan even without going into the details, is controversial and gives a lot of ground for concern. i would also like to stress the contradiction between the pakistan government’s commitment to freedom of religion and its leading role in the organisation of islamic countries in endorsing their ‘combating defamation of religion’ agenda at the united nations. in this context i would like to recall the eu council conclusion of 16 november 2009 on the relationship between international human rights law, which protects individuals and groups of individuals, and the concept of defamation of religion.

the mdgs allow us a unique point of entry into the human condition at the beginning of the 21st century. therefore, it is critically important to come up with a new and fresh perspective, instead of offering that of the 20th century. it is a fact of our time that no development is possible without security, and vice versa. this is why we have tried to offer a more balanced approach to the mdgs, paying more attention to such regions as central asia, where poverty goes hand in hand with a lack of safety, insecurity, instability, forced child labour and even slavery. in addition to the autocratic regimes in uzbekistan and turkmenistan, ethnic violence recently broke out in kyrgyzstan. dire straits, combined with instability and violence, may lead to a humanitarian catastrophe in the region. if we need proof that no durable and sustainable development exists without security, we have it here. in addition to that, we cannot forget about extreme forms of poverty in such european countries as moldova and albania. let us take, for example, some grave problems relating to health care. in ukraine and moldova 300 out of 10 000 people suffer from tuberculosis. the aforementioned countries do not fall into the category of least developed countries but they certainly fall into that of developing countries. if the mdgs do not embrace the least stable and least secure countries, we will never achieve a viable, social and moral order there. therefore, the time has come to expand our horizons. although sub-saharan africa is crucial to us, we cannot confine the mdgs to this sole, familiar context. we are facing new challenges and we have to react to them. last but not least, the mdgs are inseparable from the necessity to empower the least developed, developing and least secure countries so that they can shape their future themselves.

vice-president of the commission. − madam president, thank you very much for this debate. i listened with great interest to all your contributions. there is a great temptation to go into detail and to have a debate on the details of this discussion, but i would like only to say that i still confirm the commission position, which is built on facts. we do not have the studies mentioned here that self-employed drivers are regularly working 90 hours per week, and we think that the driving time regulation, which we would like to enforce, will harmonise practices between member states and will fulfil this task to ensure and improve road safety. road safety has improved very largely due to the use of the driving time directive. we do not have the information that they are more sick, and why do we sometimes think that small and medium-sized enterprises are behaving irresponsibly, that they do not care about road safety or about their own health, so they must be more regulated than others? the commission cannot support legislation which limits the freedom of entrepreneurs in the road transport sector to organise their working time, while in other sectors entrepreneurs are not subject to similar working time restrictions. however, if the vote in this house confirms the rejection of the commission proposal, then the commission will review all possible options, including withdrawal of the proposal, and if parliament decides to include self-employed drivers in this directive, we will enforce your will. we will immediately inquire with member states how they apply the working time rules on self-employed drivers and how they monitor compliance with these rules.
in writing. i agree with the position of the committee on employment and social affairs on the rejection of the commission proposal and agree that it is necessary for the commission and parliament to work together to present a new, improved commission proposal in the future. i would point out that the commission's proposal to exclude self-employed drivers from the scope of the directive would be a major step backwards in eu social policy and transport policy. i would like to point out that 'fictitious' self-employed workers weaken the common labour market, and the main problem is that it is difficult to prove a 'fictitious' situation in practice. without concrete action and legal measures, the problem of self-employed drivers will become one of the biggest problems in the labour market and unfair competition will not be avoided. in order to improve the working conditions of all drivers and to ensure their social rights and guarantees, to improve road safety and to avoid unfair competition on the european road transport market, we cannot accept the commission's proposal as it stands today.
in writing. − on 2 september 2009, spain submitted an application to the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for the redundancies of workers in 181 enterprises producing other non-metallic mineral products in a region of the comunidad valenciana. i consider that the application meets the requirements for establishing the financial contribution as laid down in article 10 of regulation (ec) no 1927/2006. i therefore supported this report and the commission's proposal to mobilise eur 6 598 735, as the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) only funds measures that help unemployed people to integrate into the labour market, and the egf funds also enable displaced workers to take up permanent or fixed-term employment, to participate in vocational training programmes and to acquire the necessary skills to meet the needs of the labour market, to obtain business certificates or to start self-employment. lithuania has also already benefited from the fund.
in writing. − ireland submitted an application to the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) on 7 august 2009 for the redundancies in the company 'waterford crystal' and three of its supply or downstream businesses. i consider that the application meets the requirements for establishing the financial contribution as laid down in article 10 of regulation (ec) no 1927/2006. i voted in favour of this report and the commission's proposal to authorise the mobilisation of eur 2 570 853, as the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) only funds measures that help unemployed people to integrate into the labour market, and the egf funds also enable displaced workers to take up permanent or fixed-term employment, to participate in vocational training programmes and to acquire the necessary skills to meet the needs of the labour market, to obtain business certificates or to start independent activities. lithuania has also already benefited from the fund.
in writing. − i welcome the decisions to mobilize financial assistance to redundant workers who lost their jobs due to the global financial and economic crisis in three particular instances – in the comunidad valenciana and castilla-la mancha regions in spain, as well as in the crystal production company waterford crystal in ireland. the total amount of support in these cases amounts to eur 11 million and will target 3 663 redundant workers. although the assistance from the european globalization adjustment fund will not solve all the problems caused by the financial and economic crisis, i urge eu institutions to perform effective and timely evaluation of applications, as well as the member states to participate actively in this fund. in my home country, lithuania, financial assistance has recently been allocated for workers who lost their jobs in construction, furniture, clothing manufacturing industries, as well as for workers at snaige factory in alytus city. this support has been highly appreciated among those most severely hit by the global financial and economic crisis.
in writing. − an application was submitted to the commission on 9 october 2009 for the mobilisation of a total amount of eur 1 950 000 from the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for spain. this concerns the redundancies of 585 workers in the nine-month reference period from 1 november 2008 to 31 july 2009 in 36 enterprises operating in the sectors classified as 'production of wood and wood and cork products, except furniture; production of straw and plaiting materials' in the region of castilla-la mancha. i supported the commission's assessment that the application met the eligibility criteria set out in the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) regulation and the commission's recommendation that the application be approved by the budgetary authority, as the egf only funds measures that help unemployed people to integrate into the labour market and enables redundant workers to take up permanent or fixed-term employment, to participate in vocational training programmes and to acquire the necessary knowledge to meet labour market needs, to acquire business certificates or to start a self-employed activity. lithuania has also already benefited from the fund.
in writing. − i agree with this report, as more than half of the technical assistance allocated to the commission will be used to finance and implement the studies and evaluations of the existing european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) cases, drawing on success stories and learning for the future, and part of the technical allocations will be used to develop a database on long-term reintegration into the labour market. i would also call for at least part of this appropriation to be used for technical measures which can help to shorten the application procedure, which is too long in many cases for aid applications. i would encourage member states to become familiar with and take advantage of the opportunities offered by the egf for workers made redundant on a large scale and to use these funds to help and support the reintegration of workers made redundant. i would also encourage member states to exchange examples of good practice and in particular to learn from those member states that have already set up national egf information networks involving social partners and stakeholders at local level, in order to have an appropriate support system in place in the event of mass job losses.
in writing. − i have endorsed this report and the commission's proposal to use the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) to finance technical assistance to the commission in the amount of eur 1 110 000. in accordance with article 8 (1) of the legal basis, each year 0,35% of the annual ceiling of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) may be allocated to technical assistance at the initiative of the commission. a maximum of eur 1,75 million may be mobilised each year to finance the implementation needs of the fund. i supported the commission's proposal that this amount should also be used to finance the following activities, namely activities related to the mid-term evaluation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) monitoring and implementation studies, the creation of a knowledge base; the exchange of information and experience between experts and auditors from the member states and the commission, the development of networks, the preparation of meetings of the egf expert group; the organisation of workshops on the implementation of the egf, as well as information and publicity activities and the further development of the egf website and publications in all eu languages. whereas the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) plays an important role and the purpose of the use of the funds under this fund is to provide one-off assistance to workers who have lost their jobs as a result of globalisation or the global financial or economic crisis, with a view to facilitating their reintegration into the labour market;
in writing. − the financial earthquake of recent years has left many people unemployed or underemployed. it is good that the eu is based on the principle of solidarity. since the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) was set up in 2006, almost 30 applications have been received, totalling €154 million, and 33 000 workers across europe have been helped. this aid covered a wide range of sectors, including the computer industry, mobile telephony and the automotive industry. the baltic states have been hit hard by the financial crisis. between october 2008 and july 2009, more than 1 600 workers employed in lithuanian construction companies lost their jobs. half of them have not found another job or have retired. they must receive assistance. the egf covers most of the costs and the lithuanian employment fund covers the remaining costs. i would like to thank the egf for providing a very carefully selected and socially responsible support. one specific example: up to may 2009, 651 people working for lithuanian ab snaigė (a refrigeration equipment manufacturer) and two of its suppliers lost their jobs in five months. this had a direct impact on the employees and their families, as well as on their city of alytus, where snaiga is headquartered. in view of the situation, the egf allocated a significant amount of money to workers. the provision of money will enable employees to find employment, to undergo training and to retrain.
in writing. − i have voted for this report. effective use of the structural and cohesion funds is a pre-condition for the growth of our economies and creation of jobs. in order to ensure transparency of the use of funds the european transparency initiative should be implemented in its fullest. currently there is insufficient information about commission decisions on the funding of major. member states as well use different levels to inform the public on the beneficiaries of the eu aid . i believe that transparency should be ensured at all levels as it goes hand in hand with the process of simplifying the procedures for obtaining structural funds and enables public participation in a debate how public money is spent which is essential for the efficient use of the eu funds.
in writing. − the publication of information on the recipients of eu funds is the cornerstone of the european transparency initiative. i believe that the requirement in the financial regulation for member states to provide information on how eu funds are managed in a shared way, in particular through ex post publication of information on the recipients of funds, is insufficient. the commission shall confine itself to proposing a common guideline standard for the publication of data to the public and to making eu information publicly available on the website of the directorate-general for regional policy by providing links to the electronic addresses of the member states where the required data on the beneficiaries of the european regional development fund and the cohesion fund are published. where those funds are used under shared management, such references and their content shall be the sole responsibility of the member states and shall contain information transmitted by the managing authorities. due to the diversity of conditions for the provision and use of data, it is not possible to carry out comparisons at eu level. i therefore supported the ep's proposals to ensure that member states' databases can be used for comprehensive searches and that databases are harmonised to facilitate the browsing of eu-wide data, and that the data collected are presented and processed in a structured and comparable way to ensure that they are very user-friendly. the implementation of these proposals would contribute to the european transparency initiative.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, i would like to emphasise that transparency is very important for a country like mine, lithuania. eu funds such as the structural fund and the cohesion fund have helped shape lithuania's economic, social and environmental development and will continue to do so. it is therefore important that citizens are given the opportunity to become witnesses and to influence the allocation of these resources. for this to happen, public participation is essential. public participation could take various forms in the decision-making process in the allocation and implementation of eu funds. increased public participation would reduce corruption and increase the efficiency of the use of funds, which is crucial for a country like lithuania. it is also important to disclose the recipients of eu funds. this would stimulate debate on the use of public money in the state, which is again one of the essential principles of a functioning democratic state. i would also like to stress the need for the involvement of regional and local authorities and, above all, of ordinary citizens. the report makes some remarks on the need for a comprehensive online platform to provide a better understanding of, and overview of, existing funds. this is a good start, but more should be done to include people from different backgrounds - from the richest to the poorest, from big cities to small villages. civil society and non-governmental organisations could also help by promoting the effectiveness of programmes and improving their accountability.
in writing. − i support this regulation on the creation of a competitive european rail freight network, the implementation of which is expected to bring added value to the whole community as europe will be connected by a common rail transport network. this will ensure the efficient movement of people and goods between european regions. the regulation also sets deadlines for the implementation of railway corridors. an extension of the five-year period in favour of lithuania, during which the european crane will have to be installed in lithuania, has been adopted. with lithuania currently experiencing the painful effects of the economic crisis, this decision is particularly welcome as lithuania, like other countries hard hit by the crisis, has limited financial resources to invest in rail infrastructure development projects in the near future.
in writing. − i decided to vote in favour of the recommendation for second reading, insomuch as recent series of informal trialogue meetings with the spanish presidency have produced an agreement. i consider it is important not to lose the momentum. in its first reading of april 2009, my group supported the objective of a competitive rail freight network through the creation of corridors across the european union, as set out in the commission proposal of december 2008. my support for this agreement is also strengthened by the importance of the rail network in lithuania and the economic perspective it offers my country. it is of paramount importance – not only to lithuania but also to the european union as a whole – to stop the decline of rail market share in freight transport. i am convinced the rail freight market will benefit from this agreement. it will contribute to improving its quality of service and create synergies between national rail systems.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because i believe that the derivatives market (futures trading) needs to be made more transparent and more regulated. derivatives play a positive role in redistributing financial risk in the economy, but due to a lack of transparency and proper regulation, they have exacerbated the financial crisis. i welcome the commission's initiative to better regulate derivatives, in particular otc derivatives, in order to reduce the impact of risks on the stability of all financial markets, to standardise derivative contracts, to use a centralised data repository and to organise trading venues.
in writing. − the internet, which only became widely available two decades ago, has become an indispensable satellite of civilization, like the telephone or the radio. with 1.5 billion people online today, that number will double in a few years. high technology will soon allow not only computers to be connected to the internet but also cars, even books, food, and other things. connecting cars to the network would allow information about tyre pressure to be transmitted to the driver. programmed refrigerators will be able to recognize expired products. i voted in favour of this report because the internet of things will boost the economy in crisis and help create new jobs and new services for a growing number of eu citizens and businesses. this will also allow optimisation of production processes and energy savings, which are crucial in the fight against climate change.
in writing. - i supported this report. research and innovation are the key ingredients for successfully addressing the biggest and most pressing societal and environmental challenges and delivering on the eu's strategic policy objectives, such as competitiveness, climate change, employment, demographic change and many others. to remain competitive, europe must invest in long-term technologies and secure the necessary funding. europe is still lagging behind in research and innovation because of the fragmentation of the sector and the gap between research and innovation and its application in the market. i believe that the european commission needs to address the issue of private funding for research and innovation in the future innovation plan, which would allow companies to develop innovative products and services and bring them to market.
in writing. − global competition for location has led to the relocation of not only production but also related r & d capacities to third countries. such developments pose a serious threat to europe's position as an attractive industrial region. this process must be stopped before it becomes irreversible by consistently promoting innovation opportunities. according to the eu commission, less than 1% of the eu budget is currently spent directly on innovation measures. these funds are not sufficient to meet the challenges facing society in the future. i therefore support increasing the share of the eu budget allocated to innovation. this should be taken into account in the preparation of the financial perspective for the period 2014 to 2020. it is also important, in the light of the financial and credit crisis, to allocate more resources at both community and national levels and to develop and use better financing instruments to increase the capacity of enterprises to innovate. to increase the effectiveness of innovation policy, there is a need for better coordination, more effective linkages and a stricter management of the different support instruments, i.e. more targeted support.
in writing. - i support this report. poverty reduction is one of the key objectives of eu development policy. however, the negative effects of the economic and financial recession have further slowed down the progress of developing and least developed countries. eu member states should therefore make every effort to establish specific development assistance measures in the near future, covering the areas of trade, development cooperation, the common agricultural policy. it is also necessary to facilitate the integration of developing countries into the world economy and thus to promote the development of trade between them. the commission should ensure the effective administration of aid measures for developing and least developed countries and the transparency and effectiveness of the allocation of financial assistance.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the achievement of the millennium development goals must remain a key objective of the european union. this is a very important task both at eu and international level, and despite all the difficulties, we cannot put off its implementation. the eu and the international community need to focus their efforts and take concrete action to increase the chances of achieving the mdgs. i would like to stress that this is not the time to save at the expense of the sick or the hungry, and that we need to focus on areas such as health, women and children, the fight against poverty and greater attention to employment and decent work. i would like to stress that poverty reduction in the context of the millennium development goals must be recognised as a common eu policy objective and that europe must lead the global community in taking concerted action to deliver on the eu's promises to the poorest of the planet. i support the european parliament's call for the majority of eu financial support to go to the most vulnerable, with a focus on women, children, people with disabilities and all those who need it most. particular attention should also be paid to gender equality, minority rights and the fight against discrimination.
in writing. − as the shadow rapporteur for this report, i voted strongly in favour of this report, which aims to ensure the successful implementation of the millennium development goals. i fully support my colleagues' efforts to ensure that the european union has a unified and progressive voice on this issue. however, i could not accept the two amendments which were tabled, the provisions of which, as a liberal, are unacceptable to me. i therefore voted against calls for the eu to unilaterally impose a currency and derivatives transaction tax to fund global public goods, including ict. the european union should not impose additional tax burdens on its citizens, especially those whose mechanisms and effects have not yet been fully explored. i also disagree with the legally binding nature of development aid measures. it is important that member states fulfil their commitments to increase the amount of official development assistance (oda), but the european union should not legally penalize its members for partial non-compliance in a changed context, affected by the financial crisis. not all member states have been equally affected by the crisis and not all will be able to reach the 0.7% target. the european parliament should encourage them in a more lenient and acceptable way than by means of strict legal measures.
written. - the objective set in the mdgs is indeed a noble one and i believe we must make every effort to achieve it. lithuania is a small country with a limited bilateral development budget. our worldly goals are difficult and sometimes unattainable. the majority of our bilateral aid (around 50%) is directed to countries that are part of the european neighbourhood policy. it's not because we care less about people in africa or the caribbean than we do about people closer to home. for lithuania and other countries in a similar situation, the key is not the fine-tuned allocation of funds, but its effectiveness. we need to achieve the best possible results with relatively few resources, which is not always possible in remote countries. it's important that we help where we can. if not through funding, then by sharing our integration experience and other research that could be useful. reducing poverty, whether in lithuania or in the farthest corner of africa, should be one of our top priorities. fighting corruption and helping the poor is our moral duty and the eu's long-term interest. with the number of people in low-paid and insecure jobs on the rise, it is important that we focus all our efforts on protecting the interests of the most vulnerable groups in particular.

member of the commission. − in response to the important and topical question from ms andrikienė, let me say that the commission is fully aware of all the violations of the democratic principles and procedures which have been reported by a number of ngos in the context of the local elections in belarus. the european union’s heads of mission in minsk have also pointed to inconsistencies and indeed discrepancies, in particular in the ballot counting. some amendments were made, ahead of the elections, to the belarus electoral code. according to the opinion of the osce office for democratic institutions and human rights, which was issued on 3 june 2010, this represented, and i quote, ‘a step towards removing some flaws’ in the electoral code. however, the report states that these amendments are unlikely to resolve the concerns that the electoral code continues to fall short of genuinely democratic elections. in the light of this, during the visit i intend to pay to minsk in july, i will convey to the authorities the clear message that progress needs to be made in a number of areas which are key to democratisation if belarus is serious about re-engaging with the european union. one of these areas is electoral legislation, which is one of the most evident tests of democratic standards. in this respect i will urge minsk to continue to cooperate with the osce order on the reform of the electoral code ahead of the next presidential elections. in the run up to these elections, the commission will also pay particular attention to other key aspects of democratisation, notably freedom of assembly, of the media and of ngos and civil society, more generally, where no progress has been reported in recent times.

member of the commission. − the reason why estonia has had to enforce budgetary discipline is not primarily the euro, but the fact that estonia’s economy faced a very serious financial crisis, like all of us, and economic growth stalled. there was negative economic growth, which meant that estonia’s public finances were simply no longer on a sustainable basis. estonia’s debt level is very low: 7.2% for the moment, while the european average is 75%, but the gradient was very worrying and it was only common sense in economic terms to ensure that estonia will be able to continue its stability-oriented policies and ensure the sustainability of its public finances. this was the democratic decision of the estonian parliament recently and this has been the policy followed in estonia with quite some success.

member of the commission. − firstly, many of you have mentioned funding. there are two possibilities: one is prevention, when we should look to the cohesion fund; the second relates to the funds at european level – interreg and all the activities connected with that. when disaster occurs, there is certainly another possibility – mentioned by an honourable member – namely the european solidarity fund. the european union solidarity fund has very clear guidelines concerning implementation and the nature of the damage. in the case of the irish application for the solidarity fund, the damage caused was below the threshold but the commission is in the process of assessing that application. when the amount of the damage is below the normal threshold for mobilising the solidarity fund of 0.6%, the specific criteria for exceptional mobilisation must be met. if this is assessed to be the case, then the aid can be guaranteed. however, we should not simply mix two the things – the purpose of one and the purpose of the other. i also agree about the sharing of best practice, which was mentioned – i think it is important that management plans are drawn up in a proper and correct way. regarding the cross-border effect, we are very much in support of this in the water framework directive. the river basin approach is one which looks across borders and which is establishing a kind of connection where the member states should cooperate. they are asked to cooperate and in fact are bound to do so in preparing their cross-border solutions. finally, i would mention that in 2012 we want to produce a blueprint on waters and their management. within that blueprint there will be three topics which we want to address. one of the topics to which we will give our attention will be droughts.
member of the commission. − it is true that under the environmental impact assessment (eia) directive, the responsibility for gathering the necessary environmental information and submitting it to the public authorities is given to the developer. however, this does not result in an opaque and partial eia process as the directive contains several safeguards which ensure a transparent and objective environmental assessment of projects. these safeguards are to be found, firstly, in the description of the minimum information to be included in the environmental impact assessment report which is required by the eia directive and, secondly, in the consultations with the competent environmental authorities and the public. indeed, all the information provided by the developer has to be made public to the environmental authorities and the public, which have to be consulted. the accuracy of this information can be challenged at any point of the consultations, before the final decision is taken. this means that the competent environmental authorities can use their own capacity to proceed with a proper assessment of the information provided, while ngos or interested parties can – and very often do – challenge the whole assessment on the basis of the data provided, in case they do not reflect the real situation or omit clearly to conform to the requirements of the eia provisions. the final decision to grant or refuse development consent must take into consideration the results of consultations and the information gathered and must contain the main reasons on which it is based. all this is also made available to the public. but, to be honest, your question is very relevant. after more than 20 years of implementation, we have identified the areas where improvements are needed and reached the conclusion that the eia directive should be reviewed. my objective is to present a new text which will further improve environmental protection, take on board the challenges in the areas of climate change, energy and biodiversity, integrate the ecj case-law and harmonise and simplify, to the extent possible, existing procedures. in this context, the overall quality of the environmental assessments will be a central issue, and there is certainly room for improvement. on the one hand, the quality control of the eia documentation submitted by the developer could be improved. on the other hand, the quality of the eia process itself could be reinforced. my services have already started working on the review of the eia directive. by the end of june, a broad consultation of the public and of the stakeholders will be launched. you are of course invited to participate and also express your views. once all the relevant data and information are on the table, the commission will identify the appropriate policy options for amending the eia directive, including ways of ensuring the independence of the environmental assessments. any commission proposal will have to be subject to a legislative impact assessment. i am not yet in a position to give you a date for the commission’s proposal, but my intention is clearly to present this proposal during my mandate, better sooner than later, though of course there is still some quite serious work to be done before then.

i welcome the european commission's efforts to establish a european financial stability facility to help euro area and non-euro area countries overcome their financial difficulties, and also to reform the economic governance system. experience has shown that monetary union alone does not ensure the economic stability of the community as a whole. to achieve this, we must urgently bring together the economic potential of all 27 european countries and better coordinate their economic policies. we will also create a more effective oversight mechanism through coordination. we as a union are losing and will probably lose the competitive battle through the various foreign rating agencies and international funds to allow us to assess ourselves and make decisions about the financial condition of our european countries. the oft-repeated idea that europe needs its own credit rating agency seems to be beginning to become a reality, and it is gratifying that the head of the euro area, j.c. juncker, has already spoken of the need for such an agency. the european union is also capable of having its own fund, through which we could create a european market surveillance system.

in writing. − i voted for this document, which will give more opportunities to exploit the scientific potential of the baltic sea countries. the baltic sea ecosystems are facing a major environmental threat, due to natural and human impacts, which must be addressed through science. the baltic sea states have so far implemented research and development programmes at national level, but they are not sufficiently coordinated at european level. i believe that the bonus programme will be a good example and will improve the research capacity in the baltic sea region and bring researchers from these countries into joint activities and contribute to the creation and implementation of the european research area in the baltic sea region.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the baltic sea and its coasts are increasingly being adversely affected by a variety of factors: pollution, climate change, acidification, depletion of resources and loss of biodiversity. in order to reduce pollution in the baltic sea, eight eu member states, including lithuania, will therefore seek to implement the joint baltic sea research and development programme 'bonus-169'. research and programmes are currently being carried out at national level in some baltic sea countries, but there is insufficient coordination at eu level in this area. in view of the gravity of the current situation, research programmes in the baltic sea region could therefore be more focused and systematic in order to address the complex and urgent problems of marine pollution in a coordinated and effective manner. i would point out that the commission's financial support for the joint development programme will help to make the most of the research potential. moreover, this joint programme is fully in line with the objectives of the european marine and maritime research strategy and the eu strategy for the baltic sea region. it is also in line with the new europe 2020 strategy, as investments in science and knowledge, which promote growth and job creation, are one of the key objectives of the future strategy.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, the baltic sea plays a very important role in lithuanian society. the vast majority of our resources lie on the baltic coast. for us lithuanians, the baltic sea is a real asset in terms of logistics - especially for the klaipėda seaport - but also in terms of nature and culture. unfortunately, as this report states, the baltic sea is suffering greatly from man-made hazards such as air pollution, climate change, depletion of resources and acidity. member states acting separately cannot be left to deal with the environmental problems in the baltic sea region alone. a joint action programme such as bonus is particularly needed at this time. research responds to the environmental and social and economic challenges we face. the bonus programme will create a positive common agenda for lithuania and our baltic neighbours this close cooperation could also cover political and economic issues. while the bonus programme focuses mainly on environmental research, we should not forget the other benefits that this programme can bring. the bonus programme has the potential to deliver significant social and economic benefits and to contribute to the objectives of the lisbon treaty by investing in knowledge linked to growth and jobs. this would improve the situation of various sectors - fisheries, tourism, maritime transport and aquaculture.
