in writing. − i voted in favour of this european parliament recommendation because common rules on divorce between nationals of different countries would better ensure the rights of spouses of different nationalities or living abroad in the event of divorce throughout the european union. the initiative to harmonise the rules on crossborder divorce is a historic event, as it proposes to allow some eu countries to open a procedure for closer cooperation in this area. currently, the law applicable to divorce varies from one member state to another. it is therefore not always clear in which country a couple of different nationalities, as well as a couple of other eu nationals living abroad, can terminate their marriage. in view of the legal problems faced by european citizens in the event of divorce, the new draft law provides for the possibility for both spouses to choose, by mutual written agreement, the competent country for the divorce proceedings and the country whose law will apply to the divorce. so, first of all, with regard to the rights of our citizens, we must ensure that painful stages of life such as divorce are not made more complex and painful by legal difficulties or the complexity of judicial procedures.
in writing. − i decided to vote in favour of this recommendation and fully support this historic occasion to activate – for the first time – the enhanced cooperation procedure. whereas 140 000 divorces with an international element were pronounced in the union in 2007, european citizens are still confronted with legal problems when engaging in cross-border separation procedures. it is our task to remove these inextricable judicial impediments and simplify as much as possible these intrinsically painful episodes in people's lives. although my country, lithuania, does not participate in this enhanced cooperation now, i fully support this initiative since it represents a good example of how many eu citizens can profit in practical terms from being union citizens. the european union, its citizens and the internal market will directly benefit from this historic enhanced cooperation insomuch as it contributes to eliminating further discriminations and possible obstacles to the free movement of persons. it will increase the eu's visibility, and its legitimacy.
in writing. − i am pleased that one of the baltic countries, estonia, has managed to join the euro area in the face of a difficult economic situation. the example of estonia shows that a balanced and targeted policy can achieve full convergence. and the fact that countries are still keen to join the euro area in the midst of the crisis shows that there is a high level of confidence in the currency. i am confident that the political will and efforts to introduce the euro will not be weakened in lithuania either.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because i think estonia is well placed to join the euro area. this small nordic country has demonstrated that, even in the midst of a financial crisis, monetary union is possible if pursued consistently and consistently. i think that the introduction of the euro will be beneficial for estonia - it will increase confidence in the country's economy, attract more investment, lower borrowing rates. this will also give a boost to the economies of lithuania and latvia. the important and symbolic significance of this enlargement is that estonia is the first of the former soviet union countries to join the monetary union, thus passing through the most difficult stage of eu integration. however, this development is equally important for the euro area as a whole. despite the problems facing the euro area, the single currency remains attractive, with the potential to add value to its member countries. i am pleased that the commission and the council have taken a positive view of estonia's progress and i hope that the council will also take a decision accordingly.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the surján report. the implementation of the 2009 eu budget shows a surplus of eur 2.25 billion, which mainly results from under-implementation of payments as well as from higher than expected revenues. in addition to distorting the result of the eu budget implementation, its direct effect is to lower the contributions from the member states to the eu budget already in the course of the budget year. without such an amending budget, the 2009 surplus would have been above eur 5 billion, mainly due to under-spending. annual budget surpluses have been increasing since 2007, despite those amending budgets presented in the course of the year in order to reduce surpluses. so the amounts at stake are very significant. the existing disconnection between the resources of the eu budget and emerging needs and commitments creates budgetary tensions and gaps that jeopardise some other eu priorities, such as the globalisation fund and many others. all this means that either the eu budget was not realistic, or we do not have effective tools to implement and supervise budget implementation. the main conclusion is therefore that we should significantly improve our budgetary planning and other related procedures.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the new directive of the european parliament and of the council on the equal right to interpretation in courts throughout the european union will increase the impartiality and efficiency of the courts. given that all eu member states are parties to the european convention on human rights and freedoms, they should ensure fair trial and due process standards. unfortunately, in practice, these principles of justice and the 'quality' of the judicial process are not sufficiently guaranteed. i would like to point out that the right to interpretation in criminal proceedings will strengthen the right of a person to a fair trial and to be heard by the court. human rights are often violated at the moment, as a person being questioned or tried who does not understand the language used in a particular eu country does not always have the right to interpretation or translation in court, simply because such a right to interpretation in criminal proceedings has not been uniformly regulated throughout the eu until now. in order to ensure a fair trial, we must therefore avoid problems of misunderstanding of language and we must ensure that the defendant understands what is being said during his trial.
in writing. − i voted against the commission's proposal. the commission proposed to exclude self-employed drivers from the scope of the directive. i believe that the exclusion of self-employed persons would create the conditions for fictitious work and would allow companies to exploit drivers by not concluding contracts of employment but by hiring them as self-employed persons who would not be subject to the strict working hours and rest periods provided for in the directive. in order to ensure a competitive carrier market, the commission needs to find a solution that ensures a level playing field for all drivers. parliament has repeatedly expressed concern about the current double standards applied to employment contracts and self-employed persons. this situation distorts the principles of the functioning of the internal market and poses a threat to road safety.
in writing. − i support the european parliament's position to reject the european commission's proposal, as the rules governing the internal market must apply to everyone without exception. the current commission proposal to exempt self-employed drivers from the driver working time directive could encourage the registration of dummy self-employed drivers in larger companies, which would further encourage unfair competition in the market and weaken the common labour market. self-employed bus and truck drivers are subject to the same working and rest time requirements as drivers from larger companies. i would point out that in order to improve the working conditions of all drivers and to guarantee their social rights and guarantees, to improve road safety and to avoid unfair competition in the transport market, we must take the appropriate measures together with the commission and present a new improved proposal on the organisation of drivers' working time. the legal measures should therefore be taken to combat the fake work of self-employed drivers, as the fictitious situation is a common labour market problem and should be addressed accordingly.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, if i may, i would like to give lithuania's opinion on this matter. lithuanian truck drivers have earned a good reputation throughout europe. they are the entrepreneurs and heroes of our independent young lithuanian state often separated from their families for long periods of time. the endless highways of europe became their second home. yes, there have been reports of truck drivers breaching eu safety and labour regulations. but truck drivers are not always to blame. sometimes their employers leave them no choice but to break the rules. i have received letters from lithuanian truck drivers who have come to me and to the ep to make their voices heard. the letters say that truck drivers risk losing their wages if they do not manipulate the distance traveled by their truck's tachograph. the number of days of rest legally entitled is often not granted or postponed. drivers who complain are later discriminated against. these are clear violations of the rights of drivers and of the ep regulations, as well as of the provisions of the aetr and the cmr convention. some of them appealed to lithuanian authorities, but their complaints were ignored. more needs to be done to ensure the health and safety of drivers and other mobile workers in the road transport sector. this is not just a lithuanian problem, it is a european problem. ignoring this problem can have devastating consequences.
in writing. − i voted for this resolution though i have doubts about the feasibility of proposed targets. the 2020 strategy sets out very ambitious goals to be achieved during the coming decade, such as a high quality employment and more green jobs, climate and energy targets and many others. however, i believe that the strategy is missing crucial elements such as setting out concrete measures and actions which must be taken in order to respond to the challenges. the strategy proposed by the commission is of a rather general nature and the commission should present without delay more detailed plans to clarify how the proposed initiatives will be implemented. otherwise, the strategy risks to be merely a collection of slogans without a concrete backup and repeat the failure of the lisbon strategy.
in writing. − i voted for the resolution and i fully endorse the line it takes. the european union needs to reform its system of economic governance and to ensure the long-term sustainability of public finances which is essential for stability and growth. the proposed european financial stabilisation mechanism will only be effective if proper structural reforms are implemented. i believe that europe should establish its own rating agency and the lending fund which could be used to provide financial aid for all members states in need, and not to euro zone countries only. moreover, effective economic governance calls for a stronger commission which, under the provisions of the lisbon treat , has been given the task of coordinating reform plans and measures and establishing a common strategy.
mr president, just a few remarks. firstly, violence, especially if it ends with loss of life, should always be condemned and fought against. however, it has yet to be determined who was actually the first to use violence in the case of israel’s interception of the gaza flotilla. secondly, i congratulate israel on its decision to include in the investigation process two high-ranking international observers, who will ensure that the process is carried out transparently and impartially. i think this is a wise decision. we should join the calls by various international actors to israel to reconsider the gaza blockade, the lifting of the gaza blockade, but what will the international community say when rockets start landing again on israeli citizens and the hamas militia arms itself to the teeth? the balanced approach has been mentioned, so i would like to support this and say ‘yes’.
mr president, let me first emphasise that the report on human rights and democracy in the world is very important and long awaited. it covers the most important human rights issues. however, the major problem remains that the report merely describes the actions of the eu. the european parliament has more than once expressed a recommendation that the council should develop indicators and clear benchmarks, which has been mentioned by the vice-president of the commission in order to measure the effectiveness of the european union’s human rights policies. another issue i would like to mention today is the european parliament’s access to council and commission information and relevant documents, which has been limited so far, and, despite several parliaments’ recommendations on this issue, the situation has not improved. let me take as an example human rights dialogues which are carried with the authorities of the relevant countries. recently the council held a round of human rights talks with russia. we are well aware of the situation of human rights in russia: the continued killing of journalists, the climate of lawlessness, governmental toughness in dealing with the so-called terrorists in north caucasus and so on, but what we would like to know is: what was the reaction of the russians who were present at the human rights dialogue, and are they going to take any action on this? i know my time has finished, but just let me say that i fully understand that human rights fall under the common foreign and security policy but, nevertheless, human rights issues cannot be top secret... (the president cut off the speaker.)
in writing. − i support the objectives of the lisbon treaty, which is that each member state of the european union should appoint a highly qualified eu policy officer responsible for human rights and democracy. i support the initiative that it is important to establish a focal point responsible for issues relating to human rights defenders and their activities. in addition, eu countries need to adopt a comprehensive approach to human rights defenders to make eu policy more effective and more credible to the member states. support measures and, taking into account their short-term and long-term needs, preventive and protective measures should be in place to protect the activities of human rights defenders. i would like to emphasise that in order to better protect human rights defenders in third countries, a more effective eu dialogue with third countries on political and human rights issues is needed. it is also essential that the role of the lawyer is known and supported by the public, as recognition and visibility of his or her work can also help to improve security in difficult circumstances.
in writing. − i support this report because equality between women and men is one of the european union's key objectives and therefore concrete action at eu level is needed to ensure that the gains made so far are not lost. i would like to point out that the current economic crisis may put at risk the gains made in the area of gender equality, and the consequences of this decline may particularly affect women. i regret that many women, especially those in the retail, service and tourism sectors, and those in part-time or unsecured jobs, have already lost or are likely to lose their jobs. i would also like to stress that the unemployment of women could increase significantly as a result of the cuts announced in public budgets, as the sectors of education, health and social services are dominated by women. in addition, given that many women are setting up their own businesses, we must aim to reduce the administrative burden and create new jobs in the smes sector. the integration of women into the labour market in recent decades has meant a greater direct impact of the crisis not only on women themselves but also on households, as women's income is significantly reduced when they lose their jobs.
in writing. − i voted for this report because we need to continue to improve the position of women in the labour market and in society. women often face multiple forms of discrimination on the grounds of gender and age (especially older women), disability, ethnic or racial origin, religion, nationality, migration status, socioeconomic status, including single women, and the various forms of discrimination create numerous obstacles to the empowerment of women and to the improvement of their social status. the problem of reconciling work, family and private life between women and men also remains unresolved. i would like to point out that today, the majority of the almost 85 million people living in poverty are women, who are in this situation because of unemployment, temporary and precarious work and low wages, insufficient even for the minimum subsistence level. moreover, the number of women in poverty has risen disproportionately in recent years in europe compared to the number of men. unfortunately, women are at a much higher risk of poverty, so we must increase the opportunities for women to work in the single labour market. in order to improve the future position of women and to ensure gender balance in modern society, gender equality should therefore be mainstreamed in various fields of activity, such as economics, finance, business and social affairs.
in writing. i agree that the new european union strategy for jobs and growth adopted by the european council last week will help europe to recover from the crisis and to strengthen itself both at eu and international level by increasing competitiveness, productivity and social cohesion. however, it is regrettable that this new strategy has forgotten people with disabilities, who currently represent over 12% of the eu population. while the council has consistently called for greater attention to be paid to the inclusion of persons with disabilities in the management of disability-related issues, the europe 2020 strategy does not set out specific tasks, objectives and commitments that could realistically improve the lives of more than 65 million europeans with disabilities. i would like to point out that in the new strategy the european council finally agreed on the eu's main objectives and on the quantitative expression of indicators on education, social inclusion and poverty. after a long debate and the adoption of the european strategy, the next decade will therefore see the transition to its implementation phase, which should be even more difficult than its adoption. now it is up to the member states to take action to implement the policy priorities set out in the strategy at national level, otherwise the new strategy will once again be a set of pretty slogans. i would like to stress that the commission and the council must maintain a dialogue with the member states to ensure that national decisions are in line with the eu's main objectives, as this is the only way for the strategy to deliver concrete results.
i voted for this document and i am very pleased that it has been adopted. the document refers to the baltic sea strategy and its main objective is to promote coordination of member states' actions in policies with territorial implications. the strategy is based on four main policy areas, one of which is the promotion of an environmentally sustainable environment. i am therefore very pleased that my amendments to the document also reflect the planning and implementation of energy infrastructure projects around the baltic sea, which could have some impact on the baltic sea ecosystem. we also need to ensure good navigation practices, reduce the impact of agriculture on the baltic sea, and i fully support the european parliament's view that cooperation with third countries should be strengthened as part of the strategy. especially when it comes to environmental issues. so i am very confident that by 2011, when the strategy is assessed, we will have made a big step forward and will be able to start with a new quality.
youth unemployment is a huge problem today. 20% of my peers are unemployed in the european union. and i voted for this european parliament position because i believe that we are sending another important political message to both the european commission and the member states on the situation of youth unemployment. i think i fully share the ideas expressed in the report, that programmes such as 'eurasmus' for young entrepreneurs need to be promoted and developed, that it is very important to emphasise the importance of non-formal education in the lives of young people and in their career development. the next item is the objectives of the eu 2020 strategy. however, it is expected that the economic recovery will come through innovation, green technologies and the inclusion of young people in the labour market, which is very important. and i just want to share the experience of lithuania, where there are tax incentives for employers who hire young people who are entering the labour market for the first time. i hope that this cooperation between member states will be close to achieve such results.
biological waste, like waste in general, is an important and integral concern of all citizens of the european union. the situation is quite different in the european union. in some countries, 10% of bio-waste is landfilled, in others, unfortunately, up to 90%. the tasks for all of us should therefore be: the first task waste prevention and reduction, the second task, specific to the present situation, to make the european union a society that recycles waste. as regards compost, i.e. biological waste, the possibility of using low-quality compost must be considered and evaluated in order to avoid harm to the environment and to humanity. and at european union level, a proper definition of the possibilities for using low-quality compost and a definition of when compost is considered a product and when it is considered waste would help member states to better orient themselves in their approach to compost use. appropriate legal regulation at european union level is therefore necessary.
in writing. - i strongly disagree with the remarks of mr tomasevski, mep, on the situation of the polish minority in lithuania, which i consider to be unobjective and far-fetched. but unfortunately, our seimas ethics committee's attempt to treat the mep as a lithuanian civil servant or civil servant was also quite unsuccessful and inadequate to reality. the use of moral levers, public opinion and ethical appeals in the media is one thing, but the attempt to influence the freedom of speech and thought of meps in political decisions is something else entirely. we were wrong and therefore there is no other way than to acknowledge with honour that mr tomaševskis is technically and procedurally correct in this conflict, just as the european parliament's assessment of the situation is correct. i therefore vote in favour of the ep's position. otherwise, it would be inconsistent not only with the ep but also with the eu member states vis-à-vis lithuania.
in writing. - i support this report because, under article 8 of the protocol on the privileges and immunities of the european union, mr tomaševskis cannot be considered to be criminally responsible and the facts described are not the subject of parliamentary immunity, since article 2 of the statute for members of the european parliament provides that 'members of parliament shall be free and independent'. article 3 also provides that members shall vote individually and in person. they shall not be bound by any instructions and shall not have a binding mandate. 2. agreements on how the mandate should be exercised are null and void. the decision of the supreme ethics commission therefore infringes the principles of the freedom and independence of a member of the european parliament enshrined in the statute for members of the european parliament, which is part of european union law, and the empowerment of the supreme ethics commission under the laws of the republic of lithuania to monitor the activities of members of the european parliament elected in lithuania may be in breach of provisions of community law.
in writing. - i supported this report. the baltic sea strategy is the first and so far only attempt to develop a comprehensive overall development strategy. it is already clear today that the actions undertaken by individual member states are not sufficiently effective and fragmented, and that it is necessary to promote cooperation at regional level. the current disparities in economic development and innovation in the baltic sea region require the development of the potential of all sectors, including transport, energy and tourism. the opportunity to take advantage of the additional benefits of the baltic sea strategy and other future macro-regional strategies to achieve a new level of synergy that could help to reduce existing disparities in order to create a highly competitive, stable, shared prosperity area is crucial in these conditions of an ageing society and the emergence of new challenges of globalisation.
in writing. − as a lithuanian and a liberal, i will always support a strategy that will help the baltic sea region become a best practice example of sustainable development and regional cooperation. the resolution on the baltic sea strategy, which the european parliament has today endorsed with the support of the liberals, focuses on lithuania's potential. the strategy aims to reduce the baltic region's dependence on russian energy, to link the electricity networks of the countries in the region and diversify energy resources, to ensure the extension of the railway line from berlin to the baltic sea, and to address the difficulties of crossing the border with russia. these are four very important steps for lithuania. the european parliament also adopted a resolution on the situation in the middle east, calling on the eu to take action to prevent the spread of the virus and to ensure that the situation in the region is not further worsened. all the baltic countries, including russia and belarus, have to comply with the highest standards of nuclear safety and environmental protection. i hope that my written statement on the construction of a nuclear power plant in belarus, which i have submitted to my colleagues in parliament for their signature, will be supported even more strongly by them after this vote.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report on the european union strategy for the baltic sea region and the role of microregions in the future cohesion policy, in particular because the strategy needs to be implemented more quickly, especially in the context of the energy policy, which is one of the priority areas of the strategy. it is an important part of the external relations of the eu and its member states. we must develop cooperation between the baltic states and the neighbouring countries, while reducing the region's energy dependence. it is good that this strategy has found a place for nuclear security in the region. i hope that the provision that eu countries must adhere to the highest safety and environmental standards in the case of planned nuclear energy development in the baltic sea region, and that the european commission must monitor and control whether neighbouring countries, especially those with plans to build nuclear power plants near the eu's external borders, follow the same approach and international conventions, will not remain on paper but will be implemented. we must ensure that not only the eu member states but also neighbouring countries, in particular russia and belarus, where nuclear power plants are planned, do not look the other way. the creation of the baltic nuclear triangle is not only a concern for lithuania but also for europe. the plans of russia and belarus to build new nuclear power plants on the eu's eastern borders pose a serious threat to the ecological security not only of scandinavia but also of western europe.
in writing. − it is important for the eu to have a macro-regional vision of the baltic sea region. cooperation with non-eu countries is also beneficial in the fields of environment, energy and others russia, belarus. i voted in favour of the report without hesitation because it rightly states that the eu countries should set an example in the field of the environment, nuclear safety and, in particular, the construction of new nuclear power stations. we should also demand the highest standards of goodwill and cooperation from our neighbours, especially those who plan to build their own nuclear power stations on the eu's borders. unfortunately, the decision by belarus to choose a site for the new nuclear power plant only 25 kilometres from the lithuanian border and 50 kilometres from the capital vilnius is not what i would call a friendly gesture. no other eu capital is so close to the union's external border and cannot be in such a situation. environmental ngos in belarus are sounding the alarm because they believe that the design and construction of the power plant does not comply with international safety and environmental standards. it is a matter of concern that the presentation of the project to the public in both belarus and lithuania was organised superficially, without answering the main questions. the eu cannot ban the construction of nuclear power plants right on the union's external border. but together, we can negotiate with the belarusian authorities for a site. the eu has also committed to the development of a comprehensive and effective civil aviation safety regime in the baltic sea region.
in writing. − the eu strategy holds enormous potential for us and our neighbours within several policy areas. successful implementation of the strategy could enhance the region’s prosperity and accessibility, and ensure security in the region and protect the environment. optimal results can only be achieved with the pooling of resources towards commonly defined goals. closer co-operation between baltic countries will reduce differences between our countries, help harmonise our markets and make them more accessible. its unique geographical position and interdependence puts the baltic states in a strong position to use the eu strategy to improve relations with our non-eu neighbours. possible areas for co-operation with russia include: transport connections, tourism, cross-border health threats, environmental protection and energy. we also need to address the lack of appropriate infrastructure and accessibility between various transport networks. the baltic sea is one of the most polluted sea areas in the world; only co-ordinated joint action can reverse this. the implementation of the strategy has been slow so far. we need to kick-start the process and step up our efforts. looking ahead, i believe that the strategy’s agenda will be progressed further when the eu presidency is held by baltic states – poland in 2011 and lithuania in 2013.
in writing. - i supported this report. a well-coordinated eu regional policy can boost the competitiveness and employment of eu regions. in the wake of the economic slowdown caused by the global economic and financial crisis, eu regional policy is an important part of the european economic recovery plan. i believe that regional policy in the future must be based on the priorities set out in the eu 2020 strategy, such as knowledge-based sustainable growth, achieved through the use of new sustainable economic growth pathways, better business conditions for all regions, fair competition, promoting job creation, entrepreneurship and innovation, developing smes and supporting their growth. in the context of regional policy, the use of the structural funds is of particular importance, and i believe that greater flexibility in the structural funds would allow the immediate launch of the programmes envisaged, the implementation of which would contribute to improving the economic and social situation in the individual regions.
in writing. - mr president, we are talking about the economic and financial crisis today, but can we learn from it? the consequences of the misregulation in the united states, where banks lent to customers who couldn't pay their bills, and inevitably led to the subprime crisis, will be felt for a long time to come around the world. the european union's regional policy is therefore becoming a crucial part of the european economic recovery plan and the largest source of community investment in the real economy, but there is a lack of effective mechanisms for the coordination of the european union's economy and for coordinated growth. cohesion policy, through the structural funds and other actions, is of paramount importance. regional policy is not only a means of counteracting the immediate negative effects of the economic and social crisis, but also a long-term policy instrument for overcoming the structural difficulties that have become particularly evident during the crisis, especially in terms of competitiveness and employment in the member states. it is also important for lithuania to recover from the crisis as quickly as possible and to focus on increasing competitiveness and employment.
in writing. − due to the lack of a single, effective mechanism for economic coordination and balanced growth within the european union, cohesion policy is playing a leading role in helping us get back on our feet. in particular objective 2 of the structural funds has been – and remains – one of the most important pillars of cohesion policy, especially in the context of exiting the economic crisis. for countries like lithuania, more flexible access to the eu regional funds would go a long way to help the recovery of areas worst hit by the crisis. this flexible funding could create new jobs and would have to include diverse areas: investment for infrastructure, support for small firms, professional training, and industrial and rural modernisation. it is important that these instruments are not solely used as fire-fighting interventions. our efforts must also be focused on long-term restructuring outcomes and the transition to a more sustainable economy – flexibility is also crucial for long-term policy. objective 2 and other cohesion policy branches can also help once again to narrow the gap between eu member states. for lithuania it is particularly important to catch up where it left off before the financial tsunami hit, and to regain its hard-earned status as a ‘baltic tiger’.
in writing. − i support this regulation on the rights of passengers on urban and long-distance buses. this regulation aims to establish the rights of bus passengers in order to make road passenger transport more attractive and to create a level playing field between carriers from different member states and between different modes of transport. it is also necessary to ensure that the rights of bus passengers are equivalent to those already established in the areas of air and rail transport. passengers must be entitled to compensation in the event of delay or cancellation of travel, loss of baggage, death or injury to passengers. there should also be a system for handling complaints and for compensation. it is necessary to ensure the right to mobility of disabled persons and persons with reduced mobility by providing free assistance where necessary.
in writing. − i support this proposal, as the proposal on the rights of passengers on maritime and inland waterways together with the proposal on the rights of passengers on urban and long-distance buses are part of the package proposed by the european commission in december 2008. building on the rights that air and rail passengers have acquired under current european union legislation (as an example of the very successful european union legislation that has created tangible rights for its citizens), these proposals seek to extend passenger rights to maritime and inland waterway transport, taking into account the specificities of these sectors. this would make this transport more attractive, more reliable and would create a level playing field for carriers from different member states. the adoption of a regulation laying down new rules will strengthen the protection of all passengers in general and of disabled persons and persons with reduced mobility in particular. however, an excessive extension of the scope of the regulation to small vessels would place an additional burden on operators of small vessels, which would in many cases be unlikely to meet the requirements of the regulation.
in writing. − i support this regulation on the establishment of advanced land transport systems and interfaces with other modes of transport. the use of advanced information and communication technologies is proposed to address the growing challenges of road infrastructure. however, specifications should be drawn up in particular for the use of these tools, such as information on travel by means of various modes of transport, provision of traffic services, information on safe parking places for lorries and commercial vehicles, necessary for the harmonised deployment of the emergency call throughout europe. however, the commission should first draw up an annual work programme to be implemented by the member states. member states should take the necessary measures to ensure the harmonious and efficient introduction and use of vehicles and services in the community: updating of road transport information, cooperation between traffic management centres.
in writing. − i voted for this document because it would speed up the implementation of solutions for intelligent transport systems. over the past few years, these solutions have been implemented more slowly than other modes of transport, with a rush to deploy resulting in a patchwork of national, regional and local solutions. the development of advanced transport systems must be welcomed and must be done in close cooperation with all relevant public and private sector partners. the agreement thus makes it possible to avoid the associated delays and inefficiencies in travel: transport would become more efficient, safer, cleaner, travel times would be reduced, and this would clearly benefit transport users.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because maritime transport, even for ships travelling from one port of the european union to another and not calling at a third country port, is subject to complex administrative procedures. this leads to additional administrative burden and additional costs for the transport of goods by ship. this makes maritime freight transport less attractive and its potential less exploited. the wider use of electronic data exchange tools and the application of electronic maritime systems and the one-stop shop principle should be encouraged, as well as the reduction of formalities, the optimisation of flows and the best use of space in ports, the use of a common language and the simplification of the discharge procedure for the issue of a maritime certificate.
in writing. − the transport sector is crucial to the creation of the european single market. it is also an important part of the development of the eu and its regions and has a direct impact on social cohesion, accounting for 10% of the eu's gross domestic product and more than 10 million jobs. it is therefore necessary to ensure greater accessibility of transport and to create and improve missing infrastructure links, and to promote the use of multiple modes of transport. it is also necessary to tackle the fundamental problem of the negative impact of traffic on health and the environment, to reduce co2 emissions from transport by means of sustainable measures such as the integration of energy from different sources, support for research and the development of less environmentally harmful technologies and modes of transport. however, these challenges require increased funding not only from member states but also from eu funds.
in writing. − at present, the baltic states are to a large extent isolated from the european transport network. we must strive to integrate the baltic sea region more closely into the ten-t priority axes, including the rail axis from berlin to the baltic coast (ten-t1) and the motorways of the sea (ten-t 21). more emphasis should be also be put on short sea shipping and inland waterway transport, which is cost-efficient as well as increasingly environmentally friendly. in order to remain competitive in the future, many european ports – such as klaipeda in lithuania – need to modernise their infrastructure. however, the effects of the financial crisis on the transport sector should not be overlooked. given the scale of necessary transport investments, it is vital to ensure european cooperation and to prioritise projects prudently. there is more to transport than merely trucks, traffic and tariffs. transport also relates to the freedom of movement for persons and goods, as well as the development of the european single market. it also boosts employment. above all, as recent high-profile train and plane crashes tragically reminded us, safety must continue to be one of the priority areas of future transport policy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the economic downturn has caused a huge increase in unemployment rates in the european union, which has hit young people very hard. today, more than 5.5 million young people (21.4%) under the age of 25 are unemployed, twice the overall unemployment rate. youth unemployment is one of europe's most pressing problems. it is a loss of economic growth, which can lead to a collapse of the tax base, and which is detrimental to investment in infrastructure and public services. this also leads to higher welfare costs and under-investment in education and training. all member states must take action to tackle youth unemployment in a manner that respects national specificities. it is necessary to ensure growth and create new jobs, especially for young people, and to give priority to cooperation between business and education providers. member states need to ensure equal access to education for all, guaranteeing the right to free and adequately funded education from kindergarten to university and financial support for young students. it is necessary to encourage member states to continue investing in education and training, even in the face of fiscal constraints. the financial independence of young people is important, as it guarantees them the possibility of living an economically independent life.
in writing. − tragically, the hardest hit by the economic crisis are those upon whom our future rests. with 5.5 million people unemployed among the under 25 year olds, the youth unemployment rate in the eu is nearly twice the percentage as that observed among the working population as a whole. in lithuania, last year, every eleventh young person was unemployed compared to every twenty-fourth in 2008. we need to boost youth employability by helping adapt educational skills to labour market needs. support measures such as quality internships, apprenticeships and traineeships can aid a smooth transition from education to employment. however, it is important that these internships do not replace real jobs – we need to protect young workers from exploitation; nor should the advantages of non-formal learning be neglected. for lithuania it is vital to prevent an increasing brain-drain of skilled labour. eu member states should develop youth retention strategies – although these schemes can only be successful if young people feel they are a valued part of society. if we fail to reduce youth unemployment we will be faced with a lost generation of workers who will grow up with poor job prospects while straddled with growing public debt.
in writing. − as the eu economy and territory grow, waste volumes continue to increase steadily. in order to manage the management of bio-waste effectively, the priority should be to prevent or reduce it. this requires the involvement of citizens themselves. in addition, biowaste can be used as a feedstock for the production of biofuels for the bioenergy, electricity and transport sectors. efficient management of bio-waste can help to achieve the eu's target of 20% of energy consumption from renewable sources by 2020. however, in order to achieve the objective of more efficient management of waste resources and reducing the costs of bio-waste management, the european commission should draw up a directive on bio-waste management. this directive should include the establishment of a waste collection system, which would be mandatory for member states, and provide for national methods for the recycling of biological waste in order to avoid less favourable methods such as landfilling and incineration.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report on the commission green paper on the management of bio-waste in the eu, which makes a strong commitment to combat climate change and to reducing the negative impacts of economic growth. even though bio-waste treatment has already been widely discussed in the european institutions, one cannot but notice how much the situation has changed in the past few years. whereas technological progress has to be included in the debates, no less than 11 new member states have joined the eu along with lithuania, all of them bringing specific waste management practices. while the annual amount of bio-waste in the eu is estimated at 76.5-102 mt for food and garden waste and up to 37 mt from the food and drink industry, i find it of utmost importance to pave the way for a debate on the possible need for future policy action. improving bio-waste management and developing the use of bio-waste as a resource will result in considerable economic, social and environmental gains and help turn the eu into a resource-efficient ‘recycling society’.
in fact, many of us politicians have not been able to answer one simple question: why did this financial crisis happen where we were before and why couldn't we have foreseen it? i think we left the financial market participants with a simple question, the question of control. and i think that today the creation and emergence of these three institutions is inevitable and indeed it has to be done. the data collected by these three institutions will be of great importance, and will be partly confidential. and i think that the completion of the main chain, as mentioned by commissioner barnier, also requires the establishment of a european rating agency. if we have a closed system, we will be able to use the data we collect in the future to increase our competitiveness in the european market.
in writing. i am pleased that belgium, having taken over the presidency of europe from spain, has put on its agenda a number of tasks of major importance to the european union as a whole. belgium expects a stronger, more determined europe, oriented towards the implementation of the social model, and europe must therefore strive for a closer dialogue between the european union and its citizens. employment will be one of the main social objectives of the belgian presidency. i agree that investment in human capital and job creation are integral objectives of social and economic development. it is of the utmost importance that belgium, during its presidency, focuses on creating new jobs and on increasing the opportunities for the unemployed to re-enter the labour market. youth unemployment also requires more attention, and measures that promote learning and provide young people with job opportunities immediately after graduation are welcome. as this year is the european year against poverty and social exclusion, i am pleased that belgium will pursue further action in this area. i support belgium's commitment to further strengthen the implementation of the principle of equal opportunities and to combat violence against women.
i supported this important report. rapid scientific and technological developments have led to the development of highly advanced and innovative food production processes and devices. however, food produced using genetic engineering, genetic modification of cells and nanotechnology raises serious concerns about its impact on human health. scientific and technological progress is inevitable and necessary in today's world, but it must be safe and remove doubts about the negative consequences for human health. therefore, food produced by such processes should be subject to very strict control and placing on the market rules. consumers should be given the right information about themselves and the right to choose.
in voting on the regulation on novel foods, i voted against the introduction of food products from cloned animals on the food market in the european union. i voted against the inclusion of the term 'meat of cloned animals' in this regulation. i think that we need to make a fairly clear distinction between research and practical use, in this particular case the use of such foods in our daily lives. the majority of the citizens of the european union are against the introduction of cloned meat into the food market, so we have to, we have to take that into account. first, the quality of such meat is questionable and its effects on human health have not been definitively assessed. another important factor is the ethical considerations, the ethical aspect. we are sending a rather clear political message today that we are against the introduction of cloned meat from cloned animals into the european union food market. and we ask the european commission to come up with a legislative proposal so that we can continue to talk about this.
madam president, i voted in favour of all those reports related to financial institutions and financial services, including the report by our colleague josé manuel garcía-margallo. this legislation on financial institutions, aimed at better regulation of banking activities, increasing transparency of derivative markets and implementing measures in relation to sovereign credit default swaps, is greatly needed to prevent economic and financial crises in the future. it is clear that the under-regulated financial markets and the activities of the financial institutions were the prime causes of the current crisis and the worldwide recession. the financial and economic crisis has in turn created real and serious risks to the stability of the financial system and the functioning of the eu’s internal market. to avoid the vicious circle of recession and mistrust in the financial markets, we urgently need more and wiser regulation. our partners in the united states house of representatives have already undertaken such steps with the passing of the dodd-frank bill. it is high time to do the same for the european parliament and the european union.
madam president, i voted in favour of the resolution and now i would like to express my strong support for the opening of eu accession talks with iceland and for its eventual membership of the union. iceland is without a doubt a european country with a strong democratic culture, a robust human rights system and a developed economy. indeed, the time has come for the eu to expand into areas that fulfil democratic, geographical and other criteria for accession to the eu. the enlargement of the eu should not stop now, as there are still other european countries on the waiting list, such as croatia and other balkan countries, and – possibly and hopefully – one day even ukraine, georgia and belarus. we should not forget that the enlargement of the eu has been its most successful foreign policy instrument, having completely transformed the political and economic landscape and the wider european continent. let us again set the process in motion, with iceland being the first post-lisbon newcomer to the eu.
in writing. − i voted for this report. in the financial sector remuneration policies for categories of staff whose professional activity has a material impact on the company’s risk profile have been such as to encourage transactions seeking short-term profits, with increasingly risky business models being developed to that end, to the detriment of workers, savers and investors, and sustainable growth in general. inappropriate remuneration structures of financial institutions played a big role in the accumulation of risks that led to the current financial, economic and social crisis. therefore, i very much support commission’s proposal to set up sound and transparent remuneration practices which should be continually reviewed and, where necessary, adapted in order to create uniform conditions throughout europe and secure the global competitiveness of the european finance industry.
in writing. − in the past year, lithuania and a number of other eu member states with similar fates have fallen below the bottom and have not yet reached the bottom. we do not yet know the final consequences, which may be very sad. jobs have been lost, businesses have gone bankrupt, and most of our citizens are still forced to endure painful reforms. the burden of the crisis has been too heavy on the taxpayers. all of this could have been avoided if we had the necessary international crisis management rules in the banking sector. the existing eu and international supervisory mechanisms in the financial sector have been ineffective in preventing and sufficiently containing the crisis. we are all in the same boat and we need to take strong measures to create the preventive mechanisms and safety nets that ensure the sustainability and stability of the financial system. this test must include at least three elements: first, an effective eu supervisory structure, linked to the european systemic risk board; second, the eu crisis management framework supporting interventions, with a strong eu code of conduct in the areas of governance and relevant expertise and financial resources; and third, a much-needed measure to level the playing field for business across the eu. only then could business conditions be monitored on a level playing field throughout the eu. the most important thing is that lithuania, like other eu countries, manages its public finances in an efficient, transparent and responsible manner. it is necessary to restore public confidence and economic self-respect in our countries.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, i am sure that most of you are familiar with article 49 of the treaty on european union, which states that 'any european state may express its wish to become a member of the union'. lithuania is a fairly young eu member and i think it would be selfish and hypocritical to close the door behind us. however, by the time lithuania joined the european union in 2004, it had to undergo a number of reforms and make painful compromises in order to meet the union's accession criteria. iceland is in for the same. the political and economic upheaval that led to the collapse of the icelandic banking system in 2008 sent a clear warning signal to the whole of europe. iceland is now trying to recover, but it still has a long way to go before it can restore its economic stability. compensation for 'saving' depositors and debt repayment are a good start for some eu member states. it is also important to regain the trust of both the domestic and foreign public. iceland has much to offer in itself, including its strong democratic culture, which would undoubtedly enrich our union. iceland is a major contributor to the eu's environment and energy policy, through its renewable energy and its experience in combating global warming. it will be up to iceland to fulfil the accession criteria and to decide whether it will actually join the union. in the meantime, we should continue to strengthen our relations with that country and continue the cooperation between the european parliament and the icelandic parliament.
mr president, i would like to congratulate our colleagues, who have contributed much to defending parliament’s position in the negotiations with the council and the commission. in setting up this highly important post-lisbon institutional body, the result we have largely meets the interests of the european parliament and of european integration more generally. it is very important that parliament managed to maintain the community method in the functioning of the eeas, as well as the power of budgetary oversight. it is also very important that the motion for a resolution reiterates the principle of geographical balance with regard to the recruitment process within the eeas. we should insist that this principle remains strong and is duly upheld in the later stages of the development of the service. finally, we expect a lot from the financial and staff regulations that are to be adopted to further enhance the structure of the eeas and its community nature.
member of the commission. - ladies and gentlemen of the house, thank you very much for the pronunciation. i myself - and so does mrs damanaki and my colleagues - have taken note of your comments and constructive suggestions and will be glad to return to them in the coming weeks. i would like to make four specific points. firstly, i would like to expressly advise the member states, in the light of the competence of our member states, not to undertake new authorization procedures at the moment and to achieve a result. i believe that a moratorium on the development and decision-making of new rigs is justified at the moment on the basis of applications and therefore new commissioning can be postponed. we are working on an energy strategy for 2020-2050. the crucial question is what role oil will play in the coming decades. in fact, there is a worldwide trend, a tendency, to require more energy from oil. i doubt whether europe needs more oil. i have a goal to go low on oil in our 2020-2050 strategies and to justify and plan that path accordingly. so it's about the way out of the oil crisis. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's energy policy is not affected by the crisis. airplanes, for people, for goods, for trucks, will be dependent on oil in the next few decades, despite the development of our railways. it is possible to increase energy efficiency, reduce demand, move some of it and avoid it, but without oil, transport by air and by road by truck will not work in the coming decades. cars: we are currently putting renewable energy in the transport sector at 10% by 2020 are the value we are pursuing together. we have more than 200 million registered cars in the european union, in the member states. if we can get five million of them with electric mobility on the roads by 2020, that would be a great achievement. but there are still 200 million people who run on oil, diesel and petrol. this means that electric mobility is on the rise, but in the next few decades, oil will remain - if you accept individual mobility, and if you are interested in vehicle production - necessary for all the efficiency gains. that is why we will need oil for a relatively long time to come and why we should not increase our dependence on third countries to 100%, especially since third countries are often less suited to our authority and our security requirements than member states and the companies working in the european union. i would like to address the issue of liability and damages. i am in favour of full compensation and the principle of the polluter being at fault or not at fault and of the liability of the company, first of all individually. the first step in the process of creating a new environmental policy is to establish a new framework for the protection of the environment. secondly, we should include the question of whether we propose compulsory insurance, by reason and amount. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would like to conclude by addressing the issue of jobs. bp, shell, total and others are major european employers with important jobs, and that is about livelihoods. i think it is important to preserve this in the energy sector. i do not want to speculate, but the business sections of our european newspapers are full of questions about whether bp will remain a stand-alone company based in europe in the world market. it is a question of market value and of certain possible changes. clearly, bp, based in europe, is a contact for us, one that can be held accountable, while a change in the shareholder structure - i do not want to quote what is assumed here with chinese state-owned companies - does not strengthen europe's position between politics and the private sector in all matters of security. we shall be happy to report to you in the next few days in the circle of the coordinators for the environment and for industry and energy, and i shall come back to this house in the autumn with our comprehensive proposals.
member of the commission. − madam president, i would like firstly to thank all the members of the european parliament for this really constructive debate. we have had, as mr oettinger said, a lot of proposals to take on board. for those people who are asking for an immediate and efficient reaction, i would just like to say that this moratorium will give us the time to check our legislation and see what is needed. we will return to this, and this is not the end of the procedure. i would also underline that the expansion of emsa’s remit to drilling activities will be our first priority. we have to do this, and we completely understand that we have to do this. concerning people and demands for real and tangible results as regards renewable energy, i would just say that the eu 2020 strategy, the commission’s strategy, focuses on giving research the funding and motivation to achieve results. one of our goals in the framework of the research programme under the next financial perspective will be – and it is a priority – renewable ocean energy. i consider this a positive response to your proposals.
president of the council. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, let me also welcome this debate. this was my first debate under this presidency, and i must say that i feel very fortunate to have been able to take part in such a rich, such a nourishing debate, which shows both a very broad consensus among members and a fairly strong convergence with the proposals that have been put forward by the commission. i will ensure that the debate takes place within the council and that this broad consensus and convergence can also be spread within the council. we can see clearly at the end of this debate what the outlines of a broadly revised policy for europe should be, and we can see clearly that, in the end, as the saying goes, 'to something, misfortune is good'. if this disaster that happened far from home allows us europeans to take stock of all our rules, to make sure that they are strengthened now, when we are not facing a disaster, and to ensure that such disasters do not occur, well, it will not have been in vain and we will at least have the merit of having drawn all the conclusions from it. it will be necessary - and this has been said at length - to review all our preventive rules, to examine them after thorough consultation, of course with the industry, of course with the public authorities and the regulatory authorities, but also - i stress this point to the commission - with independent experts, to make sure that we have all the views on these issues. and if so, as long as we do not have an arsenal of rules giving us the near certainty - because there is no certainty in this area - that such accidents cannot happen, the proposal made by the commission to consider a moratorium is a fundamental proposal, and i will also ensure that the council holds this debate and that a decision is taken as soon as possible. the second fundamental principle is that of the polluter-pays principle and, therefore, i understand the proposal for an insurance policy which has a double advantage: firstly, to ensure that, if a disaster occurs - which is clearly not desirable - the person responsible for the disaster cannot be put in a position to escape his financial obligations. it's the most important thing. secondly, if the insurance system is sufficiently well designed and powerful, it will have the advantage of having a deterrent effect and of ensuring that those who might risk venturing into boreholes with a number of risks, given the insurance conditions imposed there, can be dissuaded from doing so. that's one advantage of more than one such system. then, of course, we will have to review all the rules on liability and on disaster response mechanisms. i have already mentioned the usefulness of revising the emsa (european maritime safety agency) regulation to ensure that we have all the means available in our civil protection mechanisms to respond in the event of a disaster. finally, and most importantly, what this disaster reminds us is that europe was particularly visionary when we adopted the energy-climate package in december 2008, the famous 3x20. it is a kind of happy coincidence, in a way, that at the very moment when the previous us administration decided to embark again on an adventurous drilling policy, in alaska itself, and in absolutely extreme conditions, europe was doing exactly the opposite, europe was preparing to give itself the means of a real energy efficiency and a real transition to renewable energies. we can only rejoice and we must continue to say and let it be known that this commitment we made some 18 months ago is becoming more concrete every day. last june 30 was the deadline for all member states to submit their national action plans for the development of renewable energy. i do not know whether all the member states have already submitted theirs, commissioner. i'm sure your administration is putting legitimate pressure on them to get those plans on schedule. belgium did it 48 hours late, which i apologize for, but it was done. these documents show that this fundamental industrial transition is under way. this time, each member state has examined all the internal measures necessary to achieve this transition and has, at the same time, become aware of its potential and the enormous amount of work that will have to be done. the same applies to energy efficiency assessments. as you know, commissioner - and perhaps you also know, ladies and gentlemen - the presidency is very keen to see us make rapid progress on this energy efficiency issue. she's the queen of all politics. renewable energy policies are obviously very good, but it's even better not to consume energy. we know that in the field of buildings, and despite the regulations we have recently adopted on the energy performance of buildings, for example, in the field of heating, in the field of industry and even in the field of transport, there are still enormous gains to be made in europe. a lot of studies have shown that. and if we look at the evaluations that are being made on standards for private vehicles, for cars, for example, we see the effectiveness of these standards, which have contributed very largely to reducing the consumption of fossil fuels, and in particular oil, by european motorists, which shows that this policy is relevant. as you know, commissioner, we are awaiting the first elements of the evaluation of the energy efficiency action plan so that we can start a second, extremely ambitious plan as soon as possible. we shall also return to the subject of renewable marine energy, commissioner damanaki. you know that belgium is particularly attached to the north sea for obvious geographical reasons. we took the initiative some time ago, together with eight other member states and a non-member state which also happens to be a platform-owning country which is also an oil-producing country - norway - to enter into an agreement with ten other member states to form a wind farm in the north sea. this is one of the largest renewable energy development projects. in parallel, we are beginning to consider developing other renewable technologies in these wind farms in the north sea, using the power of the sea for the benefit of these productions. this is also something we will be returning to, and it is also an important debate because, under this presidency, we will have to conclude the action plan 2011-2020, that is to say, to put into practice our energy vision for the next ten years. the aim is to translate the objectives of the 2020 package into concrete and precise proposals, accompanied by financing solutions. we must also start the debate on the 2050 roadmap, the vision for 2050, which is probably the cornerstone of all the commitments we have made. some scientists are now telling us that europe can seriously imagine, by 2050, thanks to an effective energy efficiency policy and the development of renewable energies, reaching 100% renewable energy and thus being free of its fossil fuels within a horizon of about 40 years. this is a debate which we must also conduct and engage in with all the necessary ambition.
in writing. there was a lot of talk about what we should do to prevent events like mexico's in the future. this is particularly relevant for us in lithuania, as we have an oil platform operated by a non-eu state company just off our shores. in the event of an accident on this platform, we would have an ecological disaster off the eu coast, in the most polluted sea on our continent. moreover, no agreements have been concluded so far with the states responsible for the platforms on the removal of the consequences of the accident on this platform. another similar case could arise in the event of an accident on the same baltic seabed gas pipeline, the nord stream. the three cases are linked by a common feature: the environmental impact assessment procedures are paid for by the contracting authorities and are therefore not independent and objective. i urge you to start discussions on the establishment of a system for independent environmental impact assessment. a fund, funded by the companies concerned and administered by independent bodies, is a necessary part of such a system to finance environmental impact assessments, thus ensuring their objectivity. on the other hand, we must ensure that these rules on objectivity apply not only to environmental impact assessments carried out by the eu but also by third countries.
i have been following the events in kyrgyzstan closely in recent months, especially as the name of rosa otumbaeva started to appear in the media. ten years ago, she was my colleague and her country's ambassador in london. the embassies of lithuania and kyrgyzstan are located next to each other. we met often, talked to each other. the impression was that roza otumbaeva was not only an experienced diplomat but also a strong politician of democratic views, who was able to assess the situation in her country and in the world with a sober mind. i believe that her election as president of kyrgyzstan should stabilize the situation in the country, give a start to reforms, improve relations with distant and near neighbors. kyrgyzstan becomes the first parliamentary republic in the region and no longer needs to be ruled by family clans. however, the situation remains extremely complex. ethnic uzbek refugees who have not yet returned to their places of residence ignored the referendum. there is a risk of unrest, especially before the parliamentary elections in the autumn. the european union countries should urgently agree on specific eu intervention mechanisms and measures that could be implemented if the conflict continues to deepen. the eu crisis response mission should play an important role in this regard. the coordination mechanisms between the un, the eu and the osce need to be strengthened. we call on the kyrgyz law enforcement authorities to hold accountable those responsible for the bloody events by ensuring that they are given a fair trial. ensuring the human rights to healthcare, education and employment would be the most appropriate means of uniting society, promoting democracy, and fighting corruption and radicalism.
mr president, i voted in favour of the agreement between the european union and the united states to share financial data for the purposes of terrorist finance tracking. we are well aware of the threat terrorism poses to our own security as well as that of our partners across the atlantic. i do not need to remind you of all the terrorist attacks that have taken place on european soil throughout the last decade. it is also obvious that our american partners represent a very important strategic ally, not only in the area of global security, but also economically and in other areas as well. the commitment to such a strategic partnership was clearly expressed by vice president joe biden in this house just a few months ago. we should therefore be proud that we finally gave consent to the agreement that is so important for efforts to track terrorist activities, especially since the new agreement contains significant improvements, and clarifications made by the european parliament have been largely taken into account.
mr president, i voted in favour of the resolution. i would like to commend the fact that the european parliament engages in discussion with the commission and the council on the very important issue of kosovo’s future. kosovo can be considered the last piece in the complex balkan political puzzle. it is therefore vital that we put the piece in the right place and manage the process in a harmonious and peaceful way. the independence of kosovo has already been recognised by 69 countries, including 22 eu member states. it is important to note that eu integration prospects serve as the strongest incentive for the balkan countries to undertake necessary reforms and also as a factor for regional stability. kosovo is not an exception. therefore, if we want to anchor kosovo in europe and ensure regional stability in the balkans, it is vital that we come up with a common approach towards the whole region and kosovo in particular.
mr president, i also voted in favour of this resolution because i think we should once again reiterate our support for the european integration aspirations of the countries in the balkan region within the stabilisation and association process. albania is certainly a country which has made tangible progress in the context of the reform process. however, it is also a country which needs to do more to get closer to eu norms and accession criteria. more substantial efforts are needed to consolidate democracy and the rule of law and to ensure the country’s sustainable development. the political crisis following the june 2009 parliamentary elections is deplorable. we must make clear to our albanian partners that fully functioning representative institutions – parliament being the most important one – are the backbone of a consolidated democratic system and perhaps the most important political criterion for eu integration.
mr president, i voted in favour of the resolution because i would like to join my colleagues in condemning the violence that erupted in kyrgyzstan last month. it is deplorable that hundreds of armed kyrgyz men stormed the city streets, shooting civilians and setting fire to shops, choosing their targets purely on the basis of ethnicity. i would like to extend my condolences to the families of the approximately 300 dead and the 2 000 who were injured or hospitalised. it is important that the eu keeps pressure on the kyrgyz authorities to conduct a credible, impartial and independent investigation into the violence.
in writing. − the negotiations on the creation of a unique eu body, as provided for by the lisbon treaty, are coming to an end. i hope that the council will also respect the agreement and that the decision on the establishment of the european external action service will be taken by the end of july. it is necessary to ensure that the office is operational as soon as possible and contributes to the high-level formulation of european foreign and security policy, ensures the consistency of the eu's external action and enhances the role of the european union on the world stage. the european parliament must seek to strengthen parliamentary oversight in the implementation of the common foreign and security policy by exercising an active role in both the legislative and budgetary functions. it is necessary to ensure that the office's activities are based on the principle of budgetary neutrality and to avoid duplication of tasks, functions and resources of the office with other institutions. in addition, the issue of responsibility and accountability both in the use of eu budget funds and in the allocation of competences needs to be addressed.
in writing. following the difficult debates over the last few weeks on the european external action service in the relevant parliamentary committees and the various political groups, i would like to thank our colleague elmar brok for his strong involvement in this matter. today's vote lays the foundations for a strong diplomatic service at the level of the european union. the community identity of the service will thus be strengthened and its political and budgetary accountability to the european parliament guaranteed. the new service will also be staffed by at least 60% eu officials. i regret, however, that the hearings of the special representatives for foreign policy in the european parliament are still 'informal'. i hope that this matter will be settled within a few years. nevertheless, the european parliament's powers of scrutiny are strengthened, in particular on common foreign and security policy (cfsp) missions financed from the eu budget.
in writing. − i decided to vote in favour of the report on the european external action service (eeas) and fully support the setting-up of what is a key innovation of the lisbon treaty and a historic decision for the future of europe. only seven months after the entry into force of the new treaty, together we managed to reach a consensus, securing the political and budgetary accountability of the new service to the european parliament. this supervisory role will guarantee a truly democratic control of the service, which is of paramount importance to me. this report will ensure geographical balance as well as better coherence between the different aspects of the eeas. it is not about duplicating diplomatic services within the eu, but about strengthening the eu’s diplomatic services. such an agreement is clear evidence of the european union’s commitment to gathering its force and promoting its values in a more effective way. it will allow the eu to exert a real influence in the international arena. this is a chance for the european union but also for its member states.
