in writing. − both albania and kosovo have made progress in reforms to strengthen democracy, the rule of law and sustainable development in the country. however, the objectives set need to be pursued further, in particular in the fight against corruption and organised crime, by strengthening the administrative capacity of public authorities and by ensuring the rule of law. the political conflict in albania over the results of the parliamentary elections, which has been ongoing for a year, is hampering albania's aspirations to become a member of the eu. the albanian political forces need to engage in constructive dialogue in order to reach an agreement on the outcome and on a new electoral law ensuring a fully transparent electoral process. i voted in favour of the resolutions because they also call on the eu member states to look for new ways of interacting with these balkan countries. one of these is to promote human contacts through academic exchanges between pupils, students and researchers.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. the reform of the common agricultural policy is taking place in the context of an economic crisis and serious financial difficulties faced by the member states, taxpayers, farmers and consumers. the new cap will have to adapt to changing conditions in europe and the world and face a number of challenges. the european agricultural sector will need to ensure food security for consumers and help feed the world's growing population. this will be very difficult, as increasing production will be hampered by rising energy prices and the need to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. in addition, the effects of climate change will leave large additional areas of land unusable for production, requiring the eu and the global agricultural sector to produce more food with less land, water and energy. i believe that the cornerstone of the cap should remain the objective of ensuring that european agriculture can compete with well-subsidised trading partners (such as the usa, japan, switzerland or norway) and that fair trading conditions are created for eu farmers. furthermore, support for agricultural activities throughout europe must continue to be provided to ensure local food production and balanced territorial development.
in writing. − european parliament resolution on the common agricultural policy ambitious and targeted reform. she has my vote and the liberals' vote. most importantly, the injustices observed were done to farmers in the new countries. the resolution calls for the expectations of the 12 eu members who joined after 2004 to be met and for fair treatment of them in the allocation of european union funds. with the increased powers of the european parliament in this area since the entry into force of the lisbon treaty, i hope that the council will not fall back on the unequal direct payments to farmers in the old and new countries and will not agree to any additional co-financing that could undermine fair competition in the eu single market. the new cap ensures that farmers receive effective, targeted support that benefits society at large, in particular consumers. it focuses on food safety and the supply of high-quality products at affordable prices. as a liberal, i can justify the current cap budget only if the new cap creates value rather than distorts the market, makes the eu more competitive with its trading partners, creates jobs and fosters balanced agricultural and rural development, and is environmentally and landscaping friendly. the reforms proposed by the european parliament are a positive step which i support and hope to see along with the european liberal family.

in writing. - the new proposal aims to codify council regulation (eec) no 1612/68 on freedom of movement for workers within the community. this proposal preserves the content of the acts to be codified and seeks to clarify the application of the legal rules on the free movement of workers. the european union must pursue the long-term objective of ensuring the free movement of workers in all member states. all workers must be granted the right to move and to take up employment freely within the member states. the european union must ensure more transparent labour mobility, help workers to improve their living conditions, and become properly integrated into society, and therefore discrimination on grounds of nationality and employment conditions must be abolished without exception. all member states must seek better cooperation among themselves in order to implement more flexible terms and conditions of employment.
in writing. − colleagues, free movement is a fundamental right of workers and their families. this report rightly noted that labour mobility within the union should be one of the means of enabling eu citizens to improve their living and working conditions. it's a matter of freedom, but it's also a matter of dignity. in this respect, accession to the eu has had a very significant impact on my country, lithuania. with a few exceptions, we are theoretically free to work in any eu member state. we are now in the golden age of ryanair, the low-cost airline that makes it easier to travel. lithuania's three largest airports serve more than 40 european cities. it's a blessing and a curse at the same time. the fact that lithuania is experiencing 'mind-leakage' due to continuous migration is of great concern. almost 60,000 lithuanians live in the uk, compared with fewer than 5,000 in 2001. approximately 90 000 lithuanians live in ireland. we are a small nation that has educated and invested in their education, and now we are losing our most active people in their 20s and 30s. we, lithuania and some eu members, must do more if we are to stop this dangerous trend.

in writing. - i supported this report. the interoperability of business registers is an appropriate means of promoting further integration of the economic area within the european union and of ensuring greater legal clarity for businesses and consumers. business registers are normally administered at national and regional levels. however, increasingly economic activities are carried out on a cross-border basis and it is therefore necessary to ensure better interoperability of business registers in order to reduce costs and save time in the interests of legal clarity and transparency. the current financial crisis has re-emphasised the importance of transparency in all financial markets. with regard to financial recovery measures, increasing access to up-to-date and official information on companies could help to restore confidence in markets across europe. business registers are of crucial importance in this respect, as they record, analyse and store information on businesses, such as information on the legal form, registered office, capital and legal representatives of the company, and make this information available to the public. this will enable the access of creditors, business partners and consumers to official and reliable corporate information on a cross-border basis, in order to ensure adequate transparency and legal certainty in eu-wide markets.
in writing. - i support this report. a balanced economic and social development is needed to achieve a sustainable eu economy. economic growth must be decoupled from increasing resource and energy consumption, reducing harmful emissions, and combating climate change. also seek to exploit the potential for green job creation in the service and social economy sectors. to achieve this, the commission should develop a strategy for green jobs, on the basis of which regional development strategies should be adopted by regional authorities. the implementation of the strategy should be financed from eu, national and regional resources, the distribution of which should be carefully coordinated. the support would cover research, applied innovation and infrastructure, development of new technologies such as renewable energy and energy efficiency.
in writing. − i support this report because it encourages the creation of sustainable jobs, in other words jobs that meet the needs of the present generation without jeopardising the opportunities of future generations. it is also emphasised that these jobs must create social welfare, i.e. they must serve both man and nature. the objective of the new sustainable economy must be overall economic and social sustainability, creating long-term prospects for increased competitiveness, social well-being and better environmental protection. the quality of jobs is also a particular concern in the context of the increase in precarious employment contracts with low-skilled workers, and this report also encourages the creation of good jobs. these workplaces must ensure stable and socially oriented work, with particular attention to the health and safety of workers, decent work and skills needs. i would therefore urge member states not only to focus on creating high-skilled jobs, but also to give greater value to medium- and low-skilled jobs by creating good working conditions. i also support the initiative that it is essential to adapt lifelong learning strategies to the needs of older workers, with the aim of involving workers over the age of 55.
in writing. − colleagues, the pursuit of a sustainable economy is not only an ecological imperative, but also one of the cornerstones of our strategy for a social europe. but such changes will only bear fruit if they are socially fair and lead to more employment, better working conditions and more social protection in the long term. therefore, a sustainable economy must include not only social and ecological sustainability but also technological and economic sustainability. you don't have to object to that. we need more creative tools. for example, energy efficiency. the construction sector in lithuania was among the sectors most affected by the economic crisis. most old lithuanian buildings are very uneconomical imported energy resources are used inefficiently, as evidenced by the high heating bills of the population. national projects that provide insulation and increase energy efficiency in buildings are likely to bring money back into the market through wages and investments in the construction sector. at the same time, it will make lithuania less dependent on imported energy resources. of course, this is only one example and does not necessarily apply to all member states of the union. but it shows that, especially in times of crisis, we need to think more creatively and join forces where we need to build the foundation for a sustainable future.
in writing. - i supported this report and the concern it expressed for the situation of eu farmers. the global economic and financial crisis and the volatility of food and basic commodity prices have caused serious difficulties for producers of agricultural products, which have also affected consumers. although food prices have risen by 3,3% per year since 1996, prices paid to farmers have risen by only 2,1% while operating costs have increased by 3,6%, demonstrating a poor functioning of the food supply chain. in addition, average farmer incomes in the 27 eu member states fell by more than 12% in 2009, meaning that farmers are no longer able to earn the income they need from their work. nevertheless, farmers and the agri-food sector need to continue to produce food of very high quality at affordable prices for consumers. i agree that there is a need for more transparency in the agricultural sector and that the european commission should take the lead and propose mandatory annual reporting of market share by leading traders, processors, wholesalers and retailers.
in writing. − in the face of both economic hardship and climate challenges, european farmers face unavoidable challenges. a number of problems arise from the volatile prices of basic agricultural and food commodities. the european commission's communication addresses price transparency, competition and improving food quality, but some important elements are missing. the resolution adopted today is therefore of great importance, as the european parliament, in expressing its opinion, draws attention to the very unequal bargaining power of farmers, which has led to the abuse of the existing situation by various players on the agricultural market, distorting farmers' incomes, and the conclusion of unfair contracts and cartel agreements. i voted in favour of this resolution because i believe that the food market needs to be better managed in order to ensure fair returns for farmers, to promote price transparency throughout the food supply chain, to promote competition and to combat price volatility, to improve the exchange of information between market partners, and to take account of challenges such as climate change and biodiversity loss.
in writing. − i voted for the ep report on fair farmers' incomes because one of the main objectives of the eu's common agricultural policy - ensuring adequate incomes for farmers - still remains unfulfilled. while food prices in the eu have risen by 3.3% a year since 1996, prices paid to farmers have risen by only 2.1% and their running costs have risen by 3.6%. this indicates that there is an imbalance in the food supply chain due to the dominance of traders, supply undertakings, processors and retailers in this business. i therefore believe that it is necessary to encourage the development of farmers' economic organisations and the creation of cooperatives, which increase farmers' influence and their bargaining power. i support the proposals in the report to increase price transparency in the agricultural products sector, in particular to combat speculation in the main agricultural commodities, to control price fluctuations and to facilitate the exchange of information on prices and contracts between market participants. it is particularly important to prevent the abuse of buyer influence in the food supply chain. i strongly support the proposal adopted by the committee on agriculture to introduce programmes to encourage the sale of products on local markets and to achieve preferential conditions for producer organisations, farmers' cooperatives and smes in the food supply chain in public procurement. it is a pity that parliament has rejected this proposal.
in writing. - i supported this report. the egf was initially intended solely to mitigate the negative impact of globalisation on the most vulnerable and least qualified workers in some sectors, who lost their jobs as a result of major changes in world trade which seriously disrupted the economy. since 1 may 2009, the scope of the egf has been extended to cover workers made redundant as a result of the financial and economic crisis, for applications lodged from 1 may 2009 to 31 december 2011. although the number of applications has increased recently, the use of the egf remains low in the poorest regions of the eu, where the people who have lost their jobs need the most support. such uneven use of egf support is linked to the different strategies of the member states. in support of the provisions set out in the document, the financial support from the egf must be provided more quickly and efficiently in order to help as many workers as possible. it is necessary to develop and adopt new measures to allow member states to submit applications for mobilisation of the egf as soon as the collective redundancies are announced rather than after they have taken place, and to shorten the time taken to reach a decision on the granting of the aid.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the negative impact of the economic and financial crisis on employment and the labour market in europe remains enormous, with workers continuing to be made redundant on a massive scale in various sectors of the economy, and because the number of applications from eu member states for financial assistance from the european globalisation adjustment fund is also increasing sharply. however, the current procedures for financial support are too complex and the period for obtaining funding is too long. at present, the time taken for the member state to receive financial assistance from the globalisation adjustment fund is between 12 and 17 months, which means that most workers made redundant do not receive financial assistance in time and become hostages to the effects of globalisation and the crisis. it is therefore necessary to simplify the procedures of the fund as soon as possible, since this is the only way to reduce the time taken to receive financial assistance by up to half. it is also essential that the eu institutions ensure a smooth and rapid decision-making process when considering issues of financial support, as delays in such decisions can only worsen the already difficult situation of workers. i would also encourage member states to exchange examples of good practice and in particular to learn from those member states that have already set up national globalisation adjustment fund information networks involving social partners and stakeholders at local level, so that an appropriate support system can function in the event of mass job losses.
in writing. - i supported this report. the regulation we voted on today is based on the brussels convention, and it is one of the most successful pieces of eu legislation that has laid the foundations for the european judicial area. the application of european rules based on uniform examples of case law promotes greater legal specificity and predictability of decisions by avoiding multiple concurrent proceedings. the free movement of judgments is one of the essential conditions for the functioning of the european judicial area. i therefore support the position expressed in the document that this regulation needs to be updated in order to guarantee the effective circulation of judgments.
in writing. − many minority communities in the european union, especially women and girls, experience multiple forms of discrimination and are therefore more vulnerable to social exclusion and poverty than indigenous women. being a woman of a certain ethnic group is not a disadvantage in a democratic society, and the european union is therefore committed to giving women more and more rights and to promoting innovative ways of raising awareness of women's rights. both the commission and the member states must ensure that gender equality and anti-discrimination are fully implemented, that ethnic minorities have access to support services and to various educational programmes. however, the social integration of women from ethnic minority groups remains an open question in the european union. in the absence of a coherent eu policy on the integration of migrants, i would urge the commission to take action as soon as possible to develop eu guidelines to help member states to provide women from ethnic minorities with better and faster access to education, employment opportunities, healthcare, social benefits, financial support. i agree with parliament's position that gender equality policy issues must also be fully implemented in national minority groups.
in writing. −the entry into force of the lisbon treaty has made the prohibition of all forms of discrimination a matter of competence for the european union. it is therefore important to adopt a positive approach to ageing and to create opportunities for older people to live life to the full. i am pleased that parliament has produced a report calling for action on an ageing society. in particular, it is important to pay particular attention to older women living in poverty, as their pensions are generally lower due to lower pay in sectors where women work. in addition, the elderly constitute a large consumer group, which means that the demand for services for the elderly is increasing very rapidly and will increase in the future. however, barriers to access to easily accessible, quality and affordable public and private services are encountered. it is therefore important that the commission take action to regulate access to many basic services, the need to ensure a quality of life, in order to avoid physical, psychological and economic abuse. an ageing society is often viewed in a negative light. problems arise with regard to the age structure of the labour force and the sustainability of social protection and health care. older people are indeed assets and provide a key support to society and the family, and their right to live in dignity and independence must be guaranteed.
in writing. from a humanitarian perspective, we should improve services and health care for and increasingly empower a rapidly growing sector of our population - older women - since they, like any other europeans, have the right to lead a healthy, dignified and independent life. from an economic perspective, we should strive for higher employment levels and more flexible and part-time work opportunities for ageing women. tapping into this often neglected source of potential and experience will prompt economic growth, reduce old age poverty, improve the overall living standard of pensioners, and enhance societal cohesion. i voted for this resolution in the hope that tackling these issues with a two-pronged approach would open a path to addressing the more rudimentary but not less eminent problems of gender and age inequality in the workplace, undervalued and undercompensated caretakers, and ultimately, our society's ingrained preference for style over substance when solving gender and age related issues. age, gender and aging loved ones should always be treated as assets, never as liabilities.

in writing. the economic and financial crisis will continue to place a strain on the european labour market in the years ahead. in these circumstances, tackling unemployment must be one of the main policy objectives of the eu and the member states and the commission's proposed guidelines should better reflect this. i would like to emphasise that these employment guidelines generally do not pay enough attention to gender equality policy and the promotion of women's entrepreneurship. the effective reduction of the risk of poverty in europe requires the setting of binding targets to address gender stereotypes, existing discrimination in the labour market and structural causes of gender pay gaps, as well as barriers to women's access to certain occupational activities and to women's entrepreneurship. nor do the guidelines set a target for reducing illegal or shadow work, as in many countries people are working illegally in these difficult times, simply because they have no other way out of the current situation. the anti-poverty guidelines do not set any ambitious targets at all, and the fight against poverty requires, in particular, employment and education policy measures targeted at the most vulnerable groups at risk of extreme poverty, such as single mothers, older people on low pensions, and the disabled. i therefore urge the member states and the commission to make the best of the guidelines' objectives, otherwise europe will not achieve the results they seek.
president of the council. - mr president, i would simply like to answer a number of questions that have been asked. firstly, as regards the fact that there has not been the possibility of having a debate on all the budget lines as proposed by the council, i assume that these discussions will take place. i think these debates are necessary and we need to discuss the priorities to be given in the context of this 2011 budget. but agreement was not easy to reach in the council. it must be said that this budget was voted on in the council in a way that was not obvious. but this is also due to the budgetary situation of each country. this economic situation is particularly difficult. i would like to point out, however, that despite this difficult situation, despite the difficulties we are all experiencing in our countries, the european budget is increasing compared with the 2010 budget. this is an increase of 2.91%. i said that, to conclude, some would like it to be more, but it is still a significant increase compared to 2010. mrs haug raised the question of heading 1b earlier. i would just like to remind you that this heading is up by 14% compared to 2010. it is therefore not correct that the council does not take account of the specific nature of heading 1b, which has increased by 14% compared with 2010. i think there is a realization of the council that perhaps has not been sufficiently described or sufficiently noted. i would also like to say to the rapporteur for the 2011 budget that in my speech here we also wanted to draw attention to the work of parliament and you mentioned a heading 1a of the budget which parliament wanted to highlight. i wanted to emphasize in my speech that the council had been attentive and indeed wanted to show that, in its work, it was willing to be open to discussion, particularly with regard to youth policy. of course, there is an openness to discussion on this policy because parliament has very clearly identified it as one of its priorities. i would also like to point out that, as regards heading 5 and the institutional aspect as such, it is true that all the institutions will have to make efforts, but it is also true that, in the context of the budget presented by the council, we have not touched on parliament's budget as such. but i think it is important that each institution should save money and that we should all set an example in this difficult year from the budgetary point of view. in conclusion, there is no doubt that we will have a number of further fruitful meetings and discussions in the coming weeks. i hope they will be successful enough to reach an agreement on the 2011 budget and i would simply like to point out that it will not be an easy budget because we have to follow a new procedure, because there are all these new policies to be implemented - you have pointed this out - but also because we cannot ignore the difficult financial and budgetary situation of our member states, each of which has budgetary objectives to achieve. and i hope that it is by being aware of all these difficulties, challenges and constraints that we will find together a budget acceptable to everyone for 2011, and that we will demonstrate together that this new procedure of the lisbon treaty can be successfully concluded, that is to say with a budget that has been adopted for this year 2011.
member of the commission. − this is a question i am taking over from commissioner oettinger and his reply is as follows. the public consultation published on the website ‘your voice in europe’ was carried out between 31 march 2010 and 31 may 2010. in total 510 responses were received. in addition several stakeholders sent written comments to the public consultation in the form of e-mails, letters or reports. in general terms, a large majority of european citizens is in favour of binding, community legislation from which many expect enhancements as regards safety, legal certainty, public involvement and national decision-making. the report is about to be published on the commission webpage. the legislative proposal would be based on chapter 3 of the euratom treaty which deals with health and safety protection. it would aim at creating a community framework for member states to ensure the safe and sustainable management of spent fuel and radioactive waste for all types of waste and at all stages from generation to disposal, avoiding undue burdens on future generations. as the technical assessment is still ongoing, it is too early to describe what environmental requirements there will be. all member states would continue to be responsible for managing their spent fuel and radioactive waste.
thank you for your reply, but it was rather vague and not very specific, and it seems to me that the european union should nevertheless put more environmental demands in this area. i would like to ask, although i understand that there is no commissioner in charge, how the commission intends to finance these things, whether sufficient financial resources will be allocated to ensure environmentally sound solutions.
member of the commission. − this is a very important, but also a rather technical, question. in the case of multiannual programmes, the suspension of budget commitments would not have an immediate impact as payments can be made against related commitments within a period of usually two years. this should protect ongoing projects under multiannual programmes and provide sufficient time for the member state concerned to take corrective action in line with the council recommendations within the edp. the funds would be rebudgeted as soon as the member state meets the council’s recommendations. a definitive loss of payment would only occur if the member state fails to comply with the council’s recommendations. moreover, in line with the communication of 30 june, the new sanction mechanisms should exclude a situation where the reduction of eu spending would lead to a reduction of farmers’ and fishermen’s income. the concrete proposals on the range of relevant expenditure categories and the design of the sanction mechanisms will ensure that this condition is met.
member of the commission. − the last decade has seen major reforms of pension systems and these are continuing in most member states. the commission supports, monitors and assesses the impact and implementation of national reforms through the open method of coordination with a view to developing pension systems that are adequate, sustainable, as well as modern and transparent. current work to monitor and assess pension systems in the eu includes the joint review on pensions by the economic policy committee and the social protection committee. the joint review on pensions takes stock of the results of the last decade of pension reform and assesses progress made in the light of the setbacks due to the crisis and the bigger challenge to deliver pensions against a background of lower employment, lower growth, faster ageing and a much worse fiscal situation. the council adopted an interim report in june and a final report with country profiles should be adopted at the end of the year. the commission also works closely with member states on a wide range of issues relating to pension systems and pension reform. for instance, annual joint reports and peer reviews offer advice and recommendations tailored to the situation in individual member states. to measure progress towards achieving adequate and sustainable pensions, the member states, together with the commission, have developed a set of indicators within the social protection committee. these will also be used under europe 2020 to assess the adequacy and sustainability of member states’ social protection and pension systems. pension reform needs to be based on solutions that preserve the adequacy – and sustainability – of pensions, and which are not carried out at the expense of the elderly and the socially most vulnerable. in his political guidelines last september, president barroso said ‘we need to ensure that pensions do the job intended to provide the maximum support to current and future pensioners, including for vulnerable groups’. adequate pensions therefore means ensuring that older people are not placed at risk of poverty and that they can maintain their standard of living to a reasonable degree after retirement. given the current pressure on pension systems and the problems with alternatives – such as higher taxes on pension contributions or poorer pensioners – the member states need to consider the role that more people working more and longer can play in ensuring pensions are both adequate and sustainable. the commission has always stressed that pension reform is essential but cannot suffice on its own. workers must have both the opportunity and the capacity to work more and somewhat longer in order to acquire adequate pension rights. this means fighting labour market discrimination against older workers, opening job markets for them, providing training and skill updates throughout their careers, adapting working conditions to the needs of people at different stages of life and ensuring that workers’ health and safety conditions improve. much greater efforts are needed here, and europe 2020 provides a framework for such efforts. i hope the member states will set their sights high in their first national reform programmes. the current european framework for pensions – which spans everything from policy coordination to regulation – should be updated in the light of population change, the setbacks and lessons stemming from the crisis and changes in national pension systems. with our green paper on pensions published on 7 july, the commission has opened a europe-wide debate on whether and how the european framework for pensions should be developed to ensure our pensions are adequate, sustainable and safe. the green paper does not dispute the fact that the design of pension systems is largely a member state responsibility. instead, it seeks to contribute to the debate on the eu’s added value for pensions. it looks at how the eu can support the member states in their efforts to adjust their policy and make pensions adequate for the future. and it discusses the options for a more robust european framework for pensions. the green paper is an opportunity to canvass the views of all those with an interest in pensions – not just the member states, but also employers and trade unions who play a key role in some pension schemes and, of course, the european parliament. this will ensure that any measures we propose are based on solid evidence, are effective and proportionate and fully respect the principle of subsidiarity.
thank you for your answer, and i'd really like to hear a specific answer from you. what concrete measures will the european commission take to promote the employment of older people? because the green paper you have presented speaks precisely of the inevitable need to consider the extension of the retirement age as a bad thing, in view of the current demographic situation in the member states of the european union. however, in the absence of concrete measures on the employment of older people, the extension of the retirement age would be detrimental, as older people would be trapped in poverty.
mr president, i welcome the commission’s initiative. i think it is an important achievement that we have managed to agree on the harmonisation of practices in the area of animal experimentation in the eu. this directive marks an important step in ensuring that experiments on live animals for scientific purposes will be replaced as soon as it is scientifically feasible to do so. this directive will first oblige the member states to promote the advancement of alternative methods. i strongly urge the member states to ensure that adequate funding is provided for training, research, development and the implementation of scientifically satisfactory methods or testing strategies that do not entail the use of animals.
in writing. - i supported this very important agreement. the protection of animals used for scientific purposes is a very complex issue in itself, where it is necessary to take into account the interests of many different stakeholder groups, whose views and needs are sometimes very different. i think we've struck the right balance of agreement. the agreement focuses on promoting the search for alternatives to animal testing and significantly improving the conditions for their keeping and use. it also succeeded in maintaining the observations made by the european parliament at first reading on the reduction of administrative burdens and the continuity and viability of european research and industry, which still depends on animal testing. alternatives to animal testing should also be further explored. to this end, it is envisaged to set up an eu reference laboratory to validate alternative methods. the appropriate specialised laboratories established in the member states would contribute to its activities by providing additional resources. i believe that this resolution strikes the right balance between the needs of the industrial and research communities, while at the same time strengthening and harmonising the animal welfare standards applicable to animals used or intended for use in research.
in writing. − the interaction between science and nature for development is always a subject of considerable debate. the european parliament adopted today an important document on the regulation of scientific research involving animals. this document will strike a positive balance between animal protection and research and provide for several important aspects ensuring the protection of animals for scientific research. skeptics argue that the directive is contrary to the principles of animal welfare and that the use of animals for scientific purposes raises serious doubts. however, the latter directive requires that the necessity and possible alternatives to the use of animals be assessed before each test. as well as the obligation for each member state to set up national committees to look after animal welfare and ethical aspects. i voted for this document because i believe that animal testing should be more strictly controlled, but at the same time we must bear in mind the inevitable scientific progress in various fields and the continuity of research.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. in the face of the rapidly growing phenomenon of ageing, it is necessary to take measures, both at member state and european union level, to combat social exclusion and age discrimination among older people. it is necessary to ensure full support for older people in the member states, in particular by creating the conditions for the establishment of a quality health and social care system. ensure also the contribution of the member states to ensuring the provision of long-term care services. implement an information and prevention policy for older people, with particular emphasis on nutrition. the successful achievement of the objectives set requires the development of sustainable systems for the financing of long-term care services. the european commission, for its part, should make efforts to ensure acceptable standards of healthcare for all european citizens, regardless of their material situation.
in writing. − i voted for this report because the new eu member states' employment policy guidelines for 2020 will have a major impact on the labour market for several years to come. i am pleased that the european parliament, through its proposed amendments, has succeeded in setting clear objectives and providing for specific measures in the employment guidelines proposed by the commission. of course, reducing unemployment, ensuring a minimum income and combating poverty and social exclusion remain the top priorities of employment policies in all member states. i also agree that one of the main objectives is to achieve equal pay for equal work and equal working conditions for all employees. i agree with parliament's proposals that employment policy measures should be targeted at the most vulnerable groups of people, because they are the first and most affected by poverty. i also urge the commission and the member states to give maximum attention in the employment guidelines to young people and older workers who face discrimination in the labour market.
in writing. − colleagues, this report rightly emphasises the importance of raising employment levels and increasing labour market participation. it's important for our economy and our society. it is also important not to sacrifice quality for quantity. the situation of the working poor is not addressed at national or eu level. those who work but whose disposable income still forces them into poverty are a significant group. the economic downturn has turned this problem into a europe-wide one, and lithuania is particularly hard hit. these trends are well reflected in the minimum wages workers receive and the widespread reduction of wages as a strict savings measure. people without a higher education are particularly vulnerable. according to eu statistics, the risk of poverty for a working person without a university degree is 16%, twice the average in lithuania and eight times higher than for university-educated workers. unfortunately, this gap in lithuania is much larger than in the european union as a whole. the issue of working poverty has not been sufficiently addressed by member states, including my own. we need more studies on this problem and more concrete measures to reduce poverty in the workforce.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. iran remains the country with the highest number of executions per year. i therefore strongly support the european parliament's position, which strongly condemns the death penalty and calls on the iranian authorities to abolish the use of the death penalty, to renounce all forms of torture and other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment, and to prevent the persecution of those responsible for defending human rights. i support the european union's commitment to promoting human rights worldwide and the implementation of related support programmes, such as the eu-funded european instrument for democracy and human rights. with a budget of €1.1 billion for 2007-13, this instrument ensures respect for human rights and democracy worldwide. the commission and the council, together with other international organisations, should therefore continue to actively develop additional assistance measures to actively defend human rights defenders in iran.
some colleagues have already mentioned that the question put by the european people's party contains a certain amount of mystery and charade without directly stating why and how the problem is raised. a little more clarity on this question would be nice. on the other hand, it must be recognised that some countries in central and eastern europe are particularly vulnerable and feel less secure than the old eu countries. the council common position of 8 december 2008 set out certain frameworks in the area of arms exports. experts acknowledge that the country we are talking about today, france, has not left the framework, but this does not mean that the increasingly concrete implementation of security and defence policy does not require improvement and refinement of the framework and rules. i fully agree with my colleague elmar brok that we need to cooperate more on arms. the aim is to create greater confidence and less suspicion in our union in the field of arms exports, which are sometimes justified and sometimes escalated without justification. the latter pits large and small, old and new eu countries dangerously against each other. it requires more trust and solidarity, which does not come by itself. we need to talk more, consult more, and avoid exaggerated accusations and blurred parallels with the early to mid-20th century, when europe was ruled by the law of the fist. the consultation mechanism within the eu needs to be improved to achieve a concrete result. then this discussion will be useful.
mr president, with the lisbon treaty we have further improved policy coordination and solidarity in many areas, including foreign and security policy. the treaty also contains a mutual assistance clause in case any member state is threatened militarily. in light of this, the plans by france to sell four mistral warships to russia and to share some of the shipbuilding technology seem very alarming. what is more, the mistral warships are clearly offensive – i stress, offensive and not defensive – in nature. therefore, before we actually start doing something about the provisions of solidarity foreseen in the lisbon treaty, we should make sure that we do not diminish the security of our fellow eu member states or that of our neighbours. i urge the council to do everything in its power to initiate wide discussions within the eu regarding a need to develop a common definition and updated approach towards arms sales to third countries.
president of the council. − the council attaches great importance to increasing employment opportunities and participation in the labour market for all citizens, including people with disabilities. some of the main objectives of the europe 2020 strategy endorsed by the european council on 17 june 2010 are of particular interest to persons with disabilities as they aim to promote employment, improve education levels and promote social inclusion, in particular by reducing poverty. in addition, the europe 2020 strategy specifically mentions persons with disabilities in the context of the flagship initiative entitled 'a european platform against poverty'. this initiative will have an impact both at eu level - where the commission should develop and implement programmes to combat discrimination, for example against disabled people - and at national level, as member states will have to focus on creating and implementing measures that respond to the specificities of groups at risk, including disabled people. in line with the conclusions of the european council of june 2010, member states will now have to act to implement these priorities at their level. in addition, all common policies will have to support the strategy. this comprehensive approach should improve the social inclusion of persons with disabilities, as also highlighted in the council resolution on a new european disability framework adopted in june, in which the council explicitly invited member states and the commission to mainstream disability issues in all relevant flagship initiatives of the 2020 strategy and to draw on the human capital of persons with disabilities, including through the implementation of appropriate training and employment measures. in its resolution, the council also invited the commission to prepare, in collaboration with disabled people and their representative organisations, a new european strategy for disabled people for the coming decade, building on, inter alia, the europe 2020 strategy.

president of the council. - i refer you to the last part of my answer. the epsco council on 21 october, as i said, at the initiative of the presidency, will discuss the matter again and should reach conclusions on employment and social inclusion, which will of course be provided to you.

president of the council. - while i can understand that runaway inflation poses a number of problems and that a number of financial mechanisms will need to be rebalanced if this inflation persists, i can only tell you that as a member of the euro area, greece shares a responsibility with the other member states to ensure the stability of the euro area and that its economic policy is, like that of the other member states, a common concern. all member states are expected to pursue sound national policies in line with the stability and growth pact and the broad economic policy guidelines. the country must therefore take responsibility for tackling these problems effectively, to reduce the risk of jeopardising the proper functioning of the economic and monetary union.
president of the council. − in view of the 65th united nations general assembly, the council, at its meeting on 14 june 2010, adopted the priorities of the european union. i would like to remind you that, following the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, it is the high representative for foreign affairs and security policy who represents the union in matters relating to the common foreign and security policy in international organisations. the priorities adopted by the council are centred on the following themes. first, to promote the strengthening of the united nations the european union will contribute to work towards building a stronger multilateral system, including by improving the representativeness, transparency, accountability, efficiency and effectiveness of the united nations. the second priority will be to contribute to international peace and security. the european union intends to increase the visibility of its positions and contributions in the field of crisis management in the relevant un bodies, in particular the security council. the third priority theme will be environment and sustainable development, in particular with a view to achieving the millennium development goals. the fourth priority concerns human rights. since its inception, the european union has been one of the most resolute defenders of human rights and one of the most active participants in the united nations system in this field. the european union will actively promote the integration of human rights into all aspects of the work of the united nations, reaffirming that human rights are inextricably linked to the other un security and development objectives. finally, the fifth priority concerns the reform of the united nations system, with a view to improving the functioning, efficiency, transparency and representativeness of the system. as regards the reform of the human rights council human rights council review , the european union is participating in the formal and informal consultations. the union will continue to promote the capacity of the third committee, the only universal body with competence to implement human rights standards, to act. this capacity must be fully preserved. as regards the issue of diplomatic representations, as of 1 december 2009, all european commission delegations abroad have become european union delegations. in the case of geneva, the union delegation and the liaison office of the general secretariat of the council of the european union have adopted temporary working arrangements pending the establishment of the european external action service.
let me firstly point out that continued confusion over how the eu is represented in geneva and new york risks undermining the eu’s ability to act effectively and work constructively with partners. secondly, following extensive internal discussions on priorities regarding the review of the human rights council in 2011, the belgian presidency and the high representative now need to ensure that sufficient resources and political attention are given to outreach activities towards other un member states. also, leadership is required on a number of important resolutions, most notably the resolution on the death penalty and further work towards the negotiation of an international arms trade treaty. i would appreciate your comments on this.
in writing. regulating the use of new technologies in food production a welcome step forward. but we must not forget the threats as we promote innovation. one of them is the placing on the market of cloned animals, their progeny and products derived from cloned animals. this question still raises a number of problems, related to several different aspects. these include animal welfare requirements, as enshrined in the lisbon treaty, the right of consumers to be informed about the origin of the products they consume, and ethical issues. so far, we have more questions than answers on each of these aspects. therefore, until the debate on cloned animals has even started in the european union institutions, we must be very cautious about the placing on the market of these animals, their offspring and their products.
in writing. − better regulation has become a prerequisite for the functioning of the european union, which could in principle help to overcome the economic crisis more quickly and achieve faster economic growth. it is necessary that the institutions respond to the expectations of citizens, of businesses operating in the single market and of national and local administrations, and that decisions are taken as closely as possible to the needs of citizens. i support the provisions in the resolution that the better regulation process should be supported, with the aim of increasing the transparency, effectiveness and consistency of european union legislation. the role of the european commission as a legislative initiative body in this process is particularly important, as it prepares high-quality proposals, reduces administrative burdens and operating costs for businesses and cooperates with member states to ensure the proper implementation of eu legislation.
in writing. − i voted for the report on better regulation because i believe that better regulation is a prerequisite for the functioning of the european union and one of our citizens' rights, which can only be realised through legislation that is transparent and understandable to the citizen. the regulatory environment in the european union is being improved through a number of measures, such as impact assessment, reduction of administrative burdens and simplification and harmonisation of existing legislation. in particular, i support the commission's programme for reducing the administrative burden resulting from european union legislation, which has been in place since 2005 and aims to reduce the administrative burden by 25% by 2012. it is essential to reduce the operating costs of companies operating in the union in order to help them to operate in difficult economic conditions and compete on a global scale, and to streamline public administration procedures. attention should be paid to unnecessary reporting requirements and to the 'once-only' principle of the small business act. electronic means of communication are an effective tool for reducing administrative burdens. the eu institutions must cooperate actively with the member states to avoid divergent interpretations of legislation and so-called 'improvement'. according to the commission, 32% of the eu's administrative burden is due to decisions by some member states going beyond the requirements of eu law and the ineffectiveness of their administrative procedures. it should also be borne in mind that consultation with all stakeholders, in particular the social partners and smes, is essential in the preparation of draft legislation.
in writing. - i support this report. in line with the employment guidelines, member states must make every effort to achieve the overarching objective of sustainable growth and job creation. it is also necessary to address the long-term challenges facing the member states: demographic change, globalisation and the development of new technologies. to achieve this, it is necessary to invest in sustainable growth that will promote the preservation of existing jobs and the creation of new ones, with a particular focus on small and medium-sized enterprises, which generate the majority of jobs in europe. the social employment objectives must also be pursued: promoting quality education and lifelong learning for current and future workers; paying particular attention to promoting the employment of young people, the elderly, the disabled and women in the fight against unemployment; and encouraging the conclusion of atypical employment contracts.
in writing. − the demographic change in the european union is driving the ageing population and important challenges need to be addressed now: meeting the growing demand for healthcare, adapting health systems to the needs of an ageing population and ensuring their sustainability in a society with fewer people of working age. the current high prevalence of poverty among older people, as a result of drastic cuts in pensions and other social benefits in many eu countries, makes it important to develop sustainable long-term care financing systems to finance care for older people. it is also necessary to establish minimum standards for all supervisory sectors, including minimum remuneration. i would like to highlight the need to reduce health inequalities and protect older people in the community and in care facilities. we must also combat social exclusion of older people and any form of age discrimination. we must ensure the conditions for active ageing and a dignified and full life for older people.
ladies and gentlemen, the rejection by my country's parliament of amendments to the law prohibiting all forms of violence against children, including corporal punishment, has only once again shown that in today's society there is a lack of tolerance for the views of the youngest and weakest members of society, and an unwillingness and inability to appreciate the importance of the rights and interests of the child. it has once again demonstrated the urgent need to address the issue of protecting children's rights and combating violence against them. sadly, in my country, many view the spanking of children as a cultural heritage or an effective form of parenting. i strongly disagree with that, and i would like to emphasize that the mismanagement of emotions and the consequent justification of aggression by calling it education is wrong and unjustifiable. i would like to point out that violence poses a serious threat to the physical and mental health of children, their personality development and social development. given that children are the most vulnerable group in society, i call on the commission and the council to strengthen the fight against violence against children by establishing binding standards in all eu countries and to work more closely with member states and non-governmental organisations to prevent violence against children. i also call on the member states to take the necessary action and preventive measures to combat violence against children.
first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on the preparation of this very important document. the increasing dependence on imported energy, in particular gas, is one of the new challenges of the union's security policy, which will require the united and solidarity-based efforts of the union and of us all. the european commission must play a very important role not only in crisis situations but also in shaping the eu's energy policy in general and in particular in gas supply contracts with third country suppliers. i would very much like, mr commissioner, that the very manner and conditions of the signing of the yamal gas pipeline agreement between poland and russia should not become a bad example in the context of all new and future energy strategies and the document under discussion today. the european council in brussels on 14 and 15 december called on the commission to take all necessary steps to ensure that the union's energy policy is fully integrated into the union's energy policy.
in writing. i would like to agree with my colleagues and welcome the fact that such an important regulation will be adopted in such a short time. this is further proof that europe is able to react quickly and effectively to a real problem. the principle of solidarity between member states is enshrined in the gas security regulation another step towards a common energy policy. we all probably hope that there will be no more crises like the one in 2009 and that the mechanisms set out in the regulation will not have to be used, but the reality is different. the timeliness of this regulation was confirmed only recently, in june, when gas supplies to lithuania were cut in half as a result of disagreements between russia and belarus. i have repeatedly stressed that for isolated gas markets with a single supplier, such as the baltic states, this regulation will only be effective if they are integrated into the common european union gas network. private investment is often insufficient to build the new pipelines required and therefore additional financing is necessary. i would like to thank my colleagues who understood and took this into account in the negotiation of this regulation and the commission for formally committing to tackling the energy island problem in the energy infrastructure package. it remains only to call on the member states to implement this regulation properly.
the european parliament adopted a regulation on the security of gas supply today. this is a very important step towards the implementation of the common energy policy of the european union. a step towards energy solidarity between the member states of the european union and greater regional cooperation. i voted in favour of this document because i believe that this regulation will ensure that the energy islands of the european union, such as the baltic states, will not be deprived of gas supplies by the monopolists who have unilaterally closed their taps. it also opens the way for european union financing of energy infrastructure projects contributing to the removal of energy isolation. unfortunately, the regulation has abandoned provisions providing for stricter monitoring of the activities of third-country suppliers such as 'gazprom', in particular in the field of competition, and has confined itself to provisions of a general nature. the second, no less important, aspect is the environment. as the regulation also allows for the financing of projects from third countries by the european union, i hope that the european commission will speed up the implementation of the independent environmental impact assessment instrument.
the protection of biodiversity is linked to many other european union policies. both the fisheries, energy and agricultural sectors are affected by the baltic sea's difficult situation in this case. studies show that this sea, which is practically an internal sea of the european union, is one of the most polluted. the european commission is concerned about the loss of biodiversity itself, but at the same time it is not doing enough to tackle the factors that are driving this process. as far as the european union is concerned, it is perhaps fair to say that each country in the european union has to do its homework. to make efforts to ensure a sustainable fisheries policy, the good functioning of natura 2000, to reduce pollution, both co2 emissions and waste water pollution, and to reduce pesticide and phosphate levels. these are the factors which are contributing significantly to the eutrophication of the baltic sea, which is leading to the loss of biodiversity. the irresponsible implementation of large-scale, so-called commercial energy projects in the baltic sea, the lack of a critical approach to their implementation, also contributes significantly to this pollution.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. the potential of the internal market for electronic commerce in the union is not fully exploited for a number of reasons. consumers do not trust the security of the electronic market and many do not buy online, as consumer protection rules and levels of protection vary considerably. i believe that it is necessary to increase consumer confidence in the online market by informing them of their rights, secure payment methods and ensuring consumer protection in terms of security and protection of personal data. i support the proposal to create and apply a european contract trustmark to give greater credibility to online auctioneers. the disadvantages of electronic markets are not exploited by businesses either. initiatives should therefore be developed to encourage businesses to trade online by improving the mechanisms for online payments, for example by simplifying the vat information requirements, harmonising postal and banking charges, and resolving supplier-consumer conflicts more effectively.
in writing. - i support this proposal. the current eu directive on the investigation of accidents and incidents in civil aviation, adopted in 1994. however, the aviation market and the situation have changed and it is necessary to adopt a new eu regulation to reflect the current situation. in particular, a network of safety investigation authorities should be established, composed of national authorities and eu authorities (european aviation safety agency (easa)), responsible for improving the quality of investigations, classifying and analysing information, promoting cooperation between national authorities and contributing to the improvement of aviation safety. in addition, the rights of passengers and their relatives must be more clearly defined. the regulation aims to extend the requirement for a company to provide a passenger list as soon as possible, but no later than two hours after the accident, to cover not only companies operating flights to the eu but also those operating flights to europe. the aim is to introduce a mandatory requirement for airlines to offer passengers, at the time of booking, a contact person to be informed in the event of an accident. it is necessary to provide the possibility for the relatives of victims to receive, on an exceptional basis, full information on the progress of the safety investigation.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because air traffic volumes are increasing year by year. the safety of flights and aviation security are the primary objectives. ensuring the safety of passengers, the protection of passengers, crew, ground staff, the public and property from aviation accidents, and the safety and security of aircraft are becoming increasingly important. in the event of aviation accidents, accidents must be independently investigated, with the aim of identifying safety gaps and allowing for measures to be taken to address them. it is necessary to reduce the disparities in research capacity between member states. the current framework for accident investigation is the 1994 eu directive, and the changing and increasing complexity of the aviation market has necessitated the introduction of new legislation on new entrants such as the european aviation safety agency (easa). the new regulation should provide a clear legal basis for the agency's participation in safety investigations. meanwhile, the creation of a network of national research offices should help to reduce the existing disparities in research capacity in the eu. it could also improve the quality of investigations and provide a legal basis for cooperation between national authorities. it is important and necessary to implement at european level proposals to improve flight safety or the investigation mechanism. the key issues are aviation safety, timely and appropriate assistance to victims of civil aviation accidents and their families. this would be a valuable support for the families of victims, who are often confused by the many organisations and procedures they face, and at the same time are coping with the pain of losing a loved one.
in writing. − natural gas consumption in europe has increased rapidly over the past decade. as domestic gas production declines and demand increases, dependence on gas imports increases. as far back as september 2009, when i spoke to the itre committee when the draft regulation was presented, i stressed that the creation of a secure and single energy market will not be possible as long as the eu has energy islands that are entirely dependent on a single external gas supplier and do not have access to european infrastructure. the issue of security of gas supply will remain relevant in the future, which is why i voted in favour of this report, which aims to improve the security of gas supply in individual member states and the union as a whole. it is particularly important to note that the document identifies which member states become so-called gas islands due to the lack of infrastructure links with other union member states, which prevents the creation of an effectively functioning internal gas market. i believe that this document is the first step towards real european energy integration, with a focus on cross-border connections, diversification of energy sources and routes and more effective implementation of energy efficiency initiatives.
in writing. − i have endorsed this report because diversified, uninterrupted gas supply and consumer protection are of particular importance today. the russian and ukrainian gas crises have affected millions of european citizens and the eu economy. energy security is one of the eu's top priorities. this requires a solidarity approach to eu policies and coordinated action. this requires regional and cross-border cooperation and flexibility in decision-making. finally, energy policy, which was previously based on national interests alone, is being taken to the european level. the energy community must be based on competitiveness, sustainability and, in particular, security of supply. this regulation will significantly reduce the eu's vulnerability to external supply disruptions, strengthen the global leadership of european gas companies and the eu's geopolitical position. meanwhile, lithuania needs to diversify its energy resources: the discussions on the construction of a new nuclear power plant must have a real basis and not remain at the level of mere deliberations, which of course also concerns the electricity bridges to the west and the construction of a liquefied gas terminal. lithuania will be safer the more alternative sources of supply it has. we must make every effort to ensure that in the future we are not just consumers of russian energy, but also business partners providing our own channels for the flow of energy from the east to the west.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because i share the view that the eu must learn from recent history and be prepared in the event of a repeat of the gas supply crisis. as we have seen, national measures alone will not always be enough to deal with supply disruptions. eu countries must prepare and coordinate crisis management plans at regional and community level. unfortunately, some eu countries are still dependent on a single supplier of gas. the situation in case of a crisis would be particularly dangerous for those regions which are not connected to the pan-european gas network. these 'energy islands' are particularly in need of community financial support to link their gas networks to the eu's single system.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the loss of biodiversity is a serious threat. biodiversity, which is crucial for balanced development and poverty reduction, is essential for our planet, for human well-being and for human livelihoods and cultural integrity. but now, human activities are destroying biodiversity at an unprecedented rate. this trend can be reversed if local populations benefit from the conservation and balanced use of biodiversity. it should be noted that the loss of biodiversity is mainly driven by sectoral activities, as biodiversity aspects are not sufficiently taken into account in sectoral policies, including conservation of natural resources, agriculture, fisheries, regional policy and spatial planning, forestry, energy and transport, tourism, development and economic cooperation. it is urgent and necessary to halt the loss of biodiversity in europe. this objective is supported by a variety of national, eu and european policies. many of them are dedicated to specific measures to protect key species and sites, but in order to preserve biodiversity, it is essential that biodiversity needs are taken into account in the design and implementation of relevant sectoral policies. it is good to see that the aim is to halt the loss of biodiversity and the degradation of ecosystem functions in the eu by 2020, to restore them as far as possible and to increase eu support to prevent the loss of biodiversity in the world.
in writing. - i voted for this document. the number of natural disasters in the eu member states has increased in recent years, and the importance of prevention needs to be strengthened. since member states have the primary and primary responsibility for the safety of their citizens and the prevention of natural disasters, it is entirely justified to strengthen cooperation in the field of prevention, to improve the coordination of efforts, and to increase solidarity and mutual assistance. i support the proposal in the document that a financial framework at eu level should be established for the prevention of natural and man-made disasters, which would strengthen and concentrate existing instruments, including cohesion policy, regional policy and rural development policy. in the field of disaster prevention and management, it is essential to strengthen the research and development (r&d) capacities of the member states and to improve coordination and cooperation between member states in this field.
in writing. − i supported this report because the number of natural disasters in the eu has increased in recent years, with significant and long-term consequences for the environment and the economy of the affected areas. the cost of human life is often difficult to estimate. the communication highlights the need to review the rules of the solidarity fund and stresses the need to establish an appropriate disaster prevention financing system, providing sufficient financial resources for the prevention and response to natural disasters. this would strengthen and link instruments such as cohesion policy, rural development policy, regional policy, the solidarity fund, the seventh framework programme and the life+ programme. the european commission is invited to consider proposing a more coherent pooling of available resources to increase the effectiveness of eu prevention mechanisms. i am glad that the need for a european agricultural public insurance system is finally being recognised. the commission should submit a proposal for a european public insurance scheme to better address the risks and volatility of farmers' incomes in relation to natural and man-made disasters. this system should be more ambitious than the previous one in order to avoid the many different insurance systems in the eu, which lead to large disparities and differences in farmers' incomes. i believe that there is an urgent need to establish a minimum compensation scheme for natural and man-made disasters, which could be used by farmers in all member states.
in writing. - i agree with this report. reducing poverty in developing countries requires not only increasing employment but also creating new sustainable jobs. to achieve this, measures are needed to coordinate macroeconomic policies, focusing on the stability of real output, income and employment. smes, especially in the agricultural sector, need effective financial support, such as the provision of microcredit, microinsurance and public financial support. the commission and assisting countries should also review existing sources of funding for innovation and additional development and identify new sources that allow developing countries to diversify their sources of revenue to implement effective, specific and active spending programmes. in addition, special attention needs to be paid to tackling one of the biggest challenges in developing countries - child labour and access to primary education for all children. this could be achieved by implementing the right not to be subject to forced labour.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it calls for the european parliament to reduce poverty in developing countries, where hunger, malnutrition and exclusion from access to food and basic public services affect millions of people. another major problem in developing countries is that most of their populations do not have access to adequate social protection, and social protection systems are effective tools for reducing poverty and increasing social cohesion. i would also like to draw attention to the problem of child labour, as it is one of the biggest obstacles to universal access to primary education and to reducing poverty, which in turn impedes the healthy rearing and education of children. i therefore support parliament's call for support for education and for policies on child labour, i.e. for the strengthening of the global working group on education and child labour. it is also essential that the international community, all the states concerned and the eu commit themselves to making every effort to eliminate child labour as soon as possible. in addition, all development strategies should pay particular attention to the most vulnerable and socially excluded people, in particular women, children, the elderly and persons with disabilities.
in writing. − i supported the report because poverty reduction and job creation are the biggest challenges facing the world today and are a necessary condition for balanced development and economic growth, especially in developing countries. it is good to see calls for a review of privatisation policies, particularly in relation to the provision of public services such as waste collection and treatment, water and heat supply, residential maintenance and other services of general interest. the provision of public services must be strictly controlled by the municipalities or the government. this service must be equally accessible and of equal quality to all, regardless of the income or social group of the population. the example of vilnius, where public service providers are increasingly being privatised, is of great concern. the existing infrastructure of services and their providers is vulnerable to disruption. but what happens if a business stops providing a specific service or charges a very high price? the main objective of the interested parties is to demonstrate that the state or the municipality cannot provide services as efficiently as private companies can, and then the services are privatised. who can deny that a similar course has been followed in our own case? public services are too sensitive and important to be handed over to private hands. the priority must be to maintain and develop such services.

the new treaty of lisbon has given the european union a number of new and important privileges in the areas of external action, climate change, energy, tourism and other important areas. this obliges us to adopt the new measures necessary to implement the provisions of this treaty relating to the implementation of the budget and to revise the current financial perspective. more budgetary flexibility than the current multiannual perspective is needed if the european union is to be able to meet its commitments and respond more effectively to immediate and unforeseen events. looking ahead, it is necessary to review budgetary priorities, to identify adequate funding for priority areas and to provide sufficient budgetary resources to support long-term investments, which are a prerequisite for the economic recovery and growth of the european union.
member of the commission. − mr president, some small clarifications for mrs andreasen on this very issue. draft amending budget no 7 does not involve the member states’ guarantees; that is for the other part. what is guaranteed by the member states is 440 billion euros of bilateral loans. as for the structure of the budget, this is the copy of a balance-of-payments facility, and again this is a question which has been asked already. this is a typical arrangement for budgetary items which concern lending operations without a specific quantified guarantee fund. this is therefore a token entry. if the need arises for fresh money – so far activated in the balance-of-payments facility for hungary, latvia and romania up to an amount of 14.6 billion euros – then the commission will put forward a proposal on the appropriations needed by means of a transfer or an amending budget. that is the machinery, but it is nothing new – merely an extension of an existing balance-of-payments facility to the eurozone member countries – and i agree with reimer that this is a very flexible interpretation of the lisbon treaty.
