in writing. – the european parliament has always talked about the need for a single voice for europe on the international stage. the uniquely european financial supervision package is one of the steps to this direction. with the package in place, europe of the 27 would finally be able to work more coherently and with more solidarity within the framework of economic governance decisions taken at the global level. more importantly, the only way that europe can reduce systemic transnational risks and subsequently prevent or mitigate the negative impact of future financial crises is to have an integrated network that funnels and coordinates the expertise and efforts of actors of all levels to establish and maintain a sound, stable and transparent financial system. such a system serves as a prime example of the accountability of europe to its citizens. i support this package because i believe in the future of europe - a europe where our financial industry no longer needs to suffer from its own excesses, and where our citizens no longer need to suffer from the devastating, unnecessary consequences of preventable crises.
in writing. − i support this proposal, which aims to improve the regulation currently in force on the production and use of biocidal products. biocides are part of our daily lives, used in a limited and responsible way, to help prevent the spread of diseases and to ensure high standards of health and hygiene. however, it is essential that legislation ensures a high level of protection for their production and use. this could be done by establishing uniform mandatory standards for consumers and producers of biocidal products. in order to protect the consumer, it is necessary to ensure a robust system of labelling of substances and articles which corresponds to the level of risk, whether biocidal products are manufactured within or outside the european union. it is also necessary to clarify the information to be provided on the label, specifying the exact location of the information, in order to provide effective and appropriate information.
in writing. − the regulation on the placing of biocidal products on the market and their use, which was voted on today, allows us to take another step towards the introduction of a centralised system of registration of biocidal products. the system of registration of chemicals introduced by reach shows that the abandonment of individual national registration systems is indeed justified. unfortunately, we often find ourselves in a situation where a product cannot reach the market of a member state simply because of the complex and unreasonably expensive registration procedures in that state. on the other hand, when facilitating the entry of biocidal products onto the market, we must be very vigilant to ensure that a high level of protection is maintained in view of the particular health risks posed by these products.
in writing. −the recent financial and economic crisis has raised many questions that we will have to answer as soon as possible and has shown that, although the eu has a number of instruments for economic policy coordination, they have not been fully exploited and that the current governance system has significant gaps. the establishment of the esrb is an important innovation, since no one has yet carried out a european-level macroeconomic analysis, the main purpose of which is to assess systemic risk. close cooperation between the esrb and the relevant information bodies (national supervisors and european authorities responsible for specific sectors) is the basis for a coherent supervisory framework at the macro- and micro-economic level.
in writing. − the financial crisis has shown that the surveillance of micro-enterprises by national authorities is not sufficient to ensure that systemic risks are identified and quickly avoided. we need an effective european supervisory framework to monitor, assess and prevent potential risks to financial stability. the communication clearly emphasises the importance of preserving the integrity of the european single market. but it is also essential to protect the internal market from products from countries which do not cherish values such as democracy, the environment and social guarantees, which makes goods and services from those countries cheaper and our european companies unable to compete under such conditions. i agree with the remarks made this month by prime minister j.m. barroso: 'we have people. we have businesses. both need an open and modern single market. the internal market is europe's greatest asset, but we are not exploiting its potential. only 8% of europe's 20 million smes are engaged in cross-border trade. and they invest even less across borders. the european systemic risk board would be a key pillar of the eu supervisory architecture and thus contribute to a more coherent functioning of the internal market. the european single market was one of the main reasons, but not least the accession of lithuania and other fellow member states to the union, which had upheld these values. any visible division and misunderstanding would undermine decades of efforts to remove barriers and barriers.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the report by the european parliament on the establishment of a european insurance and occupational pensions authority. that authority should aim to improve the functioning of the internal market, in particular by ensuring a high, effective and consistent level of regulation and supervision, taking into account the diverse interests of all member states, protecting insurers and other beneficiaries, ensuring the integrity, efficiency and smooth functioning of financial markets, safeguarding the stability of the financial system and strengthening the coordination of international supervision for the benefit of the whole economy, including financial institutions and other stakeholders, consumers and workers. in this document, the european parliament calls on the commission to examine how progress can be made towards a more integrated insurance and occupational pension supervision structure, while seeking to create a single market for financial services. parliament also called for the establishment of international institutions for supervision at eu level in the medium term and for the establishment of a european crisis management mechanism to deal with crises that may arise in the wake of the financial and economic crisis.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, the lack of adequate financial regulation in europe and the lack of development of market surveillance mechanisms were evident during the financial crisis. i probably don't need to remind you that lithuania is one of the countries that was hit hardest by the global financial crisis. for some time, lithuania's growth was almost entirely based on borrowed funds, driven entirely by cheap credit, mostly from foreign lenders seeking quick profits. when the business failed to get back on its feet, the crisis began. it is clear that there is no point in continuing to apply mechanisms whose inefficiency was particularly evident during the crisis. what is needed now is a sound and ambitious policy to create a more efficient, integrated and sustainable european supervisory system. i am pleased to see the amendments to the commission proposal, which was largely drawn up from a banking perspective, despite the position of the countries seeking loans. it is important to include small and medium-sized enterprises, as their businesses rely more on bank financing than on large enterprises. i hope we will succeed in establishing a credible european institution with clear powers and a crisis management mechanism that will ensure that it is not the european taxpayers who bear the brunt of any short-term change in the financial system.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. infringements of intellectual property rights pose a serious threat to our economies and societies, as innovation and creativity have a high added value for the european economy. the phenomenon of infringements of intellectual property rights online has reached a worrying level, particularly in the creative content industries, and the current legal framework is not capable of effectively ensuring the protection of rightholders online while guaranteeing a balance of all interests, including those of consumers. i welcome the proposal to set up a monitoring centre as a means of collecting and exchanging data and information on all forms of intellectual property infringement, to help understand the mindset of real intellectual property infringements on the internet and to take the necessary measures at eu level to combat counterfeiting and other intellectual property infringements on the internet.

president of the council. - thank you, mr president, the 18-month programme of the council, drawn up by the spanish, belgian and hungarian presidencies, provides that the fight against poverty and social exclusion will be a major objective, with emphasis on the unemployed, children affected by poverty, poor workers and single-parent families. the fight against poverty is one of the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy adopted on 17 june 2010 by the european council, which also endorsed the objective of promoting social inclusion, in particular through poverty reduction, by removing at least 20 million people from the risk of poverty or exclusion by 2020. another objective mentioned by the european council is to raise the employment rate for women and men aged 20 to 64 to 75% and to improve education levels, in particular by reducing the rate of school drop-out. during the belgian presidency, four thematic priorities will be highlighted in the fight against poverty: minimum income and social protection, housing and the situation of homeless people, the poverty of children within their families and, finally, the participation of people living in poverty and all those involved in the fight against poverty, precariousness and social exclusion. the presidency will endeavour to deepen the debate on the minimum income at european level at the round table on poverty and social exclusion to be held on 18 and 19 october, with the ultimate aim of ensuring that each member state adopts a minimum income guaranteeing a sufficient standard of living. in these discussions, the presidency will also seek to promote at european level the importance of good governance principles that ensure the participation of people living in poverty. secondly, in order to guarantee the fundamental right to housing for all, the belgian presidency will attach particular importance to the issue of housing accessibility at a conference to be held on 9 and 10 december. e presidency will also highlight the crucial role of education in combating child poverty and in combating its intergenerational transmission, which was already the subject of a conference held on 2 and 3 september. finally, the belgian presidency will close the european year for combating poverty and social exclusion on 16 and 17 december, drawing lessons for the future.

in writing. currently, the death penalty is used in 43 countries worldwide. most of them are carried out in china, iran and iraq. china alone has 5,000 death sentences, or 88 percent of all executions in the world. there are also eu countries where the majority of citizens support the death penalty. there are more countries like that in the world. the main argument is that such punishment discourages potential offenders and reduces crime. but the statistics show otherwise. i support this resolution because i believe that instruments of execution should only be in museums. the european union is also calling on the belarusian authorities to take all necessary steps to ensure that the death penalty is abolished in the country. the execution there is still a state secret. relatives of the executed do not know whether the condemned is alive or dead, and the bodies of executed prisoners are not given to parents or children.
president of the council. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, in the absence of any new proposals from the commission in the field of oil exploration and extraction - an absence, i would say right away, which is entirely legitimate as the commission is continuing its consultations with industry and the various regulatory authorities concerned - i can only reiterate our deep concern at the gulf of mexico disaster and its too many victims, both human and natural. this disaster shows that no precaution is too great in this sector of activity and that everything must be done to prevent a repetition of a similar accident. we can only hope that, just as the exxon valdez and erika accidents in 1989 and 1999 triggered a strengthening of the legal framework in europe and the united states, so too will the framework for offshore oil exploration and exploitation this time. the aim is not, of course, to develop a framework which would be so costly to implement that it would render these activities unprofitable, but to enable them to be carried on safely, particularly in a phase when resources on land are declining. while we await legislative proposals, this matter has obviously not disappeared from our concerns and, although we were pleased, as others were, that the macondo well was closed in mid-july and finally capped on 19 september, this is clearly only a step, given the scale of the rehabilitation work to be carried out. the presidency therefore wished, at the informal meeting of the energy ministers on 6 and 7 september, that commissioner oettinger should come and give his initial assessments. we think there are two things to be learned from what he said on that occasion. firstly, the security measures already taken within the european union are at the highest level in the world. secondly, given the situation of oil activities, at the crossroads of several regulatory areas, the proposals the commission is considering will have to concern these various areas: firstly - since it is better to prevent than to cure - the improvement of safety standards applicable to all european seas, but also the strengthening of the liability regime, the strengthening of regulatory supervision and the intensification of international cooperation, for example with our opec partners, as we already did last june. a first communication from the commission is expected by mid-october and, because of the many areas it is expected to cover - as i have just said - its processing may involve several council formations and, no doubt, several committees of this parliament. the complexity of this matter should not, however, be an excuse for postponing our reply, and we intend to start its examination as soon as we receive this communication in order to bring it to the council as soon as possible. as regards more specifically environmental protection, the european union now has a fairly advanced legislative framework. the precautionary principle and the polluter pays principle are fundamental principles of european environmental policy and are included in the treaty. the oil spill caused by this platform in the gulf of mexico may also provide an opportunity for the commission and the member states to reassess certain aspects of existing environmental legislation. in this context, it should be recalled, for example, that the seveso ii directive is currently under review and that we expect a proposal from the commission before the end of the year. in addition, it should be noted that the commission is examining the european union's disaster response capacity with a view, inter alia, to improving it. in this context, we look forward to this communication by the end of november. finally, i would like to confirm that the presidency will do everything in its power to advance the proposals which the commission will no doubt present to us.

finish line for our announcement. we will take account of the expectations that your members will be communicating to us today and then, certainly in the first half of 2011, we will also come forward with legislative proposals that are foreseen after our communication.
on behalf of the s&d group the environmental disaster in the gulf of mexico has highlighted the lack of safety and reliability of oil extraction operations. this tragic disaster, which has claimed human lives and caused immeasurable damage to the environment, should also be a valuable lesson for us europeans. i welcome the commission's initiative to carry out a safety audit of offshore oil platforms operating in the european union. however, to ensure this fully, wider measures are needed. the commission should in particular assess the capacity of the european union to respond rapidly and effectively to disasters and develop a european action plan. the current regulation of both safety and liability for damage caused is clearly inadequate in terms of content and application. the european union is currently undertaking major infrastructure projects whose safety may not be fully and properly assessed. this includes the controversial nord stream pipeline and other oil and gas infrastructure that is essential for europe's energy security, but which must meet the highest environmental standards. the question of liability must therefore be clearly settled: the polluter must be compensated for the damage caused. it is also necessary to adopt compulsory insurance schemes covering the whole of the territory of the european union. in order to avoid similar disasters, special attention should be paid to preventive measures. i therefore believe that the commission should examine the possibility of developing an effective monitoring system, strengthening inspection methods and tightening the minimum mandatory safety rules of the european union. i agree with the suggestions made by my colleagues that the commission should also produce annual reports, which would allow us to assess the real situation and take timely action when the need arises. in conclusion, i would like to thank the commission, the member of the commission, for the fact that in such a short time we will certainly have a communication and will be able to start looking more specifically at what europe really needs.
first of all, i would like to thank the colleagues who contributed to the drafting of this resolution, and i would also like to thank the commissioner, who has given so much hope and joy in his speech today, for the action that the commission intends to take. and indeed, those fears that the resolution was rushed, perhaps not sufficiently explored... i do, however, believe that this is the voice of the european parliament and a message to society, a message to the council, a message to the commission. it has been a long time since the accident and i think it is very important that the european parliament is at least now having a voice. i think that, as others have said here, it is indeed very important to review the current legislation in force in the european union and legislation relating to safety and quality standards. and it's not really about stopping oil production altogether, but it's just about reviewing the situation and what further action should be taken to avoid a repeat of the kind of accident and disaster that happened in the gulf of mexico. i personally welcome the provisions in this resolution on third party liability. the european commission and the european union in general should strengthen the dialogue with third countries on certain energy infrastructure projects. it is not necessarily the baltic sea and the oil rigs or the nord stream pipeline, but it is the black sea and the mediterranean and the conflict situation with north africa. i think it is very important that third countries are included in this resolution.
biodiversity is a complex and complex issue, and it is clear that it is of importance both in the european union and globally. but it raises another question: how do we balance environmental protection and economic growth? it is perhaps the most pervasive and most headache-inducing question of all. it is well known that biodiversity loss is most closely linked to human activities, often overlooked. it is always possible to say that environmental requirements are a hindrance to competitiveness, because the standards of the european union are high and those of other countries are not, and there are certain problems and it is obviously difficult to find a balance. but there could be some preventive tools. i am talking about the activities that are carried out in the european union and the analyses that are used to support these activities. it is important that the environmental impact assessment of that analysis is of high quality and independent. of course, realistically speaking, it will be difficult to reach an agreement in nagoya, but i wish the european union and the commissioner every success.
the eu-china summit is taking place in brussels today. in addition to topics such as climate change and trade, the situation of human rights defenders in china, the death penalty and the laogai labour camps are discussed. these are certainly very important topics, but i would like to remind you of another painful issue in china - tibet. it is regrettable that the hoped-for dialogue between the representatives of the dalai lama and the authorities of the prc is taking place, but it is taking place very slowly. the last round of the competition was held in november last year. the meetings are irregular and there is an atmosphere of mistrust. despite the fact that the dalai lama has long since renounced not only tibet's independence but its sovereignty. i want to once again urge the chinese leadership to resolve the tibetan issue through dialogue, not force, not ignoring the dalai lama. the european union would certainly welcome such a dialogue and is ready to contribute, mediate and assist in any way possible.
we all know that the european union's cohesion policy aims to reduce the development gap between the countries and regions of the european union. however, i would like to draw your attention to the fact that this broad programme of regional policy must cover all the regions of europe, and i agree with the view that this policy should not be nationalised. it is necessary to ensure stable and sustainable economic growth and job creation. a strong and well-funded european union regional policy is the foundation for social, economic and territorial cohesion. as always (and as before), greater attention must also be paid to the less developed regions. it is good that the next programming period is planned to allocate financial resources to investments needed to implement projects in cities as well as in suburbs, as cities have a positive impact on the economy of the surrounding rural areas, while not reducing funding for the countryside. in this context, the main aspects of the discussion should be: the scope of the european union's cohesion policy, the reasonableness of the objectives, the link with the 2020 strategy, the implementation of the european union's cohesion policy and the possibility of simplifying procedures.
in writing. in the current economic and social crisis, the european social fund (esf) is one of the european union's main funding instruments to combat high unemployment, poverty and social exclusion. the fund also plays a major role in the areas of education of workers, lifelong vocational training, improvement of employment opportunities and integration of workers into the labour market. i agree that in order to achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy, in particular in the fields of employment and social affairs, it is necessary to strengthen the european social fund and to increase its effectiveness by improving the procedures for its financing. the current system of financing the fund is too complex and it is therefore essential that the commission simplifies and makes more flexible the procedures for financing the structural funds, so that member states can use the european social fund more effectively and efficiently to create new and better jobs. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the european union's financial contribution to the european agricultural guidance and guarantee fund (eaggf) is based on the principle of equal treatment of farmers and consumers. i would also urge the commission to make the european social fund even more effective and targeted in future and to take all possible measures to ensure that eu funds reach directly the people in poverty who need them most. only a more effective and stronger fund could contribute to reducing unemployment and poverty in europe in the future.
mr president, i propose inserting a little qualification about drilling operations in order to avoid any misinterpretations in cases where third countries are also involved. concerning drilling operations, i would like to insert ‘by eu and third countries’. please support this.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this recommendation by the european parliament's committee on employment and social affairs, as the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon and the change in the legal bases obliges the european union to re-examine the rules applicable to third-country nationals. this parliament recommendation concerns a draft regulation which aims to ensure that third-country nationals who are legally resident in the eu and who find themselves in a crossborder situation are subject to the same rules as those for the coordination of social security systems. this parliament recommendation proposes to endorse the council regulation without further delay, as the new regulation would simplify the administration of procedures and ensure equal treatment of third-country nationals legally residing in the eu in the field of social protection. in addition, as regards the fight against illegal immigration in europe, this regulation also clarifies the rights of member states with regard to their powers to decide on the lawful presence of third-country nationals on their territory.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, in addition to spain and denmark, which will be discussed during this plenary session, lithuania is aware of the potential good that the european globalisation adjustment fund can do. in total, lithuania received 2.9 million from the european globalisation adjustment fund for workers in the hard-hit construction and home-tool manufacturing sectors. however, the fund has yet to prove its worth. the european globalisation adjustment fund, which was set up as an emergency response force, is now slow and overwhelmed by bureaucracy. main disadvantage fund mobilization is required. on average, it takes 294 days to approve an application. as the financial times reported this week, more than 37 000 workers across europe are still waiting for the fund's support. of the total amount available to the european globalisation adjustment fund (eur 2 billion), only eur 140 million has been disbursed to date. the distribution of the fund's money is also uneven. only 6% of payments are granted to projects in countries with a gross domestic product below the eu average. the european globalisation adjustment fund needs to be more flexible if it is not to be mired in uncertainty. many member states, including lithuania, are also eligible for other funds that require less co-financing and printing.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, the rapporteur rightly expresses his serious concern about the lack of transparency in the procurement of the european police college. it is hoped that the investigation by the european anti-fraud office will be thorough and honest and will help to bring more clarity to this issue. however, this issue is linked to a much larger problem the frequent lack of integrity and professionalism within police and security bodies across europe. amnesty international and human rights watch have reported a record number of cases across europe of police overstepping their authority and using illegal investigative techniques. in most cases, the authorities failed to investigate and punish the perpetrators in line with international standards, thus increasing the level of impunity. often, victims are made easy targets because of their minority status, whether it be their origin or their political views. a strong police force needs a strong and honest rule of law and legal independence. the european police college has noble objectives to promote common standards of european public order, cross-border cooperation in the fight against crime, and the maintenance of law and order. whether it is in this forum or in another, the most important thing is that these objectives are achieved.
in writing. − this parliament resolution is of particular importance for the abolition of the visa regime for bosnia and herzegovina and albania. the visa-free regime gives effect to one of the fundamental rights of europe - freedom of movement within all member states. it is essential to make every effort to implement visa-free travel for citizens of bosnia and herzegovina and albania as soon as possible. in the last few months, bosnia and herzegovina and albania have made significant progress and important dialogues have been held with albania and bosnia and herzegovina. by granting visa-free travel to these countries, young people could travel, study and train abroad. it would also reduce the risk of greater ethnic and political instability, strengthen political and economic cooperation in the region and increase public support for the eu and the prospect of european integration.
in writing. − the democratic republic of congo must take immediate action to fulfil its obligations in the field of human rights violations. today, most armed movements use rape and violence as a means to achieve military and economic goals. such violence often results in the loss of women's status in society, their ability to care for their children, and even their lives. the civilian population continues to suffer and live in abject poverty. the democratic republic of the congo shall take all feasible measures to prevent any further attacks against civilians and to ensure that accountability for violations of human rights and international humanitarian law is strengthened. at the same time, it is important to promote peace and stability in the region through existing regional instruments and to make greater efforts to develop the economies of the regions. it is essential that the eu council's action plan on gender equality in development cooperation ensures that gender mainstreaming is integrated into all policies.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, i am deeply concerned by the events in the congo, where hundreds if not thousands of women and children have been raped in recent months. i'm stunned as a politician, but even more stunned as a father and a human being. this is terrible. the perpetrators must be identified, prosecuted and punished in accordance with international law. we, the international community, have stood up. but we also camped much closer to home, on our own turf. we are always quick to condemn human rights abuses outside the eu and to demand urgent action. however, when it comes to human rights violations within the eu, the reaction is usually muted. the eu will face a major crisis of confidence if it does not bridge the gap between its honourable condemnation of crimes committed outside the eu and the action it takes in its human rights field. it shouldn't be like this. the adoption of the treaty of lisbon has opened up new opportunities for strengthening human rights, in particular the eu charter of fundamental rights, which is now binding not only on the eu institutions but also on the member states. it is not beyond our power to remedy this human rights vacuum within the union, but we must act now.
in writing. − the death penalty is one of the oldest sanctions for very serious crimes, and it has not yet been abolished in some countries. the death penalty is the most cruel, inhuman and degrading punishment, which violates the right to life enshrined in the universal declaration of human rights. the european parliament calls on the member states to take immediate action to end the practice of the death penalty, which is still practised in 43 countries worldwide. the death penalty is a particular concern for minors and persons with mental or intellectual disabilities. the application of these penalties to the most vulnerable persons should be lifted as soon as possible. it is essential that the authorities of the countries commit themselves to monitoring the handling of death penalty issues in order to bring relevant cases to the attention of the national authorities and to consider the possibility of implementing initiatives on abolition of the death penalty.
in writing. - i support this resolution. in view of the current situation, with a 30% decline in biodiversity over the last 40 years, and with future projections of a tenfold increase in the rate of biodiversity loss by 2050, it is necessary to take decisive action now to conserve biodiversity and promote its sustainable use. the loss of biodiversity is a threat to food security, energy depletion and climate change. to avoid the negative impact of these processes, cooperation between the commission and the member states is necessary in developing a sustainable eu biodiversity strategy, which is aligned with the objectives of combating poverty, sustainable waste management and climate change mitigation. the implementation of the actions and strategies envisaged will inevitably require sufficient funding. public support is likely to be insufficient, and it is therefore necessary to seek to attract private investment and to seek new sources of financing. in addition, the plans drawn up must be taken into account when the new multiannual financial framework is adopted and the necessary funding must be ensured.
in writing. - i supported this resolution. cohesion policy is a crucial part of the eu's policy to reduce disparities in the level of development of europe's regions and to ensure economic, social and territorial cohesion. an effective regional policy has a high added value for the eu as a whole and for its economic growth. the european parliament has repeatedly expressed its view that cohesion policy is currently developing in the context of global challenges with different impacts on the eu regions and that there is a need to substantially improve overall coordination of cohesion policy at eu level. indeed, the complexity and rigidity of the requirements and rules applicable to cohesion policy is one of the main obstacles to the exploitation of the potential of this instrument. in order to ensure that cohesion policy achieves its objectives, it must be sufficiently flexible to be adapted to the specificities of individual regions in order to enable them to overcome specific economic and social difficulties.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because the european union must now foresee how the eu's regional and cohesion policy will be financed and coordinated after 2013. i agree with the european parliament's position that we must plan and allocate eu funds for the structural funds now to ensure that sufficient funds are allocated in the new long-term financial period to ensure the sustainable development of all eu regions. europe is a continent of solidarity and therefore essential to the achievement of the cohesion policy objective of reducing disparities in the level of development of europe's regions and ensuring economic, social and territorial cohesion. i agree with parliament's position that cohesion policy should be more results-oriented and that it should aim at greater efficiency and effectiveness, because only then will it be more convenient and more useful for consumers. this requires a fundamental improvement in administrative procedures, increased financial control, a harmonisation of national and regional procedures, and a greater flexibility and simplification of procedures. the implementation of cohesion and regional policy and the independence and coverage of all european regions are essential for the achievement of the europe 2020 objectives.
in writing. - i support this resolution. a policy for sustainable growth and jobs in europe is essential. the implementation of this policy should be supported by reforms of education, vocational training and retraining programmes to adapt them to the needs of the labour market and by supporting the activities of small and medium-sized enterprises. it is essential that the measures envisaged are implemented in a coordinated and complementary manner, so as to achieve the most effective results. the european social fund, which operates at european union level, plays an important role in the fields of employment, improving working conditions and integrating workers into the labour market. however, support from the structural funds alone is not enough. in order to ensure an efficient and effective system of financing social programmes, it is necessary to combine assistance from the structural funds with other existing programmes, such as the european microfinance facility progress, which provides micro-credit for small businesses and support for start-ups, and other instruments.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution tabled by the committee on employment and social affairs of the european parliament, as it is necessary to strengthen the european social fund (hereinafter referred to as the esf), to increase its effectiveness, especially in times of economic and social crisis, as this fund is one of the main instruments of european union funding for the fight against the kurdish people and social exclusion. improving the procedures for esf funding is also essential, as it would allow member states to use esf funds more easily and effectively to invest in skills, employment, training and retraining of workers in order to create more and better jobs. i would also like to stress the importance of the commission's targeted allocation of eu funds to the european social fund, as this is the only way in which this instrument can directly help people to get into employment, find better jobs, overcome poverty, avoid social exclusion and live a full life. moreover, only a more effective and stronger esf would enable the implementation of the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy in the fields of employment and social affairs and contribute to the reduction of unemployment and poverty in europe.
the rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on budgetary control. − i would like to thank all my fellow rapporteurs whose professionalism has enabled us to reach a political agreement between the institutions of the european union in a short time and to lay a solid foundation for the creation of the european external action service. i am confident that the office will become more effective and will contribute to the high-level shaping of european foreign and security policy and to the strengthening of the european union's role on the world stage. the office shall operate in accordance with the principles of sound and efficient financial management. this year we have allocated an additional €9.5 million to the service, but the real costs will be assessed after the first year of operation, when the european parliament will be provided with a report on all expenditure related to the service's activities. the european parliament also has a responsibility in the functioning of the office to ensure that the office's resources are used efficiently and that the money of the european union's taxpayers creates real added value for the community's policies and its people.
in writing. as we mark world day against poverty and the european year against poverty, unfortunately there are already more than 85 million people living below the poverty line in the european union. although unemployment is the main cause of poverty, many europeans, even those who have a job, struggle with poverty every day and lack the opportunity to enjoy a decent life. it is not normal for people to earn more than they need to support their families. i would like to remind you that economic growth has not in itself reduced poverty and social exclusion, that hardship only increases poverty and that minimum incomes allow the most vulnerable to be protected. the minimum income guarantee is an important and effective means of overcoming poverty, supporting social integration and access to the labour market and enabling people to live in decent conditions. i would like to point out that even in times of crisis, minimum incomes should be seen not as a cost driver but as a key tool in the fight against the crisis, as early investment in poverty alleviation is the most beneficial in reducing long-term public expenditure. in order to fulfil the commitment of the europe 2020 strategy to end the threat of poverty or exclusion for at least 20 million people in the european union, the european commission must therefore take concrete action and propose a european law that ensures a europe-wide minimum income in the fight against poverty.
in writing. − i support this proposal for a regulation which aims to amend the existing directive to make compulsory the collection of data by type of cargo for the compilation of maritime transport statistics. currently, this data is collected on a voluntary basis in 18 member states. in addition, data by type of cargo are already compulsory for the statistics on road, rail and inland waterway transport. the data collected will allow for comparison of transport by type, allow for the integration of different modes of transport in a single transport system and modernise the logistics system for freight transport. it is expected that the data collection will not impose an additional burden on respondents, as member states would collect data using existing national data sources (e.g. customs records).
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, this issue of systematic statistical data collection is of particular importance to us. in lithuania, maritime transport accounts for only a small percentage of total freight transport, so much more can be achieved in this area. in addition, our coastal tourism sector also has a high growth potential. by modernising freight transport logistics and promoting transport diversity, i.e. combining different modes of transport in a single transport system, we have the opportunity to increase the well-being of the baltic. this is crucial to maintain the competitiveness of european ports such as the lithuanian port of klaipeda in the future. closer cooperation between the baltic states will bring the baltic states closer together and make our region's markets more harmonious and accessible to the whole of the european union. the baltic states are currently largely isolated from the european transport network. the traffic between the baltic states is expected to double by 2020 and the lack of adequate infrastructure and accessibility needs to be addressed. above all, we must bear in mind that data collection does not impose an additional burden on member states. we have nothing to lose.
in writing. − i am pleased that the european parliament has initiated this important report on women's precarious work. women are the most likely to be in precarious employment, often without even the minimum social standards required for workers. women tend to work in low-paid jobs where they can combine work with family life, which forces them to give up social guarantees and to accept unfavourable working conditions. social protection is a key element in the balance between labour market flexibility and employment guarantees and it is therefore essential to encourage member states and social partners to help harmonise the rules on working conditions in legislation and contracts in order to address these problems. member states must reduce the double burden of women's work, which is one of the reasons why women are predominantly in unsecured work. it is essential to ensure equal access for all workers to social services and benefits, including maternity leave, healthcare, old-age pension, and to ensure the right to education and training, irrespective of their conditions of employment.
the rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on constitutional affairs. - i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on having found a balanced position. i believe that the proposal for the 2011 budget, which we will be voting on tomorrow, strikes the right balance between budgetary savings and economic stimulus and is in line with the priorities of the european parliament as expressed on several occasions. i understand the desire of member states to cut spending in an effort to reduce their budget deficits as much as possible, but many of the cuts proposed by the council are unjustified. we must not forget that the budget of the european union has other objectives. the european union budget is investing in our future. how we handle this crisis will depend on how well we allocate and use it. energy, innovation, research, education, mobility are the foundation of our economic growth, the creation of new jobs. the continuity of effective programmes already in place must therefore be ensured, as well as the adequate funding of new policies introduced by the lisbon treaty. i call on my colleague to approve the draft budget.

vice-president of the commission. − as the honourable member pointed out, regulation (ec) no 1371/2007 on rail passenger rights, regulation (ec) no 261/2004 on air passenger rights – and let me also add regulation (ec) no 1107/2006 on the rights of disabled persons and persons with reduced mobility when travelling by air – recognise certain minimum, common rights for citizens when they travel by rail or by air. the correct application of these regulations must be ensured by the national enforcement bodies designated in the member states. these bodies in particular have to take all reasonable measures to ensure that the rights of passengers are fully respected. the commission’s task is to ensure that eu law is correctly implemented and enforced by the member states by constantly monitoring their activity. national enforcement bodies have no legal obligation to report under the sole terms of the regulations. they answer the commission’s request in line with the general principle of loyal cooperation between the member states and the eu institutions enshrined in the treaty. concerning the volcanic ash crisis, the commission has reacted quickly and has done its utmost to ensure that, in accordance with the conclusions of the extraordinary council held on 4 may 2010, the rules on air passenger rights are applied and enforced in a uniform and harmonised way everywhere in the eu. in order to reach this aim, the commission has prepared and agreed informal interpretative guidelines with national authorities and has kept close contacts with them and all stakeholders.

vice-president of the commission. − first of all buses. we have strict regulations regulating the technical standards concerning buses, and especially school buses. i have not heard of any particular concerns about problems with school buses as such. we will of course follow the situation, and we propose – coming to the last question – to follow strictly the rules that all children must be properly accommodated and that seat belts should be used. i have not, however, received any signals concerning this problem of road safety that school buses is an issue that is badly handled. 30 000 people have died and of course we are all unhappy about this. every casualty is a tragedy, but i definitely do not agree that this road safety action plan has been a failure. there has been a 40% reduction in casualties in europe. in my own country, the reduction was fourfold over the last 15 years. we followed the same route. for those of you interested in our plans, just before the summer break we adopted policy guidelines which are the next action plan for road safety and we have the same aim – to reduce casualties by half. a lot of measures which can help to achieve this goal are described, including training the people most vulnerable in traffic. the most vulnerable are always elderly people, pedestrians, cyclists and also motorcyclists, who obviously require special attention because motorcyclists are the only category where casualties have risen – the other categories have all come down.
vice-president of the commission. − probably the numbers are a little different on my papers, but of course i can happily reply to mr paleckis’s question. maritime transport in the baltic sea has increased significantly over recent years, as a result of rapid economic growth. at the same time, the european union has one of the most advanced regulatory frameworks on maritime safety in the world today. the third maritime safety package adopted by parliament and the council in 2009 is a significant improvement of the eu's vessel traffic monitoring and information system. it covers all the seas surrounding europe and introduces strict inspections on ships visiting eu ports, also known as port state control. it contains specific measures for the baltic sea, such as requirements for safe winter navigation and reinforced ice standards for ships. the commission works closely with all the eu baltic states to ensure the smooth implementation of these new measures. the european maritime safety agency (emsa) also provides technical support. if an accident were to happen, emsa has standby pollution response vessels and equipment permanently located in the baltic. this is in addition to the pollution response measures already put in place by the baltic states. the commission also maintains a continuous dialogue with the russian federation to ensure that safety rules are applied coherently in the baltic sea. in this framework, the commission is about to launch a pilot project with the russian administration on monitoring maritime traffic in the baltic sea. a recently adopted eu strategy in the baltic sea region also addresses safety and security. i would, of course, like to respond to this interesting proposal to reduce maritime transport in the baltic sea, but any restriction on maritime transport before its introduction has to be carefully considered and be in line with the united nations convention on the law of the seas and with other internationally agreed conventions. the international maritime organisation already recognises the baltic as a particularly sensitive sea area and this status brings with it particular maritime traffic requirements to reduce the risk of accidents and to protect the marine environment. i would also like to point out that maritime transport is a relatively environmentally-friendly mode of transport. additional restrictions to commercial transport in the baltic sea could potentially promote other less environmentally-friendly modes of transport with increased emissions as a consequence.
i was glad that the commissioner mentioned the cooperation with russia. as regards the standards for environmental improvements for ships, which countries in your opinion are leading in this area and which are not among the best?
in writing. the second major ecological disaster in the last six months in a particular part of our planet the oil spill in the gulf of mexico in april, now the red mud in hungary. although different in nature, both disasters have something in common: they are, or at least their scale is, essentially avoidable through a more responsible approach to the environment. unfortunately, in many eu countries (and not just in some), environmental protection and environmental requirements are often not the first priority when designing, constructing and operating facilities with a significant environmental impact. so i'm sure that each of us, if we look around, we'll find a number of objects that sooner or later could be ecological disaster of a greater or lesser scale. in many places, environmental requirements are neglected (intentionally or through ignorance), construction is allowed to take place in the vicinity of residential areas, environmental impact assessments are carried out only superficially, without any real consultation with the residents, and the results are always in the client's favour. the exploitation of such facilities is also often a source of complaints from the local population. i call on both the european commission and the member states to open their eyes once again and, seeing the real situation, to take action to initiate legislative amendments to tighten controls on the enforcement of environmental requirements and the liability for non-compliance with such requirements, and to ensure independent environmental impact assessment.
i would like to say a few words about the work of the special crisis committee. he has indeed done a great deal of work and has given good recommendations and suggestions. of course, it would be ideal if all of them could find their place in the documents drawn up by the commission. it is essential that the commission does not forget the basic and most important points. one of them is to establish a regulatory and supervisory framework covering all financial markets, financial instruments and financial institutions. the commission should focus its action on job creation and link it to measures to combat poverty and social exclusion. all efforts in this direction must be directed primarily towards the employment of young people. the crisis requires urgent infrastructure solutions in the sectors of renewable energy, green energy, energy-efficient transport, buildings and the european energy grid. it would be very nice if, exactly one year after today's meeting, the commission could come and say: 'the provisions of your proposed resolution did not remain on paper, but we have implemented these specific proposals of the special crisis committee and they have already had a concrete effect.
the financial, economic and social crisis has affected every european, increasing social inequalities and deepening poverty. unfortunately, the difficult year has made even more evident the ineffectiveness and poor coordination of the various national economic recovery plans. moreover, some member states have not even been able to put in place genuine national economic recovery plans, including measures to boost growth and employment, because they have only reduced public spending even further during the difficult year, raising taxes to reduce public debt. unfortunately, in some member states this is at the expense of the ordinary people. i would also like to point out that the crisis has highlighted social inequalities between different social groups, for example, women are at a much higher risk of falling below the poverty line than men. the european union must now learn the lessons of this crisis and, in coordination with the member states, implement its initiatives in all areas.
in writing. − this proposal has achieved the first constructive framework agreement. the increased powers of the european parliament since the treaty of lisbon are of great importance for further cooperation with the european commission and for their future relationship with regard to the implementation of further agreements. this document therefore sets out further guidelines for cooperation between the two institutions. the european parliament will be able to develop a close dialogue with the commission on the commission's work programme and on international agreements. parliament will have the right to receive confidential documents. parliament will be kept informed of the progress of international negotiations and will also be able to act as an expert to make proposals to the commission on these issues. the framework agreement also provides for detailed parliamentary scrutiny, provisions on the election of the president of the commission and the commission itself, and on its composition, possible changes and the re-allocation of responsibilities. parliament is committed to greater transparency and better cooperation with the other institutions. i am pleased that closer cooperation between these two institutions will help member states to transpose european union legislation into national law as quickly and efficiently as possible.
in writing. − this new framework agreement for relations has the potential to consolidate the achievements of the lisbon treaty it could be a significant breakthrough. the amendments to improve legal procedures and strengthen parliamentary oversight are particularly significant. i support all amendments which contribute to improving the flow of information and to promoting the efficiency of relations between the european parliament and the european commission. it is important to ensure that this institutional partnership has as few bureaucratic knots as possible. the new framework agreement for relations governs the 'special partnership' between parliament and the commission. we must remember that the most special partnership is the one between the european union and its citizens. the european union must do more to find a common language with its citizens and to prove its importance in their daily lives. the rapporteur rightly pointed out that this agreement represents a 'new interinstitutional balance', i.e. a sound compromise. but there are some issues on which the european union cannot compromise - these are fundamental human rights and shipping. more power means more responsibility. it is one thing to talk about shared values, but it is quite another to implement and defend them. until this is achieved, the various branches of the european union's institutional framework will not be able to realise their full potential. the european union must be credible if it is to be a cohesive force.
in writing. − in negotiations with representatives of the eu council and the european commission, parliament and in particular the socialist and democratic group negotiators succeeded in ensuring that the budget of the new eu diplomatic service is used in a more transparent manner. parliament will approve its budget implementation on an annual basis, and the commission will have to keep the european parliament regularly informed of its expenditure. i voted in favour of this report because it emphasises that the recruitment of nationals of the member states of the union should ensure the widest possible geographical coverage and adequate and meaningful representation of nationals of all member states. i agree with the rapporteur that the aim must be to ensure that staff are selected on the basis of their abilities and that gender equality is taken into account. it is important that the service, which will be operational from 1 december, is rapidly effective and that it pursues first the eu's interests and, where necessary, national interests.
in writing. − i am pleased that after a long debate the european parliament has today adopted a very important directive. under this new directive, the duration of maternity leave will be increased from 14 to 20 weeks, with full pay. sharing family responsibilities is now essential to resolve demographic problems arising from low birth rates and ageing populations as quickly as possible. it is therefore essential that the directive provides for the right of men to take at least two weeks of paternity leave. it is also the child's inalienable right to maintain contact with both parents. this proposal will create a better balance in families and improve integration into the labour market. parliament has demonstrated its ability to achieve the europe 2020 goals of better reconciling work and family life for economic growth, prosperity, competitiveness and gender equality. i very much hope that this directive adopted by parliament will be adopted by the council in the near future.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, women should not be punished for choosing to have children. this is not only an ethical but also a strategic issue the eu is currently experiencing demographic changes due to low birth rates and an increasing number of older people. especially in these difficult times, we cannot keep women out of the labour market. we need more women in the workforce if the eu is to become more competitive on a global scale. it is time to challenge the stereotypes that have become entrenched in society. women in employment are often seen as high-risk or 'trailblazing' workers. it is therefore important that the new rules on holidays set out in this report help to break down these stereotypes. we should also do more to help women who are marginalized. eu statistics show that single mothers are the most at risk of poverty in lithuania. the risk of poverty for working people in this group is 24%. the union has come a long way towards legally guaranteeing equality between men and women. but we need to go further and make theoretical gender equality a reality, a concrete gender equality that applies in everyday life.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report and i am pleased that the european parliament and the council have managed to reach this agreement, which is of great importance for small businesses in particular. while smes are the backbone of the union's competitiveness and the largest creator of jobs, the crisis has made it very clear that they are also the most vulnerable and that member states' policies are not particularly conducive to the promotion and development of smes, as eu legislation such as the sme act is not fully implemented and applied. many companies went bankrupt during the crisis, which is a huge loss. i am therefore very pleased that this is a small but significant step for small businesses in creating clarity on payment settlement deadlines. we will start to create a clear and understandable environment for him and contribute to the creation of a business culture.
in writing. − i voted for this legislation, we believe that a better regulated mutual settlement system is beneficial for small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) and for the business culture in general. i think that one of the provisions proposed in this document is very advanced. this is encouraging the publication of lists of quick-selling entities. such measures would not only encourage firms (particularly smes) to settle their accounts with each other in a timely manner, thereby reducing the risk of liquidity problems, but would also increase the credibility of such firms and, in turn, their competitiveness.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because, despite the many declarations on the fight against poverty, the population of europe remains poor, social inequalities are increasing and the number of workers experiencing poverty is increasing. the european union must take more active measures to combat poverty and social exclusion, with particular attention to people in casual employment, the unemployed, families, the elderly, women, single mothers, socially dependent children, the sick or disabled. one of the key tools in the fight against poverty is a minimum income to help these people to get out of poverty and to guarantee their right to a life of dignity. i would point out that the objective of a minimum income as a means of combating poverty will only be achieved if member states take concrete steps to guarantee a minimum income and implement national programmes to combat poverty. in addition, in some member states minimum income systems are not linked to relative poverty, and it is therefore necessary for the european commission to take into account good and bad practices when evaluating national action plans. thus, a minimum income - a key component of social protection - is clearly important to ensure the protection and equal opportunities of people in poverty in society.
in writing. - colleagues, despite all the claims about poverty reduction, social inequalities have increased about 85 million eu citizens are at risk of poverty. this is a very big problem for lithuania, because 20 percent of our population is at risk of poverty. we need robust employment policies to boost growth and competitiveness in the european social market economy, to prevent macroeconomic imbalances and to ensure social inclusion. but this is not enough to combat unemployment. having a job does not protect you from poverty either. the increase in temporary jobs and low wages means that the percentage of working people at risk of poverty is increasing. according to eu reports, more than 20% of people in lithuania working full-time earn less than 60% of median income, compared to the eu average of around 14%. increasing the minimum monthly wage would help reduce poverty but would not ensure a society free of discrimination. most young people leave lithuania not only because of lack of money and jobs (in the past year, youth unemployment in lithuania reached almost 30%) but also because they feel abandoned and powerless to face decisions that affect their daily lives. that has to change.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the current financial crisis, which has affected the economic and financial sectors, has led to a global economic and social crisis in which the european population continues to suffer poverty, social inequalities are becoming more pronounced and the number of workers in poverty is increasing. i would like to emphasise that the difficult year was further aggravated by the lack of coordination between the various national recovery plans, since it is very likely that coordination at european union level would have a greater impact than that which can be achieved mainly by programmes at national level. the european union has committed itself to combating unemployment and increasing employment, reducing poverty and social exclusion under the europe 2020 strategy, but this strategy must be part of a joint effort to manage the crisis and to plan strategically for the post-crisis period. i share the parliament's position that this strategic task of the european union should also be carried out in close cooperation with national parliaments, the social partners and civil society, and with greater involvement of the european parliament.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, europe has not only been a victim of the financial and social crisis. we're also experiencing a massive crisis of public trust. we need to restore public confidence in our financial and political institutions, both in lithuania and across europe, and to build a viable and sustainable financial system that protects against future crises. we need a transparent, multi-level regulatory mechanism based on sound morals that serves the wider public. the financial crisis has hit lithuania hard in 2009, our economy contracted by 15%. regional specificities and the uneven impact of the crisis should be taken into account in the design of the exit policy. i am pleased that the special committee is emphasising the importance of cohesion instruments, which are important in providing assistance to the eu regions that need it most. they can help us to overcome the effects of the crisis by supporting the necessary investments in infrastructure, business and job creation. the success of recovery also depends heavily on the success of the eu 2020 strategy. it is important that any long-term eu investment strategy is linked to preserving competitiveness and strengthening the internal market, one of the main engines of european growth.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. the current economic, financial and social crisis has shown that the current model of economic governance in the union has not worked as effectively as intended. in recent years, the convergence process in the member states has not been sufficiently advanced, and macroeconomic and fiscal imbalances have persisted or even increased over the past decade. the supervisory framework was too weak and the rules of the stability and growth pact, in particular the preventive provisions, were not sufficiently strictly adhered to. i agree with the suggestions in the document that better coordination with the member states and coordination between member states should be pursued, in particular to prevent a repetition of the situation that has recently occurred. it is particularly important that member states fully apply the rules and decisions agreed at eu level, such as the rules and measures of the stability and growth pact. the focus must be on long-term sustainable growth, creating quality jobs, rather than short-term profits, which seriously undermine the financial stability of european markets.
in writing. − colleagues, the current rules of the stability and growth pact and the weak implementation framework have not sufficiently ensured sound fiscal and macroeconomic policies. the recommendations in this report are a good starting point. the rapporteur rightly notes the need for structural reforms in relation to social policy and labour market integration, fiscal incentives for small and medium-sized enterprises. the long-term deficit reduction process should be combined with other economic stimulus efforts, such as improved investment conditions and an improved internal market with increased competitiveness. i am also pleased that the rapporteur recognises that any new measures proposed should not disproportionately affect the most vulnerable member states, in particular the baltic states. this would hamper our economic growth and cohesion efforts. in the past year, the interest in the euro has increased somewhat in non-euro area member states, including lithuania. it is therefore important that we recognise that the decisions taken in the first half of the year to ensure the stability of the euro are temporary and must be backed up by a better economic governance system at eu level.

president of the council. - ladies and gentlemen, the initiative is excellent and, moreover, contacts between ministers and general education officials show us how different the situations are from one country of the union to another because there are sometimes very specific, very selective programmes in some of our countries to avoid those who drop out of school being marginalised. so this map is particularly important. it will take time because assessing this situation in 27 member states is not easy. i think that work on school drop-outs is beginning under the belgian presidency and that the following presidencies, the hungarian one in particular, will certainly be able to initiate a draft recommendation, not only to reduce the school drop-out rate but also to draw conclusions on this aspect of the map of the 27 member states, on what happens to students who drop out of school when they are particularly marginalised and are not taken up, as you indicate, by a more technical education system.

president of the council. - i would like to say that i agree with both of the interventions, which link, it is true, school drop-out to poverty and social exclusion. it seems to me obvious that a number of our children, depending on their family status, depending on the financial status of their parents, are not favoured in relation to their schooling and that the economic crisis has obviously amplified this phenomenon very seriously. that said, both the council and the commission have understood this well, since we have adopted, as you know, together, this 2020 strategy which combines economic development, research and development, the quality of our education but also the fight against poverty. all these elements form a whole which, it seems to me, allows a coherent answer to be given. i want to go back a little bit to the key competences because it's an important topic. the conclusions which the belgian presidency will submit to the november council for adoption will address a number of objectives. i'll give you a quick list of them: the implementation of national strategies to improve student performance in reading, math, science. it is also an analysis of the effectiveness of current strategies at national level in order to have a source of data which can feed into decision-making, as was said earlier. but it also means creating pilot projects between member states which are voluntary and aimed at improving the basic skills of young europeans. it is also, why not, a more efficient use of the instruments available and relevant, such as those which are part of the open method of coordination, the programme for education, lifelong learning or the seventh framework programme for research and technological development. these are a number of objectives which we will pursue under the belgian presidency and which will certainly be taken up by the subsequent presidencies, and in particular the hungarian presidency.
president of the council. − the european union has been working for years towards the total elimination of antipersonnel landmines in order to help solve the enormous humanitarian and development problems caused by them. as early as may 1995, by council decision 95/170/cfsp, the european union adopted its first ever joint action to help combat the use and proliferation of antipersonnel landmines throughout the world. the european union was therefore the first to act in the international community in november 1997 following the conclusion of the ottawa convention on the prohibition of the use, stockpiling, production and transfer of antipersonnel mines and on their destruction. on 18 september, the council adopted a new joint action to pursue the union's political efforts towards the total elimination of anti-personnel landmines and, more specifically, to establish a common moratorium on the transfer and production of anti-personnel mines and to enable a multi-dimensional contribution within the union to the demining effort and other related activities. the european union has since been promoting these commitments, which have been based on the european security strategy since 2003. in june 2008, a new joint action was adopted in support of the universalisation of the ottawa convention. it is also intended to provide specific assistance to states parties to the convention in the implementation of the provisions of the convention, in particular with regard to demining, assistance to victims and stockpile destruction. and although the number of states parties to the convention has increased considerably, and the number of victims of antipersonnel landmines is decreasing, the need for assistance and tangible improvement in the lives of victims remains. the european union participated in the development of the 2010-14 action plan which it fully supports , adopted in cartagena, colombia, at the second review conference of the convention from 29 november to 4 december 2009. a new council decision is currently being prepared to specifically support the action plan and its victim assistance component. in parallel with the council's more political action, the assistance programmes managed by the commission under development aid and neighbourhood policy with the national programmes of the member states have increased the european union's contribution to mine clearance and victim assistance to €1.8 billion, half the global contribution.
president of the council. - you're right, we spent 1.5 billion, two thousand people are still victims per month. the european union cannot do it alone - it is already doing half the work - all that needs to be done in this area. i think i've made it clear how much we're aware of the human side of this problem. unfortunately, we cannot go much further in the matter of decision-making. only tangible and visible progress in disarmament is likely to be possible, and we are particularly concerned with the issue of banning anti-personnel mines.
president of the council. - the two questions are very complementary and i was going to answer the first on the basis of the second's question. it is true that any research programme to locate antipersonnel mines anywhere in the world would be welcome. this requires close cooperation with the united nations and, above all, the ability to reach agreement within the un bodies and with all those who make up those bodies. this is a long-term task because it is necessary to convince others who do not necessarily agree with the resources to be invested in the development of this kind of research programme.
president of the council. - ladies and gentlemen, the fight against violence against women is a common objective of the european parliament and the council. as you know, our two institutions have been working on this for several years in many different contexts. i would remind you of the resolution you adopted last year when we celebrated the tenth anniversary of the un resolution establishing 25 november as the international day for the elimination of violence against women. in this resolution you called on the commission to come forward with a proposal for a more coherent and targeted community policy and urged the member states to create a coherent system for collecting data. as the honourable member has pointed out, last march the council called on the commission to devise a european strategy to prevent and combat violence against women. one of the priorities identified by the council in this regard is to prepare the creation of a european observatory on violence against women, building on existing structures. the commission is currently drawing up a new strategy on violence against women, which will also include the creation of this observatory, but the content of which is not yet known. we hope to receive this strategy within the next year. it is therefore very likely that a separate proposal will subsequently be necessary for the establishment of this observatory. the council can of course only act as legislator on the basis of a proposal, as you know, from the commission. such a proposal will be duly examined by the council, acting as a co-legislator with the european parliament. it is obviously a little premature at this stage to make any assumptions about the possible definition of the objectives of a future observatory on violence against women and, above all, about the date of its entry into operation.
thank you very much, mr minister, for your answer. however, it seems that there is hope that one day such a centre will be established, which will collect reliable data on the unfortunately still existing violence against women. however, i would like to ask whether you think that the data available in the member states are sufficient to make the fight against violence against women a real priority. i believe that we do not yet have such reliable data, and that is one of the reasons why there are still quite a number of different types of violence in the european union.
