i voted in favour of the resolution on an integrated maritime policy because the maritime sector is vital and i want to leave clean and safe seas for future generations. the geostrategic importance of the seas has increased considerably. we need to re-adapt the seas and their resources and coordinate our actions, and an integrated maritime policy is important in that it will bring together all the maritime policy areas: maritime transport, fisheries, aquaculture, energy, maritime safety, marine protection and research, as well as tourism. i would like to draw particular attention to the economic and social importance of the european union's 320 000 km of coastline and the fact that one third of the citizens of the european union live on the coast and this number is increasing. i believe that the seas should continue to be the engine of economic growth.
mr president, i would like to commend the rapporteur on trade with latin america for this eventually well-balanced and timely report. the deepening of trade relations with the latin american countries is already under way, as the european union has recently concluded a free trade agreement with two andean countries, colombia and peru. recently, we finished the longest-standing dispute in the wto with latin american countries on trade in bananas. we will soon start negotiating a milestone free trade agreement with all of the mercosur countries with which we conduct more than eur 62 billion of trade in goods. the eu’s investment stock in the mercosur region amounts to eur 167 billion a year. these numbers are only set to grow in the coming years. the conclusion of such an agreement, along with the upcoming free trade negotiations with japan and india, will undoubtedly be one of the most significant steps in implementing the eu’s strategy of becoming the most important trade player in the world.
there is no doubt that the relationship between parents and children during the first months of life is of particular importance, and the new european union directive will ensure that mothers have the right and the opportunity to take care of their children for longer periods without fear of losing their income and jobs. the situation varies from country to country. for example, in my country lithuania, maternity leave plus parental leave lasts up to two years, but other countries have a minimum number of maternity leave, so i think that the minimum threshold we set yesterday is a very important achievement. parental leave and leave for adoption are also welcome. although family policy is, as we say, a matter for the member states, we are talking here more about the protection of workers and some incentives for parents to reconcile family and professional life.
in writing. − conflicts in haiti, kosovo, iraq, and more recently in afghanistan have shed light on the crucial role that small arms and light weapons play in terrorism and organized crime, and in igniting new rounds of bloodshed after formal cease-fire. it is important that the community adopts a truly comprehensive approach to the problem of salw proliferation by targeting all levels of the illicit salw trade chain. given that 70% of global salw stockpile is in civilian hands, eu should recognize and support the indispensible efforts of civil society in monitoring salw trade and production at the local level and in raising public awareness. in addition, the salw issue reveals the multidimensional nature of 21st century conflicts in general, and thereby the need for a stronger focus in pre- and post-crisis capacity building. i voted in favour of this report because i believe that eu should strive to hone its ability to manage crisis in the most innovative, efficient and cost-effective manner. only then would eu be able to lead the world as a foremost global civilian power.
in writing. - i have endorsed this document because i believe that the consumer must know who the manufacturer of the product of choice is. the information, as stated in the adopted document, ensures security and consumer safety and information must be the priority principles. the member states of the european union are very often only a link here the raw material obtained in a third country is used to make a product, so it is difficult to tell who is the real producer. the indication of the country of origin is also necessary to enable small producers, who are often in competition with large companies that imitate the original, to be protected. it is also necessary to seek the introduction of common rules for the labelling of the environmental standards with which the product is produced.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this document because i believe that an integrated maritime policy is essential to ensure an adequate level of environmental protection. one of the key principles is the coordination of different policies and the need to ensure compliance with the principle of ecosystem protection enshrined in the eu marine strategy framework directive. in particular, i support the measures for the coordination of maritime surveillance and control, protection of the marine environment and research mentioned in the document. on behalf of lithuania, a country in the baltic sea region, i am pleased that this document focuses on the broadest possible range of scientific research to identify and assess the current state of the seas. the baltic sea is one of the most economically damaged seas, one of the most polluted seas in the world. as the disaster of the oil spill in the gulf of mexico demonstrated, a fragile marine ecosystem can be damaged in an instant. i therefore particularly support the call in the report for member states to take more active action by providing for a multi-faceted marine protection strategy.
mr president, the eu-us agenda is as full as ever and both parties should use the opportunity to advance common positions on issues such as financial regulation, climate change, the deepening of trade ties, nuclear proliferation and the fight against terrorism. the economic recovery, however, has to be issue number one on the agenda. the transatlantic economic relationship is vital to global prosperity as the bilateral trade of our two economies represents almost usd 4.3 trillion. our economic partnership is a key driver of global economic prosperity and represents the largest, most integrated and longest lasting economic relationship in the world. together our economies account for half of the global economy. therefore we need to devise common strategies on the further actions we are going to take to ensure stable recovery from the crisis, including regulating the financial markets, the stimulus packages, and strategies to curb currency manipulation of other major global economies. on the other hand, we should reconsider whether holding biannual eu-us summits is a good idea. president obama’s decision not to attend the previous summit in madrid earlier this year exposed the excessive eu synergy. by insisting on frequent bilateral summits we risk devaluing them to the extent that they will become irrelevant.
in writing. following president barack obama's election victory two years ago, the eu and the us have become increasingly aligned, particularly on disarmament, the fight against climate change and the suppression of international conflicts. this is a big capital, this line needs to be continued. unfortunately, some of the common transatlantic goals have not yet been achieved. a large part of the american public is opposed to action on climate change. an ambitious bill to curb greenhouse gas emissions is still stuck in the senate. no progress on the eu-wide issue of abolition of the death penalty. the eu-us summit will take place in lisbon next month. i hope that the eu will be able to convince its partners in the us to continue to make progress towards the common objectives listed. it is also increasingly important to include russia in the transatlantic dialogue.
member of the commission. − madam president, the terrorist threat is still there and we need to address it. we have lots of tools to do so, but changing and sharing of information is key to doing this. we have pnr agreements. i already have seen strong evidence that pnr is essential to identify and to prevent terrorist attacks. we will make sure that this evidence is provided to the european parliament with concrete examples both from the three countries we are discussing and from the subsequent eu pnr. this is essential to enable you to assess this properly and to discuss it with your voters. we should exchange pnr, but that should not be done without rules. we need to have the relevant information, to be able to join the dots and to have clear rules. we need to define the scope, the retention times, to have a high level of data protection and the possibility of redress for the individual. we need to have clear rules on third-country transfer and we need to be proportionate. all this is in the communication from the commission and i am very happy to see that, although the details might vary, your communication is very much in line with this. these are the issues that will be subject to negotiations with our three partners; they will be difficult, yet ambitious on our part. my goal is to do this together with the council and with the eu’s other three institutions, so that the institutions can speak with one voice and be a clear partner in this. from the beginning i have tried to involve parliament and to engage in an open and transparent way with it, with its relevant committees, the rapporteurs, the shadow rapporteurs, the coordinators and so on, and will continue to do so. i undertake that, at all stages of the negotiations, once they have begun, i will keep you fully and immediately informed, to have an open discussion and to listen to your views. although the commission will lead the negotiations, i am willing to discuss with you and to inform you and have good cooperation with you on this.
acting president of the council. - mr president, i would like to begin by saying that as minister of the interior i know that we have been able to prevent possible attacks all too often by exchanging information and interpreting it. that is why this debate and the conclusion of a proper pnr agreement are so important. i know that the commission has a very difficult task once the mandates have been approved by the council; it will have to start effective negotiations and strike a balance between what we all want here and what we all agree on, namely the protection of personal data. but on the other hand, there is also a clear tendency here in this house to say: we need a pnr, we often need information, precisely to protect us against, for example, terrorist attacks. i am glad that the european parliament, the council and the commission are on the same page and have the same balance in mind: a balance between data protection and security for which we are all responsible. i think we're at the point where we're going to approve the mandates. the difficult task of effective negotiation follows. we must ensure that we do not stray too far from our mandate and that we always maintain that balance. i should like to address a number of specific questions, such as the comment on the sunset clause: it is important to point out that this mandate is for seven years, that it is evaluated after four years and that an extension, if the agreement is reached and accepted here, will only be possible after a debate here in parliament. which i think is logical. it's not a real sunset clause, but it does tend very strongly towards it, because of the deadline, because of the evaluation after four years. the council's conclusions also rightly state that we attach great importance to articles 7 and 8 of the charter of fundamental rights of the eu. this has also been commented on. we have to keep this going. of course, it is in the interests of all of us, and that is also stated in the mandate, that there should be an independent body where individuals can lodge a complaint if they find that their data has been used improperly or for other purposes. finally, and this will be a very difficult point, the question of profiling, where we all want a risk analysis. because it's based on the data you collect that you can make risk analyses and then make the right decisions. on the other hand, it is of course absolutely necessary to avoid stigmatising certain ethnic groups, even though a group here may have advocated this. i think we absolutely do not want ethnic stigmatization. i also think that is one of the fundamental rights that we all have here as citizens in europe, and i want to make sure that we do not end up in such a situation, especially as president of the council.
in writing. parliament seeks to strengthen the procedure for the transfer of passenger data records to third parties. particular attention should be paid to the protection of passengers' personal data. the data provided by passengers may be used by law enforcement authorities for the investigation of crimes and risk assessment. the resolution stresses that the right to information and the right to privacy must be respected. parliament also wants the transfer of data to comply with european data protection standards. it is essential to provide legally binding standards for the protection of personal data, which ensure the protection of fundamental human rights and freedoms. the application of these standards must be the responsibility of independent authorities on both sides of the atlantic. parliament endorses the commission recommendation to open negotiations on an agreement between the european union and the united states on the protection of personal data in connection with the transfer and processing of such data for the purposes of the prevention, investigation, detection or prosecution of criminal offences, including terrorism. in order to ensure effective cooperation between the institutions, the commission should inform the european parliament at all stages of the negotiations on the protection of personal data.
thank you, madam president. first of all, i would also like to thank the representatives of the european commission and the rapporteurs for a very important document. the financial and economic crisis has highlighted the shortcomings in the functioning and control of our financial system. legislation is already being adopted in europe to create a robust framework at european union level to monitor and assess the activities of financial institutions and, if necessary, to make appropriate recommendations and take appropriate action. as regards alternative funds, they should also be subject to supervisory regimes. if we are to achieve greater and better stability in the financial system and better protection for our investors, that is, our citizens, then the activities of such funds must also be controlled and supervised at european union level. the rules must be the same for all those involved, and we must not leave any loopholes for uncontrolled activity. we must learn from past mistakes and not repeat them. i think that not only europe but the whole world is learning from them.
the implementation of innovation policy and technological achievements should become a decisive factor not only for the implementation of the eu 2020 strategy but also for the future growth and competitiveness of the union itself. the implementation of virtually all eu policies, such as energy efficiency, a cleaner economy, reducing the negative impact of climate change, ensuring social well-being and creating jobs, will depend directly on the effectiveness of innovation policy. europe needs an integrated innovation policy, which can only succeed if effective and coordinated cooperation is ensured at regional, national and european level. i believe that the european innovation partnership, which the commission is launching, is a major step towards a more and better coordinated innovation policy, which would allow for significantly better competitiveness performance and contribute to faster progress for the community as a whole in the future.
mr president, i fully support what has been said by our rapporteurs, mr díaz de mera and mr kovatchev. taiwan is a developed country with high technical and administrative standards. it certainly meets the highest standards of safety and security for control and customs procedures. i would therefore like to urge our colleagues to go ahead with the proposal to grant taiwan its long-overdue visa waiver status. taiwan is also a member of the world trade organisation as a single customs territory. we should not stop here – we should encourage taiwan’s accession to other international organisations as well, such as the international civil aviation organisation or the united nations framework convention on climate change. taiwan is a country that deserves such recognition, as it is an important partner of the european union in the east asian region. ties are set to grow, to the advantage of both parties. we need to look forward to the intensification of ties with taiwan in the future. one of the possible developments that we should consider is the negotiation of a more comprehensive trade regime with taiwan, especially now that it has signed the milestone economic co-operation framework agreement. if we move in this direction, the visa-free regime is an essential element in deepening relations between the eu and taiwan.
the number of older people in europe whose only source of income is a pension is increasing rapidly. people who have worked hard and honestly all their lives, raised children, and paid taxes to the government expect to live a long, prosperous life. sadly, not all such legitimate expectations are destined to be fulfilled. europeans in particular felt this during the economic crisis. as pensions kept rising, so did the security of savings for old age, and so did the number of older people who had to support their children who had lost their jobs. and in some countries, like my country, lithuania, pensioners have been hurt by the authorities, too, by reducing pensions and social benefits. there is a lot of discussion about the new commission proposal to consider the possibility of delaying retirement age. but what worries me most is that no solutions are being put forward to ensure the employment of older people. without the possibility of retirement, millions of unemployed people of retirement age may find themselves trapped in poverty. and the extent to which people's health will allow them to work longer is underestimated. it is also necessary to take into account that women are more likely than men to work in low-paid jobs because of childbirth and motherhood, care for children, disabled family members, loss of insurance guarantees and, ultimately, lower pensions. i therefore urge the commission and the council, in the light of the rapid ageing of the european population, to look for other ways of ensuring normal living conditions for current and future pensioners.
i really want to congratulate the rapporteur because i really agree with many of the points made on tackling demographic problems and strengthening solidarity between generations. however, as regards the elderly, i cannot agree with the provision for retirement before the normal retirement age has been reached. indeed, very often older people are forced into early retirement by circumstances and very often not of their own volition. however, in the aftermath of the economic crisis, early retirement is often the only way out for older people who have lost their jobs. therefore, given the differences between national pension systems in the member states, it is necessary to take into account the practice of all member states of the european union rather than following the example of one or only a few countries.
first of all, i should also like to congratulate my colleague from the group of the european people's party on her hard work in negotiating this document and in including it in the context of the energy market, which is of vital importance for the european union as a whole. in the context of energy security, it is particularly important to promote the development of energy efficiency and renewable energy sources and to ensure the necessary funding for such projects. the successful implementation of such projects would directly contribute to the achievement of the european union's energy objectives. this additional money will allow local and regional authorities to better finance sustainable energy projects and implement innovative financial incentive schemes such as guarantees and soft loans. so far, energy efficiency plans such as the renovation of buildings, which has already been discussed, have not been fully implemented, so i believe that this additional funding will be a good incentive to accelerate sustainable energy initiatives and projects, especially at regional and local level.
support for energy infrastructure projects under the european union's economic stimulus funds was discussed and allocated a year ago. the funds were then allocated to only two types of projects aimed at reducing carbon dioxide emissions into the atmosphere, i.e. ccs and wind farms in the north sea. we cannot have a one-size-fits-all answer for this support and for support for renewable energy in general. we must take account of the situation in each member state, especially as regards energy independence. the proposal of the european commission to revise the funding arrangements for the programme in the light of the promotion of energy efficiency is therefore to be welcomed. but i don't think we should be talking about this just in the light of the financial crisis, because once the economy hits a normal cycle, the energy efficiency question is not going anywhere. member states should have the flexibility to respond more flexibly to changing circumstances, thereby promoting energy efficiency, energy diversification and greater independence.
in writing. the possibility of channelling funds not used under the regulation to develop energy efficiency and renewable energy projects was already programmed in the regulation, but i am pleased that we were able to reach a consensus on concrete implementation so quickly. energy efficiency and renewable energy have been repeatedly identified as eu priorities to help deliver the 2020 strategy and ensure energy security. investment in this sector is crucial in a difficult economic period, as it will create jobs, boost competitiveness, innovation and economic sustainability. the financial instrument envisaged, the creation of a joint fund with the european financial institution, will also increase the amount available several times, thus broadening the scope for project financing. it is regrettable that the financial instrument envisaged is not continuous, but i hope that it will provide the right incentive to further promote energy efficiency and renewable energy.
i voted for this very important document. and now that the european parliament's decision to lift visas for taiwanese citizens of the republic of china travelling to the european union, a member of the schengen area, has been adopted by such a large, such a significant majority, i congratulate the people of taiwan with all my heart. they will now be able to travel to the european union without a visa. in addition, as of today, citizens of all european union member states, including cyprus, bulgaria and romania, will be able to travel to taiwan without a visa. so i think that taiwan and its people deserve this decision and i welcome and rejoice in this decision of the european parliament.
mr president, i welcome the decision of president barack obama to attend the eu-us summit this month in lisbon and i voted in favour of the resolution today. there are so many important issues to be discussed. the eu-us agenda is full as ever, and both parties should use the opportunity to advance common positions on issues such as financial regulation, climate change, deepening of treaties and the fight against terrorism. economic recovery, however, has to be the number-one issue on the agenda. our economic partnership is a key driver of global economic prosperity. together our economies account for half of the global economy. therefore we need to devise common strategies for our further actions to ensure a stable recovery from the crisis, including regulating the financial markets, the stimulus packages and strategies to efficiently address currency manipulation by other major global players.
mr president, i voted in favour of this resolution because in my view the european parliament has to demonstrate that it takes seriously the issues of security, the fight against terrorism and organised and transnational crime. we are also serious in taking into account our relations with partners such as the united states, canada and australia. after the entry into force of the lisbon treaty, the european parliament now has to give its consent to the agreements between the eu and third countries on the transfer of passenger name record (pnr) data, with a view to concluding those agreements. we must therefore use this power responsibly. in the light of the fact that parliament has already decided once, on 5 may this year, to postpone the vote on the request for consent for the agreements with the usa and australia, and that the current eu-canada agreement on the transfer of pnr is no longer valid, we should make sure that when the time comes we give the green light to these important measures, which will enhance the security in the transatlantic area and beyond.
i voted in favour of this very important resolution because i believe that the role of the european union in the osce is crucial to the achievement of the objectives pursued by the osce. it should also be noted that the european union and the osce, although having very different structures, share the same principles and values, namely respect for human rights and democracy. i support the provisions of the resolution calling on kazakhstan to take concrete steps before the summit to protect and respect the osce core values of human rights, the rule of law, freedom of expression. i would very much like the public forum, which should take place at the same time as the osce summit, to be unhindered and to be a success. and i want to stress once again that the osce must be led by states that respect human rights, that defend human rights and democratic values. and they should be an example to other osce members.
i voted for this manno document because it reflects the problems of intergenerational interaction. the main theme is the demographic problems of the european union through the role of young people. meanwhile, this document seeks to bring together the specificities of all generations and the related problems in the health, education, employment and similar sectors, seeking balanced solutions to address these problems. older people we should be grateful to them for their contribution to the development of the european union, both economically and culturally. these people must be guaranteed a good old age. but with an ageing europe, it seems that this will be quite difficult to do. this requires not only the improvement of family policy but also the development of a younger generation capable of creating added value, i.e. through education systems, non-formal education. of course, efforts must be made to integrate young people into the labour market. so thank you for this report.
i voted for a document on simplifying joint research in the european union. unfortunately, i have missed the opportunity to discuss this in a wider context in the plenary, but the document itself is certainly important and its adoption is welcome. it is important to create a level playing field for scientists from all countries of the european union. to provide for equal remuneration for scientific activities without discriminating between scientists according to their national standard of living, since scientific progress is a matter for the european union as a whole and not for individual countries. the current situation is that the work of scientists from the new member states is less appreciated than that of the old ones. such discriminatory practices are unfair and unacceptable. it is also important to ensure a level playing field for all scientific institutions, regardless of their size and financial capacity. this is particularly important for research institutions in the newest eu countries. it is not the size of organisations but the progress of science that creates value, and it is therefore important to provide opportunities not only for universities but also for non-profit research institutes and other scientific organisations to apply and participate in research programmes. so i congratulate all the scientific people, and with the easing of the research mechanisms, we will move forward.
in writing. − this regulation establishes the lists of third countries whose nationals must be in possession of visas when crossing the external borders of the union and those whose nationals are exempt from that requirement. the application of visa requirements to third countries needs to be considered on a case-by-case basis. the purpose of this proposal is to lift the visa requirement for taiwanese nationals. the european parliament endorsed the objective of this proposal. the development of a common immigration policy is essential to the development of trade and economic relations between the european union and taiwan. in recent years, taiwan's economy has been growing rapidly and its per capita income is now the highest in the world. taiwan is also a great partner for cooperation in the fields of science, investment, new technologies, education, culture, tourism. therefore, the visa waiver for taiwan will strengthen trade and economic relations with the european union.
in writing. − i thank the authors of the report for the excellent legislative initiative which i supported, as financing projects in the field of energy efficiency and renewable energy sources will help to stimulate economic recovery, create jobs, promote social inclusion and attract regions. these projects are most effective when implemented at municipal, regional and local level and, in duly justified cases, can be more effectively pursued at national level as well. the current financial and economic crisis and the resulting decline in budgetary revenues are having a particularly negative impact on the finances of local and regional authorities and it should be ensured that the difficult financial situation of these authorities does not prevent them from obtaining funding. fortunately, the short-term economic impact is required, the period between the submission of the application for project authorisation and the financial decision should not exceed six months. as we know, the eu is increasingly dependent on oil and gas suppliers. in some countries, projects for building renovation, gas and electricity infrastructure, interconnections, wind farms, and carbon capture and storage are making slow progress. unused funds from these projects could be used for this purpose. the communication is particularly relevant for lithuania, which is increasingly dependent on russian energy resources, and i therefore hope that the government will make every effort to secure funding and channel financial resources to the local level in order to create added value, direct benefits for citizens, reduce energy poverty and avoid bureaucratic obstacles.
in writing. - i support this resolution. with the entry into force of the lisbon treaty, the european union has become a major global player on the international stage. while the eu and the us remain the world's strongest economic powers, the serious impact of the financial and economic crisis has led to the creation of a more integrated transatlantic market, which would create more favourable conditions for economic growth, sustainable social development. it is therefore necessary to develop bilateral cooperation in the development of a common energy strategy that supports the diversification and security of energy resources and supply chains and promotes an environmentally efficient economy. in addition, the creation of a new business culture requires the strengthening of innovation, creativity and the use of information and communication technologies, and the intensification of cooperation in education, research and science.
in writing. − the eu and the us have many points of contact and a dominant role in solving global problems. however, we need to learn from each other's good practices and strengthen partnerships to ensure that actions are effective. these issues are relevant to climate change, a healthy environment and food security. the massive ecological disaster in the gulf of mexico has raised hopes that the u.s. administration will take appropriate action to ensure environmental stewardship and bring the problems of climate change closer to solution. the eu must play an active role at the cancún climate change conference. it is important that the us should be part of this agreement, because sometimes it seems that it is easier to reach an agreement with developing countries than with the us. it is necessary to explore ways of linking the eu greenhouse gas emissions trading scheme with regional or federal emissions trading schemes in the us. we must also seek transatlantic partnerships on strategic energy issues on security of energy supply and on the policy of transition to low-carbon energy sources. the issue of transatlantic cooperation in the area of a healthy environment the application of new food products and new technologies in food production. eu society is not yet fully prepared for innovation, so countries must respect each other's choice. a strong eu-us partnership must be mutually beneficial and in a global context. i hope that the upcoming eu-us summit will be a step towards this goal.
in writing. − i support this resolution. the global impact of acts of terrorism and the increase in organised crime in the areas of drug trafficking and human trafficking are forcing an intensification of efforts to combat crime and terrorism. to be effective, such actions must be based on more efficient, faster and more focused data exchange at european and global level. however, the main problem remains the protection of personal data against their use for purposes other than the prevention, investigation and detection of criminal offences. in my view, the commission must ensure that all agreements and arrangements for the transfer of personal data meet the requirements of proportionality and ensure the privacy of personal information. in this process, the european parliament, as the body representing citizens, must be regularly informed by the commission on all issues relating to the transfer of personal data in order to express its position on the envisaged agreements. this will enable us to find the best solutions to ensure that our citizens' data is used only under strictly regulated rules and to ensure the protection of their personal information.
in writing. − parliament is seeking to strengthen the transfer of data protection records to third country authorities. it is essential that the use of personal data is efficient, that the use of information is sufficiently flexible and that it is possible at the same time to protect the privacy of individuals. parliament called for a standardised model for passenger data to be developed and for data to be transferred and processed for the purposes of the prevention, investigation, detection or prosecution of criminal offences, including terrorism. it is essential that these personal data records are not used for data mining or automatic classification of people and that decisions are made by people rather than relying solely on an electronic system to review the data. to this end, it is essential to cooperate and exchange information with competent, independent authorities in order to ensure their effective and independent decisions on the transfer of personal data.
in writing. - i supported this resolution. the osce is an integral part of the euro-atlantic and eurasian security architecture with a broad security concept covering political and military, economic and environmental and human dimensions. however, despite its importance, the osce is the only security organisation without international legal personality, with many political and practical legal implications. the role of the organisation is crucial for addressing a wide range of issues , including non-proliferation , disarmament , economic cooperation , and the protection and promotion of human rights . however, the lack of legal status and the problems of decision-making mechanism weaken this organisation, since it is not always able to respond in a timely and appropriate manner to crises. i support the proposals in the resolution that the dialogue on the legal basis of the osce should be continued and that lithuania's chairmanship of the osce in 2011 should ensure consistency and progress in the process of strengthening this important organisation.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, as many of you are aware, lithuania will assume the chairmanship of the osce in january 2011. this presidency could be a unique opportunity for my country to take the lead in strengthening regional cooperation, enhancing energy security and fighting corruption. it is also important not to underestimate the human dimension of osce cooperation. in addition to its political-military dimension, the osce also plays an important role in promoting respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms. the eu also has a major role to play in this area. coordination of eu and osce action in the field of human rights is in the common interest of all of us. let us not forget the circumstances in which the osce was founded. this year marks the 35th anniversary of the adoption of the osce founding conference and the helsinki final act, which laid the foundations of the organisation. this event symbolized a moral and political commitment to the principles of democracy and human rights. i regret that in recent years this helsinki spirit has been lost. we need to change that. i therefore look forward with great impatience to the outcome of the osce summit in astana in december.
in writing. - i support this report. today we face a double challenge: high levels of youth unemployment and problems in financing pension systems. these two phenomena are interrelated and should therefore be addressed together. demographic change can be managed if it is adequately prepared and addressed. the main measures to ensure a balance between generations and to help eliminate unfair discrimination are left to the member states themselves. these are the under-monitoring of pension systems, national budgets, health, education and employment policies, where structural reforms should be pursued and new sustainable solutions sought. in the meantime, the eu should be responsible for the exchange of good practice between member states, as well as for monitoring the implementation of eu legislation in the member states and for taking the initiative in adopting important legislation in these areas.
in writing. − i voted for this report because we must not seek conflict and competition between generations, but equal opportunities and solidarity. however, i did not support the rapporteur's view that early retirement should be abolished. indeed, very often older people retire early because of circumstances rather than because of their own choice. in the aftermath of the economic crisis, early retirement is often the only way out for older people who have lost their jobs. given the differences in national pension systems between member states, it is therefore necessary to take into account the practice of all member states of the european union rather than following the example of one or only a few countries. it is for the member states to decide on the implementation of the early retirement provision, taking into account their national situation and practice. i would also like to point out that both demographic change and age discrimination are damaging to generational solidarity and economic growth. such discrimination severely reduces the access to the labour market for both older and younger workers.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, the report rightly notes that the continuing decline in the population and the continuing trend towards low birth rates in the european union pose a number of challenges to the european economy and society. for these reasons, the eu labour market will undergo structural changes, mostly negative. in lithuania, these demographic problems are further aggravated by the mass exodus of young lithuanians to work abroad. we are a small nation and we are rapidly losing the most active people between 25 and 40 years old, who have been invested a lot in education and training. lithuania needs the skills, experience and ability of these young people to work hard to overcome the demographic challenges mentioned above. i therefore support the initiative proposed by the rapporteur, the 'european youth guarantee', which would offer every young person in the eu a job, a place of study or further vocational training after six months of unemployment. more should also be done to increase fertility rates: access to high-quality care for young children and their education and development is the first step in the right direction. social exclusion of older people must also be prevented. our economy and our society need to make sure that older people are not a burden. their experience, achievements and knowledge can be passed on to future generations.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. science, education and innovation are the foundation for economic growth and job creation. europe must invest in innovation if it is to create new products and services. this will create new jobs and growth in conditions, making europe more competitive and improving the quality of life. the research community has long expressed concern that the implementation of research programmes and the development of innovation are not proceeding as rapidly as expected due to extremely complex bureaucratic requirements. this is particularly a problem for smaller organisations such as smes, technology companies, small institutes, universities and research centres. indeed, there is a need to improve and streamline research funding and administration. currently, different rules and procedures apply to the different measures of the framework programme, which makes it very difficult for stakeholders to submit applications. i agree that the 8th research programme should focus on simplifying administrative procedures to ensure uniform interpretation and application of the rules and procedures used in all programmes and instruments.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, there is no innovation without research excellence. innovation in cutting-edge science and education is one of the engines of job creation and economic growth. if europe wants to create new products and services, it must invest in innovation. it is therefore important - as the rapporteur rightly points out - to help us simplify the mechanisms provided for in the research framework programmes. harmonization of rules and procedures and simplification of research funding and administration have long been needed. most smaller organisations (either new high-tech companies or universities and research centres) find it difficult to cope with the complexity of the current rules. according to this year's european innovation scoreboard, which compares the innovation performance of all eu member states, lithuania's indicators are below the eu-27 average. however, it has shown good progress and has great potential. lithuania has one of the fastest internet connections and mobile penetration rates in the world. it is therefore essential to continue investing in the development of technologies and to improve the implementation of national and european digital agendas. this will make europe more competitive and improve the quality of life.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. the eu imposes the highest standards on producers in terms of food safety and quality, environmental protection, animal health and welfare and working conditions, which greatly increases costs for producers and reduces their competitiveness compared to third country producers. the eu livestock sector is facing serious problems such as rising costs due to rising fuel and fertiliser prices, high compliance costs with eu rules, increasing competition from imports from third countries, low prices for meat products from producers. to protect european producers and this important sector of the economy, the european commission must ensure that the cap is adequately financed after 2013 to guarantee the viability of all eu farmers, including livestock farmers, and to allow for fair competition.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, the food sector is one of the priority sectors for lithuania and the european union as a whole. we have already seen what can happen to energy supply and prices when the eu is dependent on imports. this cannot happen again in the food sector. livestock farms have been seriously threatened by recent increases in eu cereal prices, as well as by extreme climatic conditions and speculation. this means that certain eu sectors, such as the pigmeat sector (an important part of lithuania's agriculture), have been hit by feed prices, which account for up to 60% of total production costs. eu livestock production systems are important for maintaining livelihoods. lithuania may face serious consequences from the eu crisis in the livestock sector. the agricultural sector, which employs about 8% of our workforce, is an important component of our economy. cereals and wheat are the main crops. the eu-27 has more than 130 million livestock units. according to eu statistics, the number of cattle has fallen by 25% since 1990. further declines in the number of cattle in the eu could have a significant impact on production, which would disrupt the eu's food supply. such fluctuations can cause significant disruption in the livestock sector.
in the european union, we are declaring the principle of consumer awareness, in this case of patients, and we are seeking to make citizens partly responsible for their own health. but it's our responsibility as politicians to protect patients from misinformation, misinformation. that's what we're doing today. the situation in the european union is different as regards public access to information on medicines. it's not clear how information differs from advertising. i therefore support the parliament's position that there should be a clearer definition of the terms information and advertising. it is important that the patient receives reliable, objective information in his or her native language, not only for the benefit of professionals, but also in a way that is easily understood and accessible to the patient's needs. we need to protect not only patients but also doctors from hidden advertising, as their communication is crucial to the treatment process. we live in a time when there are no more borders, when we are free to move not only ourselves but also information, and we need to put in place safeguards to ensure the well-being of patients in the european union. so i congratulate the rapporteur on her excellent work and hope that we all get healthier and have easier access to information.
in writing. - i voted for the solidarity fund for ireland. the rains in 2009 caused massive flooding that caused extensive damage to the agricultural sector, residential and commercial buildings, the road network and other infrastructure. aid for ireland was approved exceptionally as the flood damage was not in line with the requirements of the solidarity fund. climate change is increasing the number of natural disasters in europe and worldwide, leading to a rise in human deaths and large losses. it is therefore necessary for the european union to develop instruments to provide the necessary funding in a timely manner in the event of such disasters. in its resolution in march, the european parliament clearly stated its position that a new regulation on the european union solidarity fund was needed to deal more effectively with the problems caused by natural disasters. i believe that the revision of the regulation should aim at creating a stronger and more flexible instrument to respond more effectively to the new challenges of climate change and to provide urgent assistance to those affected by natural disasters.
in writing. − i agree to the extension of the agreement between the european union and ukraine on scientific and technological cooperation for a further period of five years. this decision will provide an opportunity for both the community and ukraine to continue, improve and strengthen cooperation in areas of common scientific and technological interest. the aim of this cooperation is to help ukraine to become more actively involved in the european research area. such cooperation will help ukraine to support the reform and restructuring of its scientific governance system and research institutions, thus facilitating the creation of a competitive economy and knowledge society. this decision should allow both ukraine and the european union to benefit mutually from their scientific and technical progress through specific research programmes. this decision will allow for the exchange of specific knowledge and the transfer of practical experience for the benefit of the scientific community, industry and citizens. i therefore strongly support the continued close cooperation between ukraine and the european union in this area.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. in its proposal, the commission proposes to extend the existing requirement for eu member states to apply a minimum standard vat rate of 15% for five years. the proposal will therefore have no impact on the tax rates. the commission proposes that the extension provision enters into force on 1 january 2011 and expires on 31 december 2015. in order to maintain the level of tariff harmonisation achieved, the commission has twice submitted proposals setting the range of the standard tariff to a minimum of 15% of the tariff and a maximum of 25% of the tariff. this range was established on the basis of the tariffs practically applied in the member states, where standard tariffs always ranged from 15 to 25%. in both cases, the council amended these tariff harmonisation proposals, leaving only the minimum tariff. i agree with the commission that the extension of the validity period should not only provide businesses with the necessary legal clarity but also allow for an additional assessment at eu level of the appropriate standard rate of vat. i believe that in the forthcoming green paper on the revision of the vat system, the commission should not only examine the specific issues of the standard vat rate and the other rates, but also consider the broader issue of a new vat strategy, including the scope and exemptions of vat.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, i believe that the new vat strategy should pay particular attention to the reform of the vat rules, in line with principles that actively promote the objectives of the internal market. as the rapporteur points out, the current vat system is not only too complex in terms of tariffs but also lags behind the progress of the internal market. in january 2009, the lithuanian vat rate was increased from 18% to 19% and in september to 21%. it is important that future developments do not reduce the interest of foreign investors in lithuania. we have to offer favorable tax planning options. the new vat strategy should aim to reduce the administrative burden, remove tax barriers and improve the business environment, in particular for smes. the commission should consult the public and private sectors fully before taking a final decision. these consultations should address not only vat rates but also broader issues such as the ambition to set a maximum rate and alternatives to the structure and functioning of the vat system. i therefore look forward to the commission's green paper on the revision of the vat system.
in writing. − the european parliament resolution on the ecb's annual report 2009 notes that the requirements of the stability and growth pact have not always been respected in the past and that it is therefore necessary to ensure that the pact is applied more consistently and effectively in the member states in the future. i supported this resolution and believe that monetary union requires strict economic policy coordination and that the current gap in supervision of macroeconomic risk mitigation should be closed by setting up a european systemic risk board. i believe that the concept of the esrb issuing only warnings and recommendations and not taking any actual enforcement action does not allow for the principles of effective implementation and accountability or for the declaration of a state of emergency and that it is therefore necessary to give the board greater powers. it is particularly important to encourage the european commission not only to amend the regulation on credit rating agencies to give european credit rating agencies greater responsibility, but also to propose the creation of a european credit rating agency that would allow an objective assessment of the economic and financial situation of the member states.
in writing. − with civil and ethnic wars gradually replacing interstate wars to dominate the scene of 21st century conflicts, the line drawn between peacekeeping and peacebuilding is increasingly blurred. the changing nature of conflict calls for more extensive cooperation between civilian and military capabilities. the highly successful eu-led aceh monitoring mission (amm) from 2005 to 2006 serves as an important example and reminder of how civilian-military synergies can anchor peace and build confidence in an unstable environment in a timely and effective way. while the decommissioning team, dominated by military experts, monitored the rapid and successful disarmament process, the civilian team of amm worked to turn the process into a platform for further political dialogue and for both parties to credibly commit themselves to permanent peace. the application of civilian and military expertise should not be restricted by the technical labelling of the nature of operations, but should instead be decided on the basis of appropriateness and cost-efficiency. i voted in favour of this report because i think it plays a critical role in facilitating the further development of a non-aggressive but nevertheless coherent and competent face of eu foreign policies.
in writing. − the baltic sea region has been designated as a highly sensitive marine area by the international maritime organisation's marine environment protection committee. the baltic sea is thus one of the most valuable and sensitive marine ecosystems in the world. i voted in favour of the provisions in this document that the conditions for sustainable exploitation of fish stocks must be created without reducing trading standards. in order to effectively limit the high discards of juveniles and undersized commercial or non-commercial species, it is important to encourage the fishing industry to use selective fishing gear and not to fish in areas where there are high levels of undersized or non-commercial species. it is necessary to start a review of the discard system and i believe that this issue will be one of the most important in the 2011 review of the reform of the eu's common fisheries policy.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it calls for restrictions on fishing for river plets and otters in the baltic sea region. it is therefore necessary to put an end to commercial fishing in that sea without delay. in the absence of sufficient reliable scientific data to estimate the catches of commercial fisheries, it is necessary to start documenting catches immediately and at the same time to carry out a comprehensive monitoring of the fishing vessels involved in these fisheries. i agree with the european parliament's position that action under the common fisheries policy is urgently needed to solve the problem of fishing in the baltic sea, bearing in mind that, from an environmental point of view, such fishing is damaging to the entire baltic sea ecosystem. it is necessary to take into account that the baltic sea is currently one of the most valuable marine ecosystems in the world and that the baltic sea region has been granted the status of a highly sensitive marine area. the baltic sea is also experiencing a changing climate, and different fish species are adapting to these changes. so their migration and nesting habits are also changing. i therefore support parliament's call for the commission to carry out a review of the baltic sea protected areas.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, next year the european parliament will be debating the reform of the common fisheries policy. i agree that this process must include measures to improve the sustainable exploitation of fisheries resources and the efficient management of marine resources. as the international maritime organisation has pointed out, the baltic sea is an 'extremely sensitive area'. the baltic sea is one of the most sensitive marine ecosystems in the world, but we must also not forget the human side of the issue, which is not mentioned in the report. the fishing industry in lithuania has a particularly deep tradition. although the fisheries sector accounts for a relatively small share of lithuania's gdp, it is of great importance to the lithuanian economy. lithuania's fishing-dependent regions have experienced considerable economic and social difficulties in recent years due to declining fishing stocks and resource-saving policies. at present, it is increasingly difficult to earn a living in lithuania through fishing-related activities: the low wages make such work unattractive to young people. the commission has also adopted a communication on the implementation of the new rules on the management of fisheries resources in the baltic sea. this aid will help create new jobs, increase the value of fishery products, promote eco-tourism and more.
in writing. - i supported this report. the coal industry, although it accounts for a small share of the eu energy market, provides jobs for eu citizens working in it and economic development in sparsely populated and remote regions where coal-mining companies are mainly located. i voted in favour of the amendments extending the closure of uncompetitive coal mines until 2018, leaving the possibility of not closing mines if they are not competitive by the deadline. it is important to ensure that the closure of a non-competitive mine provides the long-term financing needed to ensure environmental protection and the restoration of the environment of the former mining sites. the closure of the mines, which are the only industries in certain regions, will leave many people without work. it is therefore necessary to ensure that they receive long-term support and various labour market measures, such as retraining, to enable them to re-enter the labour market. i therefore supported the european commission's proposal to provide such support until 2026.
in writing. − ladies and gentlemen, although the coal industry does not have a major impact on the lithuanian economy, this report is important for my country. the expiry of regulation (ec) no 1407/2002 will force some member states to close their coal mines immediately and to address the significant social and regional consequences of this closure. lithuanians are well aware of the problems encountered after such closures. the closure of the lithuanian ignalina nuclear power plant has led to the loss of business and jobs. it has also dealt a blow to our energy independence, and we must not forget that the european union is based on energy solidarity. lithuania and the baltic states are now energy islands cut off from the european gas and electricity networks. i was delighted to hear this month that the european commission is supporting the baltic sea energy market connections plan. i hope that projects such as the visagino nuclear power plant and the interconnection of the lithuanian and polish gas networks will receive financial support from the eu. this would be beneficial for all of europe. finally, it is important to take into account the safety of workers in coal mines and other energy industries. the recent disasters in chile and new zealand will not let us forget this.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution, which takes stock of the activities of the african, caribbean and pacific group of states (acp) and the eu joint parliamentary assembly in 2009. the activities of this assembly are of crucial importance in strengthening the democratic processes in these countries and i therefore support the observations made in the resolution on the need to involve parliaments closely in the democratic process and in national development strategies. i believe that the revision of the cotonou agreement will help to respond to the new developments that have taken place in the last decade since the first cotonou agreement was signed and will allow for an increased participation of national parliaments, civil society and the private sector in the political and economic life of those countries. i also support the proposal that the european development fund should be included in the eu budget in order to increase the coherence, transparency and effectiveness of development cooperation policy and to ensure proper control.
in writing. − the stockholm programme aims to create a european area of freedom, security and justice, protecting the fundamental rights of all citizens, including the freedom to conduct a business and promoting entrepreneurship in all sectors of the economy. since the conferral of competences on the european union in the fields of justice and home affairs and the creation of an area of freedom, security and justice, considerable progress has been made in the field of civil law and the harmonisation of provisions of private international law has been accelerated. this is very important as this branch of law is a key instrument in ensuring mutual recognition and respect for the various legal systems in existence. i believe that the action plan presented by the european commission is ambitious, but at the same time it is necessary to properly assess the effectiveness of the measures already in place and applied and their compliance with the objectives set in order to meet the needs of citizens, business representatives and legal practitioners.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the european parliament calls on the commission to ensure as effectively and as quickly as possible that the stockholm programme action plan genuinely addresses the needs of european citizens, in particular with regard to free movement throughout the european union, employment-related rights, business needs and equal opportunities for all. i would like to emphasise that the law of the european union must serve the interests of citizens, especially in family and civil law, and i am therefore pleased that since the european union was given competence in the fields of justice and home affairs and created an area of freedom, security and justice, tremendous progress has been made in the field of civil law. it should be noted that the stockholm programme aims to establish a european area of freedom, security and justice, in which the fundamental rights of all citizens are protected, including the freedom to conduct a business and the promotion of entrepreneurship in the various economic sectors. the european parliament therefore calls on the commission to initiate a wide-ranging debate involving all the actors concerned in this area, in particular judges and lawyers.

member of the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the commission has already made a number of proposals. the member is dealing with the subject of renewable energy, and what are we doing to move closer to our common goals? we have had ambitious climate targets set by the european council for the last three years. one of these targets concerns renewable energy, which is expected to contribute 20% of the total final energy consumption of the european union in 2020. each eu member state must use its own resources to make its own contribution. if you take this 20% and divide it up into sectors, transport, heating, electricity and other, industry, agriculture, then the most important sector is electricity, i.e. the production of electricity. to reach 20% of total final energy consumption, we need up to 35% of renewable energy in the area of electricity conversion. if we want this to be done efficiently and if we want the price, the cost factor, to be acceptable to the consumer, then we must use the best sites in proportion to the work. each member state has different strengths and weaknesses to exploit: wind from the north sea, sun from the southern part of the european union, biogas in rural areas. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's energy supply is not affected by the crisis. the commission took this into account a few days ago. we have discussed and agreed on an energy infrastructure package which focuses on the integration and use of renewable energy sources. we have set binding renewable energy targets for each member state. in a few days, on 5 december this year, the deadline for transposition will expire and the commission will monitor the implementation of the directive to ensure that appropriate incentives are created and support measures are taken to achieve the objectives that have been committed. in doing so, member states should focus on how to create the best possible framework for the use of renewable energy. the european union directive requires, among other things, that the member states draw up a national action plan setting out the measures and the national targets, which are also concrete and binding, broken down by sectors such as transport, heat, electricity conversion and industry. the second is to remove administrative barriers in favour of renewable energy. thirdly, the construction sector is also heavily affected. forty percent of our final energy consumption is in the building sector in industrial, service and residential buildings. by the end of 2012, we want to develop qualification and certification schemes for the installation of renewable plants. the distribution and transmission networks must be prepared for the further development and growth of renewable energy and must ensure priority access for renewable energy. member states are flexible and free in terms of technology. they are free to choose which renewable energy sources they use to achieve their binding targets. and we, the commission, with the funds from the seventh framework programme for research which you - the parliament - have made available to us, are supporting innovations and projects aimed at researching, developing and bringing to market the technologies for renewable energies. the commission then allocated funds from the european economic recovery programme for three years in those years specifically for specific renewable energy projects, namely for the development of offshore wind energy. i would like to stress, with regard to the new member states, that our cohesion programmes and funding for renewable energy are also available. renewable energy is a high priority in our energy and climate protection policies, but also in reducing our dependence on fossil fuel imports. only by working together will we be able to achieve our ambitious climate goals and also strengthen europe's competitiveness. i am counting on a close partnership with our member states in the use of their different energy resources.

and so on. we are therefore going to have to make a very long list of the proposals that we have put forward. they can transport colour television sets, pigs' bellies, canned goods, goods and the 19th century principalities. that means the integration of the markets, the baltic states, finland with central europe, the integration of greece, even before serbia and croatia are members of the european union. the bridges, the interconnectors for transporting electricity from spain to france, to germany, and vice versa. we need financial and legislative efforts now, in the next two decades, to make our transportation network for electricity and for gas equal in quality and capacity to our road and rail networks and our airspace for goods and goods and people with the goal of free movement. otherwise we will not have security of supply, we will not have solidarity in an emergency, we will not be able to exchange storage capacity, we will not be able to install renewable energy foundation of the european community and which must also be put into practice in the field of electricity and gas. that is why i am in favour of a remarkable evolution in favour of transport networks for electricity, gas and also for oil.
member of the commission. − as you are aware, social policy, including policy relating to people with disabilities, is primarily a national responsibility. however, stepping up support for disadvantaged groups, and in particular for people with disabilities, including people living in institutions, is a key strand for action under the european social fund. according to some estimates, people with disabilities account for at least 16% of the eu working-age population, but only 40% of people with disabilities are employed. the commission encourages a shift from institutional to community-based care and is working closely with the member states, and in particular the managing authorities of the esf and the erdf and ngos in this field. in 2009 the commission published a report by an ad hoc expert group on the switchover from institutional to community-based care. in line with the principle of shared management, the member states are responsible for selecting operations for financing under the structural funds. in the current programming period the european social and regional development funds are supporting some de-institutionalisation schemes and the switch-over from residential care to community-based services. this is particularly true in certain member states that joined the eu after 2004. thus projects are being financed in bulgaria and slovakia. as you know, the commission is currently working on new provisions on the structural funds for the post-2013 period and will put forward proposals in 2011. to step up the fight against discrimination and promote social inclusion of people with disabilities, future operational programmes may, for example, be required to identify the groups at risk of discrimination and exclusion. the commission will explore the role that national equality bodies could play and may consider stricter requirements on member states’ reporting of measures to combat discrimination, implemented with support from the structural funds. in addition, one of the aims of the european disability strategy for 2010 to 2020, which the commission adopted on 15 november, is to give impetus to member states’ efforts to ease the transition from institutional to community-based care. the strategy seeks to do this by identifying good practice in using the structural funds to encourage people to live independently and in the community and aims to develop awareness-raising initiatives that target structural fund managing bodies at eu and national level. for example, a toolkit on how to use the structural funds to support the development of community-based services will be developed for the managing authorities.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this important resolution, which aims to improve patient information about medicines for which they are prescribed. the current legal framework and situation in europe regarding access to information on prescription medicines for human use is rather problematic. different interpretations of the directive in the member states mean that patients in different parts of europe have different access to high-quality and comprehensive information on medicines. in some countries, patients are denied even basic information about prescription medicines. given the different interpretations of the directive in the member states, i believe that the provisions need to be made clearer so that all eu citizens have access to the information they need about medicines. the amendments adopted by the european parliament also ensure that information on prescription medicines is only provided to the public through specific communication channels, including specialised and certified websites, in the interests of patients and in order to limit the advertising possibilities of pharmaceutical companies.

in writing. - i supported this report. some 9.3 million tonnes of electrical and electronic equipment (eee) are sold in the eu alone each year, the bulk of which are large household appliances and information technology and telecommunications equipment. as the market expands and new innovations emerge, equipment is replaced more quickly, leading to a rapid increase in the flow of waste electrical and electronic equipment (weee). it is estimated that the amount of waste eee may increase to 12,3 million tonnes by 2020. waste eee contains some hazardous substances which, especially if handled improperly, may enter the environment and cause damage to human health and the environment. the first version of the dangerous substances directive did not address these problems. i support the suggestions in the document that the european commission should strengthen the prevention of the use of harmful substances and periodically examine the prohibition of other hazardous substances and their replacement by more environmentally friendly alternative materials or technologies that ensure an adequate level of protection of people and the environment.

the compromise resolution on ukraine is indeed very important for the people of ukraine, but let us not forget that it is also very important for the people of the european union, because ukraine is an important partner for us, especially in the context of our neighbourhood policy with eastern europe. it is therefore of the utmost importance for us to continue to pursue close cooperation, particularly in the area of visa regimes. both the commission and the council should therefore draw up an action plan for the introduction of visa-free travel as soon as possible. we also need to agree on a free trade agreement as soon as possible. the implementation of constitutional reforms is of course crucial for ukraine's long-term political stability both at home and with its neighbours, but we - the european union and its institutions - must realise that this takes time and that we must spend all our time not criticizing but helping ukraine to integrate into our family.
ukraine has been a clear leader in comparison with our other eastern neighbours in recent times. i am talking about the application of european standards, economic and legal reforms, the internal political process. of course, the events of the last year in this country are worrying, and so i think we simply have to express our concern to our partner ukraine about these abuses that we see, the special powers that have been given to the ukrainian security service, the measures that the security service has taken in trying to intimidate ngos, to control the ukrainian press. i've said that, and i've got something else to say. we must support the process of ukrainian european integration. we can use the carrot and stick policy, but there has to be more cake. we must bring ukraine closer to the european union, not push it further. i believe that ukraine's membership of the european union is also our strategic objective and we must find the appropriate means to achieve that objective.
i thank the ombudsman for his report. indeed, the role of the european ombudsman is particularly important in helping citizens to understand the problems they face and in improving the transparency and accountability of the institutions. the ombudsman's aim is to make decisions by the institutions of the european union accessible to citizens, since citizens must be able to obtain the information they want in order to have confidence in the institutions' ability to protect their rights in the event of discrimination, abuse of power, failure to respond in time to enquiries or a general refusal to provide information. the commission receives many complaints about improper work and it is therefore essential that the commission takes action to avoid delays in responding to requests for access to documents, since the proper application of european union legislation has a direct impact on the daily lives of citizens, on the exercise of their rights. in addition, the poor application of legislation leads to a loss of confidence in the institutions of the european union and generates additional costs.
i voted in favour of this resolution on the negotiation of the 2011 budget. the failure of last week's reconciliation meeting between the european parliament and the council has caused great concern throughout the european union. it is a pity that the lisbon treaty has not yet become a binding document for some. if no agreement is reached on the budget and other related issues by the end of the year, the consequences would be very serious. the creation of a european external action service, the three new european union financial institutions control bodies, would be very difficult due to lack of funding. the cohesion policy, which is one of the most important in the european union, would also not be properly implemented due to lack of funds. i am no longer talking about the europe 2020 strategy, the galileo programme, or what would happen to them. i therefore believe that the constructive and compromise-seeking attitude of the european parliament once again shows that the european parliament is indeed acting responsibly in the context of the economic and financial crisis.
