mr president, i voted in favour of the resolution. as parliament’s rapporteur on the annual report on human rights in the world this year, i can only reiterate the importance of human rights clauses and standards in international trade agreements. trade can play an important part in ensuring not only civil and political rights, but also economic and social ones. just think about the role that trade-related assistance programmes can play in ensuring development in many poor countries around the world. we must bear in mind that human rights clauses are included in all the bilateral trade agreements the eu makes and also in its gsp and gsp+ regimes with developing countries. we should make these clauses and conditionality more robust and closely monitor their implementation. we should consider conducting a human rights assessment of non-eu countries that engage in trade relations with the eu and granting trade preferences to countries that have ratified and effectively implemented key international conventions on sustainable development, social rights and good governance.
i voted in favour of this very important resolution because we cannot ignore the fact that violence against women is on the increase. this is particularly true in conflict and war zones. i agree that the implementation of un security council resolution 1325, the first resolution to address the disproportionate and specific impact of armed conflict on women, should be given due attention at the highest level in the european union. this should be a particularly important theme in the review of the european union's human rights policy in the development of comprehensive national human rights strategies, in the evaluation of the european union guidelines on violence against women and girls, as well as the eu guidelines on children and armed conflict and combating all forms of discrimination against them. i support the call to provide the necessary funding to combat violence against women, to support women's participation in peace, security and reconciliation processes.
mr president, i voted in favour of this important document. it contributes in a constructive way to the debate we are having in europe on a credible common energy strategy. energy security is certainly one of the key aspects of that strategy. the eu must foster a common – if not single – energy policy. europe-wide regulators and institutions should be given a greater role. the european commission must play a stronger role in efforts to develop alternative energy routes, first of all from azerbaijan and central asia, and from north africa and other countries as well. europe must further invest in lng terminals and help do the same for its newest members. other measures, such as improving energy efficiency, investing in nuclear energy and creating incentives for renewable energy technologies, are also required. an essential piece in the puzzle is the liberalisation of the internal eu energy market. that is why we should wholeheartedly support the initiative of president buzek and the former president of the commission, jacques delors, to establish a european energy community.
i voted in favour of this report on the european union's energy strategy, which covers a number of important subjects and important issues, and which deals with renewable energy, research, innovation, reducing pollution and, of course, improving energy security. i particularly support the european parliament's view that energy networks, even if they are commercial in nature, should be regulated by transparent crossborder agreements which do not prejudice the interests of the member states. the bilateral agreements and projects undertaken by some countries, such as the nord stream pipeline, raise doubts not only in environmental terms but also in the principle of common solidarity. the principle of solidarity between member states, as declared in the treaties of the european union, is in essence a matter of principle. for this reason, i am in favour of external pipelines and other energy networks entering the territory of the european union being governed only by transparent intergovernmental agreements concluded in strict compliance with european union law. such pipelines should be subject to the rules of the internal market of the european union, including those governing the access of third parties to the market.
mr president, finally we have a resolution on ukraine adopted by our parliament. certainly we were concerned about the recent developments in the country. if ukraine is our strategic partner, then my understanding is that it is our duty to express our concern about the shortcomings we see – for instance, the excessive powers granted to the ukraine security services and the measures they have taken to intimidate ngos operating in that country and to control the ukrainian media. we are also concerned at the political process as a whole, including the inability of the opposition parties to participate in the elections without restrictions and discrimination. on the other hand, we should continue to support ukraine’s european integration prospects and to use more carrots than sticks in trying to draw ukraine closer to europe – and not further away from it – and to do everything in our power to achieve this strategic goal of ours.
in writing. - i supported this resolution. the treaty of lisbon brought about a number of important changes in various areas and gave new powers to the european parliament. the failure to reach agreement with the council of the european union on the 2011 budget was the first serious attempt by the european parliament to use its powers. the european parliament is ready to reach an agreement as soon as possible so that the eu can start 2011 with an adopted budget and without delaying the financing of important areas and projects. however, the agreement with the council must identify the european parliament's requirements and agree on certain flexibility measures which should allow for the adequate financing of policies in 2011 and beyond in relation to the new competences conferred under the treaty of lisbon and the europe 2020 strategy. the european commission must commit to submit proposals for new eu own resources and the council must agree on a revision of the financial structure. the three institutions must also agree on a method of cooperation that includes the involvement of the ep in the negotiations on the future multiannual financial framework. the european parliament must comply with its own demands, which will not only determine the future of the eu budget, but also set a precedent for the expansion of the influence of the only directly elected institution in the european union's governing body.
in writing. - i support this report. there is an increasing number of cases of distortions of competition and the risk of environmental and social dumping in international trade. this is to the detriment of companies and workers in the eu, who, unlike their trading partners established outside the eu, are required to adhere to higher social, environmental and tax standards. therefore, eu bilateral and multilateral agreements should require compliance with the above requirements for all companies, not only those established in the eu. the implementation of eu trade agreements also requires transparency and strict procurement requirements to combat illicit capital flows. to achieve these objectives, the eu should actively cooperate with international partners in the field of trade the world trade organisation, the international labour organisation, the united nations.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it is nevertheless essential to ensure a balance between trade law and fundamental human rights and to strengthen the dialogue between the main international organisations, in particular the international labour organisation and the world trade organisation. moreover, the inclusion of human rights provisions and social and environmental standards in trade agreements can benefit international trade agreements in order to maintain political and social stability while facilitating trade itself. it should be noted that european industrial and commercial companies are required to strictly comply with social and environmental protection rules. i agree with the european parliament's position that if the european union is to comply with binding standards, it must also require the same from its trading partners, in particular from emerging economies, and promote quality and sustainability requirements, in particular for food products entering its territory, in order to protect fair and equitable trade. i would like to stress that in order to strengthen human rights and social policy in international trade, it is necessary to require that all future trade agreements include a prohibition on the use of child labour, in particular in the extraction and processing of natural stone.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because it should not be forgotten that european industrial and commercial companies must strictly respect social and environmental protection rules. if the european union respects binding standards, it must also require the same from its trading partners, especially from emerging economies, and promote quality and sustainability requirements, in particular for food products entering its territory, in order to protect fair and equitable trade. the importance of improving access to green goods and technologies for sustainable development purposes, the need to complete as soon as possible negotiations on the reduction or elimination of tariff and non-tariff barriers to green goods and services, in order to promote new forms of employment policies, the creation of jobs that meet the standards of decent work in the octs and the growth potential of european industry and small and medium-sized enterprises.
in writing. − the european ombudsman safeguards the right of eu citizens, as enshrined in the charter of fundamental rights, to have their affairs handled impartially, fairly and in the shortest possible time by the union institutions, bodies, offices and agencies. the treaty of lisbon strengthened the democratic legitimacy of the ombudsman, since he is elected by the european parliament, and extended his mandate to cover the common foreign and security policy and the activities of the european council. i voted in favour of this resolution because i am positive about the results of the 2009 work. in 2009, the average time taken to deal with complaints was reduced by four months, and more than half of the infringement procedures initiated ended in amicable settlements. this shows that the ombudsman cooperates effectively with the eu institutions. i believe that it is necessary to increase the trust of eu citizens in the eu and its institutions, and above all to ensure that citizens have full access to the information they need and to build confidence in the ability of the eu institutions to defend their rights.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the ombudsman's work helps the european institutions to make transparent decisions that are accessible to all citizens and legal persons. the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon has strengthened the democratic legitimacy of the ombudsman, since he is elected by parliament, and extended his mandate to include the common foreign and security policy and the activities of the european council. transparency, access to information and respect for the right to good administration are necessary preconditions for citizens to have confidence in the ability of the institutions to protect their rights. the most frequent allegations of maladministration related to a lack of transparency. it is therefore essential to respond promptly and fully to citizens' enquiries, complaints and petitions. the european institutions and bodies must provide citizens with the necessary information. i am pleased that the european ombudsman cooperates with other ombudsmen and similar bodies in the member states at national, regional and local levels.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution on the special report of the european ombudsman, as it draws attention to the fact that the ombudsman has successfully investigated on his own initiative the rules applied by the commission to citizens' requests for access to documents relating to infringement procedures. complaints by eu citizens are an important source of information on possible breaches of european union law. in particular, citizens have complained about the commission's maladministration. it is therefore essential that the commission take action to avoid delaying the response to requests for access to documents. efforts should be made to strengthen cooperation with the european parliament's committee on petitions and to have a regular exchange of information with the ombudsman. the european parliament should be kept informed of the inquiries carried out by the ombudsman and of the results obtained. the commission should adopt a more open and dynamic approach to information on infringement procedures.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. the treaty on european union establishes the responsibility of the member states for the application of eu law and gives the commission, as guardian of the treaties, the power and responsibility to ensure that the law is correctly applied. the scope of the eu acquis and the size of the eu raise many questions and concerns for citizens, civil society interest groups and businesses. in order to ensure that the law is correctly understood and applied, the 'eu pilot' programme was launched in 2008 to create a confidential online database for the exchange of correspondence between commission services and member states' institutions to monitor the implementation of eu law and to initiate cases. i agree with the view expressed in the resolution that this initiative is an appropriate response to the need for cooperation between all eu institutions in order to create a well-functioning system that works for the benefit of citizens. i believe that the european parliament, as the institution representing the eu citizens, should be given access to this database in order to monitor how the commission is fulfilling its role as guardian of the treaties.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because the current annual reports of the commission on the monitoring of the application of european union law do not provide citizens or other institutions with sufficient information on the actual situation of the application of european union law. the commission's summary of the monitoring report on the application of the law focuses more on the transposition of the provisions of the law than on their actual application. the commission shall only provide references to proceedings initiated against member states which have not transposed provisions of european union law into their national legal system. citizens and the european parliament should therefore be informed when the commission initiates infringement proceedings relating to the unlawful transposition of provisions of union law into national law and should be aware of the details of those infringements. the lisbon treaty also enshrines the right of citizens to participate in shaping the legislative agenda and to help ensure the application of and compliance with european union law and the transparency and credibility of the related procedures.
in writing. − broadcast media are one of the main sources of information available to eu citizens in the member states and therefore an important factor in shaping people's values and opinions. the audiovisual environment of the european union is exceptional, based on a balance between public and commercial broadcasters, which ensures a diversity of freely available programmes and promotes media pluralism, cultural and linguistic diversity, editorial competition and freedom of expression. i voted in favour of this resolution because the european parliament supports the objective of maintaining an independent, strong and viable public service broadcasting, adapting it to the requirements of the digital era and facilitating the switchover of consumers from analogue to digital television. i believe that member states should do more to tackle the digital divide and ensure that all people in all regions have equal access to public service broadcasting.
in writing. − a free and independent media is one of the main pillars of democracy. i voted in favour of this report because i agree that public service broadcasters, free from political pressure and commercial interests, are a particularly important part of this system. sadly, the current trends are not encouraging. in some countries, public service broadcasters are facing increasing political pressure, with funding for a broadcaster becoming dependent on the political party that comes to power. elsewhere, broadcasters are increasingly influenced by corporate structures. i agree with the position that culture and media will always be a matter for the states. but i think the eu institutions could also play a significant role. they could help to exchange examples of good practice and, in some cases, to highlight and condemn bad practice. in these times of economic crisis, public service broadcasters should make better use of the eib's preferential loans to modernise infrastructure, raise the profile of public service broadcasters and help them adapt to the challenges of the 21st century.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because of the need to give the utmost attention to the safety of women in peacekeeping missions and in military conflicts. i would like to stress that the european union's human rights policy should be reconsidered when developing a comprehensive national human rights strategy and when assessing the european union guidelines on violence against women and girls and the european union guidelines on children and armed conflict and combating all forms of discrimination against them. it should be noted that some of the widespread and systematic acts of rape and sexual slavery are recognised as crimes against humanity and war crimes under the geneva convention. furthermore, rape is now also recognized as part of genocide when it is committed with the intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a particular group of people. the european union must therefore take concrete action to end the impunity for sexual violence against women and children. it is also necessary to establish a code of conduct for european union personnel on military and civilian missions, which would prohibit sexual exploitation as unjustified and criminal behaviour.
in writing. − 2010 is the european year of biodiversity, to draw attention to the threats to biodiversity around the world. the beekeeping sector, which is an important source of employment and income in rural areas, is also threatened by recent increases in bee mortality and disease. i voted in favour of this resolution, in which the european parliament calls on the commission to fund specific research to improve knowledge and understanding of the factors affecting bee health. the resolution observed that the presence of toxins in genetically modified crops or in pollen may affect bee disease and mortality. given the dependence of european cereals, fruit and vegetable crops on bee pollination, these crops and agriculture in general are at high risk of various diseases. i believe that in the face of such doubts, the european commission must urgently carry out an independent study assessing the environmental and species-specific effects of the spread of genetically modified crops and toxins and ensure that such data are made public.
in writing. − i have supported this report because the beekeeping sector plays a strategic role in society by providing public services with environmental value. today, the decline of pollinating insects, including bees, is a cause for concern. 84% of plant species and 76% of food production in europe depend on bee pollination, and the economic importance of this is far greater than the value of honey produced by honeybees. 40% of the european honey market is dependent on imports and the opening of the eu market to honey from third countries has put beekeepers across the eu at a competitive disadvantage. it is therefore necessary to support the further development of european beekeeping, contributing to the conservation of biodiversity. it is important to adopt a comprehensive and sustainable approach, including aspects such as rural development, climate change and biodiversity, in particular by promoting measures supporting the maintenance and development of flowering areas. it is also important to support the european beekeeping sector more broadly and consistently through additional instruments under the future cap, including those that help to promote biodiversity, mitigate the impact of climate change, preserve the traditional national and cultural heritage that provides jobs for many european families, protect and improve the quality and the proper functioning of the beekeeping market.
in writing. - i supported this important document. the separate energy chapter in the lisbon treaty now provides a solid legal basis for developing energy initiatives based on sustainability, security of supply, interconnection and solidarity. there are significant delays or unfinished implementation problems in the union in the field of energy legislation, which the commission has not taken a strong leadership role in addressing in order to address these shortcomings. the domestic energy sector will require significant investments in new power plants, connections and transmission networks over the next decade, given that these investments will create a longer-term mix of energy types that will contribute to the creation of a sustainable and clean energy market. it is very important that energy projects have clear multiannual funding, so that the baltic sea region is also integrated into the eu's single energy market and we pay for energy resources at the same level as other eu countries.
in writing. − i have endorsed this report and i would like to thank the rapporteurs, the shadow rapporteurs, for the first energy strategy since the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. the strategy reflects the main challenges of energy solidarity and security of energy supply. first, member states must implement the legislation already adopted in the field of energy. secondly, a common energy market must be created, as well as a market for renewable energy sources. thirdly, the trans-european energy infrastructure must be renewed and modernised. the eu must therefore overcome all administrative and financial obstacles, and the interests of its members must be harmonized and mutually supportive. the same rules must apply to all projects, whether the yamal pipeline or the nordstream pipeline. as we know, the eu is increasingly dependent on oil and gas suppliers. monopoly costs eastern european member states a lot there is no competition, consumer rights are not protected, the common market cannot be created. our dependence on fossil fuels, whose resources are dwindling, makes it imperative to develop not only renewable energy but also to invest in energy efficiency, while reducing climate change. a major weakness of current eu energy policy is the inability to channel eu funds into an area which, despite the recession, remains one of the top priorities of european governments, citizens and the public. the consumer and the protection of his interests must be at the heart of energy policy, and the eu must strive to ensure favourable energy prices for consumers and businesses.
in writing. - i supported this resolution. the copenhagen climate change conference failed to reach specific commitments. further negotiations in germany and china have also failed to produce the expected results, raising hopes for a conference in cancún to agree on key decisions a comprehensive international agreement on action beyond 2012, in line with the latest scientific findings and the intention to halt global warming by 2020 and to achieve a global warming of no more than 2 degrees celsius by 2050 compared to 1990 levels. i support the position expressed in the resolution that the european union must play a leading role in the climate change negotiations and actively contribute to the concrete results of the cancún climate change conference. the european union is the only country that has adopted binding climate change mitigation targets and is going to commit to even higher ones, and it must be in the interest of the eu to see other countries commit to them, as the eu's economic growth and competitiveness depend on it.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because the european union must take a leading role in the climate change negotiations, because it is necessary to restore confidence in international climate change negotiations after the disappointing results of the copenhagen climate change conference. i support the european parliament's call for concrete measures to stop deforestation and its proposal to create an efficient global market for trading in emission allowances. i would like to stress that more efforts must be made to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by natural means, as this would encourage the conservation of biodiversity. i hope that cancun will agree on fundamental solutions that are in line with the latest scientific discoveries, so as to ensure the survival of all countries, peoples and ecosystems. it is also essential that the climate change conference takes key decisions on financing, in particular on the added value of financing adaptation, forestry, resource efficiency, technology transfer, monitoring, settlement and verification. it is also essential to ensure full transparency and strong political commitments to provide funding urgently.
in writing. − climate change is one of the biggest environmental problems of our time, and is also the cause of many natural disasters. we have already committed ourselves to reducing greenhouse gas emissions. sadly, it seems that we may not be able to reach our goals. on the other hand, no targets will be achieved unless the eu is joined by the world's major polluters. it is therefore irresponsible to set even higher targets before achieving less ambitious ones. on the other hand, if we start to pursue higher goals, we will corner ourselves. the eu industry, having to comply with stricter requirements, will not be able to compete on equal terms with the industry of countries that have not set such strict requirements. so while setting ambitious targets makes us leaders in the fight against climate change, it does not contribute to the eu's goal of becoming the most competitive region in the world. i voted against the final document because i am convinced that the eu can lead in reducing climate change and we all recognise our commitments to nature, but we must keep our targets as realistic as possible and move towards them in a consistent manner.
in writing. - i support this resolution. after more than 30 years, the process of decolonisation in western sahara remains unfinished. the situation of human rights, the violence in the civilian camp and the continuing conflict and its consequences for the entire region are of concern. i share the concern expressed in the resolution about the attacks on freedom of the press and information, which have also affected many european journalists. it is therefore necessary to seek an independent international investigation by the united nations to clarify the causes of civilian deaths and disappearances. it is also important to establish a human rights monitoring mechanism, to release human rights defenders and to allow free access to the territory of western sahara for journalists, independent observers and humanitarian organisations.
in writing. - i support this resolution. the threat to democratic freedoms, freedom of assembly, freedom of expression and freedom of the media in ukraine in recent months is a matter of concern. the public authorities should investigate and take action to rectify reports of violations of rights and freedoms. it is also important that the authorities refrain from any attempt to control, directly or indirectly, the content of the reports of national media outlets. the rules for elections are also still under discussion. it is therefore necessary to take active steps to improve the electoral system and to improve the legislation in this area before the next parliamentary elections. it is also necessary to strengthen the credibility, stability and independence of institutions and to ensure governance based on the principles of democracy and the rule of law. the ukrainian authorities should also make greater efforts to combat corruption.
in writing. − i support the parliament's resolution on ukraine's further steps towards political stability both at home and abroad. ukraine is an important partner of the european union in the implementation of the eastern neighbourhood policy and it is therefore essential to continue to pursue close cooperation, in particular in the area of visa. parliament called on the commission and the council to draw up an action plan on the introduction of visa-free regime for ukraine. in order to achieve long-term political stability both within the country and with neighbouring countries, it is important to implement constitutional reforms that help to create the right balance between the executive and the judiciary. further reforms are also needed to consolidate media freedom and pluralism. the public authorities are encouraged to take measures to ensure freedom of the press. the european union is firmly committed to helping ukraine pursue its reforms. the european union therefore continues to pursue intensive cooperation with ukraine in strengthening democracy in the country and accelerating its integration into the european union.
in writing. − the recent local elections in ukraine have extended the influence of president yanukovych's party in the regions. unfortunately, the change in the electoral system a few months before, partially returning to a minority system, further divided the position and the opposition, and provided grounds for accusing the authorities of undemocratic intentions. the resolution also expressed concern that violations of the right to freedom of expression, assembly and the media have increased in ukraine in recent years. i voted in favour of the resolution because it calls on ukraine, which is seeking long-term eu membership, to adopt laws regulating media activities in line with international standards, and stresses the need to strengthen the independence and effectiveness of state institutions. only in this way can the functioning of democracy and the rule of law in the country be ensured. i support the calls by the authors of the resolution for kiev to continue to modernise the infrastructure for the supply of energy resources and to implement projects to diversify the supply of energy sources together with the eu member states. i welcome the action plan on visa liberalisation, which sets out practical guidelines for its implementation as soon as possible: how to improve the rule of law and the implementation of fundamental rights in ukraine.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen, after the government elections in kiev earlier this year, most people thought that the end had come to ukraine. the point is that the new ukrainian government is more pragmatic and open to reforms than expected. close cooperation with ukraine is important if there is any chance of stability in the region. in recent years, ukraine has become a political football field, with russia and the west battling for influence in ukraine during the orange revolution and in recent elections. whether it chooses russia or the west, the eu now has the opportunity to pursue a new policy on ukraine that includes russia. my country, lithuania, also has a role to play. security and energy are two areas where cooperation will take a long time to recover from some of the disasters in kiev. however, it is important that ukraine's efforts to modernise its economy and integrate more closely into europe are guided by democratic principles and respect for human rights. i therefore sincerely hope that the next eu-ukraine summit in brussels on 22 november will bring about sustainable progress.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. energy conservation and renewable energy are europe's priorities in the fight against climate change. they can improve the eu's energy security and have a high potential for industrial development, innovation, regional development and job creation through the development of green energy. however, this struggle also has a negative impact on the competitiveness of the eu market as a whole. international trade rules are crucial in the fight against climate change, but the world trade organisation (wto) agreement does not make direct references to climate change, food security and the millennium development goals. i believe that the wto rules need to be amended to ensure that they are consistent with and comply with the commitments made under the kyoto protocol and multilateral environmental agreements. this would ensure that all countries adhere to the same standards, as some countries currently have a greater competitive advantage in subsidising energy prices and not applying restrictions or quotas to co2 emissions and are not interested in joining multilateral climate change agreements.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it sets out the strategic objectives and milestones that the european union could take further towards a better agreement on climate change. i would like to stress that the european union has already taken the first steps in the right direction in the areas of illegal timber imports, biofuels and aviation emissions. i would like to stress that the fight against climate change is a competitive indicator and that europe's priorities in this area are energy conservation and renewable energy, which can improve the energy security of the european union and which have a great potential in the areas of industry, innovation, regional development and job creation. the european union must now be more vocal, united and effective, as the copenhagen climate change summit resulted in a disappointing agreement. it should pay particular attention to setting targets for reducing expenditure and supporting developing countries in line with scientific advice and the european parliament's recommendations.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it contains proposals from the european parliament which provide for specific measures to promote corporate social responsibility in the context of the european union's trade policy. i would like to point out that the international economic and financial crisis has created a social crisis everywhere and has increased the need for new and stricter rules to better manage the global economy and not to expand at the expense of people. the european union's trade policy must therefore be consistent with the eu's general objectives and, in particular, its foreign policy objectives. it is also essential that europe ensures that its trade policy does not undermine but helps to defend its social model and its environmental policy. i agree with the european parliament's call for the european commission to improve its sustainability impact assessment model, as the economic, social, human rights and environmental impacts of trade negotiations, including climate change mitigation objectives, need to be properly reflected. in addition, the commission should follow up on trade agreements with eu partner countries and conduct impact assessment studies both before and after the signature of trade agreements, in particular with regard to vulnerable sectors.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because, as we know, the prosperity of international trade is justified if it actually contributes to economic development, employment and living standards. this is the only reason why the opening of trade is considered as positive and acceptable. however, it generates very strong resistance when it is carried out by destroying jobs or by degrading living conditions, social rights and the environment. trade policy cannot be reduced to the direct interests of a few economic operators. trade liberalisation has very different economic and social consequences that need to be managed and sometimes compensated for, as some benefit from liberalisation and others lose. the expansion of international trade is mutually beneficial only if certain conditions are met. this creates the need to regulate the opening of trade, its conditions, its pace, which is even more acute as globalization accelerates. europe, like the international community, is faced with the need to incorporate real guarantees of sustainable development and social rights into international trade rules. this requirement is in line with the objectives of both the un and the eu, as well as the high expectations of european citizens. until now, there has been virtually no link between trade and csr. linking trade rules to corporate social responsibility commitments would be an effective tool to help companies behave better and to help countries that sign trade agreements with the eu to comply with social and environmental standards.
in writing. − the two currently applicable block exemption regulations, specialisation (commission regulation (ec) no 2658/2000) and research and technological development (commission regulation (ec) no 2659/2000), will expire on 31 december 2010. the commission intends to adopt two new block exemption regulations replacing the previous ones and to adopt certain guidelines to help stakeholders analyse and understand whether their cooperation agreements are compatible with competition rules. there are now new rules, developed by the commission and the case law of the european court of justice, which need to be codified. i supported this resolution, in particular the european parliament's desire to ensure legal clarity for the subjects. i believe that the eu should take into account the experience of the eu's national competition authorities and of competition authorities in other countries around the world when developing guidelines. i believe that it would be useful to start international negotiations on the harmonisation of competition rules on a global scale, since many agreements and practices today are subject to different competitive regimes that do not allow entities to compete on the market on equal terms.
in writing. − i supported the proposal because horizontal agreements generally raise more competition concerns than vertical agreements and i am therefore pleased that the commission is taking a stricter approach to the market share threshold in the case of horizontal agreements. it is also considered that the regulatory framework should not be made more complex.
in recent years, the christian community in iraq has experienced an escalation of religious violence and persecution. christians, terrified of the bombings and the killings, are fleeing iraq. in condemning the brutal terrorist attacks, i would just like to say that the american invasion has exacerbated the situation in this regard and therefore places on their shoulders the responsibility to improve the situation. in general, the religious and national divisions, the hatred between the two in iraq, have reached such a level that the state is in serious danger. the democratic world expects the iraqi authorities to abolish the death penalty as soon as possible. the decision by the iraqi president not to execute tariq aziz is welcome, but we will be watching closely to see how the parliament handles this case.
according to the russian statistical agencies, in 2008 more than 1.5 million russian visas were issued to eu citizens and 3.5 million eu visas to russian citizens. this is more than a quarter of all schengen visas issued worldwide. the eu-russia visa policy is an important tool for deepening people-to-people relations and bringing russia closer to the eu. i would like to draw attention to the plight of the people of the kaliningrad region of the russian federation. most of the inhabitants of this russian island surrounded by eu countries are granted single-entry, short-stay schengen visas. many kaliningrad residents who travel through neighbouring eu countries have to pay a visa fee every time, stand in line at the consulates of eu countries. the representatives of kaliningrad's civil society organisations, who recently picketed outside the ec building in brussels, called for special conditions for the inhabitants of the enclave to travel to eu countries without tying the issue to the eu-russia visa-free regime negotiations.
for the s&d group. the single residence and work permit directive is indeed a very important directive. there has been much discussion, many opinions, many assessments, but no common solution, acceptable to all third-country workers legally living and working in the european union, has been reached. this directive should be a general framework directive on the rights of third-country workers and should provide the basis for the development of specific directives, as this is the only way in which it can contribute to the objective of the european union of establishing a common migration policy. the main problem, however, is that the framework of the directive which we have been promised is already being removed in the commission proposal, since it excludes certain categories of workers from its content, namely seasonal workers, workers on internal transfer and refugees. in other words, this directive does not establish the principle of legal equality for all third-country workers. it is therefore necessary, in order for this directive to be a reference point and a framework, to include all workers, and in particular seasonal workers, in its scope, otherwise migrant workers legally residing and working in the european union would not benefit from working conditions based on the principles of fairness, equality and equity. it should also be noted that migrant workers contribute to the economy of the european union both through their work and through their contributions in the form of taxes and social security contributions, and therefore must be guaranteed the same minimum rights and equal treatment in the labour market. if we are to vote on this directive tomorrow, we must be united, because the only way we can win this battle is to ensure that all groups of workers are included in this directive and that these workers have equal rights. i would like to stress that there can be no two-tier labour market, either within the european union or outside it. we cannot allow the creation of a second class of workers in the european union labour market, without any rights and without any guarantees. this is how we will remember all the social norms that have been established up to now.
human rights issues need to be addressed at both the european union level and the member states themselves, which must take responsibility for ensuring the fundamental rights of their citizens. for example, at european level, it is essential to develop a european union strategy on children's rights, as not all member states are able to ensure the protection of children's rights at national level. it is therefore necessary to develop practical measures to combat child abuse, sexual exploitation and child pornography. another priority area is disabled people, as they continue to face discrimination in social, professional and cultural life. a specific framework of european union legislation on the rights of persons with disabilities should therefore be established and a european union disability strategy should be developed. i would like to emphasise that the fight against trafficking in human beings, especially children and women, remains a major problem and one of the most serious human rights violations. it is also essential to tackle poverty and social exclusion, especially for the elderly, who have been the most affected by the crisis.
in writing. i congratulate both rapporteurs on their work, as this historic directive is a very important step in tackling trafficking in human beings on a large scale, with clear levels of penalties and sanctions. despite the fact that a number of european union and national legislation and multiannual political commitments have already been adopted in this area, it is estimated that several hundred thousand people are trafficked each year within and outside the european union. this shows that the fight against trafficking in human beings, especially women and children, remains a major problem and one of the most serious human rights violations. i would like to emphasise that, as children are more vulnerable and at higher risk of becoming victims of trafficking in human beings, special attention should be paid to this group of victims. given that trafficking in human beings is a modern form of slavery and a highly profitable business for organised crime, more attention is needed to prevention, protection of victims, social issues. in addition, a hostile environment must be created for traffickers. the level of penalties and sanctions for persons who profit from trafficking in human beings should reflect the seriousness of the offence and act as an effective deterrent. in addition to penalties, member states should include sanctions such as the seizure of property and take the necessary measures to seize and confiscate the means of and the profits from the criminal activity, as this would be an economic blow to the activity.
today, the european parliament has demonstrated its firm stance in taking a step towards closer cooperation between the european union and the south caucasus. the south caucasus and georgia are regions of strategic importance to the european union. my country, lithuania, has always had a special relationship with georgia. we have supported it in the most difficult moments and not only at the state level, as government structures, but also the huge support of the public has always been given to this state. i believe that the whole european union wants georgia to come into the family of european nations. this country is making a huge effort to be part of europe, and we must treat it accordingly. visa facilitation will help to ensure the mobility of people, their cooperation and, at the same time, economic and cultural links. i would like to congratulate georgia and its people on the decision taken by the european parliament today. the european union sends them a message that they are welcome in the european union.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report, which supports the proposal for a directive to cover both refugees recognised under the geneva convention and persons enjoying subsidiary protection. in its report, the european parliament endorses the european commission's proposal and takes account of the obligation to protect the interests of beneficiaries of international protection. i would like to point out that the current situation of third-country nationals who are beneficiaries of international protection is confusing and unclear, as there are no harmonised conditions for granting status. the commission's proposal will make the conditions and procedure for granting status simpler and clearer in the european union. in addition, this new directive will create favourable conditions for long-term residents who are granted international protection to reside in a member state other than the one in which their status was recognised.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution which approves the conclusion of an agreement with georgia on the facilitation of the issuance of visas. this agreement shows a clear desire for rapprochement between georgia and the eu which has been expressed in recent years through a series of significant political acts, ranging from improved bilateral cooperation between the eu and georgia to georgia's accession to the council of europe and the european convention on human rights. i supported the resolution as i strongly believe that the agreement on the facilitation of the issuance of visas will facilitate people-to-people contacts, which are a very important condition for the development of economic, social, humanitarian, cultural, scientific and other ties. i believe that the elimination of the visa requirement for the citizens of georgia will mark an important step on their path to european integration. a visa facilitation regime is of great significance in people’s lives as it strengthens contacts between peoples and brings about the realisation of the idea of free movement, which is one of the fundamental rights in europe.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this european parliament recommendation on visa facilitation for georgia because the new enlargement of the european union provided an opportunity for the european union to create a new framework for cooperation with its neighbours to the east and south. the visa facilitation agreement will in particular facilitate personal contacts between citizens, which is essential to ensure the strengthening of economic, humanitarian, cultural, scientific and other links. in addition, visa facilitation is a vital element in people's lives, which will help to implement the principle of free movement, i.e. to guarantee one of the fundamental human rights in europe. in addition, the abolition of the visa regime for georgian citizens will be an important step in their european integration. i share the european parliament's view that the agreements concluded by the european union with georgia, such as the agreement on the readmission of persons residing without authorisation and the agreement on the facilitation of the issuing of visas prima per tutti, represent a very important milestone in the progress of relations between the european union and georgia. in addition, such agreements are also important from a regional perspective, as they will support the eu's efforts to strengthen cooperation with other countries in the region.
in writing. − i supported this resolution as it is obvious that the multiplication of major disasters outside the european union in recent years has led to increased calls to improve the effectiveness of the existing eu disaster response capacity. one of the major steps towards a more integrated response has been the inclusion of civil protection and humanitarian aid under the portfolio of one single commissioner to create opportunities for enhanced coherence and coordination of the eu disaster response. it is, however, regrettable that two years after the publication of the 2008 commission communication, many actions from the action plan still remain to be implemented, while the eu response to recent crises has revealed the limitations of the existing system. if efforts have recently been made to reinforce coherence and coordination between the civil protection and humanitarian aid dimensions of the eu response, steps towards the creation of a european civil protection force have remained minimal. therefore, a strong political will is needed for a more efficient and rapid response to emergencies.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. disasters affecting people and causing economic and environmental damage are increasing in number, frequency and geographical scope worldwide. while progress has been made in recent years towards a more coordinated eu approach to disaster response, further efforts are needed to ensure effective, coordinated and coordinated eu action in this area. it is necessary to develop an action plan setting out concrete actions to develop a more integrated coordination of the different disaster response measures, to assess possible actions by the eu and the member states. in order to coordinate assistance effectively outside the european union, the newly created european external action service should be involved in these activities. it is also necessary to ensure that sufficient financial resources are available to finance the necessary aid measures.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because, in the face of the increasing frequency of major natural or man-made disasters outside the territory of the european union, there is a growing number of proposals to restructure the european union's disaster response capacity and to increase the effectiveness of the existing european union's disaster response capacity. for example, the lack of publicity of the european union's response is one of the main problems in disaster response. clear agreements and procedures are therefore necessary to ensure double visibility, at member state and union level, of the civil protection resources deployed by member states under the european union civil protection mechanism. the other problem of the european union's response is that of coordination, and the european parliament therefore expects the european external action service to create joint crisis response capacities in the future and to rationalise existing crisis platforms. in addition, the commission and the council should also ensure clear coordination mechanisms between themselves with regard to the deployment of the european union's military resources in response to disasters.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this important resolution because it is high time to pay attention to chemical, biological, radiological and nuclear (cbrn) risks which present a new dimension of possible terrorist attacks, accidents, natural disasters and/or pandemics due to their transnational character and mass-scale victimization. it is necessary to start dealing with such materials, and their proper storage, limited access, controls, etc., from the very beginning and not just create ex post facto intervention programmes. at the same time, aftermath intervention demands large-scale cooperation between different national authorities inside a member state. therefore, the cbrn action plan should ensure the efficient interaction of national and eu initiatives in addressing cbrn risks and preparing the necessary response. considering that cbrn accidents or attacks pose serious threats to the security of the people living in the european union and can disrupt critical infrastructure and the normal functioning of societies in any or several eu member states and are oblivious to borders, i support the european parliament resolution which calls on the council and the commission to rapidly review and strengthen the action plan according to the recommendations in this report and to ensure its swift implementation.
in writing. − chbrb disasters, whether accidental or as a result of terrorist attacks, pose a serious threat to the safety and health of eu citizens and cause problems of environmental pollution and contamination. although the number of incidents involving chbrbs in the eu has been relatively low so far and limited to industrial accidents or the global increase in the number of dangerous pathogens, the risk of accidents or incidents remains. i voted in favour of this report because i agree with the rapporteur's position that the security action plan proposed by the european commission, which aims to ensure effective coordination of national and eu initiatives to combat chbrb threats and to help prepare for an appropriate response, does not sufficiently assess the risks of threats that extend beyond the eu's borders. i believe that the international threat of chbrbs must be properly assessed and integrated in the security action plan, and that preventive action must therefore ensure an international dimension, in particular by emphasising the implementation of international agreements in the field of nuclear and chemical weapons.
in writing. − i voted in favour of increasing chemical, biological, radiological and nuclear safety in the eu and i am delighted to have had the opportunity to be the shadow opinion rapporteur on this issue. while the prevention and protection of accidents caused by these substances is a matter for the member states, a common approach is needed. this ec communication is a step towards a common action plan to protect the eu public from all kinds of disasters. in these times of great instability, threats are not only caused by irresponsible human activities or accidents but also by deliberate actions. this is particularly relevant in my country, lithuania, which is beset by chemical and nuclear dangers. the eu has also been involved in the development of the baltic sea region, with the aim of promoting the development of the baltic sea region. we must assess the situation, the safety standards in place and their relevance to the needs of the present. only after assessing the current situation can we decide whether we need new safety standards. this system of evaluation and standardisation must be flexible enough to adapt to changing technologies that not only make our lives easier but can also be lethal weapons in the hands of terrorists. on the other hand, security should not become a reason or pretext for over-centralising civil security. while common security standards and oversight would certainly facilitate security, we should not create new institutions or overly complex administrative procedures in every case.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this important resolution as the implementation of the cohesion policy is predominantly decentralised and based on sub-national authorities assuming responsibility. i agree that the multi-level approach should be applied not only vertically but also horizontally, among actors of the same level, in all shared-competence union policies including the cohesion policy, which plays a forerunner role in the application of multi-level governance, as an instrument to improve the quality of decision-making processes through the active involvement of sub-national authorities from the pre-legislative phase of the debates. also, it is important to stress that funding application procedures are too complicated, and an excessive number of checks are likely to discourage potential beneficiaries of cohesion policy from the efficient absorption of funds and maximisation of their impact. therefore, there must be sufficient administrative capacity both at eu and at regional and local level in order to increase the added value of the cohesion policy and ensure the sustainability of actions.
in writing. − the european union's cohesion policy is the key instrument for ensuring sustainable regional development. cohesion policy is implemented in a highly decentralised manner and is based on the increased responsibility of subnational authorities. the lisbon treaty has given territorial authorities greater scope for participation in decision-making, which would allow for better and more effective implementation of cohesion policy measures adapted to the needs of individual regions and their people. i voted in favour of this report. i believe that the potential of territorial cooperation, which has not been exploited in some regions, needs to be better exploited and that the principle of partnership needs to be promoted more actively, ensuring effective cooperation between the private and public sectors.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because cohesion policy improves decision-making processes through multi-level governance and active involvement of subnational institutions. the current supervision by the european commission is considered to be inadequate and cannot compensate for ineffective control systems at national level over the entire multiannual period. in order to improve the control system and to provide more support to subnational authorities, it is therefore necessary for the commission to strengthen its supervision and procedures. in addition, national and regional managing authorities should be guaranteed increased investment in financial assistance and training to increase the capacity and knowledge of the authorities responsible for managing the programmes. it should also be noted that the current complexity of the application procedure for funding often discourages potential beneficiaries from cohesion policy, and that a more user-friendly policy with more harmonised structural funds rules should be developed in the future.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the 2006 report of the european court of auditors shows that the cohesion policy control systems in place are not sufficiently effective and that the error rate for reimbursing expenditure is too high (12%). in the 2008 annual report, this figure is confirmed as 11% of the funds unduly paid. these errors are partly due to the complexity of the rules governing the structural funds. the system of shared responsibility inherent in cohesion policy makes the interpretation and application of the rules very difficult, as many actors are involved. the rules should therefore be simplified in order to ensure user-friendly procedures and not to discourage potential beneficiaries from participating in projects.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report, which aims to harness the know-how and experience of immigration liaison officers for the benefit of frontex and vice-versa. this was not a matter addressed in the original regulation adopted in 2004. there is a clear need for the amendments and for new issues to be included in order to improve the functioning of frontex, such as exploitation of the information obtained through the liaison officer networks and exchange of this via iconet (secure information and coordination network for member states’ migration management services), accessing the external borders fund to promote the creation of liaison officer networks and facilitate their operation and, lastly, expediting the system for the presentation of half-yearly reports. i am convinced that those amendments are very timely and necessary as the eu has to face and manage the increased flow of legal – and also illegal – immigration.
in writing. − i support the amendment to the regulation to strengthen cooperation between the frontex agency and immigration liaison officers. more active cooperation would help frontex to fulfil its key functions in combating illegal migration, preventing illegal immigration and returning illegal immigrants. as the agency's activities relate to migration within the eu, immigration liaison officers deployed in third countries could contribute to the agency's objectives outside the eu. this would enable the agency to exchange information more effectively with the competent authorities of third countries or international organisations on eu border control issues, in the training of border guards, the exchange of operational information and joint operations. the exchange of information could take place through the iconet network (a secure information and coordination network for member states' migration management services).
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because of the importance of creating a network of immigration liaison officers in the face of increased migration flows. given that the current economic, financial and social crisis has further fuelled the migration of people, the european union must do everything possible to manage migration flows properly. the establishment of the network would not only help to regulate legal, irregular and illegal immigration, but also improve operational cooperation between member states and improve the semi-annual reporting system. moreover, closer cooperation between officials would guarantee a better exchange of technical knowledge and contacts with colleagues working in third countries. i would like to emphasise that the european union institutions should continuously strive to ensure a safe and fast immigration process and to guarantee that european union citizens are protected against the potential risks posed by illegal and irregular immigration. this can only be achieved by using all the effective international and internal cooperation instruments available.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution as economic immigration is a growing challenge for the eu. therefore, the eu must develop balanced legal immigration channels which meet the needs of our labour markets while taking account of the demographic and economic challenges which the eu will face in the near future. i am convinced that the adoption of this directive will simplify procedures for the admission of third-country nationals for employment purposes, will reduce administrative costs and also will make it easier to check on people admitted to the territory of a member state and authorised to work there. with reference to the simplified procedure, i have to stress that the implementation of legal requirements and transparency of decision-making by national authorities must be ensured. i agree with the rapporteur that these decisions on the admission of third-country nationals for employment purposes have a strong impact on the life of the persons concerned, and must therefore be taken in a way which is completely transparent.
in writing. − today, the european parliament, in voting on a single directive on residence and work permits for third-country workers, blocked the european commission's proposal and returned it to the responsible parliamentary committees to further develop the content of the proposal for a directive. this vote is a victory for the group of the progressive alliance of socialists and democrats in the european parliament and for all workers in the european union. i would like to stress that since the framework for the directive promised to us was already removed in the commission proposal, because it excludes certain categories of workers, such as seasonal workers, workers on internal transfer, refugees, the european parliament could not give its consent to such a directive. this directive should be a general framework directive on the rights of third-country workers and a basis for the preparation of specific directives, since this is the only way to achieve the objective of the european union to establish a common migration policy. we have shown by our vote that the european union cannot create a double labour market with double standards, because all the social standards that have been established so far will be forgotten.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because it supports council's proposal for a directive which provides clear measures concerning the prevention of crime and victim protection in particular. it is important that victims of violence should not only be prevented in their country from being harmed by offenders, but should also have these kinds of preventive measures within the whole of the european union. the adoption and implementation of this directive would show that the eu is determined to put into practice the stockholm action plan, the european union initiative to maintain and develop an area of freedom, security and justice. it is important to stress that a european protection order should be applicable to all victims of crime, including victims of human trafficking, forced marriages, honour killings, gender violence, witnesses, victims of terrorism and organised crime, regardless of the age or sex of the victim, where there is an identified aggressor. furthermore, protection of victims does not mean just physical protection, but also includes the dignity of victims. therefore, i strongly agree that it is imperative to include protection for victims that aims to restore their dignity and the respect due to them as human beings.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the proposal for a directive on the european protection order, as its main objectives are the prevention of crime and the protection of victims. at present, following the issuance of a victim protection order, the enforcement measures imposed by it are only applicable in the issuing member state. the european union must do everything it can to prevent crime and persecution at european level. only in this way can we guarantee a safe environment for the victim and not restrict the victim's right to mobility. although criminal, civil and administrative proceedings vary across member states, all parties must work together to prevent repeat offences. i think it is very important to include the moral support aspect in this proposal. many victims suffer from the various psychological and moral effects of the crime long after the crime has been committed. the european union must provide not only prevention and protection but also moral support for the victims so that they can start a new life in any european country.
in writing. − i supported this resolution as trafficking in human beings is a modern form of slavery, a serious crime and a severe violation of fundamental human rights. the treaty of lisbon has strengthened eu action in the field of judicial and police cooperation in criminal matters, including in combating trafficking in human beings, and the european parliament, in becoming co-legislator, has a full role to play. i strongly support the ep proposal that penalties for traffickers need to be increased to the level of the 2009 proposal, the confiscation of assets should be applied, and assistance to victims, and in particular to child victims, should be further developed. i am convinced that the ep should clearly express the requirement for member states to do more in order to discourage demand for the services of victims of human trafficking, through awareness-raising campaigns, education, training and so on, all of which should fully incorporate a gender perspective. it is important to stress that criminal sanctions against employers who use trafficked labour have a strong preventive effect, so these must be developed further.
in writing. - i support this report. trafficking in human beings is a serious crime and a serious violation of fundamental human rights. the scale of this crime is widespread worldwide and includes various forms of crime related to sexual exploitation, forced labour, illicit trafficking in human organs, illegal adoption, etc. it is therefore necessary to take immediate and active action to develop an anti-trafficking strategy with a strong focus on combating and preventing trafficking in human beings. it is also necessary to ensure effective means of assistance and support for victims, including the right to compensation, necessary medical treatment, legal and psychological assistance free of charge, with particular attention to children. the strategy to combat trafficking in human beings should also include social, immigration, asylum and reintegration policy aspects. in addition, in order to reduce the scale of crime, it is necessary to provide for effective and appropriate penalties for infringements of the provisions of the directive.
in writing. − today, the european parliament adopted a historic and highly significant directive to strengthen the fight against trafficking in human beings. this directive is a major step forward in tackling the scale of this heinous crime by introducing stricter penalties and sanctions and by further strengthening prevention and protection of victims. i voted in favour of this report because traffickers in human beings must be given a stronger response to the atrocities they commit and there is a need for stronger penalties and sanctions to reflect the seriousness of these crimes and to act as an effective deterrent. the new directive contains a very important provision that in addition to penalties, member states should include sanctions such as the seizure of property and take the necessary measures to seize and confiscate the means of criminal activity and the profits derived from this business, as this is the only way to economically punish this criminal activity. i would like to point out that, as the scale of this crime increases in europe, the european union's approach must be even more human rights-oriented, with a focus on prevention, protection of victims, return and reintegration policies, social issues.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution which approves the conclusion of the agreement with georgia on readmission of persons residing without authorisation, which will push out the frontiers of the area of liberty and security, extending it beyond the european union and encompassing this neighbouring country. i am convinced that this agreement is a step forward in the relations between the european union and georgia, it shows the european aspirations of georgia, and will thus represent a strong signal on the part of the european union. it will promote friendly relations, stability, security and the well-being of citizens, which is so much needed in the region. moreover, it will encourage georgia to implement the reforms needed in the area of freedom, security and justice. i strongly support the eu-georgia agreement on readmission of persons residing without authorisation as a sufficiently sound framework is in place in georgia to ensure that the rights of those persons treated under the agreement will be respected.
in writing. − the eu's enlargement in 2004 and 2009 led the eu to create a new framework of cooperation and partnership with the countries to the east and south of the eu's external borders, to support their security, stability and development and to avoid the fragmentation of the new continent of europe. i voted for this deal. the european union must fulfil its obligations towards the states of the south caucasus and ensure the security of the area. the region is of strategic importance to the european union and it must help the region to develop economically and commercially. in its actions, the eu must, in particular, respect the principles of good governance, full respect for human rights and democracy. i believe that this agreement with georgia on readmission is important from a regional perspective and will also underpin the eu's efforts to strengthen cooperation with other countries in the region.
in writing. − i supported this resolution as it provides a very much needed framework highlighting the integrating role of cohesion policy and the contribution it makes to increasing the eu’s global competitiveness. cohesion policy is the eu’s key policy for enabling the regions to face growing challenges, such as climate change, population ageing, social migration, energy, or the economic and financial crisis, in the best possible way and hence to help increase the eu’s global economic competitiveness. i support the rapporteur that this could be achieved by ensuring common living standards for all eu citizens and supporting development by harnessing local and regional specificities, thereby generating added value and economic productivity. it is important to stress that once the objective has been met of ensuring a similar quality of life by enabling access to infrastructure and quality services, the regions can concentrate on measures aimed at developing local economic potential, with an important stage being the development of local research, development and innovation policies and the appropriate regional infrastructure.
in writing. − the competitiveness of the european union will depend directly on the ability of its internal policies to make decisions that are able to meet today's challenges. a sustainable regional and cohesion policy is a necessary factor in removing obstacles to economic development and in increasing competitiveness in the internal market and globally. the importance of a coherent and coordinated regional policy in the european union is already clear. the financial and economic crisis that followed, and the gas crisis that preceded it, have shown that the lack of a proper regional policy is damaging europe as a whole. i believe that cohesion policy is a key eu policy area, and its implementation will determine whether the regions are able to overcome the challenges they face. i agree with the rapporteur that the development of territorial cohesion policy will have a direct impact on the achievement of the objectives of the eu 2020 strategy and that it is necessary to analyse as soon as possible whether eu support to specific regions is based on concrete results that ensure the sustainability of regional policy.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because we need to discuss measures taken at european and national level on social and territorial cohesion to achieve the european union's policy objectives, including increasing global competitiveness. indeed, cohesion policy is a key eu policy area, which will enable regions to tackle problems such as the economic and financial crisis, climate change, population ageing, social migration or energy in the best possible way. these objectives can therefore be achieved by supporting development at local and regional level and, of course, by ensuring common living standards for all eu citizens. i would like to emphasise that europe is a place of solidarity and that it is therefore essential to reduce the disparities in the level of development of europe's regions and to ensure economic, social and territorial cohesion. moreover, cohesion policy must be more results-oriented and it is important to aim for even greater efficiency and utility, as this will only make it more convenient and useful for consumers. the implementation of cohesion and regional policy and the independence and coverage of all european regions are essential for the achievement of the europe 2020 objectives.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because, in accordance with the principles of the treaties, cohesion policy, which aims to reduce disparities in the development of regions and to prepare them to face long- and short-term challenges (globalisation, demographic change, rural depopulation, climate change, protection of biodiversity) taking into account their specific strengths and weaknesses, is clearly of crucial importance for the process of european integration. a strong and well-funded cohesion policy is a prerequisite for achieving the objectives of the eu 2020 strategy. all regions must develop in a sustainable way. it is welcome that the commission is being asked to examine and propose a methodology for action to promote rural-urban partnerships, combat rural depopulation and at the same time promote sustainable urban development, as almost 80% of the eu population live in cities. urban and rural areas play a dynamic role in ensuring the economic development of regions. the next programming period should focus on investments in urban and suburban projects and ensure better coordination of rural programmes. there is a need to promote entrepreneurship and support small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) in recognition of their role in increasing the competitiveness of the economy and creating new jobs, facilitate access to finance, and ensure simplified access to risk capital and microfinance. the commission should further simplify the rules for the use of the structural funds and the cohesion fund in order to reduce the administrative burden on beneficiaries.
