for the first time since the entry into force of the new treaty of lisbon, the european parliament has used its powers to participate fully in the formulation of the budget of the european union. the failure of the first attempt to agree and include the legitimate demands of the european parliament shows that there are still institutional contradictions, which should not exist in principle, and which create obstacles to effective interinstitutional cooperation. it is perhaps no secret that the aim of all the institutions of the european union is to ensure that the adoption of agreements of major importance to the european union as a whole and its citizens proceeds as smoothly as possible. the future therefore requires a fundamental change in the very principles of the institutions' functioning, and the involvement of the european parliament in all stages of the negotiations, particularly in the adoption of the budget, must be considered as crucial to the implementation of the principle of representative democracy.
first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on the preparation of this very important document. a considerable amount of work has already been done on the establishment and functioning of a financial control system in the european union. the role of credit rating agencies is of particular importance as they have a direct impact on the functioning of the financial system and on its reliability and stability. i think that the big problem is that the financial supervisory authorities of the european union, which are being created, do not yet have the real leverage to exert direct influence and make binding decisions. i therefore support the proposals to give one of the european supervisory authorities, in this case the european securities and markets authority, greater powers to ensure and monitor the transparency of the activities of the certified credit rating agencies in the european union in order to avoid distortions of the principles of the financial market and the single internal market.
i congratulate the delegation of the european parliament, the member of the commission and the entire european union on the unanimous vote in cancún. last year we regretted the copenhagen results, or rather the lack of them, today we are happy about cancún, but my joy is still genetically reserved, simply because i want more. it is clear that negotiating is a huge and complex task. having so many different positions is a challenge. but what worries me most is the public image of this issue. and, in fact, in the eyes of the public, he doesn't look very victorious. the question of the difficulty of reaching an agreement, i mean, of reaching a legally binding instrument, which is important to all of us, is becoming a pressing issue. i agree with the commission that we have to do our homework, that we have to achieve the 20% target ourselves by various means. and of course it's always easier to just go up against those who might not be doing something, and point a finger and say, well, maybe we don't need to either. and i still want to ask you, despite the fact that you say you've achieved this, did you really not expect a little bit more from cancun?
member of the commission. − women and men are exposed to health risks in different ways with consequently different health outcomes. women live on average six years longer than men but most of these additional years are lived in worse health. certain diseases, such as osteoporosis, have a much higher incidence among women. this is why the eu strategy for equality between women and men (2010-2015), which the commission has recently adopted, points to the need for medical research and healthcare to reflect these differences. the strategy stresses that health services need to continue the process of adaptation to properly reflect the specific needs of women and men. the strategy for these actions, specifically targeting women’s health, includes awareness-raising seminars on women’s health and exchanging best practices on gender-specific health policies. we are not starting from scratch here. the eu health strategy, adopted three years ago, already signalled the need to take gender-related issues into account in health policy-making. to this end, the community health programme has supported many projects on women’s health aimed at promoting a better understanding and recognition of women’s health needs. a good example of how the eu can help member states improve women’s health is cancer screening. it is estimated that 32 000 deaths from breast cancer alone could be prevented each year across the european union if effective national screening programmes for breast cancer were implemented. this is why the commission developed european guidelines on breast cancer screening. finally, i am pleased to say that earlier this year the commission published a report on women’s health, which contributes to raising awareness of the need for policies that are more responsive to gender concerns.
i'd like to make a few more clarifications. do we have enough data on women's health protection in the member states? do we know what the impact of this particular economic and financial crisis has been on women's health? and should we not also investigate the impact of poverty on women's health, especially on the health of older women? does poverty make quality health care available to women?
yes, i have a question on belarus. thank you for your presents on the eve of christmas. recently, president lukashenko visited moscow and signed very important agreements with president medvedev. how would you assess the new situation before the elections and after the signing of these agreements?
although many legislative instruments have been developed in the field of energy efficiency and savings, not all of them are conducive to achieving the desired results. scientific studies suggest that at current rates of progress we will fall short of about half of the 20% target. more binding measures and increased controls in the member states are therefore necessary. i believe that the european commission, when reviewing the energy efficiency action plan, must take very seriously the fact that member states are not making sufficient use of the possibilities for energy savings, which does not allow for increased energy independence. it is also necessary to set binding energy efficiency targets for individual member states and to monitor their implementation, otherwise the huge potential for energy savings will remain untapped.
i really agree with all the points made today about the creation of a crisis management mechanism and about the additional measures which i believe both the european council and parliament will consider in the future. indeed, there have been many conflicting assessments today and perhaps a few accusations of past mistakes. they were everywhere. there were those from the member states, from the european commission, from the council, and there were those from commercial banks, whose activities will, i believe, also be closely examined in the future. i want to talk about something else. i liked the idea expressed by president barroso that in this difficult situation we must work together, in solidarity, shoulder to shoulder, and i would really like to ask, mr commissioner, to make every effort to ensure that all countries, whether they are members of the euro area or not, participate in this new crisis management mechanism. because we have opened up our markets to the european union, we pay a single sum of money into the budget and many other things.
today is a very joyous and important day in the european parliament. i very much hope that we will adopt this mechanism, which will allow citizens to participate in shaping the future of the european union, not only by electing the european parliament, but also by actively participating in the creative process themselves. it is very important that the citizens' voice is heard. i myself had the opportunity to represent tens of thousands of citizens of the european union in a petition to the european parliament a few years ago, and i am very pleased that the european parliament then adopted a resolution on the basis of the petition. it is a great thing to feel that your voice, as a citizen, is being heard in the institutions of the european union. there must be a dialogue between the european commission and the citizens, there must be no situation which would demotivate the citizens, and this is a step towards closer cooperation not only between the institutions and the citizens, but also between the citizens themselves. it is a process of knowledge, of identifying certain problems and of solving them together, but it must be a process that is safe, transparent and based on the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. i am pleased that agreement has been reached on a regulation on the citizens' initiative less than a year after the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. this regulation is a major step towards strengthening the principles of democracy in the european union. giving one million citizens the right to initiate the legislative process will stimulate interest in and participation in european politics. it is very important that parliament has succeeded in simplifying the procedure for submitting and organising an initiative. the initiative will only be successful if it is easy to implement and accessible to everyone. it must be clear and understandable, and citizens must be able and willing to participate. on the other hand, it is also necessary to ensure transparency in the organisation and funding of this initiative, which must remain the expression of the citizens themselves and not of political or other influential groups. i believe that the final text of the regulation we are voting on today will help to achieve this balance. one of the objectives of the lisbon treaty is to bring citizens closer to europe, and the citizens' initiative will help to achieve this by establishing this new, unique form of civic participation in politics.
i welcome the very important report by the rapporteur, mr bendtsen, on the review of the energy efficiency action plan. i voted for it because i think it is important not only for economic reasons but also in the light of the cancun summit. it has achieved a number of such common approaches, and it is important to continue the work the european union has started at home on reducing co2 emissions. energy efficiency is one of the most accurate ways. member states must have effective national action plans in this area, including financial mechanisms. the commission's proposal is based on the principle of subsidiarity. every european should benefit from solutions like today's, because we are talking about many independent sectors - transport, new technologies, efficiency in buildings, production and transmission infrastructure. this document is a set of measures that not only protect the environment but also help national economies.
in writing. − i support the european parliament's decision to provide additional financial support for the lifelong learning and competitiveness and innovation programmes. the lisbon strategy 2011 aim of making the european union a competitive world and a knowledge-based economy, based on sustainable economic development and new jobs for better social cohesion, could be achieved through these programmes. in order to enhance the european union's competitiveness in the world, particular attention should be paid to small and medium-sized enterprises by providing them with the necessary assistance and financial support. in addition, investments in green innovation, research and development would promote the use of renewable energy sources, which would help create new sustainable jobs in a wide range of sectors, including energy, manufacturing, transport.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution and supported the eu budget for the year 2011. i welcome the agreement finally reached between the council, commission and european parliament and i hope it will be a sustainable budget that can be fully and predictably implemented from the beginning of the financial year. with the adoption of this resolution we, the european parliament, secure financing and continuity of the budget agreed upon by the council and the committee on budgets. i am convinced that it was necessary to allocate more funds to education, research and innovation as the eu needs to increase its efficiency and competitiveness to be able to rise from the financial and economic crisis. in order to reach this goal, the eu has to create a long-term strategy and this budget should be a part of it.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the 2011 budget. this budget was adopted under the new procedure provided for by the treaty of lisbon. these long and difficult negotiations in the search for an agreement on the 2011 budget show that, once the new budgetary principles come into force, we will be forced to seek rational institutional compromises on the most important eu policy issues. for the first time since the entry into force of the new treaty, the european parliament has used its powers to participate fully in the formulation of the eu budget. the failure of the first attempt to agree and incorporate the ep's legitimate requirements shows that there are still institutional contradictions, which in principle should not exist and which create obstacles to effective institutional cooperation. the aim of all eu institutions is to ensure that the process of adopting agreements of major importance to the eu as a whole and its citizens runs as smoothly as possible. i therefore believe that the future will require a fundamental change in the very principles of the institutions' work and that the involvement of the european parliament in all stages of the negotiations, particularly in the budget, must be regarded as a crucial element in the implementation of the principle of representative democracy.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because i believe that the eu needs to cooperate more closely in the area of the law applicable to divorce and legal separation. i believe that an eu regulation in this area should be universal, i.e. that any law of a participating member state, a non-participating member state or a non-eu member state can be declared applicable on the basis of its universal conflict-of-law rules. the eu has set itself the objective of fostering and developing an area of freedom, security and justice in which the free movement of persons is ensured, and therefore, in order to allow spouses to choose freely the law with which they are closely connected, or, if they do not choose the law, in order to have that law applicable to their divorce or separation, it should apply even if it is not the law of a participating member state. increased mobility of citizens requires more flexibility and more legal certainty, which a new eu regulation can provide.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report by the european parliament on enhanced cooperation in the area of the law applicable to divorce and legal separation, as a clear and comprehensive legal framework is needed to apply the rules relating to the applicable law. i would like to point out that one of the main objectives of the european union is to preserve and develop an area of freedom, security and justice in which the free movement of persons is ensured. the legal situation at european level is currently very confusing as regards the divorce or separation of spouses of different nationalities, as it is not clear which law should apply. this often leads to a rush to court, where one spouse files for divorce before the other, in order to ensure that the case is dealt with under a particular legal system that better protects his interests. i would stress that the proposed new regulation should provide legal certainty for the couples concerned and ensure predictability and flexibility.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because i believe that a monitoring and surveillance tool for credit rating agencies is necessary. the initiative was affected by the global financial crisis, which was also partly the fault of credit rating agencies. i support the european commission's proposal that the european securities and markets authority (esma) should accredit and supervise credit rating agencies. however, it is necessary for esma to be able to exercise its powers from the time of its establishment in order to ensure the sound supervision of credit rating agencies operating in the european union and of third-country agencies whose ratings are to be authorised for use in the european union.
in writing. − the emergence of the global financial crisis has made it clear that a tool for the monitoring and supervision of credit rating agencies is needed. there is a need for a common system of supervision and control of credit rating agencies in the european union. i voted in favor of this important document. in 2009, regulation (ec) no 1060/2009 was adopted on credit rating agencies. its adoption has enabled the establishment at european level of a system for the registration and supervision of credit rating agencies that provide credit ratings for use in the european union. it also lays down the conditions for the use of ratings provided by third country agencies in the european union. the need for a robust supervisory and control system in the european union is why i support the proposed amendments which will strengthen the european securities and markets authority. the authority should be fully involved in the supervision of credit rating agencies operating in the eu and should effectively exercise the powers conferred on it.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the economic and financial crisis which has affected the world has shown the need for a tool for the monitoring and supervision of credit rating agencies. for this reason, the european commission has submitted a proposal for a regulation setting out the procedure by which the european securities and markets authority should accredit and supervise credit rating agencies. the important point is that this authority will be given independent supervisory powers, as well as investigative powers and it will be able to punish for non-compliance with this regulation. i agree with the european parliament's position that there is a need for a common system of supervision of credit rating agencies and control of their products in the european union. parliament also proposed to focus on the involvement of the european securities and markets authority in the supervisory activities of the agencies and the definition of new tasks and new powers for the institution. it is essential that the european securities and markets authority be able to exercise its powers from the moment of its establishment in order to ensure the sound supervision of credit rating agencies operating in the european union and of third-country agencies whose ratings are to be authorised for use in the european union.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the global financial crisis, which is also partly the fault of credit rating agencies, has made it clear that a monitoring and surveillance tool for credit rating agencies is needed. to this end, the regulation on credit rating agencies was adopted in 2009. its adoption has enabled the establishment at european level of a system for the registration and supervision of credit rating agencies that provide credit ratings for use in the european union. it also lays down the conditions for the use of ratings provided by third country agencies in the european union. the establishment of the european supervisory mechanism, which will enter into force on 1 january 2011, provides for esma to exercise the supervisory powers conferred on it, in particular in relation to credit rating agencies. it is therefore necessary to amend the regulation on credit rating agencies in order to establish the procedure according to which esma should accredit and supervise credit rating agencies. the authority will be granted independent supervisory powers, including investigative powers, and will be able to impose sanctions for non-compliance with this regulation. the fines shall be fixed by the member states.
in writing. − i voted for this document because it contributes to the much needed simplification of the eu legal framework. i believe that the eight directives currently in force in the field of metrology make it more difficult than it is easy to work in this field. at the same time, i support the rapporteur's position that member states should be given more time to examine whether repealing directives would create legal uncertainty, which would require harmonisation of rules at european level. i therefore believe that a decision should be adopted repealing the directives, while allowing sufficient time to examine the possible consequences in the light of a wider review of the main legal instruments in this area.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this important resolution because i am convinced that the european citizens' initiative will be an important tool for setting thematic guidelines and will encourage more active international debate in the eu. the citizens' initiative gives european citizens the right to submit a proposal for a legislative act. the effectiveness of the initiative requires that the organisers of the european citizens' initiative are encouraged to form a citizens' committee composed of representatives from different member states. this would ensure that questions of a truly european nature are raised and help to gather signatures from the outset. i believe that the success of a citizens' initiative depends on the eu regulation being tailored to the citizens and not imposing burdensome requirements that frustrate the organisers. it is also essential that the implementation of the initiative complies with eu data protection requirements and that the implementation process of the initiative is fully transparent from start to finish. the citizens' initiative is a new instrument of participatory democracy at european level and therefore great attention should be paid to communication and information campaigns to raise awareness of the european citizens' initiative.
in writing. − i support the decision taken by the european parliament on the right of the european citizens' initiative to submit a legislative proposal. it is hoped that the initiative will bring citizens directly into contact with the eu institutions, bridge the gap between them and ensure that the eu institutions address issues of importance to citizens. the european citizens' initiative will allow citizens to ask the european commission directly to initiate legislation. the commission will decide what action should be taken in the light of successful citizens' initiatives. the european parliament, by organising public hearings or adopting resolutions, will be able to help achieve these objectives. as this initiative is new, it would be useful for the commission to report every three years on the implementation of this regulation and to propose the necessary amendments, if necessary. in order to ensure the effective application of the initiative, complicated administrative procedures should be avoided. it is also necessary to ensure that the initiative complies with eu data protection requirements.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the right of initiative of citizens of the european union, as provided for in the treaty of lisbon, is an extremely important step towards closer relations between the european union and european citizens. the new initiative will give citizens the same powers of political initiative as the european parliament and the council. it will also improve their chances of being heard and help to draw the attention of the european institutions to some of the issues of importance to society. such a relationship is mutually beneficial. the initiative will ensure that the european union institutions address specific problems of concern to citizens. the european parliament will also be able to help citizens to achieve these objectives by all the means at its disposal, by supporting selected citizens' initiatives, for example by organising public hearings or adopting resolutions. however, the european union must ensure that the implementation of the initiative complies with the data protection requirements of the european union and that the implementation process of the initiative is fully transparent from start to finish. only by ensuring a safe environment for citizens to submit proposals will we be able to win their trust and stimulate their interest in the work of the european union.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the european citizens' initiative is a new instrument of participatory democracy at european level, an instrument which should be used by citizens themselves and which aims to strengthen direct democracy, active citizenship and the influence of european citizens on the policies of the european union. this initiative was provided for in the treaty establishing a constitution for europe and subsequently in the treaty of lisbon, with the aim of giving citizens the same powers of political initiative as those enjoyed by the council of ministers and the european parliament. it is good that the report encourages the use of modern technology as a suitable instrument for participatory democracy. the initiative will bring citizens and institutions directly into contact, bridge the gap between them and ensure that the eu institutions address specific issues of concern to citizens.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the position prepared by the european parliament on the citizens' initiative, as i fully support this new right of citizenship provided for by the lisbon treaty. i am optimistic that this could be one of the main ways of bringing the eu institutions closer to ordinary people. finally, the active citizens will be able to have a direct influence on decisions taken at eu level. it must be admitted that, at a time when many european initiatives are receiving a fairly critical public response, the citizens' initiative has received a mostly positive assessment and support from the outset. i hope that in the end we will have clear rules, free from unnecessary bureaucratic requirements, which will help the european public to express its views.
in writing. − i would like to draw your attention to certain very important issues that are missing from the commission work programme for next year. these concern issues related to human rights. does the commission intend to initiate any specific actions or legislation in the area of human rights during the next year? we have heard that vice-president of the commission and high representative for foreign and security policy baroness ashton intends to pay special attention to human rights during her term in office. however, there has been very little attention paid so far to human rights in her actions and statements. the external action service is also meant to beef up eu action in the area of human rights, but now that there is no eu budget for 2011 the eeas will not be able to fully undertake its duties as from 1 january 2011. can you, mr president, tell us what your plan b is and how you intend to strengthen the eu’s credibility when it comes to defending and promoting human rights in the world?
in writing. − i voted in favour of this joint european parliament resolution because it discusses and assesses the priorities of the european commission's activities in 2011 and the challenges for the future. at the last plenary session in parliament in strasbourg, commission president josé manuel barroso presented the commission's work programme for the coming year, and the european parliament therefore believes that it is essential to establish a dialogue with the commission and to pay particular attention to the eu's key strategic objectives. parliament also called on the commission to ensure that the timetable for the most important proposals is realistic and clearer, so that it is effective, adapted to the real situation and better implemented than before. i would like to point out that, although the commission is committed to creating new jobs as quickly as possible and to accelerating the implementation of the europe 2020 strategy, unfortunately the commission's programme for the coming year in the field of employment and social affairs does not contain any concrete proposals for the creation of new and quality jobs. as in last year's action programme, the commission is again proposing the same legislative initiatives on the posting of workers and working time and is further committed to improving the rights of migrant workers across the european union. a comprehensive recovery therefore requires a common european strategy for sustainable growth and jobs, based on the necessary mandates and resources.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because the eu and the african union need to address together key common challenges in order to successfully implement the africa-european union partnership, which is based on the mutual interest in exploiting the common potential. the eu and the african union have agreed to continue to work together in the framework of the african union (au) and the african union (au) to promote the development of the african continent. i therefore support calls for all actors to take a stronger political stance in the future to make clear to african countries that the eu believes strongly in the rule of law and democracy. it is also important that all african union member states ratify the african union charter on democracy, elections and governance and fully support the work of the international criminal court. this would ensure the principles of democracy, the rule of law and respect for human rights, while opening up the possibility of effective cooperation in economic, social, cultural and other fields.
in writing. - i voted for this resolution. it is necessary to further develop the strategic partnership between africa and the european union in order to jointly address common challenges, promote sustainable economic growth, and expand cooperation in the fields of energy, trade, climate change. the development of cooperation requires strong support for the fight against poverty, the protection of human rights, including social, economic and environmental rights, and the promotion of peace and security on the african continent. i welcome the cooperation between africa and the eu in the field of renewable energy and the commitments to provide modern and sustainable energy services to the people of africa, to increase the use of renewable energy in africa and to improve energy efficiency across all sectors. the eu and africa should join efforts to reduce carbon emissions from deforestation and forest loss and take other effective measures to combat climate change. it is necessary to provide the necessary funding for the implementation of the actions identified, ensuring effective parliamentary oversight of the use of eu financial assistance.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report. the effective protection and promotion of human rights and fundamental freedoms are the cornerstones of democracy and the rule of law in the eu. i therefore support the commitment of the treaty of lisbon to ensure the protection of fundamental human rights, to combat social exclusion and discrimination, and to promote social justice and social protection. to achieve this, it is necessary to ensure cooperation between the european union and national institutions by establishing an effective interinstitutional cooperation framework for monitoring the human rights situation in the eu. the commission should monitor the implementation of human rights legislation at national level on an ongoing basis and, if shortcomings are identified, propose specific measures. it would be useful to draw up an annual report on the situation of fundamental rights in the eu. ensure that eu citizens are properly informed about the fundamental rights framework. in addition, the eu institutions should enhance cooperation with international organisations in the field of human rights.
in writing. − i welcome the fact that, with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, we have made important progress in the field of human rights, firstly because the charter of fundamental rights of the european union has become legally binding and secondly because the european union is obliged to accede to the convention for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms. i voted in favour of this report because it is of the utmost importance for the european union's strategy on the rights of the child to develop practical measures to combat violence against children, sexual exploitation and child pornography, and to develop measures to ensure safe use of the internet, child labour and the eradication of child poverty. another important and urgent human rights challenge is the fight against trafficking in human beings, especially women and children. despite the fact that a number of eu and national laws have already been adopted in this area, several hundred thousand people are trafficked into or within the eu each year, and the new proposed eu directive on combating trafficking in human beings needs to be implemented as a matter of urgency.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because it is necessary to take measures to prevent unfair commercial practices in the field of advertising, which in particular have a negative impact on consumers. in order to achieve real results, it is important to strengthen european cooperation in the fight against unfair practices in online advertising, and the most vulnerable (children, adolescents, elderly, etc.) must be given special protection. it should be noted that there is a lack of information on consumer rights in advertising, and i therefore support the proposal to facilitate access to information and increase transparency. in addition, it is necessary to encourage consumers to be critical of the media in terms of the quality of the content, because a well-informed consumer is a stronger consumer. i think that in the absence of knowledge of advertising techniques, it would be effective to develop a specific educational programme for children and adults to help them better understand, interpret and manage the advertising medium.
in writing. − with the rapid development of advertising technologies and the proliferation of advertising on the internet, mobile phones and social networks, effective measures are needed to protect the consumer from the effects of unwanted and misleading advertising. particular attention should be paid to the most vulnerable groups of persons, including children, adolescents. it is also necessary to strengthen cooperation between member states in the fight against unfair practices in online advertising in order to avoid distortions of the internal market and the consequences of unfair practices. it is also necessary to provide consumers with more information on their rights in the field of advertising, to make this information more accessible and to increase its transparency. the commission should regularly monitor and evaluate the application of the legislation regulating unfair commercial practices in the member states and draw up reports on the implementation of the legislation. as information on the social and psychological effects of advertising on the consumer is still lacking, it is necessary to take steps to investigate these effects.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it deals with unfair commercial practices in advertising and also focuses on the problems associated with the development of new advertising technologies and activities. i would like to point out that advertising has a major impact on equality between women and men, especially where advertising often contains discriminatory and/or degrading information based on various gender stereotypes. i therefore support the european parliament's call for the european commission and the member states to ensure that media and advertising professionals ensure respect for human dignity and make every effort to combat discrimination and hatred on grounds of sex, racial or ethnic origin, age, religion or other belief, sexual orientation, disability or social status. as regards the protection of vulnerable groups, the commission should carry out an investigation into the impact of misleading and aggressive advertising on vulnerable consumers. i would like to stress that children, adolescents and the elderly in particular need to be protected from the negative effects of advertising.
in writing. − i have supported this report because it deals with unfair commercial practices in advertising as provided for in the unfair commercial practices directive and focuses on the problems associated with the development of new advertising technologies and activities. advertising is a tool that benefits both the internal market as a kind of fuel for economic activity (it stimulates competition, competitiveness, innovation and creativity) and consumers (it offers a wide range of choices and lowers prices). it is a major economic sector, accounting for more than eur 14 billion of the european market for online advertising alone. however, it should not be idealised: dishonest activities, intrusion into public (e.g. advertising booths) and private (e.g. unsolicited emails) spaces, advertising to vulnerable people (e.g. children, people with high debts), creating potential barriers to entry into the internal market (when the necessary advertising costs are too high), distortion of the internal market (buying goods or services that consumers would not normally buy) can also lead to significant losses. in view of these and other problems arising from the use of new technologies, the rapporteur therefore proposes a series of measures to protect consumers. although the targeting of advertising to individuals is not a problem in itself (the goods or services offered correspond to the interests of the consumer, etc.), it should not encourage the creation of addictive advertising based on the tracking of consumers' online activities and which infringes the principles of data protection and privacy.
in writing. - modern advertising has long outgrown the legal regulation of this area. online advertising - in search engines, e-mail, social networks, online television and mobile phones - is often delivered to consumers without their consent, often using data collected about them, sometimes without their knowledge or full awareness. moreover, there are endless loopholes in the legal regulation of such advertising (e.g. even after alcohol advertising has been banned on television, it is still available to minors on internet portals). on the other hand, online advertising very easily crosses national borders. the initiative to strengthen the regulation of such advertising, protecting consumers, their privacy and personal data against illegal or disproportionate use, is therefore very welcome.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because we are committed to the environment, to finding cost-effective energy sources and to combating climate change. sustainable development is needed, but economic development is also important. these two aspects must be reconciled. you can't fight climate change without thinking about energy efficiency. this requires detailed, realistic targets and penalties to ensure that energy consumption is not based on economic benefits alone. energy efficiency is most relevant in buildings, as they have a huge potential for energy savings. i therefore support the rapporteur's view that there is a need to focus on the renovation of existing buildings, as the number of new buildings in the eu is decreasing and most old buildings, if properly renovated, can make a very significant contribution to energy savings.
in writing. − while the adoption of the energy efficiency action plan in 2006 was a major step forward, the political and economic conditions have changed considerably since then. it is therefore necessary to review the eu's energy efficiency policy and align it with current priorities and trends. energy efficiency is the cheapest way to reduce co2 and other gas emissions. the benefits are significant and include both economic development and job creation. i believe that a careful assessment of the strengths and weaknesses of the 2006 energy efficiency action plan is necessary to provide a basis for a review of eu energy efficiency policy. it is becoming increasingly clear that the eu will not reach its 20% target and that more effective measures to promote energy efficiency are needed. the renovation of existing buildings needs to be given a high priority, as progress in this area has been limited so far. building construction in the eu is declining and most old buildings have the highest savings potential if they are properly renovated. this would also reduce the eu's overall energy dependence.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because concrete measures are needed to implement the energy efficiency action plan, especially as it becomes increasingly clear that the eu will not achieve its 20% target for reducing greenhouse gas emissions. i would like to point out that energy efficiency is the most cost-effective way to reduce co2 and other emissions. it also provides a unique opportunity to support and create new jobs while reducing dependence on energy imports. the european parliament therefore calls on the european commission to develop a new action plan for energy efficiency that takes into account the needs of vulnerable energy consumers. calls on the member states to adopt appropriate measures and effective policies, such as national action plans or targeted social measures to reduce energy deficits, and to report regularly on their actions in this regard; the commission should also be responsible for providing all statistical data on the development of the main elements of the energy policy of the european union.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it aims to increase energy efficiency by at least 20% by 2020 and to make progress in the transition to a sustainable and green economy. the benefits are significant and include both economic development and job creation. jobs will be created in both rural and urban areas. it is well known that buildings have enormous potential for energy savings. this is why there is a need to focus on the renovation of existing buildings, as new construction is declining in the eu and most old buildings have the highest savings potential when properly renovated. in the field of clean technologies, there is a need to bridge the gap between the us and china on the one hand and the eu on the other. both countries are ahead of the eu in adopting legislation to promote energy-saving decisions. the eu and the member states should therefore support measures that promote funding. the use of the financial instrument at european level should accelerate the establishment of national energy saving funds supporting energy efficiency treaties (eets). energy efficiency agreements (eeas), where the customer purchases the guaranteed amount of energy saved, are beneficial because the investment is recovered over a period of 2 to 15 years. this model creates jobs for smes, lowers energy bills for consumers and reduces emissions.
the rapporteur. - your excellency, it is a great pleasure to see you here today, taking part in this very important debate. the european council also welcomed the commission's intention to continue to work towards the establishment of a european union-wide network of contacts with third countries. the basis for this debate and the european parliament's resolution on which we will vote tomorrow is the annual report on the situation of human rights in the world and the european union's policy on the matter, adopted by the council of the european union. we cannot hold ms ashton responsible for the actions discussed in the report, i.e. in 2008/2009 as she was not yet in the position of high representative for foreign and security policy at that time. we understand the great responsibility you have both now and in the future, and i want to assure you that we in the european parliament will do everything to ensure that the european union's foreign policy objectives are achieved. mrs c. ashton, the treaty of lisbon has been in force for a year now, and the european external action service has only been operational for a couple of weeks. with its report today, the european parliament is sending a clear signal to all the institutions of the european union. the european parliament has clearly stated its position that the new european external action service should give priority to strengthening democracy and the protection of human rights in the world, and that this should be reflected in both the structure and funding of the service. this may be a human rights and democracy, international law directorate or a structure with a different name but the same content. what we should avoid is the marginalization or the pushing of human rights issues off the main agenda of the european union and the structure of the european external action service. to this end, the european parliament proposes to create, within the framework of the service, the posts of special representative for human rights, who would work in specific countries or regions, in particular where the european union has no diplomatic representation. these special representatives should have a clear mandate to protect human rights in the countries in which they work. i would also like to stress once again the need for the cohom human rights working group in brussels, especially since the vast majority of the member states of the european union support this idea. the report on which we will vote tomorrow here in the european parliament discusses and evaluates the policy of the european union, the activities of the european union as a whole and its institutions, covering the whole range of themes and issues: abolition of the death penalty, the fight against terrorism and human rights, the rights of the child, the fight against violence against women, the situation of human rights defenders in different countries of the world, freedom of religion or belief. we discuss and evaluate the european union's performance in international fora such as the united nations, the un human rights council, the european union's cooperation with the international criminal court. our report under consideration, which was very much involved in the preparation of my fellow meps and which was adopted by a huge majority in the committee on foreign affairs: 50 votes in favour, 0 against and 2 abstentions, is supplemented by a list of specific human rights violations in various countries which have been brought to the attention of the european parliament. these are specific names, countries, people's fates, lives lost. i would therefore like to conclude by mentioning a few specific cases of gross violations of human rights. you are well aware that the european parliament was to award the sakharov prize today, and you were present at the ceremony, to the cuban dissident guillermo farín. we could not do that because the cuban authorities did not allow mr g. farin to come to the european parliament. we hope that in the course of your duties you will take this fact into account and find a way to express our position, our disappointment and our regret and disapproval on this matter to the cuban authorities. i would also like to draw your attention to another sad case which we have referred to in our report. one of them was scheduled to be sentenced today by the russian chamovnichevsky court, but for reasons unknown, the sentencing was postponed until the end of the month. this is the trial of mikhail khodorkovsky and platon lebedev, which we believe is a mirror of the state of the judicial system and the lack of the rule of law in russia. i would urge you, mrs c. ashton, to remember these cases and to work to make justice the norm in russia, not the exception. i believe that the current russian opening up and president medvedev's drive to modernise russia is a very good opportunity to pursue this goal.
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − madam president, honourable members, on friday we celebrated international human rights day. this year’s theme highlighted the work of individuals and organisations worldwide who fight human rights violations, specifically discrimination. over this past year i have met nobel prize winner shirin ebadi on iran and frontline award winner, afghanistan’s human rights commissioner for women dr soria sabhrang, and other human rights defenders across the world and i will continue to do so. as i have expressed previously, i fully expect my colleagues in brussels and the heads of our eu delegations to do the same. six months ago i stood before you to present a first vision of how the european union should conduct its policy on human rights. today i want to set out how work has been proceeding since then and how i see the way ahead now with the support of the european external action service. but first of all i am very grateful to dr andrikienė for her report which responds to the eu’s annual report on human rights and sets out the european parliament’s vision for how we can make our approach to human rights in the european union more effective. this is an ambition which i strongly share. the range of eu action and challenges we face are well reflected in the report before the house today: attacks on human rights defenders, sexual violence, use of new technologies to curb freedom of expression, to name but a few. i want to pay tribute to dr andrikienė for bringing together more than 400 amendments in this impressive, informative and extremely useful report. the report covers a lot of ground and i want to pick up three important developments of recent months. first and foremost, the eu has been working hard to advance the cause of human rights on the multilateral stage. in a successful session of the un human rights council, the european union held a common position on potentially divisive resolutions concerning the gaza flotilla and the goldstone report. at the general assembly third committee, the eu also achieved its chief objectives: resolutions on burma, dprk and the death penalty were all passed with increased majorities – as was the canadian resolution on iran – and the eu resolution on eliminating religious intolerance again met with consensus. secondly, as announced in june, work has begun on a review of eu human rights policy. it has been an inclusive process for which i sought input from member states’ parliamentarians, notably ms hautala and the subcommittee on human rights, as well as civil society ngos and academics. i will be asking the service and my senior team in the months ahead to consider the key themes arising from these consultations and how best we can put them into practice. i will count on parliament’s continued support in this endeavour. third, work has begun on streamlining the patchwork of policies which have grown up over the past ten years and which make up the eu's human rights policy guidelines: toolkits, other instruments, the guidelines we have got for promoting and protecting human rights. there are good reasons why policy has grown in such an organic way, but it seems like a good time to take stock and to move on and, for that ongoing work on the review, i see three lines of action. first of all, the need for europe to continue to speak up for human rights on the global stage. we are working to strengthen our action at the united nations and to resist attempts to dilute universal standards, the basis of our action. we need to find innovative ways of working with third-country partners to promote our shared values, as we have done successfully in the un general assembly vote on the death penalty resolution. we are also investing to ensure that our own record stands up to scrutiny. second, we need to tailor our approach to individual situations. that means establishing local human rights strategies for each country, reviewing our priorities and the most effective use of our assorted tools, for example by sharing experiences on child protection on the internet or how best to tackle child labour. third and finally, human rights should be visibly at the centre of eu external action. that means working human rights into the activities of all parts of the external action service, as well as the whole range of eu external action: trade, development, csdp and so on, and at all levels. that will be built into the structure at headquarters as well as throughout our delegations in order to be able to monitor the human rights situation and promote an effective realisation of eu human rights policy goals. human rights are the core of our eu identity and they go to the heart of what we do around the world. we have developed strong sets of mechanisms for promoting these values in different contexts with different partners; in the multilateral context and through support for civil society; funding specific human rights projects in over one hundred countries. nearly ten years on from the very first eu communication on human rights, and with the establishment of the new service, i want to ensure our human rights policy is effective, innovative and targeted: the silver thread that runs through all of our external action and a gold standard for our foreign policy. that is why i particularly welcome today the contribution in this report and i would also end by congratulating guillermo fariñas on the award of the sakharov prize for freedom of thought.
on behalf of the ppe group thank you, chairman. my dear mrs ashton! first of all, i would like to congratulate mrs andrikienes on the success of her report, which was adopted by virtually unanimous vote in the committee on foreign affairs. i also want to thank mrs. ashton for her very informative speech. the report on human rights in the world confirms the critical state of human rights in countries with which the european union has been engaged in human rights dialogues and consultations for many years. the report, for example, highlights that russia has still only implemented the first of the six-point agreement on georgia. half a million people are still unable to return to their homes in south ossetia and abkhazia. the european union has a serious responsibility to address such situations and i therefore call on the high representative to pay particular attention to addressing these issues. it would be unacceptable for the european union to spend a great deal of time and financial resources on human rights dialogues without producing good results. it is therefore clear that the european union's human rights policy needs to be substantially improved. first, by implementing a precise human rights strategy that sets out not only the tasks but also the structure to ensure regular performance evaluation and review of operational tactics. secondly, the european union's assessment of the human rights situation must be based solely on experience and certain criteria. we must not be swayed by pressure from a third country or by economic interests. thirdly, regular consultations with ngos at european union level should be introduced. ensuring human rights and democracy must be a priority for the eeas and an explicit criterion in bilateral agreements with third countries. thank you.
on behalf of the alde group, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur and all the members who tabled amendments and participated in the debate on their interest and efforts in producing and improving this report on the situation of human rights in the world and the european union's policy in this area. i think that the rapporteur and her colleagues on the subcommittee on human rights and the committee on foreign affairs have taken up this challenge and have done a good job. the ideas and help we received from ngos helped us to improve the report. i would therefore urge all members to vote in favour of this report, which, after lengthy debates, amendments and discussions, reflects well the european parliament's position on human rights. as regards the amendments tabled, i would urge my colleagues not to elaborate on matters which have already been the subject of extensive discussion in committee. it is true that human rights problems abound, but there are also many countries that could be mentioned and called upon. however, the value of the report lies in its conciseness. the report would lose its effectiveness if it were to be extended indefinitely, and some of the amendments under consideration do not seem to me to be necessary. i would also like to draw the attention of my colleagues to a number of important changes which were not in the previous version of the report. among them is the amendment tabled by my group on sodium thiopental, calling for the production and sale of this substance, which can be used for capital punishment, to be allowed only for medical purposes. the adoption of this amendment would be an important step in the fight against the death penalty worldwide. i would ask my colleagues to support another amendment by our group calling on the european commission to take further action in implementing the commitments of the 2007 human rights report to strengthen efforts to combat violence. this document is not only a guide for the european commission, the council and the member states, but also a very strong, clear message for the european union's neighbours, partners and other countries where human rights are not sufficiently respected and protected. i hope that all stakeholders and institutions will take this report seriously and adopt its valuable recommendations.
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − madam president, i should like to thank everyone for all the contributions to this debate, which i found extremely useful. can i just say that i am on the council side now but i was on the commission side earlier today? i move between the two, and we do keep a record to make sure that i move between the two, so you must just catch me when i am here. again, i would like to thank ms andrikienė very very much for the work that she has done, and congratulate her on the report, which has been extremely helpful. i am going to respond on three particular areas. i will begin with the issue which ms hautala raised right at the end and which a number of colleagues have mentioned. let me start by being absolutely clear: i talk about mainstreaming because i have seen, too often in my life, human rights relegated to the corner of an organisation and done as an afterthought in a way that is not going to make any kind of difference. i am not suggesting that is what the eu has done before. i am suggesting that i am determined to make sure it cannot happen again. as you will see in the structure when we finalise it, and we have not yet done so – human rights will be fully recognised in the structure and we are going to make sure we have the expertise that colleagues here have asked for within the structure. but i do not want it to sit in a corner. i want it to be part of everything that we do: a silver thread, not a filigree thread, a silver thread that is strong but that shines and is there for everyone to witness. that is what i want to achieve in my time in office and i feel 100% committed to trying to achieve that. it will take time and there will be things we cannot do as well as i would wish, but we will absolutely do our best. it matters to me, however, that this parliament understands what i am trying to do and that i am trying to make sure that it is everybody’s responsibility. yes, it should be monitored; yes, we need expertise; but it is not down to just a few people within the eas. it is the responsibility of everybody. that is the first thing to say. the second thing is that you will see the structures very soon, and it is in there. it is there for you all to witness as part of what we will be doing. on the working group chairs, we are going to put forward our proposals for appointments. i will be appointing the working group chair who will be responsible for human rights, and that will enable them to pick up the point that was made about whether capitals should have people gathered from the capitals or whether it should be based in brussels. we will pick that point up in that conversation. finally on structures: we have already done the training programme for new heads of delegations, and one session was about human rights. they are very clear about their responsibilities and about my and your expectations in that regard. so please accept that it is part – and a fundamental core – of the structure, but i will be determined to make it everybody’s responsibility. the second point was about the breadth of the issues. i recognise what a number of people have said about religious faith and i accept that this is an important issue. it was, in fact, part of our discussions at the foreign affairs council this week as well. colleagues have also mentioned the need to address the rights of women, of children and of lesbian, gay, transgender and bisexual people. i read into all that was said, about the different people and the different countries, the fundamental point about human rights, which is this: for them to apply to you, you simply need to be human and here. those are the criteria for human rights and that is, again, how we will approach this within the external action service. they belong to everybody. the third thing i wanted to say was just a bit about some of the things we are beginning to see happen and in which we are playing a part. this is particularly true on some of the work that we are doing internationally. the international criminal court this year saw four countries recognise and ratify the statute. that is very important because we played a part: i personally wrote to the ministers in every country that had not yet ratified, asking them to do so. these were countries which responded directly to us and which have ratified. equally, i would mention the importance, in the un general assembly committee, of trying to extend support for the moratorium on the death penalty. i think this is a core and fundamental part of the work that we should do. it is universal, it is something we all believe in, and it is the way forward that we should be practical in what we do. so three areas: firstly, making sure the structures are right and the people know and understand the core work they need to do; secondly, ensuring that we cover everyone in the work we do on human rights; and thirdly, ways in which we can make a difference. the final point i want to make is how i think that, more than anything, we have to look, within the review, for practical and innovative ways of getting our messages across. i want us to be really creative in working with people on the ground in different countries where we meet human rights defenders – and i do wherever i go. i want us to be innovative and creative in finding new ways of raising these issues of human rights, new ways beyond what is so important in any event: the statements that i make, the statements that we make as 27 member states and the statements that we make as the institutions of the european union. it is about finding ways in which we can do as much as possible to address the concerns. it is about individuals, organisations and the collective will to be able to make a real difference.
rapporteur. − madam president, first of all i have been told that the english interpretation of my speech was not accurate, and i am very sorry about this. immediately after this discussion i will translate my speech into english myself and will send it to all the colleagues concerned and also to the high representative. secondly, i would like to thank all the colleagues who participated in today’s debate for their positive assessment and for their critical remarks. with regard to my colleagues mr obermayr and mr poręba, who spoke about christian minorities in different countries, saying that this issue is not addressed in the report, i would like to draw their attention to paragraph 126 among others. we have a chapter that deals with freedom of religion, and christian minorities are also mentioned in some specific paragraphs. on a final note, i would like to assure baroness ashton that parliament will continue to scrutinise closely the work of the european external action service and will criticise inaction or the ineffectiveness of action taken in the area of human rights. in this year’s report, we clearly state that we are not satisfied by the fact that the eu still does not have clear benchmarks for assessing whether actions on human rights have produced results. we have also expressed our disappointment at the lack of progress achieved by the human rights dialogues and consultations. baroness ashton, parliament has come a long way to find common ground on human rights issues, especially the most sensitive ones. this shows that parliament stands united in the promotion of democracy and the protection of human rights in the world. it also shows that the days when parliament would merely applaud the commission or the council are over. parliament is now a serious player in the area of human rights. all actors operating in this field will have to take note of that. i would like to thank all those who have taken part in this debate.
citizens often debate the future of the european union. the foundation of a strong union and its future success depend on trust and mutual understanding, just as in the family. the european heritage label is a way for people to get to know each other, to discover the objects, the people and the movements that symbolise the creation of europe. the identification of a common cultural heritage is a way of establishing a common and united europe. all nations, all states, we all have different, diverse historical experiences, often painful, but it is these differences that make up our family, that form our european identity. i would like to highlight in particular the long-standing division of europe, the different possibilities or the complete absence of any for the development of democracy. i'm talking about eastern europe and the soviet occupation. but there has always been a struggle for the idea of a united europe and we have to assess the... i congratulate the rapporteur, doris pack, and fully support her recommendation on the 'youth in action' and lifelong learning programme and the participation of switzerland in this programme. i would say that switzerland is not only a beautiful and orderly country, but it is very interesting for the european union because of its direct democracy, because of the cantonal confederation. on the other hand, the european union cannot be uninteresting to switzerland, since switzerland is entirely surrounded by the countries of the european union. and finally, switzerland is matching its pulse with the european union. i think that this programme will make it possible to do that even better. switzerland will be closer to the european union, the european union will understand switzerland better, and, as our colleague doris pack has said, better results can be expected in the referendums.
i thank you for your work, ms doris pack, and i certainly support your recommendation that the european parliament should support the council decision on the participation of switzerland in the 'youth in action' and lifelong learning programmes, as the commission's agreement with the swiss authorities meets all the requirements, provisions and conditions. in addition, the swiss national agency will cooperate on the implementation of the programmes at national level and will contribute financially to each programme on an annual basis. this bilateral agreement is of great importance both for switzerland and for the european union, as young people in switzerland will be able to take advantage of the various opportunities offered by the 'youth in action' programme and vice versa. young european union citizens will be able to participate in all projects together with young swiss people. i am therefore pleased that switzerland, although not a member of the european union's economic area, is not a european union state, but cooperates closely with the european union in the fields of education, training and youth.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution on the commission's proposal to use the european heritage label as one of the most effective means of bridging the gap between the european union and its citizens, by highlighting that europe's history is made up of a diverse and at the same time rich and complex common cultural heritage. i agree that the decision by the member states to implement the european heritage label initiative at eu level would not only provide greater visibility, prestige and credibility, but would also contribute to the long-held ideal of cohesion and solidarity among european citizens. i support the rapporteur's position that, given the symbolic importance of the sign promoting european integration, special attention should be given to heritage sites of international value, as they promote community-building and encourage links and cooperation between member states or regions. i believe that the idea of the european union heritage label, which aims to strengthen the sense of connection of european citizens with their common heritage (through the knowledge of history and participation in activities aimed at promoting intercultural dialogue) and to help to understand the eu's creation as a long ongoing process, is a step in this direction.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because european history is made up of a diverse and at the same time rich and complex common cultural heritage. it is a further demonstration of the european union's founding values of freedom, democracy, respect for human rights, cultural diversity, tolerance and solidarity. the european parliament, in the light of the acknowledged contribution of all european cultures to world culture, endorses the idea of a label which would be widely used outside the european union and used as an ambitious instrument of external policy. in order to achieve the political objective of the new european heritage label, it is proposed to pay particular attention to the symbolic and/or illustrative value of heritage sites. in addition, in order to share best practices and initiate joint projects, close cooperation between heritage sites that have been awarded a heritage label should be encouraged. i would like to emphasise that the decision by the member states to implement the european heritage label initiative at eu level would not only ensure greater visibility, prestige and credibility, but also contribute to the long-held ideal of cohesion and solidarity among european citizens.
